# SHJ3 — Delivery Plan

> Created 2026-09-08 · Phase A in progress
> Each phase is gated. A phase is not done until its gate passes: **build + lint + typecheck + unit + integration + E2E + security scan**, plus the tenant-isolation suite, which gates every phase without exception.

---

## Phase A — Intake & Design

**Gate:** every item below written to `docs/` before the first line of implementation code.

### Decisions (answered by the product owner, 2026-09-08)

- [x] Architecture style — **Modular monolith** (across two runtimes, per ADR-0001)
- [x] Tenancy model — **Multi-tenant, schema-per-tenant** (+ Neo4j DB-per-tenant, Qdrant collection-per-tenant, per ADR-0002)
- [x] Backend stack — **Next.js (web/BFF) + Python FastAPI & Google ADK (AI)**, SQL Server + Neo4j + Qdrant + Redis
- [x] Frontend stack — **Next.js App Router**
- [x] UI library & design system — **shadcn/ui + Tailwind**, runtime CSS-variable tokens (ADR-0007)
- [x] Model providers — **OpenRouter** (chat), **OpenAI** `text-embedding-3-large` (embeddings), **Cohere** `rerank-v3.5` (ADR-0004)
- [x] Data access — **Prisma owns the schema**, generated SQLAlchemy for Python (ADR-0005)
- [x] Auth — **local accounts now behind a port**, SSO/UAE PASS later (ADR-0006)
- [x] Deployment targets — **Docker, Compose, Helm**; CI/CD declined (ADR-0008)
- [x] Theming scope — **[ASSUMPTION]** resolution user → tenant → system default; tenant branding is admin-only; default + dark skins ship. *Confirm with product owner.*

### Open before build starts

Blocking or near-blocking:

- [x] **RISK-003 CLOSED 2026-09-08 — resolved negative.** No Neo4j Enterprise licence. [ADR-0009](../docs/adr/0009-logical-graph-partitioning.md) supersedes ADR-0002 for the graph store: single Community database with logical partitioning. **B-0 is unblocked.** Replaced by the standing **RISK-024** — graph isolation is now enforced in application code with no infrastructure or database-level fallback, reviewed every release.
- [ ] **RISK-001** resolve residency conflict — signed exception, or swap to self-hosted BGE-M3 + `bge-reranker-v2-m3`. Blocks production, not development.
- [ ] **RISK-004** start UAE PASS onboarding now — long-lead procurement, independent of code readiness.

- [ ] **RISK-010** decide whether tenant provisioning is an in-product `Super Admin` operation rather than a runbook step. Recommended — atomicity is easier to guarantee in code than in a runbook.

### Wireframe self-contradictions

Documenting the requirements surfaced eleven places where the source specification disagrees with itself. Nine are settled; two still need an answer.

Still open — each has a working assumption, so nothing is blocked:

- [ ] **RISK-006** — assurance ladder. Assumed four levels L0–L3, with L3 (document-verified) gating nothing yet. *Confirm the ladder, and whether kiosk account linkage should require L3.*
- [ ] **RISK-008** — KPI definitions. Assumed containment = resolved without a human; deflection = would otherwise have become a channel contact, and a subset of containment. *Confirm both, and the baseline each target is measured against.* Stored as counts with rates computed on read, so settling this later needs no migration and no backfill.

Decided by the product owner, 2026-09-08:

- [x] **RISK-005** — grounding-threshold precedence. Below threshold: escalate automatically where the flow has a handover node and agents are staffed; otherwise refuse with the no-agent-available message.
- [x] **RISK-022** — retention. `memoryRetentionDays` is **retired**; B14 tab 4 becomes the single retention authority. B11 tab 5 keeps only its scope control, and B14's 7-year option becomes reachable.
- [x] **RISK-023** — tool catalogue is **per-entity**. No cross-entity share mechanism; adding one would need its own ADR. Chosen because platform-global would put one entity's mTLS credentials in another's reach.
- [x] Theming scope — resolution user → tenant → system default, tenant branding admin-only, default + dark skins ship.

Closed by requirement or construction:

- [x] **RISK-007** — Arabic readiness is two numbers; the gate must name *every* failing condition, not the first.
- [x] **RISK-009** — publish vs deploy separated onto three tables, so neither operation can be performed by accident while doing the other.
- [x] **RISK-012** — payment idempotency keys moved off unbacked Redis onto the SQL transaction row; ADR-0003 amended.

## Phase B — Implementation

Ten slices. Order is not arbitrary: tokens and tenancy must exist before any feature code, config must exist before the runtime that reads it, and knowledge must exist before the runtime that grounds answers in it.

### B-0 · Foundation

Nothing here is a feature; everything here is expensive to retrofit.

**A load-bearing defect found, fixed, then fixed better (2026-09-08).** The isolation suite proved `getTenantDb()`'s core mechanism — one `PrismaClient` per tenant via a `schema=` connection-string parameter — silently did not scope queries at all: every tenant's client emitted identical SQL against the literal `tenant_template` schema, confirmed by reading the actual generated SQL text, not inferred. Had this shipped, every future feature module's ORM writes for every government entity would have landed in one shared schema.

Root cause chased in two passes. First pass (ADR-0010): concluded Prisma cannot do per-tenant schema routing via connection string at all, and designed a raw hand-built SQL query layer to replace the ORM for all ~112 tenant models. **Before that was built**, a minimal single-model no-`multiSchema` Prisma client was tested directly against the live database and correctly routed both reads and writes via the identical connection-string mechanism — proving the real defect was narrower: Prisma's `multiSchema` feature specifically overrides connection-string routing, not "Prisma can't do this." ADR-0011 supersedes ADR-0010 same-day: split the Prisma schema into two separate, non-multiSchema files (platform / tenant), restoring full ORM ergonomics for 107 of 112 tenant models, at the cost of 5 relationships that cross the platform/tenant boundary becoming plain foreign-key fields with application-level joins instead of Prisma relation sugar.

The already-running implementation agent was working from the first (correct but suboptimal) plan — caught and redirected mid-flight before it wrote any code, once the better option was confirmed. Lesson captured in `tasks/lessons.md`: verify the *scope* of a "this doesn't work" finding at the smallest reproducible case before designing a fix around the broadest reading of it.

Two architectural decisions in this session required stopping for the user's explicit confirmation rather than proceeding on the general "continue autonomous" authorization: the live-database reset needed for the `rowCount` fix (Prisma's own safety guard required it), and the choice between the raw-SQL and schema-split fixes for this defect (twice — the trade-off changed materially between the two ADRs, so the user was asked again rather than treating the first answer as covering the revised choice).

**ADR-0011 implemented and independently verified (2026-09-08) — not just the implementing agent's self-report.** After the agent reported success, this was re-checked from scratch rather than accepted at face value, given how much rode on it: `pnpm verify` re-run (14/14), the real isolation suite re-run directly (26/26, all four spec files, against the live stack), and a **separate verification script**, written independently and using the real production `getTenantDb()` — provisioning two fresh tenants via the real `SqlStoreProvisioner` and `PrismaTenantRegistry`, seeding distinguishable data, and confirming each tenant's handle reads only its own row. All three independently corroborate the fix. Probe tenants and all throwaway scripts were torn down via the real `destroy()` path afterward — verified no leftover schema, registry row or file remains. The isolation suite's own SQL spec grew from what was failing 2 of 26 to a clean 14 tests within `sql.spec.ts` alone, all green.

A second real bug surfaced by the implementing agent along the way: `001_constraints.sql`'s idempotency guards check `sys.check_constraints`/`sys.foreign_keys` by name only, unscoped by schema — since a constraint name is unique per-schema, not per-database, the second-provisioned tenant can silently skip a constraint the first tenant already has. The agent's own 5 new FK constraints (from the relationship fixes) were scoped correctly via `parent_object_id`, but this pattern affects roughly 100+ pre-existing checks across the rest of the file. **Not yet fixed — tracked as a new risk below.** This is a real gap in the constraint-application idempotency story and should be closed before a second tenant is ever provisioned outside a test run.

**Gate status: 13/13 stages green** via `pnpm verify` (both runtimes). 741 TypeScript tests, 169 Python tests.

**Proven against real containers, not just fakes (2026-09-08).** Brought up the full local stack — SQL Server, Neo4j Community, Qdrant, Redis, all four via `docker compose`, plus a live `shj3-ai` FastAPI instance — and ran the actual `SqlStoreProvisioner`, `RedisStoreProvisioner`, `AiGraphProvisioner` and `AiVectorProvisioner` against it end to end: **create → verify true → destroy → verify false, for all four stores.** This is the first point in the build where the isolation architecture was checked against real infrastructure rather than only against unit-test fakes, and it found three genuine bugs no fake could have caught:

1. **`TranscriptExports.rowCount`** — SQL Server rejects a bare `rowCount` identifier as a syntax error in some statement positions (confirmed directly: even `CREATE TABLE #t (rowCount int)` fails). Renamed to `exportedRowCount` in `schema.prisma` and the audit trigger.
2. **`EXEC @param = CONCAT(...)`** — T-SQL's named-parameter grammar for `EXEC` does not accept a function call as a parameter value, only a literal or a variable. Fixed by assigning to a local `@summary` first. Confirmed with an isolated minimal repro before touching the real file.
3. **Qdrant server/client version mismatch** — `qdrant-client` resolved to 1.19.x but `docker-compose.yml` pinned the server at `v1.12.4`; collection-level metadata (which records the embedding model + dimension for RISK-016) is a server feature absent before Qdrant ~1.16, and older servers silently accept the field on `create_collection` and then return `None` on read — no error, just a `verify()` that fails with nothing in between to explain why. Bumped the pinned image to `v1.19.0`, matching the client; documented the constraint in `docker-compose.yml` and `deployment.md` §6.2 so it doesn't drift again.

Also found and fixed one real design defect in the provisioning router: `/vector/destroy` shared a request model with `/vector/create` and `/vector/verify` that required the embedding model/dimension pair, even though deletion has no use for it. Fixed by giving `QdrantProvisioner`'s embedding contract an honest `EmbeddingContract | None` type — `None` valid only for `destroy()`, with `create()`/`verify()` raising a clear `RuntimeError` if misused — rather than satisfying the old required-field validator with a fake placeholder value.

One live-database action required explicit user confirmation before proceeding: Prisma's own agent-safety guard blocked `migrate reset` (dropping the dev database to apply the `rowCount` rename) and required a human answer rather than inferring consent from the general "continue autonomous" instruction. Asked directly, user confirmed, proceeded.

- [x] Monorepo: `apps/web` (Next.js, **TypeScript only**), `apps/ai` (Python 3.12, uv), pnpm workspace
- [x] Tooling in the first commit — ESLint 9 flat config, Prettier, Ruff, mypy `--strict`, `pnpm verify`
- [x] **One verification command covering both runtimes.** `make` is absent on Windows, so `pnpm verify` runs the Python stages too — a single gate that only works on one platform is two gates with extra steps
- [x] Module-boundary enforcement — `eslint-plugin-boundaries` (web), `import-linter` (ai). **Both verified against deliberate violations**
- [x] The swap test — vendor imports banned in `domain/` and `application/`, enforced in both languages
- [x] Static gates: `no-raw-cypher` (ADR-0009), `no-unscoped-store-clients` (ADR-0002 rule 3), `no-hardcoded-design-values` (ADR-0007), `prisma-sqlalchemy-drift` (ADR-0005 rule 4). **All four verified against deliberate violations**
- [x] `docker compose` — both runtimes, worker and all four stores with healthchecks and dependency ordering
- [x] Prisma → SQLAlchemy generation + drift gate (136 models generating cleanly)
- [x] Tenant context binding — `AsyncLocalStorage` (web) / `contextvars` (ai); **no unscoped store client exported**
- [x] Tenant slug validation as a security control — the only path from input to a store identifier, since a schema, label and collection name cannot be bound parameters
- [x] **Four-store atomic provisioning** (FR-PLAT-02) — single visible commit point, per-store verify after create, compensating rollback in reverse order, erasure proved rather than assumed
- [x] **Tenant-aware Cypher builder** (ADR-0009) — dual encoding, closed label/relationship allowlists, and `Query` validates *its own output* so a forgotten predicate raises at construction
- [x] RBAC — B9's 7×8 matrix, deny-by-default, no wildcard and no role hierarchy. Matrix independently re-transcribed in the test so a transcription error fails rather than silently widening access
- [x] Prisma schema + constraint SQL — 135 models (23 platform + 112 tenant), 476 constraint objects (362 CHECK, 70 indexes, 33 triggers, a view, a stored procedure, computed columns, full-text search), applied live against real SQL Server
- [x] `packages/tokens` — three-layer token architecture (22 primitives / 147 emitted semantic + 4 build-time breakpoints / 41 component), skin schema validator treating an import as untrusted input, CSS emitter that re-validates every value at emit time, and the WCAG 2.1 AA contrast gate that blocks text pairs. Both shipped skins assert §8's published ratios to two decimal places
- [x] Store provisioner adapters (SQL / Neo4j / Qdrant / Redis) behind the ports — **now proven against real containers**, see above. **Deliberately asymmetric** — ADR-0003 gives Neo4j and Qdrant to `shj3-ai` as sole writer and the NetworkPolicy enforces it, so the web-side graph and vector provisioners hold no driver and call the AI service's internal provisioning API instead; the real work is `graph_provisioner.py` and `qdrant_provisioner.py`. SQL and Redis are done in-process. Erasure is proved against both graph encodings (ADR-0009 rule 6) rather than reported; the Qdrant collection records its embedding model and dimension so a same-dimension model swap fails instead of silently poisoning retrieval (RISK-016); the Redis sentinel is treated as *evidence*, not authority — `verify` reports the literal truth, nothing in the request path reads it, and `create` is the repair path (RB-11)
- [x] N-tenant migration orchestrator (`RunTenantMigrations`) — resumable, checksum-conflict detection on an edited-and-reapplied migration, fail-fast in production without treating a stopped run as success, `assertNoDivergence` gating deploys
- [x] `IdentityProvider` port + local adapter — Argon2id (OWASP RFC 9106 baseline params), TOTP hand-rolled on `node:crypto` against all five RFC 6238 Appendix B vectors (a new auth-path dependency needs more justification than usual with no CI scanning, RISK-002), opaque server-side Redis sessions (never JWTs, so B9's `Suspended` state ends a session *now*), lockout with progressive backoff. The session cookie's tenant segment is proven to be a lookup hint only, never an authorization input — including the sharper case where a user who is a genuine member of both tenants still cannot rebind a session issued for one to the other
- [x] Append-only audit log adapter — calls the hash-chained `usp_WriteAuditLogEntry` stored procedure (tamper-evidence lives in the database, not the adapter); found and fixed a real gap, `usp_WritePlatformAuditLogEntry` didn't exist for the platform-scoped sink
- [x] OpenTelemetry with one trace id spanning web → ai → tool call — unified on the W3C `traceparent` standard end to end (replacing an earlier ad hoc `x-trace-id`), both runtimes' real SDK propagators, not hand-parsed
- [x] **`tests/isolation/*.spec.ts` against real containers — the release gate.** 26/26 passing across four spec files (SQL, Redis, forged-tenant, cross-tenant-analytics) against the live stack, independently re-run and separately re-verified with a from-scratch script using the real production `getTenantDb()`. This suite is what caught the ADR-0010/0011 defect in the first place — proof the "test against real infrastructure early" discipline earns its cost.
- [x] Deterministic seed data matching the wireframe's sample state, two tenants minimum — **closed 2026-09-09 in B-2**, for the users/teams/roles slice: `pnpm db:seed:iam` (`scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts`), four real tenants (`sewa`/`customs`/`libraries`/`sharjah`), B9's exact five sample users. Agents/source-conflicts sample content remains open for whichever wave builds B-3/B-4.
- [x] **RISK-025 — closed 2026-09-08.** 311 of 362 tenant-section `sys.check_constraints` idempotency guards were schema-unscoped (a constraint name is unique per schema, not per database — the second tenant provisioned would silently miss any constraint the first already had). Rewrote all 311 with `parent_object_id = OBJECT_ID('[{{SCHEMA}}].[Table]')` scoping, mirroring the pattern ADR-0011's 5 FK checks already used. **Independently re-verified**, not just accepted: a from-scratch static audit of the file confirms zero unscoped guards remain in the tenant section and the scoped-guard count matches exactly (316 = 311 + ADR-0011's 5); the full gate and the real isolation suite were both re-run directly (14/14, 26/26) after the fix; no leftover probe schemas. The implementing agent's own two-tenant live proof (311/311 CHECK, 163/163 FK, identical on both) was corroborating, not the sole evidence.

**B-0 gate: closed.** All items above are done and independently verified against real infrastructure — foundation, tenancy enforcement, four-store provisioning, the Cypher builder, RBAC, local auth, N-tenant migrations, the audit log, distributed tracing, the isolation suite, and now constraint idempotency across N tenants. Two open items remain by design, not oversight: deterministic wireframe seed data (tracked above) and the theming/design-system component library, which is B-1's actual subject matter.

**Gate:** isolation suite green; boundary linters green; a migration applies to two tenants and resumes after an injected partial failure.

**Lesson recorded:** two gates were **silently passing on everything** when first written — `eslint-plugin-boundaries` could not resolve NodeNext `.js` specifiers to `.ts` sources, and `import-linter` needs `include_external_packages = true` for a forbidden contract to see third-party packages (plus `python -m importlinter.cli lint` exits 0 without running anything). Every gate now gets a deliberate violation fed to it before it is trusted. With no CI (RISK-002) these gates are the only enforcement, so a gate that never fires buys false confidence.

### B-1 · Design system & app shell

**Infra scaffolding — complete 2026-09-08, independently re-verified.** Before any of the 57 components, landed the ground the whole slice stands on:

- [x] **next-intl locale routing.** `app/[locale]/` is now the root for every page (root `app/layout.tsx` genuinely does not need to exist once there's no root `page.tsx` — confirmed with a real `next build`, not assumed from docs). `localePrefix: "always"` (`/en/...`, `/ar/...`) — chosen over `"as-needed"` because a bare, unprefixed URL cannot honestly answer "which locale is this" for a widget embedded in sharjah.ae or a staff bookmark. `dir` computed from locale via a lookup table (§11.2), not a ternary chain. Message catalogues (`messages/en.json`/`ar.json`) confirmed to have identical key sets. Independently re-verified: `pnpm --filter @shj3/web typecheck` and `build` clean, `GET /` → 307 → `/en`, `/ar` genuinely renders `dir="rtl"` (read the raw response, not assumed), full `verify.mjs` 14/14 with zero regressions.
- [x] **shadcn/ui baseline.** Hand-authored `components.json` (not the interactive wizard — our tokens predate shadcn's defaults and already match its naming convention, ADR-0007). `lib/utils.ts`'s `cn()` is the one canonical class-merge helper.
- [x] **Component-library lint boundary** — new `components` element in `eslint.config.mjs`'s boundaries config: `apps/web/src/components/**` may depend on itself and `shared` only, never on `platform`/`feature`/`crosscutting` — a component that reaches into backend code stops being swappable independently of the app that consumes it (design-system.md §5.1). `feature`/`crosscutting`/`app` extended to allow it.
- [x] Component test infra — new `components` vitest project (`*.test.tsx`, jsdom), proven with a real render via Testing Library, kept cleanly separate from the existing node-environment `unit` project.
- [x] **shadcn CLI bases scaffolded for the 14 atoms with a direct equivalent** (button, input, textarea, select, checkbox, radio-group, switch, badge, progress, avatar, label, separator, skeleton, tooltip) — done directly rather than delegated, specifically to avoid two parallel agents racing `pnpm add`/`shadcn add` writes to the same `package.json`. **Found and fixed a real breakage in the CLI's own current output**, not shipped as-is: every generated file imported `cn` from a stray npm package (`cn@0.2.6`) instead of this project's own documented `@/lib/utils` helper, and `class-variance-authority` + `lucide-react` were imported but never actually installed (`pnpm why` confirmed absent from the lockfile — a build that would have failed the moment anything exercised these files). Fixed: real deps installed, all 14 imports rewritten to `@/lib/utils`, the redundant `cn` package removed, typecheck and build both re-verified clean afterward.
- [x] **Reconciled 2026-09-10 (Phase D follow-up).** The 14 files (`apps/web/src/components/ui/{button,input,textarea,select,checkbox,radio-group,switch,badge,progress-bar,avatar,label,separator,skeleton,tooltip}.tsx`) were re-read in full and re-gated directly: `node scripts/gates/no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` → clean (`ok no-hardcoded-design-values`, 17 documented `design-gate-allow` exceptions elsewhere in the tree, none in these 14 files). No arbitrary-bracket values, no raw hex/rgb/hsl, no numeric z-index remain — every one now reads a real token (`var(--z-dropdown)`, `var(--focus-ring-width)`, etc.), each file's own doc comment names the original shadcn violation and its fix (e.g. `switch.tsx`'s RTL-thumb comment, `tooltip.tsx`'s "`z-50`/`rounded-[2px]` are all gone"). This was almost certainly closed incrementally as Wave 2A/2B adapted these exact files (see this section's own `Switch`/`ProgressBar`/`Tooltip` entries above) — the checkbox was simply never flipped afterward. Genuinely still open, named rather than silently folded in: this check covered only the token-gate violations the box's own text names; the broader "full spec in design-system.md §5.3 (states, RTL, required props)" adaptation was not separately re-audited component-by-component in this pass (Wave 2A/2B's own entries above document several of these directly — RTL thumb direction, required-prop shapes — but not as an exhaustive §5.3 checklist run against all 14).

**Enforcement gates for design-system.md §12 — complete 2026-09-08, independently re-verified.** Extended the existing `scripts/gates/*.mjs` pattern from B-0 (chosen over a parallel ESLint-custom-plugin mechanism, since `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` already partially implemented §12.1/§12.2 and the four other B-0 gates set the house style) rather than duplicating it:

- [x] `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` extended to the full §12.1/§12.2 list — colour functions (`rgb`/`hsl`/`oklch`), raw length literals, hardcoded `font-family`, numeric z-index (utility and CSS), and a paired-absence check that `outline-none` never ships without a replacement ring (§10.3).
- [x] `eslint-plugin-jsx-a11y` installed and wired (`recommended` preset, scoped to `apps/web/src/**`) — gives `jsx-a11y/tabindex-no-positive` (§12.2) plus its full static a11y net for free.
- [x] `scripts/gates/no-undefined-token.mjs` (new) — every `var(--x)` in feature code must name a real semantic/component token; a `--shj3-*` primitive reference outside `packages/tokens/` is separately banned. Imports `toCssCustomPropertyName` and the real name lists **from `@shj3/tokens` itself**, so the gate cannot drift from the emitter it's checking against. Carries one narrow, evidence-based exclusion for Radix's own runtime-injected `--radix-*` variables (found necessary because `components/ui/select.tsx` legitimately uses `--radix-select-trigger-height`).
- [x] `scripts/gates/no-raw-table.mjs` (new) — bans `<table` outside `components/patterns/data-table/`.
- [x] `scripts/gates/no-hardcoded-user-string.mjs` (new) — two checks: hardcoded JSX text/prop literals not routed through next-intl, and `message-placeholders-isolated` (§11.3 rule 3). The latter needed real research, not assumption: a plausible-looking `bidiIsolation` option exists, but belongs to the unshipped TC39 `Intl.MessageFormat` proposal, not next-intl — confirmed by reading the actually-installed `intl-messageformat`/`@formatjs/icu-messageformat-parser` source directly, which carries no bidi support at all. So this lint check is the *only* enforcement for that rule, not a backstop.
- [x] Deliberately not built: `badge-requires-label`/`icon-button-requires-label`/`no-bare-input` — these belong to required-prop shapes on the real components (Wave 2), not a parallel lint rule that could disagree with them.

Every rule proven against a real, deliberately-introduced violation with a negative control before being trusted (one of its own test fixtures caught a genuine bug in the outline/ring check along the way — an explanatory comment containing the word "ring" was masking a true positive, fixed by excluding comment lines from the window scan). Wired as three new `verify.mjs` stages (`gate:token-refs`, `gate:table`, `gate:i18n-strings`).

**Independently re-verified by me, not just accepted:** re-ran all three new gates directly (clean), `eslint . --max-warnings=0` (clean), `pnpm -r run typecheck` (clean), and the full `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — which now gets past every stage and fails **only** at `gate:tokens`, **entirely and exactly confined to the 14 shadcn scaffold files** (110 violations: arbitrary-value syntax, raw lengths, numeric z-index — the precise, expected, deliberately-deferred adaptation work, not a defect in the gate). Also fixed the one real loose end from the scaffolding step myself: the 14 files weren't Prettier-formatted, which was blocking the `format` stage; formatted them directly.

Landing this *before* Wave 2 writes any of the 57 real components was the point — retrofitting it after is exactly the "four-figure violation count" failure mode design-system.md §12 itself warns about. Wave 2 now inherits a gate set that will catch every violation as it's written, not months later.

**A11y test wiring — done directly, 2026-09-08.** `jest-axe@11` (not `vitest-axe`, which has no real stable release — its `latest` npm tag sits at `0.1.0` against `jest-axe`'s mature, stable `11.0.0`) plus `@testing-library/jest-dom`, registered once in `apps/web/vitest.setup.ts` rather than per test file. `jest-axe` ships **no types at all** (checked its actual package contents, not assumed) and the community `@types/jest-axe` is pinned to a stale pre-v4 API, so wrote `apps/web/src/types/jest-axe.d.ts` by hand from the package's real runtime source. **Two real TypeScript bugs found and fixed while doing it, not shipped broken:** (1) a `declare module "vitest" { ... }` augmentation without a preceding `import "vitest"` doesn't extend vitest's types, it *replaces* them — silently deleted `describe`/`it`/`expect` for every `*.test.ts` file in the repo until caught by a full `pnpm -r run typecheck` and fixed; (2) combining the `jest-axe` ambient module declaration and the `vitest` matcher augmentation in one file made the `jest-axe` half stop resolving for a reason not fully root-caused beyond "empirically, splitting them into two files fixes it" — done, matching how `@testing-library/jest-dom` itself ships its own vitest augmentation as a dedicated file. Verified end-to-end with a throwaway smoke test carrying a genuine negative control (an `<img>` with no `alt` — a real, known axe violation) to prove the matcher can actually fail, not just pass silently; deleted after confirming both directions.

- [x] Three-layer token architecture; full token catalogue — `packages/tokens`, see B-0
- [x] **Tailwind v4 wired to the token package, not a parallel system.** `bg-primary`, `rounded-md`, `p-4` etc. compile to `var(--primary)`, `var(--radius-md)`, `var(--space-4)` — never a literal value (grepped the generated CSS to confirm: zero hex literals in the theme bridge file). No `tailwind.config.ts` — v4's CSS-first `@theme inline` fully replaces it. The `@theme inline` block itself is **generated**, not hand-typed, from the same token name lists `@shj3/tokens` already exports, with its own drift gate (verified against an injected violation) as the repo's 14th standing gate. Font-size and line-height stay deliberately decoupled rather than force-paired, matching how the tokens are actually designed. Two Tailwind v4 namespace gaps found empirically (compiled and observed zero utility output, not assumed from docs): no `--z-*` or `--duration-*` theme namespace exists, so those tokens are consumed as raw `var(--z-modal)` in component CSS instead — design-system.md §12.2's own prescribed pattern, and the only route that doesn't fight the arbitrary-value ban.
- [x] **Found and fixed a repo-wide build blocker in the same pass:** `next build` could not resolve *any* NodeNext `.js`-suffixed import to its `.ts` source — this affects every file in the codebase, not the Tailwind work specifically. Fixed via `webpack.resolve.extensionAlias` in `next.config.ts`.
- [x] **Wave 2A (controls) — complete 2026-09-08, independently verified.** Button, IconButton, Input, Textarea, Select, Checkbox, RadioGroup, Switch, SelectablePill, Badge — all built, gate-clean, typecheck/lint-clean, 55/55 tests passing (own report), and independently spot-checked by me rather than accepted at face value: read `switch.tsx`/`selectable-pill.tsx` directly, and re-derived the hardest claim — `Switch`'s RTL thumb direction — completely from scratch via a throwaway probe route through the *real* `next build` pipeline (not the agent's pasted CSS, a fresh independent compile), confirming the production output genuinely emits `--tw-translate-x: 100%` under LTR and `-100%` under RTL for a checked thumb. Probe route deleted after, confirmed no trace. Found and fixed two shared vitest-infra gaps along the way (flagged proactively, not hidden): `components` project had no `@` path alias, and Testing Library's `afterEach(cleanup)` was never registered — both fixed in `vitest.config.ts`/`apps/web/vitest.setup.ts`, verified to help both waves' tests, not just its own.
- [x] **Gate false positive found and fixed, 2026-09-08.** `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs`'s arbitrary-*value* regex was also matching Tailwind's arbitrary-*variant* syntax (`data-[state=checked]:`, `group-data-[size=sm]/switch:`) — a CSS attribute-selector condition, not a design value at all. This forced Wave 2A into avoiding the whole `data-[...]:` family via hand-rolled JS state-mirroring, which every future Radix-heavy molecule/organism (Tabs, Dialog, DropdownMenu, Popover, Slider all lean on this pattern) would have kept re-paying. Root-caused rather than patched around per-component: added a negative lookahead so a bracket immediately followed by `:` (optionally after `/group-name`) — the position only a variant separator occupies — is excluded, while a real arbitrary value (never followed by `:`) still matches. Verified both directions before landing: all 6 real arbitrary-variant shapes now pass, all 8 real arbitrary-value violations (including the ones still live in the unadapted scaffold) still fail; full-repo gate re-run afterward shows zero regressions. Not a retroactive fix to Wave 2A's already-correct, already-tested components — just removes the tax on what comes next.
- [x] **Wave 2B (display/decoration) — complete 2026-09-08, independently verified.** MonoSubLine, ProgressBar, Avatar, Icon, Label, Separator, Skeleton, Spinner, Kbd, Tooltip — all built, gate-clean, typecheck/lint-clean, 111/111 tests passing across both waves combined (own report), independently spot-checked by reading `progress-bar.tsx` and `tooltip.tsx` directly. **`ProgressBar`'s RTL fill** uses plain CSS logical properties (`insetInlineStart` + `inlineSize` on an absolutely-positioned indicator) rather than a Tailwind utility — correct by construction, and lower-risk than `Switch`'s Tailwind-compiled approach since inline styles bypass the compiler entirely, so no separate from-scratch compile proof was needed here. **`Tooltip`'s logical `side`** surfaced a real inaccuracy in this very document (§10.1's Radix capability table, and this component's own §5.3 row): Radix's `align` is genuinely RTL-aware (floating-ui resolves it against computed `direction`), but `side` is physical screen geometry with no direction translation anywhere in `@radix-ui/react-popper` — confirmed by reading its compiled source, not by trusting what this doc previously claimed. **Both doc rows corrected in this commit** (§5.3 #20, §10.1) rather than left wrong for the next wave to re-discover — `Select`, `DropdownMenu`, `Popover` and `Combobox` all share the same Popper positioning and will need the same `side`-translation pattern `Tooltip` now demonstrates. `jest-axe` caught a real bug along the way (`ProgressBar`'s `role="progressbar"` had no accessible name — fixed with `aria-labelledby`), and confirmed two more Tailwind v4 theme-namespace gaps beyond the already-known `--z-*`/`--duration-*` one: `--animate-*` and `--container-*` also reset to nothing with no bridge, worked around the same established way (raw `var(--x)` in component code, `atom-motion.css` for keyframes) rather than fought. A reported Windows build flake in Next's post-build trace step was reproduced as a non-issue by me — a clean `rm -rf .next` + fresh `pnpm --filter @shj3/web build` completed with zero errors, consistent with a transient file-lock collision from both waves' concurrent builds overlapping, not a code defect.

Substituted with real Testing Library + `jest-axe` render/interaction/a11y tests per atom (111 tests) and, for the two components where RTL correctness was genuinely non-obvious, from-scratch proof against the real compiled/built output rather than trusting a class name. Revisit whether to build the Storybook/visual-regression pipeline once more of the component tree exists to make it worth the setup cost.

Spot-checked `summary-strip.tsx` directly (the RISK-007 fix): `conditions: [BlockingCondition, ...BlockingCondition[]]` genuinely is a compile-time non-empty tuple, not a runtime length check, and `recomputing` correctly retains prior content rather than blanking it. Independently re-ran both token gates, typecheck, `eslint --max-warnings=0`, and `vitest run --project components` myself (40 files / 262 tests green) rather than accepting the count as reported. One honest, flagged (not hidden) gap worth tracking: `SummaryStrip`'s `BlockingConditionRow` still concatenates hardcoded English connective words around dynamic values — the same "sentence by concatenation" shape §11.3 rule 4 warns about — left as-is because no component in this batch wires real `next-intl` yet, so per-word override props would be premature surface for zero consumers; **revisit when B-13/B-14's publish-gate screens actually consume this component.** `DropdownMenuTrigger` opens on `pointerdown` not `click`, confirmed by reading Radix's own source rather than assuming parity with `Select`.
- [x] **Wave 3A (structural/input) — complete 2026-09-08, independently re-verified.** SubTabBar, ToggleRow, FormField, SearchField, Slider, FilterBar, ChipInput, Pagination, EmptyState, DateRangeToggle.

  **The most significant finding of the whole B-1 slice so far, and independently confirmed by me before trusting it — not taken on the agent's word.** `design-system.md` §10.1 claimed Radix supplies RTL arrow-key reversal "for free" for `Tabs`/`ToggleGroup`/`Slider`. False: read `@radix-ui/react-direction`'s actual compiled source myself — `useDirection(localDir) { return localDir || useContext(DirectionContext) || "ltr" }` — and confirmed by grep that this app renders **no** `DirectionProvider` anywhere. With no `dir` prop passed and no provider, every one of these primitives silently resolves `"ltr"` forever regardless of the page's real direction — meaning arrow-key navigation in `SubTabBar`, `ToggleRow` and `Slider` would have been silently backwards for every Arabic-locale user, with nothing in the type system or a casual read flagging it. Fixed with a shared `use-resolved-dir.ts` hook (read directly, reasoning holds): renders `"ltr"` on first paint (matching what the server necessarily also renders, avoiding a hydration-mismatch warning) and self-corrects via `useLayoutEffect` reading `document.documentElement.dir`, landing before the browser's next paint. `docs/design-system.md` corrected in the same commit (§10.1's table, plus the `SubTabBar`/`ToggleRow`/`Slider` rows in §5.4).

  **Follow-up for the App Shell wave, not urgent but concrete:** the current fix is a safe, correctly-scoped interim measure, not the maximally elegant end state — `[locale]/layout.tsx` already computes `dir` server-side, so the root layout wiring the App Shell wave does next should wrap the tree in Radix's own real `<DirectionProvider dir={dir}>` using that already-known value. That removes the client-only guess-then-correct dance entirely (no brief incorrect-direction paint on a cold RTL load, which the current per-component hook cannot fully avoid) and lets `use-resolved-dir.ts` and `tooltip.tsx`'s `resolvePhysicalSide` both simplify to Radix's own `useDirection()` reading real context instead of `document` directly. Tracked here so it isn't lost before that wave starts.

  Two more real, independently-plausible bugs fixed along the way: `ToggleRow` selected nothing by default (forwarded only the raw, usually-`undefined` `value`/`defaultValue` straight to Radix — confirmed by reading the fix, `internalValue` now seeds from `defaultValue ?? options[0]?.value`), and `useUrlSyncedValue` derived its render value from `useSearchParams()` directly, which doesn't reflect a same-tick `router.replace()`, so a click wouldn't visually respond until the async round-trip finished — fixed by making local state authoritative, seeded once from the URL. Also found and fixed a false positive in the shared `no-hardcoded-user-string.mjs` gate (an arrow function's `=>` was misread as a JSX text boundary, sweeping unrelated code into a false match) — a real, likely-to-recur pattern now closed for every future wave, not just this one.

  **Final independent verification, matching both waves' own reports exactly:** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` → **17/17 stages, zero findings anywhere** (not confined to anyone's files — genuinely clean, both molecule batches' work is done). `rm -rf apps/web/.next && pnpm --filter @shj3/web build` → clean production build, both locales prerendered. All **40 components** (20 atoms + 20 molecules) now exist, gate-clean, tested, building end to end through the real pipeline.
- [x] **Organisms (17) — all 17 complete.** 15 dispatched as three parallel waves 2026-09-08; `SkinEditor` and `HelpGuideShell` deliberately held back (Phase E needed new Appearance persistence/schema work first; Phase F needs real nav data from later modules) as dedicated follow-ups rather than bundled in. `SkinEditor` complete 2026-09-09 (see Review). **`HelpGuideShell` complete 2026-09-10 (see Review) — all 57 named design-system components now exist.**
  - [x] **Wave 4A (shell/overlay) — complete, independently re-verified.** `AppShell`, `Dialog`/`Sheet`, `DiagnosticsRail`, `RuleListEditor`, `ChartFrame` — 75 tests. **Wired a real Radix `DirectionProvider` into the root `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx`**, using the already server-resolved `dir` — closes the cold-RTL-load gap the interim `use-resolved-dir.ts` fix (Wave 3A) couldn't fully avoid, with zero flash of wrong-direction content on first paint now. Proved it actually works, not just present in the tree: `DiagnosticsRail`'s test wraps raw Radix `Tabs` (no local `dir` prop, no `useResolvedDir()`) in the real provider and asserts a genuine behavioral divergence — `ArrowRight` is a no-op under RTL from the first tab but moves focus under LTR. I read `[locale]/layout.tsx` directly and confirm this is exactly right; `use-resolved-dir.ts`/`tooltip.tsx` correctly left untouched (different concern — Popper's `side`, not Radix's internal direction context). Found and fixed 6 real bugs along the way (a redundant `outline-none` defeating its own focus ring, a duplicate `style` spread clobbering `z-index: var(--z-modal)`, a backwards RTL/mobile visibility class, invalid CSS `background-color: url(#pattern)`, an empty `<th>` axe violation, a broken test fixture). Extended `scripts/gates/no-raw-table.mjs` with a `chart-frame/` exemption (verified my own brief's claim that this was already covered was wrong, then fixed it the precedented way) — landed alongside a concurrent, independent `permission-matrix/` exemption from Wave 4B with no conflict, confirmed by reading the merged file myself.
  - [x] **Wave 4B (data/authoring) — complete, independently re-verified.** `DataTable`, `Wizard`, `PermissionMatrix`, `Composer` — 109 tests, plus two new shared hooks (`use-media-query.ts` for genuinely-testable breakpoint collapses, mirroring `use-resolved-dir.ts`'s pattern; a `matchMedia` test-mock). New deps: `@tanstack/react-table@8` (deliberately not `@tanstack/react-table@9`, the current `latest` npm tag — v9 is real and stable, not a prerelease, but is a significant rewrite of the library's core API shape; pinning to the mature, extremely widely-proven v8 line for the single highest-leverage component in the UI is the conservative call RISK-002's own "no CI dependency scanning" logic argues for, and I independently checked v9's actual npm listing before accepting this rather than taking the characterization on faith), `@tanstack/react-virtual`. Spot-checked `use-permission-matrix-grid.ts` directly (the hook backing "the hardest keyboard problem in the product") against the doc's full 9-row keyboard table: every single key — `Arrow`, `Home`/`End`, `Ctrl+Home`/`Ctrl+End`, `PageUp`/`PageDown` (real 5-row page size), `Space`, `Shift+Space`, `Ctrl+Space`, `Ctrl+Z` — maps to a real, correctly-scoped function; the bulk row/column toggles correctly compute all-granted-vs-any-ungranted before choosing a direction (proper "select all/deselect all" semantics, respecting locked cells); undo stores actual diffs (not grid snapshots) so a bulk toggle reverses atomically as one `Ctrl+Z`, exactly matching the spec. `confirmChange` is a generic seam with no B9-specific role/permission names baked in — correct agnostic-design call, not a shortcut. Independently re-ran all three token gates, typecheck, and reviewed every `design-gate-allow` exception across the whole tree by hand (9 total by this point, up from 1) — all legitimate and precisely justified, no abuse of the escape hatch. `vitest run --project components` confirmed at 527/527 across 56 files (matches the agent's own count).
  - [x] **Wave 4C (canvases/chat) — complete 2026-09-09, independently re-verified.** `GraphCanvas`, `FlowCanvas` + `NodeInspector`, `DiffTraceViewer`, `ChatThread`, `AssistantWidgetShell` — 29 files (`apps/web/src/components/patterns/{graph-canvas,flow-canvas,diff-trace-viewer,chat-thread,assistant-widget-shell}/`), 91 tests across 6 test files, all `data-*.ts` models built first so each canvas's SVG/DOM view and its mandatory list/outline view provably render from one shared source (`describeNode`/`describeFlowNode`, `computeTopologicalOrder` — asserted directly in tests, not just documented). **Spot-checked the no-remount proof directly**: `expect(composerAfter).toBe(composerBefore)` on `AssistantWidgetShell`'s composer across a docked→expanded rerender is genuine DOM-node reference equality — the one unambiguous way to prove React didn't unmount/remount — backed up by real focus-retention and typed-draft-survives assertions, not just a class-name check. Real bugs found and fixed: a roving-focus effect that silently no-op'd when Escape returned focus to the *already-current* node (React bails out on an unchanged state value; fixed by keying on a monotonic per-request token), a detail panel that's a DOM sibling rather than descendant of the canvas SVG so Escape inside it never reached the canvas handler, a canvas-level Enter handler that bypassed a per-node `readonly` gate. **A genuine blind spot found in this repo's line-based gate architecture**: `no-raw-table.mjs`'s `design-gate-allow:` comment must sit on the *same source line* as the `<table` it excuses, but Prettier reformats a `<table>` carrying attributes onto multiple lines, silently detaching the tag from its own justifying comment — a false negative the gate would never report, since it just stops matching at all. Worked around correctly (the one exempted table now carries zero attributes so tag and comment stay on one line); worth remembering as a fragility of every line-scoped gate here, not just this one, should a future reformat ever split a previously-compliant line.

  **All 15 dispatched organisms complete.** Final aggregate, independently re-run by me: `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — 17/17 stages, 537 component tests, 741 unit tests, full Python suite, zero findings. One transient, **non-reproducible** flake hit once during my own re-verification pass (a `pnpm -r run typecheck` Windows path-casing mismatch that momentarily pulled the root package's own `tsc --build --force` script into scope and failed on a missing root `tsconfig.json`, unrelated to anything any wave built) — re-ran immediately with no other change and it passed clean; a plain direct `pnpm -r run typecheck` confirms the signal that actually matters (both real sub-projects typecheck cleanly) has been consistent all session. Flagged as environmental tooling flakiness, not a code defect, rather than either ignored or chased into a pnpm-internals rabbit hole disproportionate to a one-off blip.

    **Both dual-representation canvases ship real, independently-derived geometric/topological keyboard models**, not DOM-order tabbing: `GraphCanvas`'s `findNearestNeighbor` is a pure, unit-tested function scoring candidates by dominant-axis qualification + Euclidean distance (a deliberately asymmetric L-shaped test fixture, so a DOM-order bug and a geometry bug would fail differently); `FlowCanvas`'s `useTopologicalFocus` walks `computeTopologicalOrder` for `ArrowUp`/`Down` and `siblingIdsOf` for `ArrowLeft`/`Right`, both reversed under RTL (vertical semantics deliberately not reversed) — real focus-target assertions per key in both, matching the molecules wave's own bar for RTL claims, not just prop-forwarding checks.

    **Two real, independently-caught focus-management bugs, not shipped blind:** (1) a roving-focus hook that keyed its imperative `.focus()` effect on `[focusedId]` silently did nothing when a request re-targeted the *already-current* id — exactly what "Escape returns focus to the node that opened the detail panel" needs — because `useState`'s setter bails out of scheduling work when the value is `Object.is`-unchanged; fixed by keying the effect on a monotonic per-request token instead (`use-nearest-neighbor-focus.ts`, mirrored in `use-topological-focus.ts`), verified by the same test that first caught it, not merely reasoned about. (2) `GraphCanvas`'s detail panel is a DOM *sibling* of the SVG canvas, not a descendant — Escape pressed inside it never reached the canvas's own keydown handler; fixed with the panel's own dedicated Escape delegation, same pattern then reused in `NodeInspector` and `AssistantWidgetShell`. A third bug (not focus-related): the canvas-level Enter handler had its own independent path to open the inspector, bypassing the per-node `readonly` gate entirely — caught by a `readonly`-variant test, fixed by gating both paths.

    **Streaming-announcement discipline proven as two deliberately different behaviours, per the brief's own instruction, not accidentally identical:** `DiffTraceViewer` announces every appended step but never more than the latest one (a live region's text is replaced per append — test appends three times and asserts the region never contains an earlier step's text); `ChatThread` announces *nothing* about a streaming turn until it finalizes (interim text lives inside a nested `aria-live="off"` region the whole time it streams). Both documented against each other in code so a future edit doesn't "fix" one to match the other by mistake.

    **`ChatThread`'s scroll-not-focus and RTL bubble-alignment claims are proven, not assumed**: a real harness with an external focused `<input>` standing in for the composer, asserting focus is unmoved after a turn is appended (and that `scrollIntoView` *was* called, so the scroll half is real too); bubble alignment asserted via `items-end`/`items-start` on real rendered turns under a real `dir="rtl"` ancestor, plus a regex assertion that no physical `ml-`/`mr-`/`pl-`/`pr-`/`text-left`/`text-right` utility appears anywhere in the rendered output (belt-and-suspenders alongside the token gate, which would already fail the build on any such class).

    **`AssistantWidgetShell`'s no-remount guarantee is proven three independent ways**, deliberately going beyond the brief's own suggested check: DOM node identity (`===` on the composer's `<textarea>` and the thread's `role="log"` element before/after a docked→expanded switch — the definitive proof), real focus retention (a focused element cannot survive an actual unmount/remount in a real DOM, jsdom included — so this is not a weaker restatement of the identity check, it is an independent one), and the brief's own suggested typed-draft-survives check (flagged in code as *not* independently conclusive for a fully-controlled `Composer`, since a controlled value reapplies correctly even to a freshly-mounted node — kept anyway as the brief's literal ask, alongside the two checks that actually distinguish remount from no-remount).

    **Two real, load-bearing gate/token gaps found and resolved, both flagged rather than silently worked around:**
    1. `no-raw-table.mjs` allowlists only `components/patterns/data-table/`, but §5.5 #44 requires a real `<table>` in `GraphCanvas`'s own list view. Resolved with the gate's own documented `design-gate-allow:` escape hatch — but only after discovering by direct experiment (three throwaway `prettier --write` probes, output inspected each time) that a `<table className="…" data-slot="…">` with the allow-comment anywhere in its attribute list gets silently separated from `<table` the moment Prettier wraps a multi-attribute tag onto several lines, which `no-raw-table.mjs` would then simply fail to *detect at all* — no violation, no counted exception, an invisible false-negative that would break the moment the attribute list or Prettier's own wrapping heuristic next changed. Fixed by giving `<table>` zero attributes (styling moved to the wrapping `<div>` and, where it was genuinely load-bearing, down onto each cell) so the comment reliably survives formatting attached to `<table` itself — confirmed against the real, formatted file, not just the pre-format source.
    2. No component token exists for `AssistantWidgetShell`'s literal 400px/560px renderings. Resolved with local `rem` constants (`25rem`/`35rem`) — outside `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs`'s px/pt/em literal ban and consistent with `packages/tokens`'s own precedent for a genuinely-geometric, non-derived value (`sidebarWidth: "16rem"`) — deliberately *not* added to shared `packages/tokens`, out of this wave's scope. A closely related, smaller version of the same mistake shipped once and was caught by the gate itself: an early comment spelling out "400px at the standard 16px root" tripped `raw-length-literal` from inside a trailing `//` comment on a code line (the gate does not blank comments-on-code-lines the way it blanks comment-only lines) — reworded rather than suppressed.

    **Two cross-wave dependencies resolved, one each way, both documented rather than assumed:** `Dialog`/`Sheet` (§5.5 #53) did not exist yet when `GraphCanvas`'s merge-confirmation and `FlowCanvas`'s ≤820px inspector sheet were built — both accept a `render*` override prop and ship a real (not stubbed) default built directly on the raw `radix-ui` `Dialog` primitive, the same primitive the real organisms will themselves be built on, so focus trap/`Escape`/scroll-lock/focus-return all come from Radix now, not from logic to later delete. `Composer` (§5.5 #49), by contrast, *did* already exist by the time `AssistantWidgetShell` was reached — complete, documented to the same house standard, its own 14 tests passing — so it is composed directly with no fallback, after independently re-running its test file rather than assuming a sibling wave's self-report.

    **Independently re-verified by me, not just accepted:** every organism's own gates (`no-hardcoded-design-values`, `no-undefined-token`, `no-hardcoded-user-string`, `no-raw-table`) run and re-run clean after each fix, `eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean repo-wide, `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean repo-wide, `pnpm exec vitest run --project components` **57/57 files, 537/537 tests green** (my 91 plus the other two concurrent waves' — nothing conflicts), and the full `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` → **17/17 stages passed**, including the Python side (169 tests) neither this wave nor its siblings touch. The one pre-existing typecheck error observed mid-wave (`data-table.tsx`, Wave 4B's file) was gone by the time of this final pass — the owning agent's own fix, confirmed by re-running rather than assumed.

    **A `no-noninteractive-element-interactions` pattern recurs four times across this wave** (`GraphCanvas`'s detail panel, `NodeInspector`'s root, `FlowCanvas`'s `role="application"` canvas, `AssistantWidgetShell`'s dialog region) — every one is real `Escape`/arrow-key *delegation* from whichever descendant holds focus, not fake interactivity bolted onto a landmark, and every one carries a targeted, justified `eslint-disable-next-line` rather than a broad rule-level suppression. Flagged together here in case a future pass wants a real fix (a shared "keydown delegation" hook with its own lint allowance) rather than four repeated inline disables.

    **Judgment calls made on sparse spec detail, all flagged in code at the point made, not silently resolved:** `GraphCanvas`/`FlowCanvas` node **layout is caller-supplied data** (`GraphNode.x`/`y`) or a locally-computed BFS depth/column assignment (`FlowCanvas`) — no force-directed or other real layout algorithm was built, matching the brief's own "don't reach for a graph-visualization library" instruction read as also excluding hand-rolling one; connections/edges render as real SVG lines/arrowheads for `GraphCanvas` (explicitly an SVG canvas) but as text-based "→ target" indicators for `FlowCanvas` (HTML boxes with rich per-type form content, where a full SVG-line-over-HTML-grid overlay was judged not worth the added complexity against this wave's actual graded surface — the a11y/keyboard model, not line art); `AssistantWidgetShell` does not use real CSS `@container` queries despite the anatomy naming "a container query root" — reasoned in the component's own doc comment that every rendering is prop-driven, not ambient-size-driven, so a container query would add a new, unprecedented CSS-authoring pattern to the codebase for no behaviour a plain conditional class doesn't already give; `DiffTraceViewer`'s "rail" (a coloured `border-inline-start` per step) was kept even though not explicitly named in §5.5 #47's own anatomy bullet, since it is mechanically gate-clean and visually consistent with `SummaryStrip`/`Card`'s established rail motif elsewhere in this library.

#### Pre-implementation research (2026-09-09) — full plan before any code

Read in full myself, as instructed, rather than delegated: every named Prisma model (`Agent` through `CircuitBreakerEvent`, plus `McpTool`/`RateLimitPolicy`/`Channel`/`AgentUsageDaily`/`PolicyOverride`/`OverridablePolicy`/`Permission` which the brief didn't name but the schema needs), the `Wizard`/`useWizardDraft` organism source, `PrismaRoleRepository` (the trigger-rejection precedent), the IAM seed script + seeder port/adapter, `next-request-context.ts`/`require-permission.ts`, `ai-client.ts`. Delegated to three parallel research agents (all independently spot-checked against primary sources afterward, not accepted blind): `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql`'s real trigger bodies for every named table, whether a real MCP client exists anywhere in the repo, and the exact prop contracts of `DataTable`/`SubTabBar`/`DiffTraceViewer`/`Badge`/`StatusCell`/`DestructiveConfirmDialog`/`SummaryStrip`/`ChipInput`/`SelectablePill` plus how IAM actually wires them.

**Module boundary decision.** `apps/web/src/modules/agents/` owns `Agent`, `AgentVersion`, `AgentVersionHistoryEntry`, the four per-version binding tables (Knowledge/Flow/Channel/Locale), `AgentSandboxRun`, `AgentWizardDraft`, and — because it FKs to `Agent` not `AgentVersion` — `PolicyOverride` (guardrails). `apps/web/src/modules/tools/` owns `Skill`, `McpServer`/`McpTool`, `ApiConnector`, `RateLimitPolicy`, `ToolBinding`, `CircuitBreakerConfig`/`CircuitBreakerEvent`. Reasoning: `eslint.config.mjs`'s `boundaries/element-types` rule forbids **any** file in one feature module importing **any** file in another, symmetrically, regardless of layer — so `ToolBinding` needing to be readable/writable from both the wizard route (`agents` concern) and `/tools` (its own module) is resolved the same way `theming` composes into `settings/appearance`: **the app/route layer composes both modules**; neither module imports the other. B5's tabs 1–3 (per `api.md` §6.5's real endpoint list: `GET /tools/skills` returns "per-agent attachment **counts**", not a bind toggle) are catalogue CRUD + connect/discover + test-connection + aggregate counts — **not** an independent bind surface; the actual bind/unbind action is wizard-step-4-only, called from both the wizard's and `/tools`'s Server Actions against the *same* `modules/tools/application/{bind,unbind}-tool.ts` — this is what satisfies "shared data path," not a shared UI control. Flagged as a judgment call resolving an apparent tension between the wireframe's loose prose ("identical controls") and the more precise, later-written `api.md`.

**The one deliberate exception to "no cross-module import," and why.** `api.md` §4.4 says Clone is "one transaction" that also deep-copies `ToolBindings` for the source version. `agents`' `CloneAgent` cannot import anything from `modules/tools/` to do that. Resolved the same way `PrismaRoleRepository`'s own doc comment justifies a five-line duplicated query instead of a forced cross-port abstraction: `PrismaAgentRepository.cloneAgent()` reads/writes the `ToolBindings` table directly via `getTenantDb()` (a `platform` port every module may use) inside its own `$transaction`, using a narrow, locally-typed row shape — zero TypeScript import from `modules/tools/`, so the lint boundary holds literally, while the real atomicity `api.md` asks for is real. `tools` remains the sole owner of `ToolBinding`'s actual domain logic (bind/unbind, the trigger-rejection translations, the shared counts query) — this is a one-off, transaction-scoped read+copy, not new business logic living in two places.

**`Agent.status` vs `AgentVersion.status` — resolved by cross-referencing every CHECK/trigger, not assumed.** `TR_AgentVersions_publishedImmutable` guards exactly nine scalar columns and never fires on a row whose *old* status wasn't already `'Published'`; `CK_AgentVersions_publishedPaired` is symmetric (`status='Published' <=> publishedAt NOT NULL`); `CK_Agents_publishedHasVersion` is one-directional (`Agent.status='Published' => currentVersionId NOT NULL`, never the reverse). Together these only hang together if a **Published `AgentVersion` never leaves `'Published'` once reached** (a permanent historical snapshot — matching every doc's "immutable" framing) and **`Agent.status` is the independent, reversible "is this agent currently live" flag**. So: **Publish** = first transition of a Draft version to Published (permanent) + `Agent.status→Published` + `currentVersionId` set. **Unpublish** = `Agent.status: Published→Draft` **only** — never touches `AgentVersion.status`, so no trigger/CHECK interaction at all, and re-publishing is a fast flag flip, not a new version. **Editing an already-published agent** needs a brand-new mechanism nothing in the brief named explicitly but the product cannot work without: **"get-or-create wizard draft"** forks a new Draft `AgentVersion` (major unchanged, minor+1) off the current Published one, copying scalar config + all four binding tables (+ `ToolBindings`, per the exception above) when no draft already exists for that owner+agent — this is what makes `v1.2→v1.3→v1.4` possible at all. Flagged plainly as the single largest inference in this wave, reached only after reconciling every constraint above, not asserted from the wireframe (which never shows this flow explicitly).

**Version numbering rule** (nothing in the brief specifies one): new agent → v0.1 Draft. Publishing a v0.x draft (first-ever publish) → v1.0. Forking a new draft off a Published v`M.N` → v`M.(N+1)`, published as-is (no further bump). Seed data's exact wireframe version numbers/history text (v1.2→v1.3→v1.4 etc.) are written directly as seed rows, not derived from this algorithm — matching how the IAM seed script hardcodes exact wireframe rows rather than generating them.

**MCP ownership — confirmed, not inferred: `apps/ai` owns the `McpClient` port (`AdkMcpClient` adapter), `apps/web`'s route is documented as a proxy (`api.md` §6.5, §9.12; `architecture.md`'s module map).** Neither exists yet (verified: no MCP package anywhere in either runtime's dependency graph, `apps/ai`'s `ports`/`application` are empty files, only `provisioning_router` is mounted). **Scope decision**: build the real port (`McpDiscoveryClient`) and a real tenant-scoped adapter that calls the documented internal endpoint shape (mirroring `ai-client.ts`'s trace-propagation/error-translation pattern, not its platform-scoped credential), following the same "web holds no vendor driver, calls the AI service's HTTP API" rule `AiGraphProvisioner` already establishes. Will **not** build the `apps/ai`-side Python MCP implementation itself (Google ADK MCP wiring + a FastAPI router) — that is a substantial, separate undertaking that belongs to whichever wave actually builds the agent runtime (B-5), not a web-focused backoffice wave. A real "Connect & discover" click against the real stack will therefore genuinely fail (the callee doesn't exist yet); the adapter's job is to translate that into a distinct, honestly-labelled outcome (not the documented `dns|tls|auth|timeout|protocol` handshake-failure reasons, which would misrepresent an absent endpoint as a broken one) rather than fake success. Seeded `McpServer`/`McpTool` rows (static, matching the wireframe's sample state) are unaffected — seeding sets rows directly, it doesn't exercise the live call.

**Guardrails (step 7) — real, not stubbed, per the brief's own "real toggles" instruction.** `mask_pii_in_transcripts` (confirmed real key, `data-model.md` §4.4) is locked — shown checked and disabled, no override possible (no `OverridablePolicy` row to reference). `grounding_threshold` (confirmed real key, used in `api.md`'s guardrail-trace examples) is the second toggle. No existing key for "allow competitor discussion" anywhere in the repo (grepped); since no `governance` module/seed exists yet to own this, seeding the three narrow `Policy`/`OverridablePolicy` catalogue rows this one wizard step needs is in scope here — named `allow_competitor_discussion` (my own choice, flagged as such, first use).

**What's genuinely stubbed, per the brief's explicit instruction not to fake it**: step 5 (Knowledge) and step 6 (Flows) bind against the real `AgentKnowledgeBinding`/`AgentFlowBinding` tables but there is no real `KnowledgeCollection`/`Flow` data yet (B-4/B-5) — the UI will show a plain "no knowledge collections/flows exist yet" state rather than fake rows. Step 9 (Test/sandbox) shows a real "sandbox not available until the runtime exists" state — no `AgentSandboxRun` writes at all, since there is nothing real to record. Step 10's environment is fixed to Development only (no fake promotion mechanism — that's B-9).

**Known, real SQL-layer gaps found while reading `001_constraints.sql`, both flagged, one to fix if time allows, neither blocking:** (1) `TR_ToolBindings_serverMustBeConnected`'s first block has no `isEnabled` condition (unlike its `AgentFlowBindings` sibling, which correctly checks `isEnabled = 1`) — so *disabling* a binding whose target has since disconnected would be spuriously rejected, not just creating/enabling one. Will fix to mirror the sibling's pattern if time allows, since it's small and precedented; not blocking since my own binding flow won't disconnect a server mid-test. (2) `TR_ApiConnectors_projectSkill` only syncs `deletedAt` on UPDATE, never `name`/schemas/`rateLimitPolicyId` — my `UpdateApiConnector` use case will re-sync the paired `Skill` row itself at the application layer rather than leave it to silently drift. (3) `ApiConnectors.authMode` has no CHECK constraint at all (unlike `McpServers.authMode`) — validated at the application/domain layer only, DB will accept anything.

Every row of `SHJ3-wireframes-guide.md` §6 is an integration test, not a nice-to-have. These are the highest-value tests in the suite.

Proven directly against the real `sewa` tenant SQL Server schema via a throwaway script (`ApproveMessageTemplate` then `EnableCampaign`, both real application use cases, no raw SQL): starting state confirmed (`template.approvalStatus=Pending, campaign.isEnabled=false`), `EnableCampaign` genuinely refused by the real `TR_Campaigns_templateMustBeApproved` trigger, template approved for real, `EnableCampaign` retried and genuinely succeeded, fixture restored to its original seeded state afterward (confirmed by re-query) so `e2e/backoffice/channels.spec.ts`'s own golden path still finds the seeded Blocked starting state. **A real, related bug found and fixed while building this proof's own E2E coverage**: `whatsapp-tab.tsx`/`campaigns-tab.tsx` rendered Approve/Reject/"Send now" to any `agents:manage` principal regardless of holding the stricter `agents:publish` the underlying action actually requires — fixed at the root (`channels/page.tsx`'s new `canPublish`, threaded through both tabs), with a committed regression test (`channels.spec.ts`) proving the fix. See `tasks/lessons.md`'s new "a page-level permission gate does not automatically gate every control" entry.

Proving this for real needs the `apps/ai` Python runtime actually executing a failing tool call against the seeded SEWA connector N times — out of this pass's time budget; named honestly rather than faked or silently dropped.

Needs a real, seeded open conflict plus a real retrieval-playground query against `apps/ai`'s embedding/scoring pipeline and a real refusal-policy evaluation — deeper than this pass's remaining time allowed; named honestly rather than faked.
- [ ] Handover node fires → ticket carries that escalation reason — **still genuinely open.** B-7's own live proof exercises `RequestHandover` as an application use case directly, not a flow engine **Handover node** firing mid-flow (one of B-5's five node kinds), and does not confirm the resulting ticket's escalation-reason field specifically. `FR-HAND-20` names this exact requirement. Proving it needs a real flow execution through `apps/ai`'s flow engine reaching a Handover node for real — out of this pass's scope; named honestly rather than faked.
- [x] **Reconciled 2026-09-10 — already proven live in B-7's own review entry, and now also a permanent, committed, regression-checked E2E test.** Reorder a routing rule → tester returns a different route. B-7's throwaway script proved this once, live, against the seeded `sewa` rule set (reordering Topic-then-Priority to Priority-then-Topic changes which team the identical test ticket routes to). `e2e/backoffice/escalations.spec.ts`'s new golden path makes the identical mechanism permanent: two real rules created live (since the routing-rules-holding principal's own tenant, `sharjah`, has no seeded rules of its own), default order fires one target, an in-memory-only reorder (no save) makes the tester fire the other target and flags the result as reflecting an unsaved change, and a fresh page reload confirms the *persisted* order — and therefore the first target — is unchanged. Passing, and re-run twice to confirm idempotency.

## Review

*Populated as phases complete.*

### Phase A — complete 2026-09-08

**Delivered.** ~12,700 lines across eight documents and **nine** ADRs. 294 functional and 84 non-functional requirements, all 378 mapped to planned tests and verified by set equality in both directions.

**Two intake decisions were in genuine tension and are resolved explicitly rather than papered over:**

- A "modular monolith" cannot be one deployable with a Next.js + Python stack → ADR-0001 fixes it at exactly two, with a no-third-deployable rule and the operational justification written down.
- "Schema-per-tenant" is meaningless for Neo4j, Qdrant and Redis → ADR-0002 extends it to physical isolation per store, and states plainly that this makes a Neo4j Enterprise licence a hard dependency.

**The documentation pass found eleven contradictions in the wireframe itself.** This was the highest-value output of the phase — every one would have surfaced as a defect mid-build:

- Five resolved by requirement or construction: RISK-007 (Arabic readiness is two numbers, so the gate must name every failing condition), RISK-009 (publish ≠ deploy — separated onto three tables so neither operation can be performed by accident while doing the other), RISK-012 (payment idempotency keys were specified in an unbacked store — ADR-0003 amended, durable key moved to the SQL transaction row).
- Six need a product-owner answer: RISK-005, 006, 008, 010, 022, 023. All have working assumptions so nothing is blocked, but RISK-023 (tool-catalogue tenancy) must be answered before the tools module is built, since the wrong reading puts one government entity's mTLS credentials inside another's reach.

**Design-system findings worth noting:** the wireframe's own attention colour `#B4553F` measures 4.46:1 and fails WCAG AA as text; its two-badge palette cannot express the admin screens' three distinct meanings; and its single accent was doing duty as both primary action and success, so tenant re-branding would have repainted every `Healthy` badge.

**The Neo4j licence decision landed inside Phase A and was absorbed properly.** RISK-003 resolved negative, so [ADR-0009](../docs/adr/0009-logical-graph-partitioning.md) amends ADR-0002 for the graph store and the change was propagated through `data-model.md`, `deployment.md`, `testing.md`, `requirements.md`, `architecture.md` and the index — not left as a note to reconcile later. It cost more than multi-database: Community also has no RBAC (no database-level fallback, and property-existence constraints become undeclarable, so integrity rules are *detected* rather than *prevented*) and no clustering (single instance, so routine node drains interrupt graph retrieval). Absorbed because the graph is a derived store — outage degrades retrieval to vector-only with grounding confidence dropping honestly, and loss is recovered by re-index. The graph went from the strongest isolation guarantee to the weakest, so its isolation cases went from 1 to 16, parametrised from the `GraphStore` port so an untested query path fails the suite.

**Two design gaps were found by reviewing the propagation, and fixed in ADR-0009 rather than shipped:** the encoding invariant is now asserted on the write path rather than only by an hourly sweep (an interval-only detective control means a leak is already in a citizen transcript before anyone knows), and gating node drains is recorded as required follow-up rather than left to operator convention.

**Carried risks:** 24 tracked, one closed. One needs a decision from outside engineering — RISK-001 (residency). RISK-024 (graph isolation is code-only, with no infrastructure or database-level fallback) is standing for the life of the system and reviewed every release.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this phase:** the no-CI decision (RISK-002) is worse than it first appears. It compounds with RISK-011 and RISK-014 — a `--no-verify` bypass plus a partially applied N-tenant migration leaves `shj3-ai` reading columns that exist for some entities and not others, surfacing only as conversations failing for one government entity. Three layers of defence in depth were added, and none of them catches a local bypass.

### B-1 infra scaffolding — complete 2026-09-08

Scaffolding only, ahead of the actual component library: next-intl routing, the shadcn/ui baseline, the `components` lint boundary, and a `components` vitest project. No atoms/molecules/organisms, app shell or theming-module UI — that's the rest of B-1, now unblocked rather than done.

**Files created:** `apps/web/src/i18n/routing.ts`, `apps/web/src/i18n/request.ts`, `apps/web/src/middleware.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx`, `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/page.tsx`, `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json`, `apps/web/components.json`, `apps/web/src/lib/utils.ts`, `apps/web/src/components/{ui,patterns}/.gitkeep`. **Deleted:** `apps/web/src/app/layout.tsx` and `app/page.tsx` (moved into `[locale]/`). **Modified:** `apps/web/src/app/globals.css`, `apps/web/next.config.ts`, `eslint.config.mjs`, `vitest.config.ts`, `pnpm-workspace.yaml`, both `package.json` files.

**Two claims in the brief were verified rather than trusted, and one didn't survive contact:**

1. **Root `app/layout.tsx` really is deletable once there's no root `page.tsx`** — confirmed by a real `next build` (not assumed): compiled clean, `/en` and `/ar` both statically prerendered via `generateStaticParams`, `/api/healthz` unaffected.
2. **next-intl does *not* auto-wire from `src/i18n/request.ts` alone**, despite next-intl's own docs describing that path as supported "out of the box." A real build failed with "Couldn't find next-intl config file" until `next.config.ts` was wrapped with `createNextIntlPlugin()`. Read the plugin's actual shipped source (`plugin/getNextConfig.js`) rather than re-guessing from docs a second time: "out of the box" means the plugin doesn't need an explicit path argument for this location — it does not mean the plugin is optional. Without it, `next-intl/config` resolves to a packaged placeholder module that unconditionally throws. The wrapper chains cleanly into the pre-existing custom `webpack()` (NodeNext `.js`→`.ts` extension-alias patch from B-0) rather than replacing it.
3. **`next-intl`'s own types mark `requestLocale` `@deprecated` in favour of `next/root-params`.** Checked whether that's actually usable on this repo's pinned Next 15.5.25 before switching to it: `next/root-params`'s own shipped source is a placeholder that throws *"this is a bug in Next.js"* if ever executed — not a working API yet on Next 15. Used `requestLocale` (still fully functional) instead, documented why in `i18n/request.ts`.

**A supply-chain check that mattered more than expected.** Installing `next-intl@4.14.2` pulled in `@swc/core` and `@parcel/watcher` as transitive dependencies, both blocked by `pnpm-workspace.yaml`'s `allowBuilds` gate — which, tellingly, already had placeholder entries for exactly these two packages (`set this to true or false`), left by whoever wrote that file anticipating this exact moment. Their names don't match next-intl's historically minimal dependency footprint from training-data-era knowledge, and next-intl's own `package.json` now also lists unfamiliar packages (`@eloqnt/config`, `@eloqnt/format-json`, `@eloqnt/format-po`, `icu-minify`, `next-intl-swc-plugin-extractor`) — presumably v4's SWC-based compile-time extraction plugin, introduced after this system's knowledge cutoff. Given RISK-002 (no CI dependency scanning) makes this kind of manual check the only line of defence, verified rather than assumed: `next-intl` carries npm's GitHub Actions OIDC trusted-publisher + SLSA provenance attestation, all the unfamiliar packages share its maintainer (`jan@amann.me`, the same person who has published `next-intl` since 2020) and, where a repo field is present, its exact repo. `@swc/core` and `@parcel/watcher` are themselves long-established, high-adoption OSS (swc-project/swc, parcel-bundler/watcher) whose install scripts — read directly from the installed `package.json`, not assumed — are the ordinary prebuilt-native-binary pattern. Approved both, with the justification written into `pnpm-workspace.yaml` itself rather than left as a bare `true`.

**One real config bug found via the required smoke test, not shipped blind.** The `components` vitest project failed its throwaway JSX smoke test with `React is not defined` — no `@vitejs/plugin-react` is installed (correctly; the task's dependency list didn't call for it), and Vitest's default esbuild transform is the classic runtime without it. Fixed with `esbuild: { jsx: "automatic" }`. First attempt placed it at `vitest.config.ts`'s root level on the assumption it would apply to every inline project entry — re-ran the smoke test rather than trusting the edit, and it still failed identically, so that assumption was wrong; moved the option inside the `components` project entry specifically, re-ran again, confirmed green, then deleted the smoke test file per the no-scratch-files discipline.
