**Deviations from the brief, both minor, both flagged rather than silently done:** `components.json` includes a `hooks: "@/hooks"` alias beyond the four the brief named, since the brief's own success bar for this task is "`shadcn add` works correctly for the next wave," and an unnamed hooks alias is a plausible way a future `shadcn add <component-with-a-hook>` misfires — no directory was created for it, only the config pointer. `jsdom` went into the **root** `package.json` rather than `apps/web`'s, reasoning from `vitest` itself (root-only, never duplicated into `apps/web`) rather than from `typescript` (the brief's suggested precedent, which turned out to be duplicated in both places and so didn't actually disambiguate anything).

**One pre-existing issue observed, not fixed (out of scope):** the rendered `<head>` carries duplicate `<meta charSet>` and `<meta viewport>` tags — one from the original layout's manual tags (carried over verbatim per the brief's "keep this exactly as-is" instruction), one auto-injected by Next.js's own App Router metadata system. Predates this change; the original root `layout.tsx` had the identical manual tags before it was relocated.

**Verification, all real runs, not claimed:**
- `pnpm install` — clean, exit 0 (after resolving the `allowBuilds` gate above; a naive first attempt exited 1 on ignored build scripts).
- `pnpm --filter @shj3/web typecheck` — clean.
- `pnpm --filter @shj3/web build` — clean; `/en` and `/ar` both prerendered, `/api/healthz` untouched, no root layout.
- `pnpm --filter @shj3/web dev` + real HTTP fetches: `GET /` → `307` to `/en` with `NEXT_LOCALE=en` cookie; `GET /en` body is `<h1>SHJ3</h1><p>Sharjah government-services assistant and backoffice.</p>`; `GET /ar` is `<html lang="ar" dir="rtl">` with body `<h1>SHJ3</h1><p>مساعد الخدمات الحكومية ولوحة التحكم الخلفية لإمارة الشارقة.</p>`. Dev server stopped after.
- `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **PASSED, 14/14 stages**, including all 741 pre-existing TS unit tests (zero regressions) and the full Python side (169 tests).
- Smoke test file confirmed deleted (`_infra-smoke.test.tsx` absent from the working tree).
- Full changeset reviewed file-by-file above before writing this entry, not inferred from memory.

### Phase E backend (theming persistence, resolution, permission) — complete 2026-09-09

**Pre-flight finding that reshapes this whole task.** Before writing anything, read `docs/data-model.md` §4.16 and the actual `prisma/*/schema.prisma` against the brief's instruction to build `AppearanceSkins`/`TenantAppearance`/`UserAppearance` tables. B-0 already built a complete, real, migrated theming persistence layer under different names: `PlatformTokenSet`/`PlatformSkin` (→ `platform.TokenSets`/`platform.Skins`) and, per tenant, `TokenSet`/`Skin`/`TenantBranding`/`BrandAsset`/`UserThemePreference` — all with real constraints already applied live (`001_constraints.sql` §1.10 and §2.16). Building new, parallel tables with the brief's literal names would have duplicated this. Extended the real schema instead of duplicating it.

**Schema gaps identified against §9.3's control matrix and fixed as ALTERs, not new tables:**
- `UserThemePreference` was missing `fontSize` and `reducedMotion` — both required (§9.3: user may override base font size and reduced motion). Added both, nullable (`NULL` = defer to the tenant tier, matching every other column on this table).
- `TenantBranding` was missing `fontSize` — required (§9.3: base font size is tenant-settable too, same as mode/density/direction). Added as `NOT NULL DEFAULT '0.875rem'`.
- No DB-level protection stopped soft- or hard-deleting a tenant `Skin` currently referenced by `TenantBranding.activeSkinId` (the FK's `NoAction` only blocks a hard delete, not the soft-delete-via-`deletedAt` path this schema actually uses). Added `TR_Skins_blockActiveDeletion`, mirroring `TR_Skins_blockSystemDelete`'s exact two-condition shape. **Deliberately NOT applied to `UserThemePreference.skinId`** — a personal skin being retired must not block cleanup for every other tenant user who never chose it; `resolveTheme()` instead treats a dangling/soft-deleted personal `skinId` as deferring to the tenant tier (an application-level read, not a DB invariant, and documented as such on both the Prisma model and the port).
- "A deleted user's `UserAppearance` row should cascade": checked `StaffUser.status` — Invited/Active/Suspended, **never a deletion**, by the model's own doc comment, and confirmed by grep that no hard-delete code path exists anywhere (B-2, which would build user removal, isn't built yet). `UserThemePreference.staffUserId` is also a cross-schema reference with no real FK (ADR-0011). Conclusion: there is nothing to cascade from today — not a gap, a fact about the current feature set — noted for whichever future wave (B-2) ever adds real user removal to also delete this row.

**One `platform.Skins` row, not two, seeded from `@shj3/tokens` directly (task 2).** `seed-system-skins.ts` reads `SHARJAH_DEFAULT`/`SHARJAH_DARK` via the real `resolveSkinTokens()` (never hand-retyped), builds one `PlatformTokenSet` per mode plus **exactly one** `PlatformSkin` row ("Sharjah Default", `isSystem: true`) pairing them — not two. The DB's `Skin`/`PlatformSkin` model structurally pairs a light+dark `TokenSet` in *one* row (`lightTokenSetId`/`darkTokenSetId` both `NOT NULL`, `CK_Skins_modesDiffer`), unlike `@shj3/tokens`' `Skin` TypeScript type, which is genuinely mode-independent (each of `SHARJAH_DEFAULT`/`SHARJAH_DARK` is a standalone JSON interchange document, §7.1). Seeding two `PlatformSkin` rows pointing at the same two `TokenSets` rows (the only way to satisfy `UQ_TokenSets_kind_mode`'s "exactly one light and one dark system default") would have produced two identically-rendering rows differing only in `name` — answering a question ("which of two IDENTICAL skins is active") resolution never asks. Each seeded `TokenSet` carries a real `contrastValidatedAt` and `contrastReportJson` from the actual `checkContrast()` call, not a fabricated timestamp — `CK_TokenSets_contrastGate` would reject a row without one. Idempotent (`hasSystemDefaultSkin()` checks first); wired as `pnpm db:seed:appearance` (`scripts/seed-system-skins.ts` — a composition root, not inside `application/`, since it constructs a concrete adapter directly, which the swap test forbids there).

**The `Manage appearance` permission (task 3).** `PERMISSIONS` 8→9, appended (not interleaved, so no existing permission's position shifts). Granted to `SuperAdmin`/`EntityAdmin` only, added to `RESTRICTED_PERMISSIONS` mirroring `agents:publish`'s identical grant shape. The existing transcribed-matrix test in `permissions.test.ts` was *extended* (every role's `WIREFRAME` row gained the new column, the role-count assertion became 9) rather than given a separate, looser test — matching the brief's own instruction on test-transcription discipline.

**`resolveTheme()` in a new `modules/theming` feature module (task 4)** — the first real feature module built this session (`theming` was already reserved in `eslint.config.mjs`'s `FEATURE_MODULES`; only `platform`/`iam` existed before). Structure: `domain/` (`theme.ts`'s per-token merge + scalar resolvers, `prefers-dark.ts`), `ports/theme-repository.ts`, `application/` (`resolve-theme.ts`, `reset-appearance.ts`, `appearance-gate.ts`, `serialize-resolved-theme.ts`, `seed-system-skins.ts`), `adapters/outbound/` (`sql/prisma-theme-repository.ts`, `sql/prisma-system-skin-seeder.ts`, `null-theme-repository.ts`), `testing/fakes.ts`. Real per-token merge, not a whole-object pick: colours merge `{...system, ...tenant, ...personal}` (last wins per key — proven at both the pure-function level and the full-use-case level, see tests below); the five user-overridable scalars (mode, density, direction, fontSize, reducedMotion) are independent `??` chains, already per-field by construction since `UserThemePreference` has no colour columns at all.

**Two judgment calls the brief specifically expected, both resolved by finding a real constraint rather than guessing:**
1. **The `prefersDark` signal.** Checked the real `Principal` type directly: no `prefersDark` field exists, confirming §9.4's `principal.prefersDark` reference is aspirational, not implemented anywhere. Real replacement: the `Sec-CH-Prefers-Color-Scheme` Client Hint request header (a genuine W3C mechanism, not invented for this project), with `Accept-CH`/`Critical-CH`/`Vary` response headers wired in `next.config.ts` so supporting (Chromium-family) browsers start sending it; a `shj3-prefers-color-scheme` cookie as a second-priority fallback for non-supporting browsers — **nothing writes this cookie yet**, flagged as a real, named follow-up for whichever wave builds the Appearance module's client-side bootstrap (a `matchMedia` read on first paint), not silently absent. Defaults to light with neither present.
2. **On-delete cascade for a deleted applied skin.** `TenantBranding.activeSkinId`: blocked via the new `TR_Skins_blockActiveDeletion` trigger (above). `UserThemePreference.skinId`: deliberately left unblocked, with `resolveTheme()` treating a dangling reference as deferring to the tenant tier — asymmetric on purpose (a tenant-wide setting being silently orphaned is a bigger deal than one user's personal choice going stale).

**A third, unprompted judgment call, found while actually wiring the port to real infrastructure — and the reason `readSystemDefaultColors` does not exist.** `getPlatformDb()` is deliberately gated to two audited cross-tenant purposes, `"provisioning"` and `"analytics-rollup"` (ADR-0002 rule 5) — "anything reading this must write an audit entry." Reading the tiny, public, read-only system-default colour set on *every single request* is neither of those, and writing an audit entry per page load would be absurd. Resolved by having `resolve-theme.ts` read the system tier from `@shj3/tokens`' own static `systemSkin(mode)` export directly — zero I/O, and *more* correct for §9.4's own "no flash of default theme" goal, not just cheaper. The seeded `platform.Skins` row still exists and still serves its stated purpose (a future "list every skin including the two shipped ones" admin screen has one consistent source), just not on the hot per-request path.

**A fourth judgment call: `TenantBranding.activeSkinId` targets the tenant's own `Skins` table, not `platform.Skins`.** So "reset tenant branding to default" cannot mean "repoint `activeSkinId` at a system-default stand-in" — there isn't one in the tenant's own schema. Implemented as **deleting** the `TenantBranding` singleton row instead (it carries no `deletedAt`, so this is a real hard delete), which makes the tenant fall through the exact same "every tenant with no tenant branding" path §9.3 already documents as a first-class resolution destination — one fallback mechanism, not two.

**Two real, pre-existing bugs found by actually exercising this code end-to-end against a live database (not fakes) — the same discipline this session has applied everywhere else:**
1. My own bug, self-inflicted and self-caught: the new migration file's header comment explained the tenant-template replay mechanism in prose that *contained the literal substring* `tenant_template` several times. `tenantTemplateStatements()`'s tenant-statement extraction is a lexical substring match with no notion of a SQL comment — it retained the whole comment block plus the file's leading `BEGIN TRY`/`BEGIN TRAN` as a "tenant statement" and executed it, producing `Incorrect syntax near 'TRAN'`. Fixed by rewriting the comment to describe the mechanism without spelling the identifier with its underscore (the exact same landmine this fix's own comment is careful to avoid re-planting).
2. Not mine, and load-bearing: `CK_TenantMigrations_state`'s original closed set (`Pending`/`Applied`/`Failed`/`Skipped`) was missing `Running` — which `RunTenantMigrations.execute()` unconditionally writes first, on *every* invocation, for *every* tenant. This blocked the happy path entirely, not an edge case (unlike the structurally similar, deliberately-undone `CK_Tenants_status`/`"Failed"` gap `tenant-registry.ts`'s own module comment already documents, which only guards a rare failure-recovery path). Found only because this wave built the first production `MigrationExecutor`/`MigrationStatusStore` and actually ran a migration through `RunTenantMigrations` for real — no fake exercises a real CHECK constraint. Fixed in `001_constraints.sql`'s source text and, since T-SQL cannot alter an existing CHECK's definition in place, applied live (`DROP CONSTRAINT` + re-`ADD`) against the shared dev database as part of the proof run.

**Filled a real, pre-existing gap found along the way, scoped narrowly: `RunTenantMigrations` had no production `MigrationExecutor`/`MigrationStatusStore` anywhere in this codebase** — only `run-tenant-migrations.test.ts`'s fakes existed, and `package.json`'s `db:migrate` script points at `scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs`, which does not exist on disk. Built `PrismaMigrationExecutor` (reuses `sql-script.ts`'s existing `tenantTemplateStatements`/`substituteSchema`/`tenantSection` primitives — the same ones `SqlStoreProvisioner` already uses for a brand-new tenant's initial DDL replay; `applyToTenant` additionally re-applies `001_constraints.sql`'s whole tenant section unconditionally afterwards, safe because that file is fully idempotent by construction (RISK-025), which is what keeps an incrementally-migrated tenant converging on the same end state as a freshly-provisioned one) and `PrismaMigrationStatusStore` (backed by the real `platform.TenantMigrations`/`MigrationRuns`). `applyToPlatform` runs only genuinely `[platform].`-touching statements (a new `platformStatements()` helper, tested), not the whole migration file verbatim — this wave's own migration has none, so it is a safe no-op on every invocation, including a resumed run, since Prisma's raw DDL carries no `IF NOT EXISTS` guards and a second run of the whole file would fail. **Did not build the general migration CLI** (`scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs` itself — argument parsing, environment selection, dry-run wiring) — that is a real, separate, pre-existing gap, found and flagged, not silently expanded into scope.

**A required permission-scope widening, small and necessary, not scope creep:** `TenantContext.platformScope` was `"provisioning" | "analytics-rollup"` — running a migration is the same class of platform-level, cross-tenant, already-audited operation (`RunTenantMigrations.execute()` already calls `audit.record({ action: "migration.run", ... })`), so it needed its own declared value rather than misusing `"provisioning"`. Added `"migration"`; extended `tenant-context.test.ts`'s existing `it.each` over declarable scopes to include it (the same transcription discipline as the permission matrix).

**The `/settings/appearance/reset` route (task 5).** Renders using literal values read from `SHARJAH_DEFAULT.tokens` (never a `var(--…)` custom property), so it stays legible even if the ambient theme is genuinely broken — the one `design-gate-allow` exception in this file is exactly that literal `1px solid` border, justified inline. Fully i18n'd via `next-intl` (`messages/{en,ar}.json`'s new `appearanceReset` section) after the `no-hardcoded-user-string` gate correctly flagged the first draft's hardcoded prose — extended properly rather than suppressed. Two Server Actions (`actions.ts`, a dedicated file with a module-level `"use server"` — required, see below): resetting your own preference needs no permission (§9.3: "the user, for themselves"); resetting tenant branding is gated by `requirePermission(principal, "appearance:manage", ...)`, matching api.md §12 invariant 2's convention that the *caller* checks, not the use case. Both call through `ResetAppearance`, which routes the tenant-wide path through the real `assertContrastPasses` seam a future arbitrary-skin save flow will reuse.

**A found, documented, environment-specific `next build` limitation — not a defect in the reachable code.** Adding this route's Server Action (the first one anywhere in this app) reproducibly broke `next build` on this Windows machine — `TypeError: Cannot read properties of undefined (reading 'server')` inside Next's `FlightClientEntryPlugin.createActionAssets`, preceded by `EPERM: operation not permitted, scandir 'C:\Users\<user>\Application Data'` (or a sibling legacy profile junction) — specifically and only when a Prisma-touching import is reachable from a `"use server"` file. Isolated by direct experiment, not guessed at: a Prisma-free stand-in for both actions builds cleanly; the real ones reproduce the failure whether the import is static, a plain dynamic `import()`, or `/* webpackIgnore: true */`. Matches a documented, Windows-only Next.js + Prisma interaction (prisma/prisma#27934, vercel/next.js#62281) triggered by a *custom* Prisma generator `output` path on a non-`C:\` drive — exactly ADR-0011's two-generated-clients setup on this `D:\` checkout. `outputFileTracingExcludes` (tried) does not help — that option governs the `output: "standalone"` deployment trace, a different step from the one this crashes in. Removing the custom Prisma output path is not an option (ADR-0011 depends on it). No config-only fix was found after several well-targeted attempts; documented in full in `actions.ts`'s own doc comment rather than hidden. Does not affect correctness or the actual verification bar: `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`'s own stage list has no `next build` step, `next dev` serves the route correctly (verified directly — real `GET` returned 200 with the expected content, sign-in prompts rendering correctly for the current no-session state), and every other check (typecheck, lint, every gate, the full unit suite) is clean.

**The root layout, wired for real, with one honest, load-bearing gap named rather than papered over.** `[locale]/layout.tsx` now calls `resolveTheme()` and `serializeResolvedTheme()` in place of the `serializeBaseStylesheet("light")` placeholder, sets `data-theme`/`data-density` per §9.4, and resolves the *effective* direction (a user/tenant override can win over the locale's own default) for both `dir` and Radix's `DirectionProvider`. `theme.appTitle` (per-tenant, white-labelling) is surfaced via `generateMetadata`, not a static `export const metadata` — a static export cannot vary per request — sharing one resolved value with the layout itself via React's `cache()`. **The gap:** nothing in this codebase yet binds a `TenantContext` for an ordinary page request — `middleware.ts` only negotiates the locale; resolving a tenant from a citizen-facing channel is B-6's job, and wiring staff session resolution into the App Router (the already-built, framework-agnostic `AuthMiddleware` needs a route-level caller that doesn't exist yet) is B-2's. So `tryGetTenantContext()` returns `undefined` for every real request today, and the layout falls back to `NullThemeRepository` — a real, production null-object adapter (not a test double) that resolves to pure system default via the *same* code path a bound-but-unbranded tenant already uses, proven correct by both the unit suite and the live proof's own "fresh tenant, no branding yet" checks. Verified directly: real `GET /en` and `/ar` responses carry the correct `lang`/`dir`/`data-theme`/`data-density`, a real resolved `<style id="shj3-theme">` block, and the `Accept-CH`/`Critical-CH` headers.

**`Cache-Control`/`Vary` — §9.4's own explicit cache-isolation requirement, found while checking the live response, not in the original five tasks.** Added `Cache-Control: private` and a combined `Vary: sec-ch-prefers-color-scheme, Cookie` in `next.config.ts`. Verified directly against a real response: for the actual themed pages (dynamically rendered, since the layout reads `cookies()`/`headers()`), Next's own automatic `Cache-Control: no-store, must-revalidate` for dynamic rendering wins over this header and is stricter than §9.4 asks for; Next's own computed `Vary` (RSC payload variance) similarly wins over the value set here. This header is the defence-in-depth statement of intent and the effective setting for anything *not* dynamically rendered (e.g. `/api/healthz`) — not redundant, since Next's stricter default only exists because this layout reads request-scoped data.

**Verification, all real runs, transcribed in full, nothing accepted on faith:**
- `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **PASSED, 17/17 stages**: format, lint, typecheck, all 8 gates (`no-hardcoded-design-values` — 10 documented exceptions, one newly mine and justified; `no-raw-cypher`; `no-unscoped-store-clients`; `prisma-sqlalchemy-drift` — regenerated via `pnpm db:models` after the schema edit, 135 models, same count as before since only fields were added to existing models; `tailwind-theme-drift`; `no-undefined-token`; `no-raw-table`; `no-hardcoded-user-string`), `test:unit` (**795/795**, up from 741 pre-existing — 54 new, zero regressions), and the full Python side (`py:format`/`py:lint`/`py:types`/`py:arch`/`py:test`, 169 passed).
- `pnpm -r run typecheck` — clean, both real sub-projects (`packages/tokens`, `apps/web`). (The bare, non-recursive `pnpm run typecheck` at repo root fails identically and reproducibly with `error TS5083: Cannot read file 'tsconfig.json'` — there is genuinely no root `tsconfig.json`, only `tsconfig.base.json`; this is the same class of pre-existing, environment-level tooling gap the B-1 wave already documented for this exact command, not something this wave introduced or needs to fix.)
- `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` — clean.
- **The live migration-against-a-real-tenant proof, mandatory and not skipped.** A throwaway script (`_theming_migration_proof.ts`, deleted after — see below) drove the real `ProvisionTenant`, `RunTenantMigrations`, `PrismaMigrationExecutor`, `PrismaMigrationStatusStore`, `seedSystemSkins`, `PrismaThemeRepository` and `ResolveTheme` against a real probe tenant (`themingprobe`) on the live Compose stack (SQL Server, Neo4j, Qdrant, Redis, all four via the real provisioners — no fakes), plus a live `shj3-ai` instance for the graph/vector legs:
  1. Provisioned the probe tenant with the full current codebase — proved the brand-new-tenant path already carries this wave's columns/constraints/trigger.
  2. Directly reverted *only the probe tenant's live schema* to the pre-migration shape (dropped the new columns/constraints/trigger) — the realistic way to simulate an existing, not-yet-migrated tenant, after an earlier attempt (temporarily moving the new migration folder aside before provisioning) surfaced the "poisoned migration comment" bug above and was abandoned as unrealistic (`001_constraints.sql` is not independently versioned from the Prisma migration history — both move together in one commit, so faking one without the other produces a state that could never occur).
  3. Confirmed via direct `sys.columns`/`sys.check_constraints`/`sys.triggers` queries that the revert genuinely removed them.
  4. Ran `RunTenantMigrations.execute()` for real, targeting only the probe tenant — `{"applied":["themingprobe"],"skipped":[],"failed":[],"notAttempted":[],"stoppedEarly":false}` — and (after the `CK_TenantMigrations_state` fix above) it completed.
  5. Confirmed every column/constraint/trigger restored, and `platform.TenantMigrations` records `Applied` with a checksum matching the real migration file's SHA-256, not a placeholder.
  6. Seeded the platform shipped skins for real: exactly one `isSystem` `platform.Skins` row named "Sharjah Default", exactly two `SystemDefault` `platform.TokenSets` rows, both carrying a real `contrastValidatedAt`.
  7. Inserted a real custom tenant skin (distinct primary colours in *both* modes, deliberately, after a first attempt only varied the light mode and accidentally passed for the wrong reason since the resolved mode was dark) plus a `TenantBranding` and one `UserThemePreference` row (`mode: "Dark"` only, every other column `NULL`), then called the real `ResolveTheme`/`PrismaThemeRepository` against this live data: resolved `mode: "dark"` (the user's one override, honoured), `density: "Compact"` and `appTitle: "Probe Tenant Assistant"` (the tenant's branding, untouched), `primary: "#7A3FB0"` (the tenant's real dark-mode brand colour, genuinely read back from the database) — **the single most important behavioural guarantee, now proven against a real database, not only fakes.**
  8. Attempted to soft-delete the tenant's active skin directly against the live database — rejected by the real `TR_Skins_blockActiveDeletion` trigger with error 51021, exactly as defined.
  9. Tore down the probe tenant across all four stores (`ProvisionTenant`'s real provisioners' `destroy()`), then independently re-verified in a second, separate script: `registry row present: false`, `SQL schema present: false`.
  Both throwaway scripts (`_theming_migration_proof.ts`, `_verify_teardown_temp.ts`) confirmed deleted from the repo root afterward — nothing left behind.
- **The per-token merge order test** (verification item 4): `theme.test.ts` (pure `mergeColorTokens`, seven cases including a deliberately partial tenant object simulating a pre-schema-evolution skin) and `resolve-theme.test.ts` (full use-case level, fakes) both assert a user override on one token leaves every unrelated tenant-branding token untouched — and the live proof (step 7 above) proves the identical guarantee against a real database.
- **The contrast-gate-blocks test** (verification item 5): `appearance-gate.test.ts` calls the real `checkContrast`/`isBlocking` from `@shj3/tokens` (never reimplemented or mocked) against the shipped default skin with one token deliberately corrupted (`foreground` set equal to `background`, a genuine 1:1 ratio), asserting `ContrastBlockedError` with the real failing pair named in the report; a companion case corrupts two tokens and asserts both are named, not just the first.
- Confirmed no probe tenants or throwaway scripts left behind (above) — the only remaining trace of the proof is the real, permanent artefacts it was proving: the migration file, the seeded platform skins, and the `CK_TenantMigrations_state` live fix.

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/modules/theming/**` (20 files — domain/ports/application/adapters/testing, listed in full above the review section this replaces), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/sql/{ulid,migration-executor,migration-executor.test,migration-status-store}.ts`, `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/settings/appearance/reset/{page.tsx,actions.ts}`, `prisma/migrations/20260908221045_theming_appearance_overrides/migration.sql`, `scripts/seed-system-skins.ts`. Modified: `prisma/tenant/schema.prisma` (3 new fields across 2 models), `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` (2 new CHECK constraints, 1 new trigger, 1 bug-fixed CHECK), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/domain/permissions.ts`+`.test.ts` (9th permission), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/tenancy/tenant-context.ts`+`.test.ts` (`platformScope: "migration"`), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx`, `apps/web/next.config.ts`, `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json`, `package.json` (`db:seed:appearance`), `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/sql/_generated_models.py` (regenerated, not hand-edited).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave:** two real, separate gaps now exist in the general (not theming-specific) migration story, both found rather than assumed, neither fixed because both are outside this wave's scope: `scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs` is referenced by `package.json` but absent from disk (no general migration CLI exists yet, only the `MigrationExecutor`/`MigrationStatusStore` it would need, which this wave built for real), and `next build` cannot yet succeed on Windows for *any* future Server Action that touches Prisma, not just this one, until either Next.js or Prisma resolves the upstream interaction (or the project moves off Windows dev, or off the custom Prisma output path ADR-0011 requires). Both are named here so the next wave that hits either does not have to rediscover them.

**Independently re-verified by me, not accepted on the strength of this write-up alone.** Confirmed by direct grep against the real `prisma/*/schema.prisma` files that the pre-flight finding is accurate — `PlatformTokenSet`/`PlatformSkin`/`TokenSet`/`Skin`/`TenantBranding`/`BrandAsset`/`UserThemePreference` all genuinely pre-date this wave; captured as a new lesson in `tasks/lessons.md` (my own brief was the thing wrong here, not this wave's work — worth remembering before the next "add persistence for X" brief). Re-ran `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` myself (17/17). Read the migration SQL, the new `TR_Skins_blockActiveDeletion` trigger, and `resolve-theme.ts` directly rather than trusting the description: the trigger correctly distinguishes a hard `DELETE` from the schema's real soft-delete `UPDATE` path and is soundly, deliberately asymmetric between tenant-wide and personal skins; `resolve-theme.ts` holds zero vendor imports and its per-token merge is structural, not a discipline that could quietly regress — each resolved field is its own independent call over the user/tenant tiers, so a whole-object-pick bug is unreachable by construction, not merely avoided by convention. `CK_TenantBrandings_fontSize`/`CK_UserThemePreferences_fontSize` confirmed `parent_object_id`-scoped — no RISK-025 regression.

### `SkinEditor` organism + Settings → Appearance route (Phase E, final piece) — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The real `SkinEditor` organism (design-system.md §5.5 #55) and the real `/settings/appearance` route it lives on, built against the theming backend wave's actual (not illustrative) API surface — never a second, parallel implementation of validation, contrast-gating, or the skin schema. This was the last piece of Phase E and the 56th of 57 named components; only `HelpGuideShell` (Phase F) remains.

**Task 1 — `ThemeRepository` extended, not replaced.** Eight new methods on the port (`listTenantSkins`, `readTenantSkinDetail`, `saveTenantAppearance`, `applyExistingTenantSkin`, `createTenantSkin`, `renameTenantSkin`, `deleteTenantSkin`, `applyUserSkin`, `saveUserPreferenceScalars` — nine, counting `saveUserPreferenceScalars` separately from `applyUserSkin`), implemented for real in `PrismaThemeRepository`, faked in `FakeThemeRepository`, and given honest, loud-failure no-ops in `NullThemeRepository` (see that file's own doc comment on why "throw" is correct there and "no-op" is correct for the pre-existing reset methods — two different contracts for two different reasons, not an inconsistency). Two structural decisions, both because the schema already had the shape and using it beat inventing a parallel one:
- **A `TokenSet` is never mutated in place.** Editing a skin's colours creates a NEW `TokenSet` pair via the lineage FK (`parentTokenSetId`, already in the schema and unused before this) and soft-deletes the pair it replaces — a skin's colour history stays reconstructable, and using an existing-but-idle relation is more architecturally consistent than overwriting `tokensJson` in place.
- **`Skin.isTenantDefault` is kept in sync with `TenantBranding.activeSkinId`** by this adapter — true on exactly the active skin, false on every sibling — rather than treated as a second, independently-set source of truth. `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault` (at most one row WHERE `isTenantDefault = 1`) is what makes this a real constraint the write must satisfy, not a convention — see the bug below, which is exactly that constraint doing its job.

**A real, serious bug found live, not by inspection: mid-transaction unique-constraint violations on `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault`.** All three places that flip which skin is active (`saveTenantAppearance`'s create and update branches, `applyExistingTenantSkin`) originally set the NEW skin's `isTenantDefault: true` *before* clearing the previous holder's. SQL Server checks a filtered unique index after each statement in a transaction, not only at commit, so for one statement in the middle of the transaction two rows simultaneously satisfied "at most one `isTenantDefault = 1`" and the whole transaction failed with P2002 — every single time a tenant applied a different skin, or saved while any skin was already active, which is the overwhelmingly common case, not an edge one. `FakeThemeRepository` does not model the unique constraint at all (it just overwrites a single `activeSkinId` field), so every fake-backed test — including the one written specifically to prove "cross-row consistency" — passed while this was broken. **Found only because the live-infrastructure proof below actually exercised it against a real SQL Server**, exactly the scenario this session's own precedent warns a fake cannot cover. Fixed in all three places by reordering: unset every other skin's flag first, then set the target one, with a comment at each site pointing at this entry so the reasoning doesn't have to be rediscovered. Re-proved live afterward (see below) — this is not a "should be fixed now" note, it is fixed and independently re-verified against the real database.

**Task 2 — `application/manage-appearance.ts` (new file), the write-side orchestration.** `ManageAppearance` runs the real `assertContrastPasses` (never reimplemented) on both modes independently before persisting either, so a failure in one mode reports that mode's specific blockers rather than a generic refusal. `SYSTEM_DEFAULT_SKIN_ID` is a synthetic, never-persisted sentinel for the Skin Manager's always-present "system default" row; applying it routes through the *existing* `ResetAppearance.resetTenantBranding` rather than inventing a second "apply" meaning for the one entry with no row to repoint `activeSkinId` at. Duplicating the system default needs no `getPlatformDb()` call — it reads `@shj3/tokens`' static `systemSkin()` export, the identical reasoning `resolve-theme.ts` already established for the read path. A small, genuine DRY fix landed alongside this: `resolve-theme.ts`'s fallback appTitle/shadowDepth/sidebarStyle expressions (for "no `TenantBranding` row yet") were duplicated by hand into what would have been a second copy for `manage-appearance.ts`'s baseline-creation path — extracted instead into one shared `systemFallbackAppearance()` in `domain/theme.ts`, used by both, with a test of its own; `resolve-theme.test.ts` continues to pass unchanged, confirming the refactor is behaviour-preserving.

**A real architecture violation caught before it shipped, not after.** Early section components imported `DbMode`/`DbDirection`/`DbDensity`/`FontSizeValue` directly from `modules/theming/domain/theme.ts` and `TenantBrandingScalars`/`SkinManagerEntry`/`TenantSkinDeletionResult` from the feature module's port/application files. `eslint-plugin-boundaries`' `components` element rule (`apps/web/src/components/**` may depend on `components`/`shared` only, never `feature`) exists exactly to stop this — "a component that reaches into backend code stops being swappable independently of the app that consumes it." Caught by running `eslint . --max-warnings=0` before treating the organism as done, not by a slower afterthought. Fixed properly rather than suppressed: `components/patterns/skin-editor/skin-editor-types.ts` now declares the organism's *own* vocabulary (`SkinEditorMode`, `SkinEditorDirection`, `SkinEditorDensity`, `SkinEditorFontSize`, `SkinEditorBrandingScalars`, `SkinEditorPersonalPreference`, `SkinEditorSkinEntry`, `SkinEditorDeletionResult`) — structurally identical to the feature module's types, deliberately duplicated rather than imported, mirroring the precedent `PermissionMatrix`'s agnostic `confirmChange` seam already set. The Settings → Appearance *route* (`app`-type, allowed to depend on both `feature` and `components`) is what maps between the two. Every remaining `@shj3/tokens` import in the organism (`SemanticColorTokens`, `ContrastReport`, `isHexColor`, `checkContrast`, etc.) is fine as-is — that package lives outside `apps/web/src/**` entirely and the boundary rule never covered it.

**Task 2 — the organism itself.** Fifteen new files under `apps/web/src/components/patterns/skin-editor/` (~2,550 lines including tests): `apply-candidate.ts` (§9.2's live-preview closure, verbatim to the brief's reference implementation, built on `@shj3/tokens`' own `toCssCustomPropertyName` rather than a second kebab-case helper), `use-skin-candidate.ts` (the light/dark-independent candidate hook), `contrast-report-to-conditions.ts` (maps the real `ContrastReport` onto `SummaryStrip`'s `blocking` variant), `color-field.tsx` (the one screen with a real, native colour swatch plus the `Input` atom's `mono` variant for the hex text — the directory's own standing `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` exemption, confirmed present and used correctly, with zero new exceptions added anywhere), five section components (`brand-`, `semantic-`, `typography-`, `layout-`, `mode-direction-section.tsx`), `skin-manager.tsx` (list/apply/duplicate/rename/delete/export/import, a real `DataTable` — `no-raw-table.mjs` has no exemption for this directory, matching every other feature screen), `skin-preview.tsx` (the real `Card`/`Badge`/`Button`/`DataTable`/`SubTabBar`/`Switch`/`ProgressBar`/`SummaryStrip`/`ChatThread`, never static markup), and `skin-editor.tsx` (the composing organism). All six of §9.2's never-half-applied rules are real, not claimed, each with its own test: atomic save (one `saveTenantAppearance` call, never per-field), blocked-not-warned on contrast failure, discard-by-closure (never a refetch), a navigation guard, an always-available reset link, and light/dark mode independence.

**Judgment calls, each flagged in code at the point made:**
1. **Font family, scale ratio, base weight, corner radius, sidebar width, and focus ring width/offset render as honest, non-interactive, translated notes, not controls.** No column exists for any of them anywhere in this schema — confirmed directly, not assumed — and the `TenantBranding` Prisma model's own pre-existing comment already states why ("the shipped skins' typography and geometry are otherwise identical... served from `@shj3/tokens`' static exports rather than round-tripped through a row"). This is the prior wave's own established scope boundary, extended rather than re-litigated; building a control that silently no-ops on Save would be the actual lie §9.1 warns against, not the honest note in its place.
2. **Personal skin authoring is narrower than §9.3's table taken literally.** Any authenticated user may apply an *already-saved* skin (system or tenant) to their own preference — real, wired, tested. Authoring a brand-new custom-colour skin as a plain user is not built: the schema has no "who owns this skin" column, so a tenant `Skin` row is shared/visible the moment it exists regardless of who created it, and there is no clean way to let a non-admin create colour rows without also, structurally, letting them create rows an admin could later apply tenant-wide. Every colour-authoring method is gated by `appearance:manage` in the Server Action layer.
3. **No suggested nearest-passing colour value.** §10.2's prose describes one computed by walking OKLCH lightness; neither the real `ContrastCheck` shape nor `appearance-gate.ts` computes one today (checked directly). The brief's own instruction was to use one if it exists, not invent a second contrast-fixing algorithm — it doesn't exist, so the failure report names the pair, the measured ratio, and the required ratio, and stops there. A named, out-of-scope gap for a future wave.
4. **Export produces two JSON files, one per mode, never a combined document.** `@shj3/tokens`' real, already-tested skin schema is genuinely per-mode (exactly how `SHARJAH_DEFAULT`/`SHARJAH_DARK` ship as two files, not one); a combined-pair wrapper format would be a second, parallel schema `validateSkin` cannot recognise.
5. **Import lands the validated, gap-filled colour set into whichever mode tab is open in the candidate — it does not create a second, parallel "unapplied imported skin" persistence path.** This gives import every one of the never-half-applied guarantees (atomic save, contrast gate, discard-by-closure) for free, rather than needing its own.
6. **Logo/favicon upload is not built.** No `BrandAsset`-writing adapter exists anywhere in this codebase (checked directly). Rendered as an honest note in the Brand section rather than a non-functional upload control.
7. **The navigation guard covers real browser navigation (`beforeunload`) and this page's own "Reset to default" link; it does not intercept `AppShell`'s sidebar.** A page-agnostic guard belongs to `AppShell` itself, a different, already-built organism this wave does not touch.
8. **`applyTenantSkinAction`'s fallback locale direction (`"LTR"`) is reached only by the narrow "Apply from the Skin Manager with no prior save at all" path** — a Server Action has no request-scoped locale parameter. The common path (the main editor's own Save) always supplies the real `defaultDirection` from form state, and the *route's* own baseline-creation path (see below) captures the admin's actual current locale rather than this fallback.

**Two small, real infrastructure additions, both flagged rather than silently bundled in:**
- **`NextIntlClientProvider` added to `[locale]/layout.tsx`.** `SkinEditor` is the first Client Component in this app carrying enough text (dozens of labels across five sections, dialogs, the manager) that prop-drilling every string from its Server Component page would mean an unwieldy, ever-growing surface rather than `useTranslations()` — which requires this provider. Purely additive: every existing `getTranslations()` call (Server Components) is unaffected.
- **`apps/web/src/i18n/locale-direction.ts` extracted** from `[locale]/layout.tsx`'s own inline `LOCALE_DIRECTIONS` lookup, because a second real caller now needs it: the appearance route derives a brand-new tenant's starting `defaultDirection` from the admin's *own current locale* rather than hardcoding `"LTR"` — hardcoding would silently flip an Arabic-locale tenant's default direction the first time any admin saved an unrelated appearance setting, a real, subtle bug this project's own Arabic-parity concerns make worth avoiding. One shared source instead of two hand-typed copies.

**Task 3 — the route.** `app/[locale]/settings/appearance/{page.tsx,actions.ts}`, sibling to `reset/`, untouched. Nine Server Actions, each requiring a real principal via `requirePrincipal`, with `appearance:manage` additionally required for every tenant-wide write (`requirePermission`, checked by the caller, matching api.md §12 invariant 2 — `ManageAppearance` itself does not check it). Degrades honestly rather than crashing for the same real, named gap `reset/page.tsx` already renders around (no `TenantContext` binding into the App Router yet, B-2): no context → "no tenant context available yet"; context but no principal → a sign-in prompt; principal but no `appearance:manage` → the real editor, with tenant-wide controls disabled and a visible notice, never silently hidden. Reproduces the identical, already-documented Windows `next build` + Prisma limitation `reset/actions.ts` describes in full (not re-litigated here) — irrelevant to the actual bar, which is `next dev` plus the gates.

**Testing — real commands, real behaviour.** `apply-candidate.test.ts` (6 tests) asserts actual inline `style` properties before/after apply and after discard directly against jsdom's `CSSStyleDeclaration`, including that reverting never touches the server-rendered `<style id="shj3-theme">` block. `use-skin-candidate.test.tsx` (6 tests) proves mode independence: editing one mode leaves the other's colours byte-for-byte untouched, and `discard()`/`markSaved()` revert to the correct baseline. `contrast-report-to-conditions.test.ts` (5 tests) maps the *real* `checkContrast()`'s output, never a hand-built fixture. `manage-appearance.test.ts` (18 tests, `FakeThemeRepository`) covers the full CRUD surface including the trigger-block simulation. `skin-editor.test.tsx` (5 tests) renders the real, fully composed organism and proves: the light/dark independent-save guarantee end to end (edit light, switch, edit dark, Save sends both in one call), the contrast gate genuinely blocking a bad save (real `SummaryStrip` text asserted, unsaved badge still present), the `beforeunload` listener registering/deregistering with dirty state, the Reset-link nav-guard raising a real confirmation before navigating, and discard reverting the rendered DOM value. Chose fakes as the primary proof for CRUD *logic* (fast, deterministic, already the established pattern for every other `application/*.test.ts` in this module) but — given the brief's explicit steer toward proving DB-touching code against something real when the stakes are this high, and given the live Compose stack was actually running in this environment (checked, not assumed) — also ran a full live-infrastructure proof, described next, which is what actually caught the one serious bug above.

**The live-infrastructure proof, in full, mirroring the theming backend wave's own `_theming_migration_proof.ts` pattern exactly.** A throwaway script (`_skin_editor_proof.ts`, deleted after) provisioned a real probe tenant (`skineditorproof`) against the live Compose SQL Server and Redis (Neo4j/Qdrant faked via two trivial no-op objects satisfying `StoreProvisioner` — no `shj3-ai` instance needed for this proof), then drove the real `ManageAppearance` + `PrismaThemeRepository` end to end:
1. `saveTenantAppearance` create path — persisted, read back verbatim (the full 66-key colour set, byte-for-byte).
2. `saveTenantAppearance` update path — same skin id reused, the edited colour genuinely persisted, still exactly one row (the lineage-FK replace, not an in-place mutation, working correctly).
3. `duplicateSystemDefaultSkin` + `applyTenantSkin` — **this is where the `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault` bug above was first caught**, on the very first live run, before the ordering fix; re-run afterward and passed, with real cross-row consistency confirmed (the newly applied skin `isActive: true`, the previously active one correctly flipped to `false`).
4. `TR_Skins_blockActiveDeletion` — attempted deleting the active skin; genuinely blocked, and the *exact* real error text (`"A skin currently applied as active tenant branding cannot be soft-deleted. Apply a different skin first."`, SQL Server error 51021) confirmed `blockingTriggerReason()`'s substring-matching correctly recognises the real, live-wrapped Prisma error — this was written without ever having observed the actual wrapping shape (no live database at design time), and it matched on the first real attempt.
5. Deleting the non-active skin — succeeded, genuinely gone on re-read.
6. `applyPersonalSkin` + `savePersonalPreferenceScalars` — the personal pointer resolves to the real skin's real colours; scalars persist independently of it.
7. `renameTenantSkin` — persisted.
8. `listSkinsForManager` — the synthetic system entry and the real active tenant skin both present and correctly flagged.
9. Teardown — all four stores destroyed, the platform registry row and `VectorCollectionRegistryEntry` rows hard-deleted, then independently re-verified from a fresh read: registry row gone, SQL schema gone.

The proof script's own robustness needed one fix along the way, applied and kept in the deleted file's final state before deletion: teardown moved into a `finally` block, because the first (buggy) run crashed mid-proof and left the probe tenant registered — "a slug is never reused" then blocked a bare retry until a separate manual cleanup pass hard-deleted the leftover registry row. Both throwaway scripts (`_skin_editor_proof.ts`, and the one-off `_cleanup_probe.ts` written to recover from that) confirmed deleted from the repo root afterward.

**Verification, all real runs, transcribed in full:**
- `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **PASSED, 17/17 stages** (format, lint, typecheck, all 8 gates, `test:unit` 37 files / 820 tests, full Python side 169 tests) — re-run *after* the `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault` fix, not before it.
- `pnpm exec vitest run --project components` — **60/60 files, 554/554 tests**, up from 57/537 pre-existing (zero regressions; the +3 files/+17 tests are `skin-editor.test.tsx` plus `apply-candidate`/`use-skin-candidate`, both renamed `.test.ts` → `.test.tsx` after discovering `document is not defined` in the node-environment `unit` project — hook/DOM tests route to the jsdom `components` project by file extension in this repo's vitest config, matching the existing `use-wizard-draft.test.tsx` precedent, not by content).
- `pnpm -r run typecheck` and `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` — clean, including the `eslint-plugin-boundaries` check the architecture violation above was caught by.
- `node scripts/gates/{no-hardcoded-design-values,no-undefined-token,no-raw-table,no-hardcoded-user-string}.mjs` — all four clean. **Zero new `design-gate-allow` exceptions added anywhere** (confirmed by direct grep before and after) — the organism relies entirely on real tokens plus the standing, already-present `skin-editor/` directory exemption in `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs`'s `ALLOWED_PREFIXES` (added ahead of time in B-1's enforcement wave specifically for this component; confirmed still present and used only for the one genuinely-legitimate raw-colour surface, never a second exception).
- Message-catalogue parity: `messages/en.json` and `messages/ar.json` independently verified to hold identical key sets (152 keys each) via a throwaway key-diff script, not eyeballed.
- `next dev` — the real, required check: started twice (once before the bug fix, once after), both times compiling `/[locale]/settings/appearance` cleanly (1810–1823 modules, zero errors) and returning `200` for `/en/settings/appearance`, `/ar/settings/appearance`, and the sibling `/en/settings/appearance/reset` (confirming no regression to the already-shipped route). Server stopped after each check.

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/components/patterns/skin-editor/**` (15 files), `apps/web/src/modules/theming/application/{manage-appearance.ts,manage-appearance.test.ts}`, `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/settings/appearance/{page.tsx,actions.ts}`, `apps/web/src/i18n/locale-direction.ts`. Modified: `apps/web/src/modules/theming/ports/theme-repository.ts` (8 new methods + supporting types), `apps/web/src/modules/theming/adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-theme-repository.ts` (same 8, plus the `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault` ordering fix), `apps/web/src/modules/theming/adapters/outbound/null-theme-repository.ts`, `apps/web/src/modules/theming/testing/fakes.ts`, `apps/web/src/modules/theming/domain/theme.ts`+`.test.ts` (`systemFallbackAppearance`), `apps/web/src/modules/theming/application/resolve-theme.ts` (refactored to use it, behaviour-preserving), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/layout.tsx` (`NextIntlClientProvider`, `locale-direction.ts` extraction), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (+22 new keys net of nesting, 152 total each, parity verified).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave:** the `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault` bug is the important one — it shipped in my own first draft, was invisible to 18 passing fake-backed tests, and would have broken "apply a different skin" for every tenant in production on the first real use. It was only caught because this session's own precedent (checked against the actual gap identified earlier in the wireframe-review phase: "a gate that never fires buys false confidence") pushed toward proving DB-touching code live rather than trusting fakes alone — worth restating plainly since it is exactly the kind of bug that "all tests green" would otherwise have shipped with confidence. Two real, named, out-of-scope gaps carried forward rather than silently expanded into: no `suggestedForeground` contrast-fix computation (§10.2's own prose describes one that does not exist in code today), and no `BrandAsset` upload adapter (logo/favicon controls are honest notes, not fake buttons).

**Independently re-verified by me.** Read the actual `UQ_Skins_tenantDefault` fix directly at all three call sites (`saveTenantAppearance`'s create and update branches, plus the Skin-Manager apply-only path) — each correctly unsets every other skin's flag as a *separate, sequential* statement inside the same `db.$transaction([...])` array *before* the statement that sets the new skin's flag true, which is exactly what SQL Server's per-statement (not per-commit) filtered-unique-index check requires; the reasoning in the code comments matches real SQL Server semantics, not just a plausible-sounding story. Read `skin-editor-types.ts` — the component-boundary fix is a clean, correct application of the same generic-vocabulary pattern `PermissionMatrix`'s `confirmChange` seam already established (nice confirmation that the B-1 infra wave's `components` boundary lint rule is doing real, load-bearing work now that a genuinely complex organism pushed against it, not just passing because nothing has tested it yet). Re-ran `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` myself three times in a row — clean 17/17 every time. **Found and fixed one more thing myself while re-verifying**: the root `package.json`'s `"typecheck": "tsc --build --force"` script had no corresponding root `tsconfig.json` (confirmed missing) and was never actually invoked by the real gate (`pnpm -r run typecheck` doesn't count the root package as an iterable workspace project) — but an intermittent Windows/pnpm path-casing quirk twice pulled it into scope during my own re-verification passes and failed on the missing file, a flake no agent this session ever hit (each starts a fresh shell; mine had been alive and bounced between directories for the whole session). Removed the dead script rather than keep re-running past the flake it enabled — confirmed both the fix (three clean `verify.mjs` runs in a row afterward) and that it changed nothing real (`pnpm -r run typecheck` still correctly covers both actual sub-projects, unchanged).

**56 of 57 named design-system components now complete.** Only `HelpGuideShell` (Phase F) remains, still correctly deferred — it needs real navigation data from the feature modules (B-2 through B-9) that don't exist yet, so building it now would mean building its side-menu-mirrors-the-app model against nothing real.

### B-2 · IAM (B9: Users, Teams, Roles & permissions) — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The real IAM feature module: 9 new application use cases, two new ports/adapters (`TeamRepository`, `RoleRepository`), the first real `UserRepository`/`CredentialRepository` SQL adapters, the first-ever `(backoffice)` route group with the real `/iam` screen (first real `AppShell` mount, first real staff session resolution in the App Router), the separation-of-duties confirmation, the protected-cell `isLocked` wiring, and the long-open deterministic seed data — closed here since users/teams/roles are exactly this module's own data.

**Pre-flight findings, all verified directly against real files before writing anything (per the standing lesson) — several genuine gaps beyond what the brief anticipated:**

1. **No SQL adapter implemented `UserRepository` anywhere** — only `testing/fakes.ts`'s double. `LocalPasswordProvider` (already built, already tested) had never been run against a real database. Built `PrismaUserRepository` for real, and extended the port itself (below).
2. **`platform.Permissions` was never seeded** — zero `INSERT`s anywhere in the codebase, despite the brief's "already seeded presumably from B-0" assumption. Confirmed by grep, not assumed. `docs/data-model.md`'s prose names (`manage_agents`, `manage_users_teams`) are illustrative, not real — the actual, tested, load-bearing keys are `domain/permissions.ts`'s colon-form `PERMISSIONS` (`"users:manage"`, etc.), exactly the pattern `tasks/lessons.md`'s second entry already warns about, now hit a second time from a different angle.
3. **A real, load-bearing bug in `TR_RolePermissions_protectSuperAdmin`**: the trigger's literal was `'manage_users_teams'`, which no code path in this system ever persists — the real, tested convention is `'users:manage'`. The protection would never have fired for any row this system could actually write. Fixed the literal in `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql`; proved live (see below) that it now genuinely blocks the revoke.
4. **Nothing created a `TenantProfile` row for a provisioned tenant** — `TR_Tenants_syncProfiles` only *updates* an existing row; the row's own doc comment says the provisioning path is supposed to create it, but grep found no call site anywhere. Without it, `TR_Teams_crossEntityScope` could never legitimately admit an `AllEntities`-scoped team for *any* tenant — including B9 tab 2's own "Platform → All entities" row. Fixed at the root: `SqlStoreProvisioner.create()` now creates the singleton row, deriving `isPlatformTenant` from the tenant's `entityKind`.
5. **Nothing could register a tenant as `PlatformOperator`** — `TenantRegistry.register()`/`ProvisionTenantInput` had no `entityKind` field at all; `PrismaTenantRegistry` hardcoded `GovernmentEntity` for every tenant. Extended both (optional, defaulted — every existing caller, including the isolation suite's own fixture, is unaffected) so the seed script could provision a genuine platform-operator tenant for Ahmed Saeed's "Platform" team.
6. **`Role.key`/`Permission` display-name conventions didn't exist yet** — nothing had ever persisted a `Role` row. Added `ROLE_KEYS` (snake_case, adopted from the trigger's own pre-existing `'super_admin'` literal — the one part of this convention that already existed) and `SEEDED_ROLE_DISPLAY_NAMES`/`PERMISSION_CATALOG` (transcribed from the wireframe, zero I/O — mirrors `resolve-theme.ts`'s established "small code-defined catalog, not a database round trip" precedent) to `domain/permissions.ts`.
7. **`StaffUser.status` (Invited/Active/Suspended) and `StaffUser.deletedAt` are two different mechanisms, not one** — the model's own doc comment says status is "never a deletion", yet a `deletedAt` column exists and `UQ_StaffUsers_email` is filtered on it. Resolved as a judgment call, flagged plainly: B9's **Remove** action is `TenantMembership.revokedAt` (a tenant-scoped "no longer on this entity's roster"), not a global `StaffUsers.deletedAt` soft-delete — the latter would suspend a person's access to *every* tenant they belong to from *one* tenant's IAM screen, which B9's own per-tenant scoping does not ask for. `StaffUsers.deletedAt` stays real and genuinely unused by this wave.
8. **Empirically confirmed, not assumed (a throwaway `next dev` experiment, deleted after use): an `AsyncLocalStorage` context bound in a layout via `runWithTenant(ctx, () => children)` propagates to *neither* a nested page render *nor* a nested Server Action** — both came back unbound in a real, running test. This settles, with evidence, exactly what the theming wave's own doc comments left open ("wiring staff session resolution into the App Router is B-2['s job]"): there is no ambient, bind-once mechanism available, matching how Next's own `cookies()`/`headers()` are freshly established per invocation rather than inherited. Every real entry point (the page, every Server Action) now resolves its own context explicitly via `withStaffAuth()`, reusing `AuthMiddleware.handle()` as-is. **A real, out-of-scope consequence flagged, not fixed**: `settings/appearance/page.tsx`/`actions.ts`'s existing bare, unwrapped `tryGetTenantContext()`/`requirePrincipal()` calls will keep seeing no context under this finding too — that module's own routes, not touched here.
9. **A fourth, distinct `platformScope` value was needed**: `getPlatformDb()`'s gate (provisioning/analytics-rollup/migration) never anticipated the *routine*, per-request class of platform-schema access `UserRepository`'s real adapter now does on every authenticated request (reading `platform.StaffUsers`/`TenantMemberships`). Added `"identity"`, with its own doc comment on why it does *not* carry the same "every such path writes an audit entry" expectation as the other three (meaningful state changes already write their own explicit audit entries; routine reads are not the bulk/administrative operation class that requirement was written for).

**Task 1 — application use cases.** All 9 (`invite-user`, `edit-user`, `reactivate-user`, `remove-user`, `list-users`, `create-team`, `list-teams-with-live-membership`, `update-role-permissions`, `create-custom-role`) dispatched to a background agent once every port/convention above was frozen, with an extremely literal brief (exact type shapes, exact file-by-file judgment calls to make, exact verification bar). Independently re-verified by me: read `list-users.ts`, `update-role-permissions.ts` and `reactivate-user.ts` directly against the brief — all three matched precisely, including judgment calls I hadn't fully specified (e.g. `ReactivateUserResult` as `Record<string, never>` rather than an empty interface, to satisfy this repo's own `no-empty-object-type` lint rule). One real bug in the agent's own test (`list-teams-with-live-membership.test.ts`'s expected array was alphabetically mis-ordered — "Second Person" sorts after "Sara Al Mazrouei", not before), fixed directly. Final count: **21 test files, 339 tests, all green** in the `iam` module.

**New ports, sibling to `user-repository.ts` exactly as instructed** (`team-repository.ts`, `role-repository.ts`) plus real adapters (`PrismaTeamRepository`, `PrismaRoleRepository`, `PrismaCredentialRepository`, and — since none of B-2's own screens could render without it — `PrismaUserRepository`). `PrismaRoleRepository.updatePermissions()` implements the brief's own instruction precisely: all-or-nothing via one `$transaction([...])` array, and the protected-cell rejection is caught and translated into a structured `{ ok: false, reason: "protected", ... }` result (`findProtectedChange`, mirroring `prisma-theme-repository.ts`'s `blockingTriggerReason()` precedent) rather than a raw SQL error reaching the caller.

**Task 2 — the real `(backoffice)` route group and `/iam` screen.** `(backoffice)/layout.tsx` mounts `AppShell` for the first time (a thin `backoffice-shell.tsx` client wrapper supplies the callback props `AppShell` needs — `renderLink`/`onLocaleChange`/`onThemeModeChange` — which cannot cross the Server→Client boundary as plain functions). Three tabs via the real `SubTabBar`, URL-synced (`?tab=`) via its own `urlParam` mechanism — checked `SkinEditor`'s own section switcher first, confirmed it uses plain internal state (a different, narrower case), not the same precedent, so `SubTabBar`'s own established `urlParam` convention was used directly rather than a second URL-sync mechanism invented to match a wave that didn't actually set one. Server-side permission check (`requirePermission(principal, "users:manage", ...)`) gates the whole route, run inside `withStaffAuth`'s bound context before any of the route's own data is read. **Confirmed live, not assumed: Next's route groups are genuinely invisible in the URL** — `/en/(backoffice)/iam` 404s, `/en/iam` is the real path.

**`apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/inbound/next-request-context.ts` (new)** — the "route wrapper" the theming wave's own doc comments named as missing. `withStaffAuth()` builds a fresh `InboundRequest` from `next/headers` and drives the real `AuthMiddleware.handle()` at every call site (the page, and every Server Action in `actions.ts`) — never a shared ambient binding (see pre-flight finding 8). Composes the full real dependency graph for `LocalPasswordProvider` (`PrismaUserRepository`, `PrismaCredentialRepository`, `RedisChallengeStore`/`RedisReplayGuard`/`RedisEnrolmentTokenIssuer` — all of which *already existed* as real adapters, confirmed by reading `redis-session-store.ts` in full rather than assuming they needed building) — every dependency `LocalPasswordProvider` declares now has a real implementation behind it, which is what makes B-2 the first wave to actually exercise real staff sign-in end-to-end.

**A new envelope-encryption module, found necessary while wiring `PrismaCredentialRepository`.** `StaffCredentials.totpSecretCipher` is documented as "envelope-encrypted... the repository adapter is the only thing that ever sees the ciphertext or holds the key" — no such mechanism existed anywhere in the codebase. Built `platform/adapters/outbound/crypto/envelope-encryption.ts` (AES-256-GCM, hand-rolled from `node:crypto` — the same "don't add a supply-chain dependency for forty lines of well-specified arithmetic" reasoning `totp.ts` already established for RFC 6238), backed by a new `SHJ3_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (deployment.md §16 var #12 — documented but never actually consumed by any code before this). Extended `config.ts`'s boot-time validation to require it, unconditionally in every environment (not just deployed ones — AES-256's key length is a structural fact, not a "stronger in production" policy, unlike `SHJ3_SESSION_SECRET`'s own placeholder-rejection check). 6 new round-trip/tamper/wrong-key/truncation tests against the *real* cipher (no fake — an AES-GCM round trip is fast enough that hiding it behind one would buy nothing).

**Task 3 — deterministic seed data, closing the B-0 open item.** `scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts` (+ `modules/iam/ports/demo-data-seeder.ts`, `application/seed-demo-data.ts`, `adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-demo-data-seeder.ts`) — a dedicated port rather than reusing the B9 CRUD ports, because seeding needs operations B9's own screens must never expose (`isSystem: true` roles, direct status assignment bypassing the invite-and-accept path). Provisions four real tenants (`sewa`/`customs` — reusing the isolation suite's own established slugs/display names — plus new `libraries` and `sharjah`, the platform operator) and seeds B9's exact five sample rows. Fully idempotent — proved by running it three times against the live stack, confirming stable row counts each time, not just an absence of errors.

**A real, live-infrastructure interaction with the isolation suite, documented rather than silently risked**: `tests/isolation/setup.ts` provisions-then-destroys `sewa`/`customs` on every isolation run, a different lifecycle than this script's "seed once, leave it" data. Running the isolation suite after this script wipes both tenants; re-running this script repairs them fully (idempotent). Documented in the script's own header comment and here, not treated as a design flaw — the isolation suite is an occasional release-gate action, not part of the routine `verify.mjs --staged` loop.

**A real bug found on the very first live run of the seed script, not shipped blind**: `TenantMemberships_grantedByStaffUserId_fkey` is a real, same-platform-schema Prisma relation (unlike `TeamMember`/`RolePermission`/`UserRoleAssignment`'s sibling `*ByStaffUserId` columns, genuinely not FK-enforced per ADR-0011's cross-client limitation — confirmed by grep, not re-guessed, after getting burned once). A synthetic `SYSTEM_SEED_ACTOR_ID` placeholder satisfied every one of those but violated this one. Fixed by self-referencing — the newly created user's own id, inserted in the same transaction — "this account's own first membership was self-granted at bootstrap" being an honest fact, not a workaround.

**Task 4 — the separation-of-duties confirmation.** `roles-tab.tsx`'s `useSeparationOfDutiesConfirm` bridges `PermissionMatrix`'s callback-shaped `confirmChange` (`(changes) => boolean | Promise<boolean>`) to the real, imperative `DestructiveConfirmDialog` (`open`/`onOpenChange`/`onConfirm`): a proposed batch is inspected for exactly one shape (granting `agents:publish` to a role that is neither Super Admin nor Entity Admin, matching `RESTRICTED_PERMISSIONS`), and when it matches, the returned `Promise<boolean>` resolves only when a human actually clicks Confirm or dismisses the dialog — `PermissionMatrix` genuinely waits. The rule is composed here, in the feature module, never hardcoded inside the generic, correctly-agnostic `PermissionMatrix`/`usePermissionMatrixGrid` (confirmed unmodified).

**Task 5 — the `isLocked` wiring.** `roles-tab.tsx`'s `isLocked` renders Super Admin's `users:manage` cell as visually locked proactively; the real enforcement is `PrismaRoleRepository.updatePermissions()`'s server-side rejection regardless of what the client believes (proved live — see below). A rejected change triggers `router.refresh()` to reconcile the optimistic grid back to the real, persisted matrix, plus a real translated notice naming why.

**Live-infrastructure verification, mandatory and not skipped — the same bar as every prior wave:**
- **The seed script**, run three times against the real stack (SQL Server, Neo4j, Qdrant, Redis, `shj3-ai`, all already up from earlier in this session): first run provisioned all four tenants and seeded everything; a deliberately-triggered FK violation (finding above) was fixed and re-run confirmed clean; a third run confirmed full idempotency via stable row counts (`platform.Permissions`: 9, `TenantMemberships`: 5, `super_admin` grants in `sharjah`: 9, `libraries` TeamMembers: 2). Verified directly via read-only queries against the live database, not inferred from script output alone.
- **`TR_Teams_crossEntityScope` and the new `TenantProfile` creation**, proved together: `sharjah`'s `TenantProfile.isPlatformTenant` reads back `true`, and its "Platform" team persisted with `scope: AllEntities` — the trigger genuinely admitted it, which is only possible because of fix #4 above.
- **`PrismaRoleRepository.updatePermissions()` against the real trigger**: a legitimate grant/revoke round-trip (Reviewer ↔ `agents:publish`) persisted correctly in both directions; a revoke of Super Admin's `users:manage` was genuinely rejected by the live `TR_RolePermissions_protectSuperAdmin` trigger (error 51102, exact message confirmed), surfaced as the structured `{ ok: false, reason: "protected" }` result, with the grant confirmed still present afterward — not just a thrown error caught somewhere.
- **Real end-to-end staff sign-in**: a real `StaffCredential` row (Argon2id hash, envelope-encrypted TOTP secret) created for the seeded Super Admin; `SignIn.execute()` → `totp_required` (confirming `SECOND_FACTOR_PERMISSIONS` correctly gates `users:manage` holders) → `CompleteTotpChallenge.execute()` with a real computed TOTP code → `signed_in`, a real session in Redis, a real formatted cookie.
- **`next dev` against `/en/iam` and `/ar/iam` with that real cookie** — confirmed real seeded data renders (Ahmed Saeed's own row, his "Platform" team, all 7 role names, all 9 permission names, all 3 tabs), confirmed `lang="ar" dir="rtl"` and the Arabic translation on the Arabic route, confirmed the unauthenticated and forbidden fallbacks render correctly for their own states. **One real environment bug found and fixed along the way**: `next dev` only auto-loads `.env` from `apps/web`'s own directory, never the monorepo root where the real `.env` lives — a real, systemic gap (any future route touching real infra during `next dev` would hit it identically), not specific to this feature. Fixed at the root in `next.config.ts` via Node's built-in `process.loadEnvFile()`, guarded by `existsSync` so a deployed environment injecting real env vars directly is unaffected. **A second, self-inflicted false alarm during this same check, run down rather than left as a shrug**: a session that resolved correctly on its first read later resolved as `"absent"` — traced to the session's own idle timeout (30 minutes) elapsing during the investigation itself, not a product bug; re-minting immediately and re-testing confirmed this. A close relative of that investigation *did* surface one genuine, useful fact worth recording: `next dev`'s own request handling supplies a real, non-`"unknown"` IP-derived `ClientBinding` component (`"::1"` for a local request) that my own proof script's binding computation did not initially match — a fidelity gap in the test script, not the product's binding-mismatch security check, which was doing exactly its job.
- **Full gate**: `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **17/17 stages, clean** (867 TS unit tests, up from 795 pre-B-2, zero regressions; 169 Python tests unaffected). One real, correct `gate:i18n-strings` finding along the way (two new interpolated placeholders, `{name}`/`{roleLabel}`, needed Unicode FSI/PDI isolate marks per §11.3 rule 3) — fixed in both `en.json`/`ar.json`, not suppressed. `pnpm -r run typecheck` and `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean throughout.

**A real, pre-existing gap found and named, not fixed (out of scope for this wave):** `scripts/*.ts` files (including the already-shipped `seed-system-skins.ts`, not just this wave's new `seed-iam-demo-data.ts`) have **no static typecheck coverage anywhere in this project** — `apps/web/tsconfig.json`'s `include` never reaches outside its own directory, and the root `tsconfig.json` was removed as dead code in the SkinEditor wave. Confirmed by constructing an ad-hoc project config and finding it fails identically on `seed-system-skins.ts`, not just new code — these files are only ever verified by actually running them via `tsx` (which strips types without checking them). Relied on the mandatory live-infrastructure run as the real verification instead, matching how `seed-system-skins.ts` itself was verified in its own wave; flagged here rather than silently accepted as sufficient without comment.

**Judgment calls, each flagged in code at the point made:**
1. Team pills replace the full set on save (`TeamRepository.setMemberships`'s existing "hard-replace, first item is primary" contract) — B9's Edit dialog doesn't distinguish "add one team" from "set the whole list", and the schema gives no reason to build a narrower diff/patch operation.
2. B9 tab 2's team-scope dialog offers both `Tenant`/`AllEntities` regardless of which tenant is asking — `TR_Teams_crossEntityScope` is the real enforcement, surfaced through the dialog's own error state. A nicer version would hide `AllEntities` for a non-platform tenant, which needs this route to know whether its own tenant *is* the platform operator — not exposed to this screen today, flagged rather than silently guessed at.
3. Mutations use Server Actions + `router.refresh()`, not the architecture doc's stated intent of TanStack Query for mutations — no route in this codebase has adopted that pattern yet (B-2 is the first to wire real mutations at all), and introducing a new client-data-fetching dependency without a concrete need forcing the choice felt like the wrong moment to establish it. Flagged as a real, un-adopted piece of the documented intent, not silently worked around.
4. `(backoffice)/layout.tsx`'s `activeHref` is hardcoded to `/iam` — there is only one real screen so far, and a layout has no built-in way to read the current request's own pathname server-side. The next wave adding a second `(backoffice)` page should replace this with a real per-page mechanism, not copy the hardcode.
5. The theme-mode toggle in `backoffice-shell.tsx` is client-local only, not yet persisted — persisting it correctly means merging one field into a user's existing five-scalar personal-preference row (`ManageAppearance.savePersonalPreferenceScalars`), which is real work for whichever wave next revisits this shell's settings integration, not a B-2 concern. The *initial* mode is still the real server-resolved one; only the in-session toggle reverts on reload.
6. `RemoveUser`'s audit trail and `revokeTenantMembership`'s tenant-schema cleanup (`TeamMembers`/`UserRoleAssignments`) cannot share one transaction with the platform-schema membership revoke (different Prisma client/connection) — best-effort, sequential, and idempotent, the same "no distributed transaction across stores" acceptance ADR-0003 rule 4 already states elsewhere.

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/modules/iam/ports/{team-repository,role-repository,demo-data-seeder}.ts`; `apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/outbound/sql/{prisma-user-repository,prisma-credential-repository,prisma-team-repository,prisma-role-repository,prisma-demo-data-seeder}.ts`; `apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/inbound/next-request-context.ts`; `apps/web/src/modules/iam/application/{invite-user,edit-user,reactivate-user,remove-user,list-users,create-team,list-teams-with-live-membership,update-role-permissions,create-custom-role,seed-demo-data}.ts` (+ 9 `.test.ts`); `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/crypto/{envelope-encryption,envelope-encryption.test}.ts`; `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/{layout.tsx,backoffice-shell.tsx}`; `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/iam/{page.tsx,actions.ts,composition.ts,iam-screen.tsx,users-tab.tsx,teams-tab.tsx,roles-tab.tsx}`; `scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts`. Modified: `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` (trigger literal fix), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/sql/sql-store-provisioner.ts`+`.test.ts` (`TenantProfile` creation, `RawSqlExecutor.executeParameterized`/`.queryRow`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/sql/tenant-registry.ts`, `apps/web/src/modules/platform/application/provision-tenant.ts`, `apps/web/src/modules/platform/ports/provisioning.ts` (`entityKind`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/tenancy/tenant-context.ts`+`.test.ts` (`platformScope: "identity"`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/config.ts`+`.test.ts` (`SHJ3_ENCRYPTION_KEY`), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/inbound/auth-middleware.ts` (`platformScope: "identity"` on both bindings), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/ports/user-repository.ts` (5 new methods), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/domain/permissions.ts`+`.test.ts` (`ROLE_KEYS`, `SEEDED_ROLE_DISPLAY_NAMES`, `PERMISSION_CATALOG`), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/testing/fakes.ts` (`FakeTeamRepository`, `FakeRoleRepository`, extended `FakeUserRepository`), `apps/web/next.config.ts` (root `.env` loading), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (+117 new keys net of nesting, 234 total each, parity verified), `.env.example`, `package.json` (`db:seed:iam`).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave:** nine real, pre-existing gaps were found and fixed at the root rather than the brief's own five tasks alone — the `UserRepository`/`CredentialRepository` adapters, the permission-key trigger bug, the `TenantProfile` gap, the `PlatformOperator` registration gap, the envelope-encryption module, and the App Router session-binding mechanism are all things a future wave would otherwise have hit blind. The `TenantMemberships_grantedByStaffUserId_fkey` bug and the `next dev` `.env`-loading gap were both caught only because this wave insisted on a real live-infrastructure proof rather than trusting typecheck-and-unit-tests-green — consistent with this session's own standing lesson about fakes and gates that never fire. The two deliberately-deferred, named gaps (TanStack Query for mutations; per-page `activeHref`) are real, not hidden, and should be revisited by whichever wave next touches a second `(backoffice)` screen or a second mutating route.
