**Independently re-verified by me — this is the highest-impact single wave of the session so far, and I did not accept it on the strength of its own (oddly front-loaded-with-docs-cleanup) summary alone.** Read `next-request-context.ts`'s `withStaffAuth()` in full: sound, precise, and honestly reasoned — it never tries to make `AsyncLocalStorage` propagate across a boundary Next.js doesn't preserve it across; it re-resolves fresh at every real entry point instead, which is the only correct fix for what was actually found. Read the `TR_RolePermissions_protectSuperAdmin` trigger fix directly in `001_constraints.sql`: a genuinely serious finding, stated plainly in its own "BUG FOUND AND FIXED" comment — a safety trigger checking a permission key (`'manage_users_teams'`) that no code path in this entire system had ever persisted, meaning it could never have fired for any real data. A protection that silently protects nothing is arguably worse than an absent one, since it manufactures false confidence — exactly the class of bug this session's own "a gate that never fires buys false confidence" lesson warns about, just found in a database trigger instead of a lint rule this time. Re-ran `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` myself: clean, 17/17.

**One real, already-diagnosed bug this wave correctly flagged but left unfixed (correctly out of its own scope): `settings/appearance/page.tsx`/`actions.ts` have the identical `AsyncLocalStorage`-propagation bug** — every real request to the Settings → Appearance screen currently falls through to the "no context yet" placeholder, and all 9 of its Server Actions call a bare `requirePrincipal()` that can never resolve. Given the fix is well-understood, contained, and reuses B-2's own proven `withStaffAuth()` pattern exactly, dispatched a small, focused follow-up to apply it rather than leave a known, already-diagnosed defect sitting in shipped code.

**Both fixed now — closed 2026-09-09.** The follow-up fixed `settings/appearance/{page.tsx,actions.ts}` exactly as briefed: `withStaffAuth()` replacing the bare `tryGetTenantContext()`/`requirePrincipal()` calls, the old three-tier fallback correctly collapsed to two-tier once confirmed (by reading `AuthMiddleware.handle()` directly) that the old distinction never corresponded to two real states, all 9 actions wrapped with `{ method: "POST", body: <args> }`. Verified end-to-end against real infrastructure — real sign-in (password + TOTP) as the seeded Super Admin, a real permission-gated render, a real `savePersonalPreferenceAction` write confirmed to persist to and revert from the real database — independently corroborated twice (the fixing agent's own proof, then its own nested verification sub-agent's separate pass), including correctly running down a first-pass false alarm (a hand-minted test session's mismatched User-Agent/IP tripping the real, correctly-working client-binding security check, not a regression) rather than either panicking or shrugging it off — now itself a `tasks/lessons.md` entry. **`reset/page.tsx`/`reset/actions.ts` were confirmed to share the identical bug and deliberately left unfixed by that wave** (explicitly out of its own scope) — closed here directly by me, applying the identical, by-then-twice-proven pattern (a two-tier `UnauthenticatedError` catch in the page, `withStaffAuth`-wrapped actions with no `body` needed since both take zero arguments). Verified: `pnpm -r run typecheck`, both token gates, and the full `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` all clean (17/17); a real `next dev` check confirmed both `/en/settings/appearance/reset` and `/ar/settings/appearance/reset` render the correct unauthenticated fallback (real `dir="rtl"` on the Arabic route) with no crash — a full sign-in cycle wasn't independently re-run a third time for this specific fix, since it calls the exact same, already-twice-proven `withStaffAuth()` function on mechanically identical code, not a new implementation of the pattern. Dev server confirmed stopped (by PID, after an initial soft-kill attempt silently failed) and temp files cleaned up afterward.

**All three known instances of the `AsyncLocalStorage` bug are now closed.** No known-broken auth-context resolution remains anywhere in the app.

### B-2 follow-up · Settings → Appearance real staff auth — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The exact bug B-2's own review flagged and correctly left out of scope, above: `settings/appearance/page.tsx`/`actions.ts` called bare `tryGetTenantContext()`/`requirePrincipal()`, which never resolves a real context in the App Router (`next-request-context.ts`'s doc comment; `tasks/lessons.md`'s AsyncLocalStorage-propagation entry) — every real request silently fell through to a placeholder/sign-in state even for a genuinely signed-in staff user, and none of the screen's 9 Server Actions could ever resolve a real principal. Fixed by applying B-2's own proven `withStaffAuth()` pattern to both files, matching `(backoffice)/iam/page.tsx`'s and `actions.ts`'s established convention exactly — no new mechanism invented, no scope beyond the two files named.

**`page.tsx`.** Replaced the bare context read with `withStaffAuth(async ({ principal }) => {...})`, catching `UnauthenticatedError` the way `iam/page.tsx` does. Collapsed the old three-tier fallback (`!context` generic placeholder / `!principal` sign-in prompt / full editor) to a two-tier `PageData` union (`unauthenticated`/`ok`): read `AuthMiddleware.handle()` directly and confirmed the old `!context`/`!principal` split never corresponded to two different real states on the staff route — it either throws `UnauthenticatedError` or resolves a fully-populated `Principal` (`handleAnonymous`, the only path tolerating a null principal, is citizen-surface only and never called here). The view-only vs. full-access split *within* `ok` (`canManageTenantAppearance`, gated by `appearance:manage`) is untouched — a real, load-bearing distinction this screen's permission model makes on purpose, unlike IAM's all-or-nothing gate. Removed the now-unreachable `noTenantContextHeading`/`noTenantContextBody` strings from `messages/{en,ar}.json` — copy describing a state that can no longer occur, rather than leaving it dead and increasingly inaccurate.

**`actions.ts`.** Wrapped each of the 9 actions' bodies in `withStaffAuth(handler, { method: "POST", body: <the action's own arguments> })`, matching `iam/actions.ts`'s convention of passing real caller-supplied data through for `assertNoTenantOverride`'s tamper scan. Removed the now-redundant `requirePrincipal()` call from every handler — `withStaffAuth`'s callback is only ever invoked with a fully-resolved, non-null `Principal` (it throws `UnauthenticatedError` instead of invoking the handler otherwise), so the second check was dead code once the real one existed. The permission split is unchanged: 5 actions still require `appearance:manage` (`saveTenantAppearanceAction`, `applyTenantSkinAction`, `duplicateTenantSkinAction`, `renameTenantSkinAction`, `deleteTenantSkinAction`), 4 remain authenticated-only (`applyPersonalSkinAction`, `exportTenantSkinAction`, `importSkinModeAction`, `savePersonalPreferenceAction`).

**`reset/page.tsx`/`reset/actions.ts` — confirmed to share the identical bug, deliberately left unfixed.** Read both directly: they also call bare `tryGetTenantContext()`, so a real signed-in Entity Admin/Super Admin hitting `/settings/appearance/reset` today also sees no principal — both of that route's real mutations are currently unreachable for a real session. This is genuinely the same defect, not a different by-design "works while nothing else does" behaviour — that route's own "escape hatch" doc comment is about surviving a broken tenant *theme* (literal inline styles, never CSS custom properties), never about working without real authentication. Out of scope for this fix as instructed; named here rather than silently left for the next person to rediscover.

**Verification — real, live-infrastructure, not just green gates:**
- `pnpm -r run typecheck`, `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0`, `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: all clean (867 TS unit tests + 169 Python tests — the same count B-2 left, zero regressions, since no test file needed to change: there is no unit-test layer at the page/Server-Action tier anywhere in this codebase, matching how B-2 itself verified `/iam` — only a real, running proof does).
- Real sign-in against the real stack: reused the seeded Super Admin `ahmed.saeed@shj.ae` (`scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts`, unmodified — no second seed path built). That script deliberately seeds no credentials, so a real `StaffCredential` (Argon2id password + TOTP secret) was created directly, mirroring B-2's own precedent for this exact account; real `SignIn` → `totp_required` → `CompleteTotpChallenge` → `signed_in`, real Redis session, real cookie. One local-only environment gap found and fixed along the way: `SHJ3_ENCRYPTION_KEY` (required for TOTP secret encryption since B-2) was missing from this machine's own `.env` — added the value already committed in `.env.example` for exactly this purpose. `.env` is gitignored; no code touched.
- `next dev`, real cookie: `/en/settings/appearance` now renders the real editor (`canManageTenantAppearance: true`, real tenant skin data) instead of the placeholder; the no-cookie case still correctly shows the sign-in prompt (the unauthenticated branch still works, it isn't just gone). `/en/iam` unaffected (regression check — real seeded data still renders). `/en/settings/appearance/reset` still shows its own sign-in-equivalent placeholder even with a valid cookie — confirms it is unchanged, not newly broken.
- A real write, against the real database: `savePersonalPreferenceAction` invoked as a genuine Server Action — the real `Next-Action` POST, with the action id read from the compiled `.next` server-reference manifest (chosen over adding Playwright as a new tracked dependency for one proof; confirmed Playwright is not an existing dependency of this repo). Direct DB read confirmed `null` → `{"mode":"Dark",...,"reducedMotion":true,...}`, then reverted.
- Cleanup: personal-preference row restored to its pre-test state, test session destroyed in Redis, `next dev` stopped, throwaway proof scripts deleted. The seeded Super Admin's real credential was intentionally left in place (legitimate, permanent demo infrastructure, matching B-2's own precedent) rather than torn down.
- Confirmed, by direct re-read (not `git diff` — this checkout is not git-tracked, see below) rather than trusting a report alone: `(backoffice)/iam/page.tsx`, `.../actions.ts`, `settings/appearance/reset/page.tsx`, `.../actions.ts` are byte-for-byte unchanged from before this fix.

**A false alarm run down rather than accepted at face value.** The first live-verification pass found what looked like a serious regression: a real, valid session was destroyed on the very first real HTTP request to Settings → Appearance — and identically on the untouched `/iam` route. Traced to `ResolveSession.execute()`'s `bindingMatches()` check (`resolve-session.ts`): the throwaway sign-in script recorded a client binding (User-Agent/IP) that didn't match the follow-up HTTP requests' real ones, so the binding-mismatch security check correctly revoked the session — the same class of test-script fidelity gap B-2's own review already hit once for this identical mechanism (now promoted to a proper `tasks/lessons.md` entry, since this is its second occurrence). Confirmed by re-running with a consistent, explicit binding (matching `User-Agent` plus a forced `X-Forwarded-For`) on both sides; no product code was touched to "fix" this.

**One real, out-of-scope nuance found and flagged, not chased.** Getting the binding fix to hold required forcing the IP-derived component via an explicit `X-Forwarded-For` header — an exact-match User-Agent alone, with no forwarded header, still failed. `next-request-context.ts`'s own doc comment states a Server Component/Action's `ipAddress` is always `null` ("the only client-address signal ... has access to at all"), which should make the IP side of the binding a fixed sentinel regardless of the real request's origin when no forwarded header is sent — worth a closer look by whoever next touches that file, but it does not affect this fix's correctness (the security check firing is the check doing its job, not a defect in `page.tsx`/`actions.ts`) and was not chased further here.

**An aside, unrelated to the fix itself, worth recording once:** this checkout (`D:\work\products\SHJ3`) is not its own git repository — the real repo root is one level up (`D:\work\products`), and the entire `SHJ3/` directory is untracked there. `git status`/`git diff` are therefore not meaningful regression signals for anything under this checkout; verification here relied on direct file re-reads and the real test/gate suite instead.

**Files.** Modified: `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/settings/appearance/page.tsx`, `.../actions.ts`, `apps/web/messages/en.json`, `apps/web/messages/ar.json`.

### Phase C · Docker Compose, both Dockerfiles — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The two missing Dockerfiles `docker-compose.yml` referenced all session (`docker/` did not exist at all), a real `docker compose up` for the whole seven-service stack proven from empty volumes, and the deterministic-seed gap closed at its actual root, which turned out to be one layer deeper than "wire the existing seed scripts into compose."

**Task 1, the Linux-vs-Windows `next build` question — answered empirically, recorded in `tasks/lessons.md`.** A throwaway `node:22-slim` image running `pnpm install --frozen-lockfile` → `pnpm db:generate` → `pnpm --filter @shj3/web build` built clean, including the exact `/[locale]/settings/appearance/reset` route that reproduces the documented Windows-only EPERM/scandir failure on this host. **The bug does not reproduce on Linux** — confirmed, not assumed, and `docker/web.Dockerfile`'s `build` stage is trusted at face value with no workaround baked in. One real secondary finding from the same test: Prisma's engine-postinstall step cannot detect libssl on bare `node:22-slim` and silently defaults to the wrong engine target — fixed at the root in both real Dockerfiles by installing `openssl` explicitly. Throwaway image and Dockerfile deleted once the answer was known.

**Task 2, `docker/web.Dockerfile`.** Five stages (`base` → `deps` → `dev`/`build` → `runtime`). Pinned digest, not just a tag: `node:22-slim@sha256:83f487e0a63425e5b4d146fb5e5be574bcbe1b7b843d3ebafdd95eaf7767a7e5` (resolved via a real `docker pull` + `docker inspect`, not guessed). Non-root `shj3` user (uid/gid 10001) in `runtime`. `HEALTHCHECK` hitting `/api/healthz` (liveness-only, matching that route's own documented philosophy). `org.opencontainers.image.version`/`.revision` from `VERSION`/`GIT_SHA` build args, defaulting to the checked-in package version / `"unknown"` absent CI (ADR-0008 declined CI/CD; no release pipeline exists yet to supply these automatically — a documented, deliberate gap, not an oversight). Built and run standalone for real: boots in ~800ms, `/api/healthz` returns a real 200, Docker's own `.State.Health` reports `healthy`, confirmed running as uid 10001 via `docker exec ... id`.

**Task 3, `docker/ai.Dockerfile`.** Same standard, `uv`-based per this project's real convention (not the Makefile's older `uv pip install -e` — that predates `uv.lock`'s existence and doesn't use it at all, a real inconsistency found and flagged rather than silently mirrored). Base images pinned by digest, resolved empirically rather than copying deployment.md §5.3's own `[ASSUMPTION]`-flagged draft tags: `ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv:python3.12-bookworm-slim` for `deps`/`build` (real, current `uv --version` is `0.9.30` — deployment.md's draft named `pip install uv==0.4.27`, nine months stale against what this environment actually has), `python:3.12-slim-bookworm` for `runtime`. Installs Microsoft's real ODBC Driver 18 + `unixodbc` in `runtime` — a genuine runtime requirement `deployment.md` never mentions at all: `aioodbc`/`pyodbc` resolve a prebuilt manylinux wheel (verified in `uv.lock`, no compilation), but that wheel dynamically loads `libodbc.so.2` and the specific SQL Server driver at connect time, so without this the image would build and boot cleanly and only fail the moment it tried a real database connection. **A real bug found building this, not shipped**: the first attempt `sed`-rewrote Microsoft's fetched `prod.list` to inject a `signed-by=` option, producing a malformed double-bracket apt source line ("Malformed entry ... URI parse") — fetched the real file directly (`curl` the URL) before re-fixing, and it already carries the exact `signed-by=/usr/share/keyrings/microsoft-prod.gpg` path the key step writes to; the `sed` was solving a problem that didn't exist. Built and run standalone: `/healthz` returns the full `{"status":"ok","version":"0.1.0","git_sha":"test-build","env":"development"}`, ODBC Driver 18 confirmed registered (`odbcinst -q -d`), non-root confirmed, worker-role dispatch confirmed to idle honestly with no crash.

**New: `shj3_ai/entrypoint.py` and `shj3_ai/adapters/inbound/health_router.py`.** `app.py`'s own doc comment named `entrypoint.py` before it existed ("dispatches on `SHJ3_AI_ROLE` ... between the `api` and `worker` workloads ... that module does not exist yet") — built exactly that, nothing more: `api` imports and serves the real `shj3_ai.app:app` via `uvicorn`; `worker` logs a clear, honest statement that no scheduled jobs are implemented yet (confirmed by search before writing this: zero celery/arq/rq dependency, nothing reads `SHJ3_AI_ROLE` anywhere in existing code, `OutboxEvent` is migrated but unread by any application code) and idles, rather than inventing fake reconciliation logic that would look real while doing nothing. `health_router.py` closes a real, confirmed-missing gap (`apps/ai` had no health endpoint at all, unlike `apps/web`'s `/api/healthz`) — liveness-only by the same reasoning, `version`/`gitSha`/`env` wired for real from build-arg-sourced env vars, `residency` from deployment.md's illustrative example deliberately NOT reproduced (no real config source exists for it — RISK-001 is still open — and fabricating one would be exactly the "looks right but isn't" shortcut this project's CLAUDE.md rules out). Both files clean on `ruff format --check`/`ruff check`/`mypy --strict`; 169/169 existing Python tests unaffected.

**`worker` reusing `docker/ai.Dockerfile` — confirmed sound, not just left as-is.** ADR-0001 constraint 1 ("background work is a replica of shj3-ai with another role, never a third deployable") is now actually implemented rather than aspirational vapor: one image, `SHJ3_AI_ROLE` as the sole differentiator, `entrypoint.py` doing the real dispatch. `worker`'s compose `healthcheck: disable: true` is a deliberate, flagged decision, not an oversight: the role starts no HTTP server at all yet (no real job exists to serve health for), so the inherited `HEALTHCHECK` would fail forever for a reason unrelated to actual health; deployment.md §6.2's own representative example names a separate future metrics port (9100) for this, building which now would be speculative infrastructure ahead of the code it would report on.

**Task 4, a real `docker compose up` for the whole stack, from empty volumes — six real bugs found and fixed before it worked, each confirmed by an actual failing container, not reasoned about in advance:**
1. **`ai`'s `dev` stage never installed the local `shj3_ai` package** (`uv sync --no-install-project`, never re-run after `COPY apps/ai/src`) — `ModuleNotFoundError: No module named 'shj3_ai'` on the very first real container start. Fixed: `uv sync --frozen` (no `--no-install-project`) after the source copy.
2. **`ai`/`worker`'s bind mount (`./apps/ai:/app`) shadows the image's own `.venv`** with this Windows host's real, Windows-built `apps/ai/.venv` (native `.exe` launchers) the moment the container starts — unlike `web`'s bind mount, `ai.Dockerfile`'s `WORKDIR` is `/app` itself, so the mount replaces the whole thing, not a subdirectory. Fixed with an anonymous-volume carve-out (`- /app/.venv`), the same idiom as web's `prisma/generated` fix below.
3. **Neither `dev` stage had a `HEALTHCHECK`** — not inherited across sibling stages (`dev` and `runtime` both `FROM deps`, not one from the other) — `docker compose`'s `condition: service_healthy` refused with "no healthcheck configured". Fixed: added the identical `HEALTHCHECK` to both `dev` stages.
4. **`web`'s compose environment was missing two variables `config.ts`'s boot-time validator REQUIRES**: `SHJ3_VERIFICATION_ADAPTER`, `SHJ3_ENCRYPTION_KEY`. Never caught before because every earlier verification pass ran `web` via host `next dev`, which loads the root `.env` directly (`next.config.ts`'s own `process.loadEnvFile()`) — compose's `environment:` block is a separate, explicit list that never had these added. Without them the container refuses to start at all, before serving even `/api/healthz`.
5. **`SHJ3_AI_PLATFORM_TOKEN` was on neither `web` nor `ai`/`worker`/the new `init`** — `provisioning_router.py`'s `require_platform_scope()` closes every provisioning endpoint (503) when it's unset, per its own documented trust model. Same root cause as #4: never exercised through compose before.
6. **`web`'s bind mounts shadow pnpm's own per-package `node_modules`** (`apps/web/node_modules`, `packages/tokens/node_modules`) with this host's real symlinks pointing at a Windows absolute path (confirmed by reading one directly: `typescript -> /d/work/products/SHJ3/node_modules/.pnpm/...`), on top of the already-known `prisma/generated` case. Fixed with the same anonymous-volume carve-out pattern, one entry per shadowed path.

**A seventh, deeper finding: no checked-in mechanism anywhere in this codebase ever bootstrapped `platform`/`tenant_template` from an empty database.** Investigated directly (not assumed) before wiring seed data: `SqlStoreProvisioner.create()` throws outright if `tenant_template` holds zero tables ("run the platform migration first, then provision"); `tests/isolation/setup.ts` — the suite that has provisioned real tenants against real containers all session — never bootstraps anything, it assumes both already exist; `package.json`'s `db:migrate` (`scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs`) has never existed on disk. New `scripts/bootstrap-platform-schema.ts` closes this, narrowly: creates the `shj3` database (a throwaway client pointed at `master`, since `getPlatformDb()` cannot even connect before it exists), replays each migration's platform/tenant_template DDL, applies `001_constraints.sql`'s platform section (new `platformSection()` in `sql-script.ts` — the never-before-called complement of the `tenantSection()` every tenant-scoped caller already uses). It does **not** provision any tenant — `scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts` already does that, unmodified, once its precondition is finally real.

**Inside that fix, a genuine, previously-untested bug found in already-shipped production code, and deliberately NOT fixed there.** `migration-executor.ts`'s `PrismaMigrationExecutor.applyToPlatform()` — real, tested, used by `RunTenantMigrations` — was the obvious tool to reuse. It fails against a genuinely fresh environment: `20260908130800_init/migration.sql` contains ADR-0011's five cross-schema foreign keys (e.g. `ALTER TABLE [tenant_template].[RolePermissions] ADD CONSTRAINT ... REFERENCES [platform].[Permissions]`), which `platformStatements()` deliberately matches too (its own doc comment says so). Running "all of platform, then all of tenant_template" as two independently-ordered passes breaks the moment such a statement exists — confirmed directly: `applyToPlatform()` run before `tenant_template.RolePermissions` existed failed with real SQL Server error 4902. Not a defect in `platformStatements()` for the path it's actually tested against (incremental per-tenant migrations, where both schemas already exist and ordering between two passes never matters) — a gap in using it to bootstrap an empty environment, which nothing needed until this script. Fixed in the bootstrap script's own approach instead of widening a shared, tested production code path: new `platformAndTemplateStatements()` (`sql-script.ts`) replays a migration's schema-touching statements in the migration's own original file order (one pass, not two), safe because Prisma's own generation order already puts every `CREATE TABLE`/`CREATE INDEX` (both schemas) before every `ALTER TABLE ... ADD CONSTRAINT` (both schemas) — confirmed by reading the real file. Full account in that function's own doc comment. **Flagged here plainly for whoever next builds the real `scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs` CLI or exercises `RunTenantMigrations` against a migration with a cross-schema FK**: `platformStatements()` itself still has this latent gap.

**A second real gap found in my own first draft, before it ever left this session**: the bootstrap script's own doc comment originally *claimed* full idempotency ("every statement ... already guarded ... safe and a no-op") — false for the migration replay, since Prisma's raw DDL carries no `IF NOT EXISTS` guards. Found by literally hitting it: an earlier, partially-failed test run (mine, from finding the bug above) left `platform.Tenants` behind, and a clean re-run of the fixed script failed with "There is already an object named 'Tenants'". Fixed with `platformAlreadyBootstrapped()`, a table-count guard mirroring `SqlStoreProvisioner.create()`'s own established pattern for the identical class of problem — "skip the whole replay once `platform` plainly already has tables" rather than trying to make every individual Prisma-generated statement independently idempotent, consistent with that class's own stated philosophy ("refusing is the honest answer; guessing is how one entity ends up on a different schema version from the rest"). Verified by re-running the isolated test project's `up` a second time, without a teardown in between, against the now-already-bootstrapped volumes — clean.

**New `init` compose service** — one-shot (`restart: "no"`), reuses `docker/web.Dockerfile`'s `dev` target rather than a third Dockerfile (same TypeScript/`tsx` composition-root tooling `web` already has), `command: pnpm db:bootstrap-platform && pnpm db:seed:iam && pnpm db:seed:appearance`, `depends_on: sqlserver (healthy), ai (healthy)`. `web`/`worker` now `depends_on: init (service_completed_successfully)`, in addition to their existing store/`ai` health dependencies.

**The real proof, from empty volumes, in an isolated project so as not to disturb the already-running, concurrently-used `shj3` project or the B-3 wave depending on it**: brought up all seven services (`sqlserver`/`neo4j`/`qdrant`/`redis`/`ai`/`init`/`web`/`worker`) under project name `shj3verify` with remapped host ports, twice from a full `down -v` in between (the first full pass surfaced bugs 1-6 above one at a time; the second, after all fixes, succeeded outright). `init`'s log confirms the complete real pipeline: database created, both migrations' platform+tenant_template DDL applied, constraints applied, **all four government-entity tenants provisioned** (`sewa`/`customs`/`libraries`/`sharjah` — exceeding NFR-OPS-02's "at least two" floor) across all four real stores via the unmodified `ProvisionTenant` use case (proving `ai`'s Neo4j/Qdrant provisioning endpoints work correctly over the compose network, platform token included), system skins seeded. `ai` and `web` both report Docker-native `healthy`; `worker` runs with its healthcheck deliberately disabled (see above). **Internal compose-network connectivity confirmed directly, not inferred**: `docker compose exec web` reaching `http://ai:8000/healthz` (real 200 JSON) and raw TCP to `sqlserver:1433`/`redis:6379`, all via service DNS names, never a host-mapped port. **A real external request returns a real response**: `/api/healthz` → 200, `/` → 307 to `/en`, and — now that real seed data exists — `/en/iam` → a real 200 rendering the seeded IAM screen. Clean teardown (`down -v`): confirmed zero `shj3verify` containers/volumes/networks remain, and the pre-existing `shj3` project's four store containers (up since earlier in this session) are confirmed **untouched and still healthy** throughout. Every throwaway image (`shj3-web:runtime-test`, `shj3-ai:runtime-test`, all four `shj3verify-*` build images) removed; final `docker images`/`docker ps -a`/`docker network ls` match the session's pre-existing baseline exactly, plus only the four new legitimately-pinned base images (`ghcr.io/astral-sh/uv`, `node:22-slim`, `python:3.12-bookworm`, `python:3.12-slim-bookworm`) the real Dockerfiles now depend on.

**Deliberately did not run the new `init`/seed pipeline against the shared, already-running `shj3` project** (the one other waves and the concurrent B-3 agent use) — provisioning real tenants and seeding real data into infrastructure another agent might be depending on the current (empty-of-schema) state of felt like a real risk of interference, not a controlled test. Confirmed via a direct query that the shared project's `shj3` database currently holds zero `platform`/`tenant_template`/tenant schemas (apparently wiped by the `migrate reset` B-0's review mentions), so running `docker compose up` there today would exercise the identical, now-proven-correct path — left for whoever next actually wants that outcome on the shared instance, rather than done unilaterally mid-session.

**Task 6, `.dockerignore`.** Did not exist (confirmed by search). One file at the repo root, not one per app — both Dockerfiles build with `context: .`, so a single file governs both; excludes `node_modules`, `.next`, `.venv`, `__pycache__`, every cache dir, `prisma/generated/` (must be regenerated fresh inside Linux, never copied from this Windows host — the same reasoning as bug #2/#6 above), `.env*`, `tests/`, `docs/`, `.claude/`, `tasks/`.

**Explicitly not done this pass, as instructed — real, open gaps, not hidden**: Helm/Kubernetes manifests, NetworkPolicies, HPAs/PDBs, resource requests/limits, and formal migration/provisioning/rollback/restore runbooks (Phase C's own checklist lists these separately; `docs/deployment.md` already has aspirational drafts for several, marked `[ASSUMPTION]`, not yet reconciled with what actually got built here). `agents/`/`tools/` modules and backoffice routes for them were not touched, per this wave's own boundary.

**Helm/Kubernetes — dispatched 2026-09-09 as the deferred remaining piece, running in parallel with B-3.** `docs/deployment.md` §7 turns out to already be an extremely detailed, near-implementation-ready spec (real chart structure, real YAML for values/probes/HPA/PDB/NetworkPolicies/StatefulSets) — briefed to implement it faithfully rather than redesign it. One real, new piece of application code required first: neither app has a `/readyz` route yet (only the liveness-only `/healthz` endpoints the Docker wave built), and §7.4's probe design needs a real one — real dependency checks (model-client init, active-turn ceiling for `shj3-ai`), not a hardcoded always-ready value.

**Judgment calls, each flagged in code or here at the point made:**
1. Runtime images ship full `node_modules` (dev dependencies included), not pruned to production-only — pnpm's symlink layout (root `.pnpm` store + per-package symlink directories) made a `pnpm install --prod` pruning stage a real, untested risk of its own for a first working pass; correctness and reliability over image size, flagged as a reasonable future optimization rather than attempted blind under time pressure.
2. No Next.js `output: "standalone"` — the idiomatic, smaller-image pattern, deliberately not adopted this pass: it would be the first real exercise of Next's aggressive file-tracing (`@vercel/nft`) against exactly the Prisma-generated-client interaction this session has already found fragile once (the Windows EPERM bug, a *different* tracing step but the same general area), on Linux this time, untested. Plain `next start` against the full build output is simpler and lower-risk; flagged as a legitimate future size optimization, not an oversight.
3. `db:bootstrap-platform`'s master-database connection constructs a `PrismaClient` directly outside `tenant-db.ts` — confirmed first that `scripts/gates/no-unscoped-store-clients.mjs` only walks `apps/` and `packages/`, never `scripts/`, consistent with this file family's own established "composition root" precedent (`seed-iam-demo-data.ts`'s identical reasoning) rather than a quiet gap in the gate.
4. Version/revision labels have no automated CI to supply real values yet (ADR-0008 declined CI) — defaults are honest placeholders (`0.1.0`/`unknown`), not fabricated-looking real values; a future release script is the intended real caller, named in both Dockerfiles' own comments.

**Verification — real, and honestly reported against a genuinely concurrent, uncommitted shared checkout.** This checkout is not its own git repository (confirmed by a prior wave, `git status`/`git diff` are not meaningful signals here — see the B-2-follow-up review above), and another agent is concurrently editing `apps/web/src/modules/agents/**`/`tools/**` throughout this wave. `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` **cannot get past its first stage right now**: `prettier --check .` fails on 22 files, every one of them either under `agents/`/`tools/` (this wave's explicit no-touch boundary) or `ai-client.ts`/`.test.ts` (not touched by this wave either) — confirmed by fixing the one file in that list that *was* mine (`sql-script.ts`, now clean) and watching the count drop from 23 to 22, with zero files of mine remaining in it. Ran every subsequent stage by hand instead, since `verify.mjs` stops at the first failure: `eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean; all 8 custom gates (`tokens`/`cypher`/`clients`/`drift`/`tailwind-theme`/`token-refs`/`table`/`i18n-strings`) clean; `vitest run --project unit` — **52 files, 914 tests, all passing**; the full Python stage (`ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, `mypy --strict`, `import-linter` 2/2 contracts, `pytest`) — **169/169, unchanged from baseline**. `pnpm -r run typecheck` was confirmed clean twice, immediately after each of my own edits — but flickered to a **7-error failure in `apps/web/src/modules/agents/testing/fakes.ts`** between two consecutive checks made without any edit of my own in between (error count observably grew 6→7), proving live, concurrent, mid-refactor editing by the other agent rather than anything in this wave's own diff. Reported here plainly rather than either claiming a clean "N/N stages" that the shared repo's current, real state cannot honestly produce, or silently working around it by touching files outside this wave's boundary.

**Files.** New: `docker/web.Dockerfile`, `docker/ai.Dockerfile`, `.dockerignore`, `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/entrypoint.py`, `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/adapters/inbound/health_router.py`, `scripts/bootstrap-platform-schema.ts`. Modified: `docker-compose.yml` (`init` service; healthchecks and `SHJ3_VERIFICATION_ADAPTER`/`SHJ3_ENCRYPTION_KEY`/`SHJ3_AI_PLATFORM_TOKEN` env vars and anonymous-volume carve-outs on `web`/`ai`/`worker`), `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/app.py` (mounts `health_router`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/sql/sql-script.ts` (`platformSection`, `platformAndTemplateStatements`), `package.json` (`db:bootstrap-platform`), `tasks/lessons.md` (Linux-vs-Windows `next build` finding).

**Independently re-verified by me.** Confirmed directly, not accepted on the write-up alone: `docker ps -a` shows zero `shj3verify-*` containers remaining and the original `shj3-*` baseline (4 stores) still healthy, matching the claimed clean teardown exactly. Read `docker/web.Dockerfile` in full — the five-stage structure, the real pinned digest, the non-root `runtime` user, and the dual dev/runtime `HEALTHCHECK`s are all exactly as reported, with doc comments precise enough to trust without re-deriving the reasoning myself. Read `scripts/bootstrap-platform-schema.ts` in full — the decision *not* to reuse `PrismaMigrationExecutor.applyToPlatform()` is correctly reasoned (a real SQL Server error 4902 on a genuinely empty database, not a hypothetical), and the fix (`platformAndTemplateStatements()` replaying each migration in its own original statement order) is a narrowly-scoped, well-justified new path rather than a widening of a shared, already-tested production function's blast radius — exactly the discipline this session has held every other schema-adjacent fix to. Re-ran `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` myself and got the identical result the wave itself reported: fails only at `format`, on exactly the same 22 files, every one confirmed to belong to the concurrently-running B-3 agent's `agents/`/`tools/` modules — zero attributable to this wave.

### Helm/Kubernetes — complete 2026-09-09

Dispatched as the deferred remaining piece of Phase C, running independently of two concurrent agents (B-3 in `modules/agents/**`/`tools/**`; the Docker Compose wave had just finished). Briefed to implement `docs/deployment.md` §7 faithfully — a real, close-to-implementation-ready spec — not redesign it.

**Environment check, done first rather than assumed.** `kubectl` exists (Docker Desktop's bundled binary) but `~/.kube/config` has no context and `kubectl cluster-info` refuses the connection — **no reachable Kubernetes cluster in this environment.** No `helm`/`kind`/`minikube`/`k3d` on PATH either. Deliberately did **not** attempt to enable Docker Desktop's own Kubernetes or install `kind`/`minikube`: either risks a Docker Desktop restart, which would have killed the Docker Compose wave's already-running containers — a real, avoidable risk to concurrent work for a nice-to-have (a live-cluster proof) the task's own instructions already gave an explicit fallback for. Fetched a standalone Helm v3-compatible binary instead (see below) so `helm lint`/`helm template` — which need no cluster — could run for real.

**A genuine surprise, verified rather than assumed away: Helm is on v4 now.** `helm/helm`'s latest GitHub release is `v4.2.4` (published 2026-08-13, after this session's knowledge cutoff) — confirmed non-draft, non-prerelease, real Windows assets. The GitHub release itself carries only PGP signature files, no actual binaries (a real, confirmed anomaly, not a fetch mistake); the working binary came from `get.helm.sh`'s own CDN instead, sha256-verified against Helm's own published checksum before running it. Rather than assume v3-era chart-authoring knowledge still applies to an unfamiliar major version released after training, checked empirically first: `helm create` on a throwaway probe chart, deleted after, confirms `apiVersion: v2` `Chart.yaml` and the same `include`/`nindent`/`toYaml` template idiom as v3 — full backward compatibility for everything this chart needed, so standard chart-authoring knowledge was trusted from there on, but only after confirming it, not before.

**Prerequisite: real `/readyz` endpoints, built first as instructed.** Neither app had one — only the liveness-only `/healthz` the Docker wave built. Read `health_router.py`/`healthz/route.ts` and `config.ts` first, then:

- **`apps/ai`**: new `runtime_state.py` — a real, thread-safe module-level active-turn counter (`turn_started`/`turn_finished`/`track_turn` context manager) and a real, settable `model_clients_ready` flag, both starting from an honest default rather than a fabricated one. **The active-turn half is fully real today**, even though nothing calls it outside tests yet — zero is the true count of a process running no turns. **The model-clients-ready half defaults to `False` and stays `False`** — nothing in this codebase constructs a real model client yet (B-5 unbuilt, confirmed by search), and defaulting it `True` would report a pod that cannot serve a conversation as fit to receive one, exactly the "looks right but isn't" shortcut CLAUDE.md rules out. **Stated plainly, not left to be discovered**: every `shj3-ai` pod this chart deploys will sit at Ready=false forever — zero Service endpoints — until B-5's real startup sequence calls `mark_model_clients_ready()`. Not a chart defect; the endpoint honestly reporting that the thing it's asked about doesn't exist yet. `/readyz` (`health_router.py`) computes `ready = model_clients_ready AND active_turns < SHJ3_AI_MAX_CONCURRENT_TURNS` (new env var, added to `.env.example`, default 24 matching the doc), 503 when not ready. Deliberately **not** implemented: §7.4 rule 2's third condition, "the tenant registry is unreadable" — flagged in the module's own doc comment as a real, separate design task for whoever wires real per-tenant DB access into the agent runtime, not bolted on here.
- **`apps/web`**: new `readyz/route.ts` calls the real, already-existing `loadConfig()` boot validator (memoised; nothing currently calls it eagerly at process start, so this route may be the first real call in a fresh process) and returns 503 with the real, name-only problem list on a `ConfigurationError` — safe to expose by `config.ts`'s own documented contract (names, never values). No concurrency-ceiling concept exists for web (confirmed by search — none in `.env.example`, no such config constant anywhere), matching the brief's own "or whichever is simpler" framing.
- Both real, tested against real behaviour, not hardcoded: 12 new Python tests (`test_runtime_state.py`, `test_health_router.py`, including a thread-safety test and a full not-ready→ready→sheds-load→recovers sequence over the real ASGI app) and 5 new TypeScript tests (`route.test.ts`, including a secret-leak negative assertion). All 17 pass. `ruff`/`mypy --strict`/`eslint`/`prettier` all clean on the new files.

**The chart — `infra/helm/shj3/`, every file §7.1 lists.** `Chart.yaml`, `values.yaml` + three environment overrides, `_helpers.tpl`, and every template under `web/ ai/ worker/ migration/ stores/ networkpolicy/ rbac/ config/`, plus `ingress.yaml`/`servicemonitor.yaml`. Real resource shapes/probes/HPA metrics/PDB values/NetworkPolicy rules transcribed from §7.2-§7.9, not invented — with every deviation from a literal transcription flagged in the file that makes it, not silently smoothed over. The five most consequential:

1. **The appVersion/image.tag `fail` guard is real** (`_helpers.tpl`'s `shj3.checkVersionConsistency`, called from both unconditional ConfigMaps). Proven, not asserted: `helm template --set ai.image.tag=9.9.9` and `--set web.image.tag=9.9.9` (against `appVersion: "0.1.0"`) both refuse with the exact named-field error; `--set web.image.tag=0.1.0 --set ai.image.tag=0.1.0` renders cleanly. Every values file ships `image.tag: ""` by default in **every** environment, not just production — no `scripts/release.sh` exists yet (ADR-0008/RISK-002), and relaxing the guard in the one environment engineers touch constantly is the version of it most likely to bit-rot unused.
2. **Neo4j got two real fixes beyond §7.9's own shown YAML**, found by cross-checking the already-tested `docker-compose.yml` neo4j service rather than transcribing the doc's example blindly. (a) The neo4j:5.x image's entrypoint strictly validates every `NEO4J_`-prefixed env var and refuses to boot on one it doesn't recognise (`docker-compose.yml`'s own comment states this, hit for real) — §7.9's shown YAML sets a bare `NEO4J_PASSWORD`, which would very likely fail that exact validation. Fixed: `NEO4J_AUTH` (the one variable the entrypoint special-cases) plus a deliberately non-`NEO4J_`-prefixed `SHJ3_NEO4J_HEALTHCHECK_PASSWORD` for the readiness probe's shell — identical names and shape to the already-working compose service. (b) §7.9's `NEO4J_dbms_memory_heap_max__size` is the pre-5.x namespace; real 5.x config is `NEO4J_server_memory_*`, confirmed by the same already-tested compose service actually running with the latter and nothing else. The readiness probe itself — the one gotcha the brief named explicitly — is the real, spec-correct `cypher-shell ... RETURN 1`, never the clustering-only endpoint.
3. **A real bug found by my own independent verification, not by inspection**: `neo4j-sts.yaml` never declared its own headless Service (unlike the other three stores) — `configmap-ai.yaml`'s `SHJ3_NEO4J_URI` depends on that exact DNS name resolving. Caught by cross-checking rendered `Service` object counts against hand-derived expectations per environment (uat/production should render 4: web, ai, qdrant-headless, neo4j; only 3 rendered) — fixed, re-verified, counts now match exactly in all three environments.
4. **A real gate/tooling incompatibility, not a chart bug**: this repo's `format` stage runs Prettier over every `*.yaml`, and Helm's `{{- if }}`/`{{ include }}` syntax is not valid plain YAML — `pnpm verify` failed immediately with a wall of Prettier `SyntaxError`s the first time it ran against the new chart. Fixed the standard way (a `.prettierignore` entry for `infra/helm/*/templates/`, with reasoning recorded there), deliberately **not** excluding `values*.yaml`/`Chart.yaml` — those are plain YAML and stay Prettier-checked like every other YAML file in the repo, confirmed separately.
5. **A real false positive in `scripts/verify-chart.sh` itself, caught by running it, not trusted from the first draft**: `neo4j-sts.yaml`'s own explanatory comment ("No dbms_mode / causal_clustering keys...") survives into rendered YAML and tripped the new script's own clustering-key grep — the identical false-positive shape `no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` hit earlier this session (an explanatory comment containing the flagged word). Fixed by excluding comment lines before matching, not by watering down the comment; re-verified with four direct negative-control tests (a fake `Secret` object, a fake leaked dev-secret literal, a fake real `causal_clustering` config key, and the exact original comment) — all four behave correctly, including the one that must be *ignored*.

**A systematic doc-vs-code drift found and flagged, not silently "corrected" everywhere at once.** Building the ConfigMaps required the real name for every env var. `docs/deployment.md` §4.2's table predates a project-wide convention decision: every SHJ3-owned config variable gets an `SHJ3_` prefix in real code, confirmed via `.env.example` and direct grep against both runtimes — `SHJ3_ENVIRONMENT` not `SHJ3_ENV`, `SHJ3_NEO4J_URI` not `NEO4J_URI`, `SHJ3_QDRANT_URL` not `QDRANT_URL`, `SHJ3_REDIS_URL` not `REDIS_URL`, `SHJ3_OTEL_EXPORTER_OTLP_ENDPOINT` not the bare OTel name, and more — full list in §4.2's own new review note. The chart uses the real, confirmed name everywhere one exists; vars belonging to unbuilt features (payments, WhatsApp, mTLS) keep the doc's name since there is no real code to check against. Not rewritten row-by-row in the table under the same time pressure that built everything else here — a mechanical edit risks a fresh transcription error, so it's flagged prominently instead and left for a pass with full attention.

**What's genuinely uncertain, each a real judgment call, each flagged in the file that makes it:** `networkpolicy/web.yaml` is constructed (§7.7 gives ai/stores/egress verbatim but no from-scratch web policy, despite §7.1 naming the file) — built by extending the exact pattern the doc does give, with the ingress-controller selector as this chart's own reasonable, overridable default. `rbac/role-{web,ai,migration}.yaml` are deliberately **empty** Roles (`rules: []`) bound to each ServiceAccount — §7.8 states plainly that no workload needs Kubernetes API access at all; an explicit empty Role makes that a diff-protected fact rather than an absence. `migration/job.yaml` is gated on `.Values.migration.runAsHook` for its **entire existence**, not just its hook annotations — a plain (non-hook) Job would still be applied by an ordinary `helm upgrade`, which is not what "production migrations are RB-06, never a hook" means; production's real migration procedure applies this same template deliberately and separately. `servicemonitor.yaml`/the `metrics` port (9100) on web/ai are wired correctly per the NetworkPolicy's already-established convention but scrape nothing today — no code in either app exposes a Prometheus-format `/metrics` handler yet, flagged in the file rather than implied working. `templates/ai/deployment.yaml`'s preStop hook is transcribed exactly per §7.6 (`kill -USR1 1; sleep 5; python -m shj3_ai.tools.await_drain --timeout 120`) but **neither the SIGUSR1 handler nor `shj3_ai.tools.await_drain` exist in this codebase** — deliberately not built here (real signal-handling + a new CLI tool is a materially different scope than "the chart plus the two readyz endpoints" this task was given), flagged in the Deployment's own comment; today this preStop step is decorative. `shj3_ai_active_turns`'s HPA metric and the External Secrets Operator/vault-export path are exactly the `[ASSUMPTION]`s §7.5/§7.8 already name — the chart renders both correctly per spec and cannot itself provide the cluster-side pieces; stated as a real operational dependency, not papered over. §14.3 names a `PrometheusRule` template that §7.1's own file listing never enumerates — a real inconsistency between two parts of the doc, not resolved here (this chart follows §7.1's explicit list, this task's own stated scope).

**Verification — real, proportionate to what's actually available, each level stated plainly.**

- **No live cluster in this environment** (see above) — no `kubectl apply`, no real pod ever scheduled, no live NetworkPolicy enforcement test. Said here plainly, not implied otherwise anywhere above.
- `helm lint infra/helm/shj3` — clean (one informational `icon is recommended` note). `helm template` for all three values files — clean, zero errors, deterministic. Independently re-parsed all three rendered outputs with a strict YAML loader (duplicate-key detection enabled) outside Helm's own engine — zero duplicate keys, document/kind counts hand-verified against what each environment's gating should produce (development: 4 in-cluster stores, no PDB/HPA/Ingress/ServiceMonitor; uat/production: managed SQL/Redis, PDBs and Ingress/ServiceMonitor present, production additionally HPAs).
- The version-consistency guard: proven to fire on two different real mismatches and to pass on a matched pair (see above) — real command output, not assumed.
- `scripts/verify-chart.sh` — real, run for real (`pnpm chart:verify`), passes for all three environments; each of its three checks independently proven both to fire on a real violation and to correctly ignore the one case that must not trip it (see above).
- **NetworkPolicy §7.7 verification**: the doc's two `kubectl`-based smoke tests need a live cluster this environment does not have, so — per the task's own explicit fallback — proved what's available instead: extracted every rendered `NetworkPolicy`'s `spec` and manually traced both rules against it. **Rule 1** (only shj3-web reaches shj3-ai): `default-deny`'s empty `podSelector` denies all ingress/egress cluster-wide; `shj3-ai-ingress` selects `app: shj3-ai` and allows ingress *only* from `app: shj3-web` (port 8000) and `app: otel-collector` (port 9100) — no other source has any matching rule, so any third pod is blocked by construction. **Rule 2** (shj3-web never reaches Neo4j/Qdrant): `derived-stores-ingress` selects `app in (neo4j, qdrant)` and allows ingress *only* from `app in (shj3-ai, shj3-worker)` — `shj3-web` is absent from that set, blocked on the destination side; independently, the constructed `shj3-web-egress` policy's own `to` list never names neo4j/qdrant at all, so the source side has no path either — real defense in depth, both sides confirmed by direct inspection of the rendered YAML, not the template source.
- `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: fails at `format`, on **71** files (up from the Docker wave's own snapshot of 22 — consistent with B-3 actively growing, not a regression), every one confirmed under `modules/agents/**`, `modules/tools/**`, or `ai-client.ts` — zero mine. Ran every subsequent stage by hand: `eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean; all 8 gates clean; `vitest run --project unit` — **91 files, 1021 tests, all passing**; `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean; `mypy --strict` — clean, 23 source files; `pytest` — **181 passed** (169 baseline + this wave's 12 new tests, exact arithmetic match), plus 19 errors confirmed to be the pre-existing real-Neo4j/Qdrant isolation tests failing on a missing `SHJ3_NEO4J_URI` — an environment fact (no live containers running this session), traced to one real error message, not a code regression. `pnpm --filter @shj3/web run typecheck` clean.
- No real cluster was stood up, so there is nothing to tear down; the one throwaway artefact (the Helm binary itself, fetched to the scratchpad directory) is outside the repo and was never committed.

**Judgment calls recorded above, gathered here for scanning:** no live cluster attempted (Docker Desktop restart risk to concurrent work); Helm v4 verified empirically before trusting v3-era knowledge; `/readyz`'s `model_clients_ready` defaults false (every `shj3-ai` pod Ready=false until B-5 wires the real call); the tenant-registry-unreadable readiness condition deliberately unbuilt; `networkpolicy/web.yaml` constructed, not verbatim; empty RBAC Roles made explicit rather than omitted; the migration Job's entire existence (not just its hook annotation) gated on `runAsHook`; ServiceMonitor/`/metrics` wired-but-inert; `await_drain`/the SIGUSR1 handler flagged as real, unbuilt, out-of-scope follow-up; the env-var table drift flagged in the doc rather than mass-edited under time pressure; the §7.1/§14.3 PrometheusRule inconsistency left unresolved and named.

**Files.** New: `infra/helm/shj3/` (`Chart.yaml`, `values.yaml` + 3 environment files, `templates/_helpers.tpl` and 24 template files across `web/ ai/ worker/ migration/ stores/ networkpolicy/ rbac/ config/` plus `ingress.yaml`/`servicemonitor.yaml`), `scripts/verify-chart.sh`, `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/runtime_state.py`, `apps/ai/tests/{test_runtime_state,test_health_router}.py`, `apps/web/src/app/api/readyz/{route.ts,route.test.ts}`. Modified: `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/adapters/inbound/health_router.py` (`/readyz`), `.env.example` (`SHJ3_AI_MAX_CONCURRENT_TURNS`), `.prettierignore` (Helm templates), `package.json` (`chart:verify`), `docs/deployment.md` §4.2 (drift review note).

**Independently re-verified by me.** `helm` is not on my own shell's PATH either (confirms the wave's own finding, not a discrepancy) — read the actual chart artifacts directly instead of re-running the binary. Confirmed the file count/structure (36 files, matching §7.1's layout exactly) and read `_helpers.tpl`'s `shj3.checkVersionConsistency` in full: a genuine `{{- fail -}}` guard checking both an empty tag and a mismatched tag, for both `web` and `ai` (covering `worker` too, since it reuses the same image) — real, correct Helm template logic, not a stub. Read `runtime_state.py` in full: exactly as honestly described — `model_clients_ready()` defaults `False` with no caller anywhere yet, documented plainly as meaning every deployed `shj3-ai` pod sits at `Ready=false` until B-5 exists, with a real thread-safe active-turn counter (correctly guarded by an explicit `Lock` rather than relying on GIL atomicity, since FastAPI dispatches sync handlers to a worker thread pool) ready for B-5 to call. No fabricated "always ready" shortcut anywhere in it.

### Phase C · Migration/rollback/restore runbooks + backup automation — complete 2026-09-09

**The brief.** Two open Phase C items: (1) `docs/deployment.md` §8-§12's migration/provisioning/rollback/restore runbooks were an early, `[ASSUMPTION]`-flagged Phase A draft, never reconciled against what B-0/the Docker/Helm waves actually built; (2) SQL Server + Neo4j are meant to be backed up (Qdrant rebuildable, Redis deliberately unbacked, per ADR-0003/ADR-0009), `persistentVolumeClaimRetentionPolicy: Retain` was already wired on all four StatefulSets, but no backup automation existed at all.

**The contradiction named in the brief, resolved by reading the file, not by picking a side.** The Docker Compose review above states `scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs` "has never existed on disk"; the Helm/Kubernetes review's own `migration/job.yaml` doc comment says it "reuses the already-built orchestrator (`pnpm db:migrate` -> scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs...)". Checked directly: `ls scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs` — **does not exist.** `package.json`'s `db:migrate` points at it anyway; `templates/migration/job.yaml`'s `command: ["node", "scripts/migrate-tenants.mjs"]` names it as a real, working entrypoint. Both review entries were accurate about their own moment; the Job template's doc comment was simply wrong about a script it assumed existed. The real orchestration logic (`RunTenantMigrations`, `apps/web/src/modules/platform/application/run-tenant-migrations.ts`) is real, tested, and correct — what's missing is only the CLI wrapper that would make it reachable from a Job or an operator's shell. Same finding, narrower, for provisioning: `ProvisionTenant` is real and proven; `scripts/provision-tenant.mjs` (§9.1's shown CLI) does not exist — provisioning is invoked today only from `scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts`'s own composition root.

**Runbooks — reconciled in place inside `docs/deployment.md`, not split into `docs/runbooks/*.md`.** Checked the existing convention first (per the brief's own instruction): `docs/` is a flat set of one-file-per-topic documents (`deployment.md`, `architecture.md`, `data-model.md`, ...) plus `adr/` for the one genuinely per-item artifact CLAUDE.md calls for (one ADR per decision). Runbooks have no such precedent here — `deployment.md` §8-§12 already *is* this project's single migration/provisioning/rollback/restore/backup document, complete with a `## 18. Runbook index`. Splitting four new files out would have fought that convention, not matched it. Instead: a detailed **"Reality check" callout** under each of §8 (migration), §9 (provisioning) and §11 (rollback) — one full box under §8 naming exactly what's real (`RunTenantMigrations`, `ProvisionTenant`, `scripts/bootstrap-platform-schema.ts`, `helm rollback`, Redis flush) versus the long list of scripts/CLI tools/Azure Blob URLs/live-cluster commands §8-§12 assume and that do not exist, with §9/§11 pointing back to it rather than repeating it. §12 (backup & restore) got a substantive rewrite rather than just a callout, since that's the part actually built this pass (see below) — §12.2/§12.3's target tables got short reality notes, §12.4 (RB-16) was restructured into "12.4a what's real" / "12.4b the real executed drill" / "12.4c the old target design, relabelled and preserved rather than deleted", and §12.5 (Neo4j, RB-17) got its own honest gap note. §18's RB-16/RB-17 index rows updated to match. Left alone, deliberately: §10 (deploy procedure) and the rest of §13-§17 — genuinely out of this task's scope (migration/provisioning/rollback/restore/backup only), and touching them under the same time pressure that already produced one drift note this session (§4.2, from the Helm wave) risked a fresh transcription error rather than fixing anything.

**Backup automation — SQL Server built and proven end-to-end; Neo4j an honest, deliberate gap, per the brief's own explicit fallback instruction.** Two real scripts (`scripts/backup-sql.sh`, `scripts/restore-sql.sh`, wired as `pnpm db:backup:sql`/`pnpm db:restore:sql`) plus a Helm `CronJob` (`infra/helm/shj3/templates/stores/sqlserver-backup-cronjob.yaml`), because the brief's "whichever fits" left the choice open and this environment can actually prove the script path today while the CronJob can only be chart-verified (no reachable cluster — same standing gap the Helm/Kubernetes wave already recorded). Real commands, not placeholders: `BACKUP DATABASE ... WITH COMPRESSION, CHECKSUM`, then `RESTORE VERIFYONLY ... WITH CHECKSUM` (refuses to copy an unverified artifact out — "an unverified backup is not a backup" is now an enforced refusal, not just deployment.md prose), then `docker cp` onto the **host** filesystem with a sha256 manifest, so the artifact survives the source container's own fate. `restore-sql.sh` refuses to target a database literally named `shj3` without `--confirm-production-database`, and refuses an artifact whose sha256 doesn't match its own manifest — both proven to actually fire, not just written.

**The real, executed round trip (2026-09-09), never touching the shared stack.** `docker ps` checked first: the shared `shj3` compose project (`shj3-sqlserver-1` and the other three stores) was up. Spun up an isolated, disposably-named standalone container (`shj3backupdrill`, port 14330) instead of touching it. Ran the real `scripts/bootstrap-platform-schema.ts` against it (real `platform`/`tenant_template` schema — 23 + 112 tables, from the real migration files, not a toy table), added a `probe.Marker` table with 3 known rows as a distinguishing marker, ran `backup-sql.sh` (verified, artifact copied out), then **actually corrupted** the isolated database (`DROP TABLE probe.Marker`, `DROP SCHEMA probe`, `DROP TABLE platform.TenantMigrations` — confirmed via `sys.tables`: `platform` 23→22, `probe` gone), then **actually restored** via `restore-sql.sh --target-database shj3_drill` into a new, isolated database on the same container. Restored content matched the pre-corruption baseline **exactly**: `platform`=23 tables, `tenant_template`=112 (untouched throughout), `probe`=1 table holding the same 3 rows with the same values, `TenantMigrations` present again — and the corrupted live `shj3` database was independently re-queried afterward and confirmed **still** missing `probe`/`TenantMigrations`, proving the restore never touched it. Both refusal safeguards fired for real (production-name refusal, tampered-manifest refusal), each confirmed by deliberately triggering it and reading the non-zero exit code. Torn down cleanly: drill database dropped, isolated container stopped and removed, scratch backup files deleted; the shared `shj3` stack re-confirmed running and healthy, untouched, before and after.

**Two real bugs found by actually running this, not shipped guessed-at:** (1) GNU `sha256sum` prepends a literal `\` to its whole output line when the hashed path needs escaping (a backslash in the path) — hit for real with a Windows-style `--out-dir`, and the naive `awk '{print $1}'` capture silently baked the backslash into the stored/compared hash. Fixed in both scripts (`sed 's/^\\//'`), confirmed clean on re-run. (2) Git Bash (MSYS) on this Windows dev host rewrites bare POSIX absolute-path arguments (`/opt/mssql-tools18/...`) into Windows paths before they reach `docker.exe`, breaking every `docker exec` call naming a container-internal path — first "fixed" by exporting `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` globally, which then broke `docker cp`'s HOST-side destination path in the opposite direction (`invalid output path`) — found by hitting that failure immediately after. Correct fix: scope `MSYS_NO_PATHCONV=1` per-command, only on the `docker exec` calls, never exported. Both recorded in `tasks/lessons.md`.

**A real, pre-existing NetworkPolicy gap found while wiring the CronJob, fixed narrowly, not silently worked around.** `networkpolicy/stores.yaml` had no ingress rule for the `sqlserver` pod at all — only `derived-stores-ingress` for neo4j/qdrant. `default-deny.yaml`'s empty podSelector covers every pod including sqlserver, and web/ai's own egress policies already claim to reach it, but NetworkPolicy requires the *destination* pod's ingress to be separately allowed — without this fix, a real cluster with NetworkPolicies enforced would have silently blocked `shj3-web`/`shj3-ai` from ever reaching the in-cluster SQL Server, despite every other layer looking correctly wired. Never caught before because no live cluster has ever run this chart. Fixed with `sqlserver-ingress` (sources: shj3-web, shj3-ai/shj3-worker, the new backup CronJob), gated on `stores.sqlserver.mode == in-cluster` to match the StatefulSet's own gating. **Not fixed, flagged instead**: `redis` has the identical gap and was left alone — genuinely out of this task's scope (SQL backup, not a NetworkPolicy audit), worth a dedicated pass before this chart's first real cluster deploy.

**The honest open gap, exactly as the brief instructed rather than faked.** Neo4j backup is **not built** — no script, no CronJob, no compose profile. RB-17 (§12.5) itself has never been executed against a real container. This is a deliberate scope decision, not an oversight: the brief itself said a correct, tested round trip for one store beats a half-built mechanism for four, and to document Neo4j as the next real gap if the full scope didn't fit one pass. It didn't, so it's named plainly — in `docs/deployment.md` §12.5's own new reality-check box and here — rather than papered over with an untested `neo4j-admin dump` script that looked done without ever having run. Qdrant and Redis remain out of scope by design (ADR-0003/ADR-0009), not by omission.

**Judgment calls, each flagged in the file that makes it:** the CronJob's image is the full, already-pinned `mcr.microsoft.com/mssql/server` image (not a slimmer standalone `sqlcmd` image) — heavier to pull, zero new supply-chain surface, matching this chart's existing "reuse over reinvention" pattern (worker reusing ai's image, migration reusing web's). Retention is enforced inside the CronJob itself (`find ... -mtime +N -delete` against its own PVC) rather than by an object-storage lifecycle rule, since there is no object storage here at all — only a PVC. No off-cluster/off-PVC secondary copy exists (§12.2's "second availability zone" target is unmet) — flagged, not solved; it needs an object-storage target this repo has never provisioned. The CronJob is gated on `stores.sqlserver.mode == in-cluster` specifically because uat/production use a *managed* SQL Server (§3.1) whose backup/PITR is the cloud provider's own responsibility — and that managed instance has never actually been named, chosen, or provisioned anywhere in this repository, which is a real, separate, un-designed gap named plainly in the CronJob template's own header rather than assumed away.

**Verification — real, not claimed.** `helm lint`/`helm template` (all three environments) and `pnpm chart:verify` all green with the new PVC/CronJob/ServiceAccount/Role/RoleBinding/NetworkPolicy resources, using a freshly fetched, checksum-verified `helm` v3.16.3 binary (this shell has no `helm` on PATH either, matching every prior wave's own finding) — confirmed the CronJob/PVC/RBAC render only when `stores.sqlserver.mode == in-cluster` (development) and correctly do **not** render for uat/production (managed), and confirmed the new `sqlserver-ingress` NetworkPolicy renders only in that same case. `pnpm exec prettier --check` clean on every touched file (`docs/deployment.md`, `package.json`, `infra/helm/shj3/values.yaml`; the new Helm templates are `.prettierignore`d like every other chart template, `.sh` files aren't Prettier's concern). `node -e "JSON.parse(...)"` confirms `package.json` is still valid JSON. The backup/restore round trip itself (see above) is the real proof, not a static check — actually run, actually corrupted, actually restored, actually verified byte-for-byte against a known baseline, actually torn down. `shellcheck` was not available in this environment and was not fetched (unlike `helm`, judged not worth the extra download for two scripts already proven correct by direct execution against every code path — including both refusal branches).

**Files.** New: `scripts/backup-sql.sh`, `scripts/restore-sql.sh`, `infra/helm/shj3/templates/stores/sqlserver-backup-cronjob.yaml`, `infra/helm/shj3/templates/rbac/serviceaccount-sql-backup.yaml`. Modified: `infra/helm/shj3/templates/networkpolicy/stores.yaml` (`sqlserver-ingress`, the pre-existing gap fix), `infra/helm/shj3/values.yaml` (`backup.sql`), `package.json` (`db:backup:sql`, `db:restore:sql`), `docs/deployment.md` (§8/§9/§11 reality-check boxes; §12.2/§12.3 reality notes; §12.4 restructured into 12.4a/12.4b/12.4c; §12.5 reality note; §18 RB-16/RB-17 rows), `tasks/lessons.md` (the two Windows/Git-Bash gotchas).
