### Phase C · Neo4j backup automation — complete 2026-09-09

**The brief.** Close the one honest gap the SQL backup wave named plainly rather than faked: mirror `scripts/backup-sql.sh`/`restore-sql.sh` for Neo4j, add a nightly-shaped Helm `CronJob`, and update `deployment.md` §12.5 to describe the real mechanism instead of the "not built" note — same verification bar as the SQL wave (real round trip against an isolated container, both safety refusals proven, no live cluster available so the Helm chart is verified by `helm lint`/`helm template` only).

**Two real, load-bearing findings from actually running the commands, not assumed from the doc.** Both corrected a specific, previously-written claim in `deployment.md` §12.5 that had never been executed:

1. **`STOP DATABASE` is Enterprise-only.** §12.5's existing RB-17 procedure showed `cypher-shell -d system "STOP DATABASE neo4j;"` as the way to quiesce Neo4j Community for an offline dump. Run for real against `neo4j:5.26-community`, it returns `Unsupported administration command`. Confirmed directly, not inferred from documentation.
2. **`neo4j-admin database dump`/`load` refuse any database still mounted in a running server** ("The database is in use. Stop database 'neo4j' and try again.") — meaning `kubectl exec`-ing into the still-running `neo4j-0` pod (§12.5's other pre-existing assumption) can never work either, `STOP DATABASE` or not. The only way to make Community's one database "not in use" is to stop the **whole server process**, which for a single-instance StatefulSet with no Enterprise clustering means scaling it to 0 replicas — not issuing a command inside its pod. This reshaped both the scripts and the CronJob: dump/load always run in a *separate*, throwaway container/pod that mounts the *same* data volume/PVC after the original is stopped, never inside the original container/pod itself.

A third, smaller but real gotcha, found the same way: `neo4j-admin database load --info <db>` — used here as the closest analogue to SQL's `RESTORE VERIFYONLY`, since it validates an archive without loading it — **silently exits 0 with no output at all** when its `--from-path` directory does not contain a file named exactly `<database>.dump` (`neo4j.dump` here; the tool has no free-form filename option). Confirmed for real: exit code alone is not proof of anything for this command. Both scripts and the CronJob's worker-pod script grep the "Database: neo4j" line the tool prints only on a genuine, found, valid archive — the same "never trust exit 0 alone" discipline `backup-sql.sh` already applies to `RESTORE VERIFYONLY`'s "is valid" text, now proven necessary a second time for a different tool. (`--info` against a genuinely truncated archive was also confirmed to exit 1 with "Truncated source" — it does detect real corruption, just not a missing file.)

**Scripts — built and proven end to end.** `scripts/backup-neo4j.sh`/`scripts/restore-neo4j.sh` (wired as `pnpm db:backup:neo4j`/`db:restore:neo4j`) mirror `backup-sql.sh`/`restore-sql.sh`'s structure (host-copied artifact, sha256 manifest, refusal safeguards) with the real differences named in both files' own headers: no SA/password needed (`neo4j-admin` is a local filesystem tool, not a Bolt operation); each run gets its own timestamped subdirectory rather than a timestamped filename (the exact-filename constraint above); the safety boundary for "don't overwrite the real thing" is the **container**, not a database name — Neo4j Community only ever has one user database, always called `neo4j`, so there is no equivalent to `restore-sql.sh`'s `--target-database` escape hatch. `restore-neo4j.sh` refuses a container carrying the `com.docker.compose.project=shj3` label (the real, shared stack's own Docker Compose project name) without `--confirm-production-container` — checked structurally via real container metadata, confirmed present on the actual `shj3-neo4j-1` container and absent on a standalone drill container, rather than guessed from a naming convention.

**The real, executed round trip (2026-09-09), never touching the shared stack.** `docker ps` confirmed the shared `shj3` compose project (`shj3-neo4j-1` and the other three stores) was up. Spun up an isolated, disposably-named standalone container (`shj3neo4jbackupdrill`, ports 17475/17688) instead. Seeded 3 real `Marker` nodes via `cypher-shell`, ran `backup-neo4j.sh` for real (container stopped, dumped via a throwaway container on the same data volume, restarted, verified via `neo4j-admin database load --info`, sha256 manifest written), then **actually corrupted** the drill database (`MATCH (n:Marker) DETACH DELETE n` — confirmed count 3→0), then **actually restored** via `restore-neo4j.sh --container shj3neo4jbackupdrill --dump-dir ...`. Restored content matched the pre-corruption baseline **exactly** (`m1/alpha`, `m2/beta`, `m3/gamma` all present with correct values). **Both refusal safeguards proven, not just asserted**: targeting the real `shj3-neo4j-1` container without `--confirm-production-container` refused with exit code 1 *before* calling `docker stop` — confirmed by re-inspecting `shj3-neo4j-1`'s own `RestartCount` (0, unchanged) immediately after; a deliberately tampered manifest (`sha256` overwritten with zeros) was refused with exit code 1 before the artifact was even inspected further. Torn down cleanly: drill container and volume removed; the shared `shj3` stack re-confirmed running, healthy, `RestartCount=0` throughout and after.

**The Helm CronJob — built on the same proven commands, structurally different from the SQL one, and the first template in this chart needing real Kubernetes RBAC.** `infra/helm/shj3/templates/stores/neo4j-backup-cronjob.yaml` cannot be a single-container Job the way `sqlserver-backup-cronjob.yaml` is, for the same reason #2 above: the Job's own pod cannot both stop the pod holding the data PVC and then use that PVC in one container sequence (a Kubernetes volume attaches before any container in a pod starts). It runs in two phases: an **orchestrator** container (`bitnami/kubectl:1.30`, no PVC mounted, real but narrowly-scoped RBAC — `statefulsets/scale` patch resourceName-scoped to the one neo4j StatefulSet, `pods` get/create/delete/watch, `pods/log` get) that scales the StatefulSet to 0, waits for the pod to terminate, `kubectl apply`s a fixed-name throwaway **worker** pod (the `neo4j:5.26-community` image, both the data PVC and a new dedicated backup PVC mounted) that does the actual dump/verify/manifest/prune, waits for it, logs it, deletes it, then scales the StatefulSet back to 1 regardless of outcome — so a failed backup never leaves the real database down. This is a real, deliberate, narrowly-scoped departure from this chart's otherwise-universal "every workload holds zero Kubernetes RBAC" convention (every other ServiceAccount in this chart — web, ai, migration, sql-backup — sets `rules: []`, stated explicitly each time); forced by Neo4j Community's offline-only backup having no alternative, not chosen for convenience, and documented at length in `serviceaccount-neo4j-backup.yaml`'s own header. It also needed this chart's first cross-namespace egress rule to the Kubernetes API server itself (`networkpolicy/egress.yaml`'s new `neo4j-backup-egress` block, using a new `networkPolicy.apiServerCidr` placeholder value mirroring the chart's existing `managedStoreCidr` placeholder pattern).

**A real design correction caught by re-reading `deployment.md` §12.1/§12.2 before finalizing, not after.** The CronJob's first draft defaulted to a nightly schedule, matching the SQL job's own cadence. `deployment.md` §12.1 already explicitly rules this out for Neo4j: *"do not 'improve' it by restoring Neo4j to a nightly obligation — a nightly stop-the-instance dump would deliberately create a graph outage every night to protect data that regenerates from SQL Server on demand."* Fixed before this was ever proposed as final: `backup.neo4j.schedule` defaults to **weekly, Sunday 03:00** (`0 3 * * 0`), matching §12.2's own target table row exactly, with the mismatch and the fix both recorded in the CronJob's own header so the next person doesn't have to rediscover the same reasoning. A second, related, deliberate simplification flagged rather than hidden: unlike §12.2's stated target ("in a maintenance window with `shj3-ai`/`shj3-worker` writes quiesced") and unlike RB-17's own restore procedure, the backup CronJob does **not** scale `shj3-ai`/`shj3-worker` to 0 first — a write attempted during the (brief, weekly, off-hours) window simply fails at the connection, which is exactly the failure mode ADR-0009 already designed the whole system to absorb gracefully (degrade to vector-only). Judged not worth a second scaled resource and more RBAC to suppress an already-tolerated failure mode in this pass.

**`deployment.md` §12.5 corrected, not just annotated.** The runbook's own shown `kubectl exec neo4j-0` / `STOP DATABASE` sequence — never previously executed — has been replaced with the real, corrected sequence (scale StatefulSet to 0, throwaway pod on the shared PVC, scale back to 1), built directly from what the scripts and the CronJob actually do. §12.1's table row and §12.2's reality-check box (extended, not duplicated) now state what's real for Neo4j alongside the pre-existing SQL Server account; §18's RB-17 index row updated from "NOT YET BUILT" to the real, honest state (script-level proven, CronJob cluster-unverified).

**Verification — real, not claimed.** The backup/restore round trip above is the primary proof — actually run, actually corrupted, actually restored, actually verified against a known baseline, actually torn down, shared stack confirmed untouched throughout via its own `RestartCount`. The Helm CronJob's *orchestration* (the part no Docker drill can exercise) was verified three ways short of a live cluster: `helm lint`/`helm template`/`pnpm chart:verify` all green across all three environments (confirming the CronJob/PVC/RBAC/NetworkPolicy render whenever `stores.neo4j.mode == in-cluster`, which per §3.1 is every environment — unlike the SQL job, this one actually renders in uat/production too); the orchestrator's own embedded bash script was extracted from the rendered YAML and both syntax-checked (`bash -n`) and actually **executed** against a fake `kubectl` stub that records what it's called with, confirming the real control flow (scale down → wait → apply worker pod → wait → scale up) and capturing the exact worker-pod YAML it generates; that captured YAML was parsed with PyYAML to confirm it is valid Kubernetes YAML, and the worker pod's own embedded script was extracted from *that* and **run for real** inside a fresh isolated Neo4j container (stopped, mounted, dumped, verified, manifest written, pruned) — proving the exact script text that will run in the real cluster, not a hand-typed equivalent. (One harness-only false alarm along the way: extracting the worker script via Windows Python's default text-mode file write silently introduced `\r\n` line endings, which real Linux bash rejected on `set -euo pipefail` — traced to the extraction step, not the template; the real `kubectl apply -f -` pipe in the actual CronJob never passes through Windows Python and is unaffected. Re-extracted with `newline="\n"` to confirm.) `helm` itself was freshly fetched (v3.16.3, sha256-verified) since this shell has none on PATH, matching every prior wave's own finding. `pnpm exec prettier --check` clean on every touched non-template file; `node -e "JSON.parse(...)"` confirms `package.json` is still valid JSON.

**What this does NOT prove, stated plainly, matching the SQL CronJob's own honesty bar.** The CronJob's actual orchestration — scaling a real StatefulSet, a real PVC handoff between pods, a real `kubectl wait` against a real API server — has never run against a live cluster; none is reachable in this environment. Treat the first real production `helm upgrade` that actually triggers it as its true first live test. `networkPolicy.apiServerCidr` is a placeholder (mirroring `managedStoreCidr`'s own existing placeholder) that must be confirmed against the real target cluster. No off-cluster/off-PVC secondary copy exists, same real, separate, out-of-scope gap the SQL CronJob already named. `shj3-ai`/`shj3-worker` are not quiesced during the backup window (see above). Qdrant and Redis remain out of scope by design (ADR-0003/ADR-0009).

**Files.** New: `scripts/backup-neo4j.sh`, `scripts/restore-neo4j.sh`, `infra/helm/shj3/templates/stores/neo4j-backup-cronjob.yaml`, `infra/helm/shj3/templates/rbac/serviceaccount-neo4j-backup.yaml`. Modified: `infra/helm/shj3/templates/networkpolicy/egress.yaml` (`neo4j-backup-egress`), `infra/helm/shj3/values.yaml` (`backup.neo4j`, `networkPolicy.apiServerCidr`), `infra/helm/shj3/values-uat.yaml`/`values-production.yaml` (`backup.neo4j.storage` overrides), `package.json` (`db:backup:neo4j`, `db:restore:neo4j`), `docs/deployment.md` (§12.1 table row, §12.2 reality-check box extended, §12.5 rewritten with the corrected `kubectl` sequence, §18 RB-17 row), `tasks/lessons.md` (new entry on verifying a doc's own prior unexecuted claim, not just a doc's worked example).

---

### B-3 · Agent authoring & tools — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The four routes and the seed script B-3's own checklist had left unbuilt after an earlier, concurrent session had already completed the module layers in full: `/agents` (B2 registry — list, clone/publish/unpublish/archive/rollback, an on-demand version-history dialog), `/agents/new` + `/agents/[id]/edit` (B3's 10-step wizard — freely navigable, state persisted, composing both `modules/agents` and `modules/tools`), and `/tools` (B5's 4-tab platform-wide estate view — skills/MCP servers/API connectors/circuit breakers). Plus `scripts/seed-agents-tools-demo-data.ts` (wired as `db:seed:agents`) — the CLI entry point the two `modules/{agents,tools}/application/seed-demo-data.ts` halves had been written against but which had never existed — the nav entries, the full EN/AR `agents`/`tools` i18n namespaces, and one missing application-layer use case the wizard needed. Along the way, this wave found and fixed four real, load-bearing bugs (below), one of them a genuine 500-on-every-real-request defect that would have shipped invisibly past every static gate.

**Pre-flight: what was actually missing, confirmed by direct filesystem check, not from the brief's own summary.** `apps/web/src/modules/agents/` (54 files) and `apps/web/src/modules/tools/` (62 files) were genuinely complete — domain, ports, Prisma adapters, and one use case per operation, each with a co-located passing test. What did not exist: any route under `(backoffice)/agents` or `(backoffice)/tools`, the seed script, the `agents`/`tools` i18n namespaces, and nav entries. Also found and closed before writing a single route: **no application-layer wrapper existed for `AgentRepository.updateVersionConfig`** — every other repository method had a thin `application/*.ts` use case; this was the one asymmetric exception, and the wizard's steps 2-3 (system prompt/tone, model/temperature) would otherwise have had to reach past the application layer straight into a port from route code. Added `modules/agents/application/update-agent-version-config.ts` (+ test, including the 51110 immutability-rejection path) matching `EditAgent`'s exact thin-passthrough shape.

**Orchestration for this wave.** Given the scope (four routes, a 10-step wizard, a 4-tab registry, a seed script, real live-infra verification, all against a codebase with an extremely high existing bar for rigor), work was split three ways under one orchestrator: the `/agents` registry + wizard built directly; `/tools`'s 4-tab registry, the seed script, and the live-infrastructure proof each dispatched to a separately-briefed background agent with an exact reference of every use case's real signature (constructor deps, `execute()` input/output, every port method) compiled first-hand from the real source rather than assumed. The orchestrator's own coordinator additionally dispatched an independent verification agent partway through, specifically to avoid two live-infra proof attempts colliding on shared tenant state — the coordination discipline that finding required is recorded below since it is itself a real, reusable lesson (now in `tasks/lessons.md`).

**Route/wizard architecture, judgment calls flagged at the point made:**
1. **`/agents/new` is a standalone, minimal step-1 form, not the full wizard shell.** `GetOrCreateWizardDraft` needs an `agentId` to fork a draft against, and B3's own 10-step contract ("state persists across steps") only makes sense once an agent exists to persist state *against* — so `/agents/new` collects name/description, calls `CreateAgent` for real, then does a real client-side navigation to `/agents/{id}/edit`, which mounts the full wizard from step "instructions" onward (step "identity" is also editable there, via the same `updateIdentityAction`). This is the one point where the wireframe's "10-step wizard" framing and the domain model's real precondition (an agent must exist before a draft can) had to be reconciled — flagged rather than forced into one artificial screen.
2. **`(backoffice)/layout.tsx`'s `activeHref` — B-2's own flagged, deferred simplification — fixed here, as its own review entry asked whoever added the second real backoffice screen to do.** It was hardcoded to `/iam` because a Server Component layout has no built-in way to read the current request's own pathname. Replaced with a real mechanism: `backoffice-shell.tsx` (a Client Component) now resolves `activeHref` itself from a live `usePathname()` read, longest-nav-item-href-prefix-wins (so `/agents/new` and `/agents/[id]/edit` both correctly highlight the `/agents` nav item), falling back to the raw pathname if nothing matches. `layout.tsx` now supplies `iam`/`agents`/`tools` nav entries with translated labels only — no more hardcoded active state.
3. **`resolveOwnerTenantId()` — a real gap in what was assumed reachable from a normal staff request, found before writing `CreateAgent`'s Server Action.** `Agent.ownerTenantId` is a real, ADR-0011 cross-schema FK to `platform.Tenants.id` — but `getPlatformDb()` refuses any call without `platformScope` set, which `withStaffAuth` deliberately never sets for routine tenant-scoped traffic (ADR-0002 rule 5: cross-tenant reads are provisioning/analytics-rollup only). Resolved via `TenantProfile` — the mirrored singleton row every tenant schema already carries for exactly this (`prisma/tenant/schema.prisma`'s own doc comment) — `getTenantDb().tenantProfile.findUniqueOrThrow({where:{singletonKey:1}}).tenantId`, reachable from an ordinary tenant-scoped request with no platform-scope escalation needed.
4. **B5 (`/tools`) is read-only with respect to binding; the wizard's step 4 is the one bind/unbind surface — the judgment call B-3's own pre-implementation research had already resolved, honored exactly as written.** Both routes read and write through the *same* `modules/tools` `BindTool`/`UnbindTool`/catalogue-with-counts use cases — that shared data path, not a shared UI control, is what "binding in the wizard and in B5 are the same data" means. `/tools`'s three catalogue tabs show `boundAgentVersionCount`/per-tool binding counts as plain numbers, nothing clickable to bind.
5. **Steps 5 ("Knowledge")/6 ("Flows")/9 ("Test") are honestly stubbed, per the brief's own explicit instruction not to fake them ahead of B-4/B-5 existing** — a plain `EmptyState` naming why, no fabricated rows, no `AgentSandboxRun` writes. Step 7 ("Guardrails") is real: `mask_pii_in_transcripts` locked/disabled (no `OverridablePolicy` row ever backs it), `grounding_threshold` and `allow_competitor_discussion` real, live per-agent overrides written immediately via `SetGuardrailOverride` (not batched into "Save & continue", matching how step 4's bind/unbind is also immediate). Step 10 ("Publish")'s environment is fixed to Development with no fake promotion mechanism — that is B-9.
6. **The registry's own "Publish" row action is deliberately narrower than the wizard's Publish step, and that is correct, not incomplete.** It only appears for Draft-status agents (`agent.currentVersionId` is, for a never-published agent, the only version that could ever need publishing) and publishes `currentVersionId` directly. Re-publishing an already-published agent after a further round of edits is wizard-only, via `draft.agentVersionId` — the registry never guesses at which draft a Published agent might have in flight.

**Four real bugs found and fixed — the load-bearing part of this entry.**

1. **`AgentRepository.updateVersionConfig` had no application-layer wrapper** (pre-flight finding, above) — closed by adding `update-agent-version-config.ts`, not a workaround.

2. **`tests/isolation/setup.ts`'s `hardDeleteRegistryRow()` — a real, pre-existing bug, unrelated to this wave's own code, that this wave's own live-infra verification tripped over and that had to be fixed to make verification possible at all.** It deleted only `TenantProvisioningStep`/`VectorCollectionRegistryEntry` before deleting a `Tenant` row. Once `db:seed:iam` gives `sewa`/`customs` real `StaffUsers` (`homeTenantId` FK) and `TenantMemberships`, the delete throws P2003 — but `destroyStoresIfPresent()` (called immediately before it, errors swallowed via `console.warn`) had *already* dropped the SQL/Redis/Neo4j/Qdrant stores by then, leaving `sewa` registered `Active` in `platform.Tenants` with **no SQL schema at all**, and aborting the cleanup loop before it ever reached `customs`. A real, secondary consequence follows directly: `seed-iam-demo-data.ts`'s own module comment claims re-running it "fully repairs" a wiped-by-isolation-suite tenant — false once this defect exists, since `ensureTenantProvisioned` sees `status === "Active"` and skips, printing a success line having repaired nothing. Fixed in `tests/isolation/setup.ts`: `hardDeleteRegistryRow` now also deletes `TenantMembership` (by `tenantId` OR `staffUserId`) and `StaffUser` (by `homeTenantId`) before the `Tenant` delete, with its own doc comment naming the reproduction and one deliberately-unhandled edge it does not claim to solve (a `StaffUser` cross-referenced by `PlatformAuditLogEntry`/`GuideEntry`/`PlatformSkin` actor FKs would still throw — a real retention-semantics decision for whoever next owns that, not a mechanical gap). Verified with a real, isolated `vitest run --project isolation` pass: 26/26, self-healing both `sewa` and `customs` from the half-destroyed state this defect had left them in.

3. **`forkOrReuseDraftVersion` recreated an already-existing version number after a rollback — a real bug in `agents`' own new code this wave shipped, found by the live-infra proof exercising exactly the sequence B2's own `[rule]` describes ("Rollback and environment promotion are distinct… Rollback changes *which version is current*") and no unit test had exercised.** Sequence: publish v1.0 → fork+publish v1.1 → roll back to v1.0 (current again; v1.1 still exists, Published, per Rollback's own documented semantics — it never deletes or renumbers history) → reopen the wizard. `PrismaAgentRepository.forkOrReuseDraftVersion` computed the next draft version from `current` alone (`nextDraftVersion(v1.0)` → v1.1), which is still a real row — `UQ_AgentVersions_agentId_major_minor` correctly rejected the duplicate `(agentId, 1, 1)` insert, and the failed `$transaction` left the SQL connection in a state that made an unrelated page (`/en/agents`, the plain registry list, no forking involved) intermittently 500 downstream in the same proof run — a real, if secondary, symptom worth naming since it briefly looked like two unrelated bugs. **Root cause and fix**: the next version number must be strictly newer than the agent's *entire* version history, not just whichever version happens to be current — `current` is always one of the candidates, so this is a no-op in the ordinary (non-rollback) case. Fixed by querying the agent's highest-ever `(major, minor)` and forking from that instead of from `current` alone, in both the real `PrismaAgentRepository` and the matching `FakeAgentRepository` (the fake carried the identical bug, confirmed by reading it directly rather than assumed fixed). Added a regression test (`get-or-create-wizard-draft.test.ts`, case "(d)") that reproduces the exact publish→fork→publish→rollback→reopen sequence and asserts the re-fork lands on v1.2, not v1.1 — this is the only one of the four bugs a unit test can exercise; the other three are only reachable through real infrastructure or a real browser. Re-verified directly against real SQL Server with a small throwaway probe script (written, run, deleted) exercising the identical sequence end-to-end: `PASS — A.9 repro no longer collides; fix confirmed against real SQL Server`, cleanup self-verified (probe agent and all its rows re-queried as absent afterward).

4. **`<Button asChild>` could not work at all — a real, pre-existing defect in the shared B-1 `button.tsx` atom, invisible until this wave's own `agents-screen.tsx` became its first real consumer, and the one bug in this entry that reached a live route as a genuine HTTP 500 for every authenticated request.** `button.tsx`'s render body always passes three children to `Comp` (the loading-spinner-or-`iconStart` slot, `children`, `iconEnd`) — when `asChild` is true, `Comp` is Radix `Slot.Root`, which throws (`Slot failed to slot onto its children. Expected a single React element child or Slottable.`) unless given exactly one element child or a child explicitly wrapped in `Slot.Slottable`. Confirmed by reading the installed `@radix-ui/react-slot@1.3.3` package directly, not inferred from Radix's docs. `agents-screen.tsx`'s `<Button asChild size="sm"><Link href="agents/new">{t("newAgentAction")}</Link></Button>` — the "New agent" button — was the **only** `asChild` consumer in the entire app, and `button.test.tsx` had zero `asChild` coverage: a documented, exported prop that had simply never been exercised until B-3's registry needed it. Why it hid so completely: the unauthenticated and permission-denied page states both return early and never render `AgentsScreen` at all, so a read-only, unauthenticated `curl`/`fetch` check of `/en/agents` — exactly the kind of check this wave's own initial `next dev` verification pass ran first — returns a perfectly clean 200 with the real sign-in copy, while the *identical URL* 500s for any real, authenticated Agent Designer. It surfaced only once a real session was minted and a real authenticated `GET /en/agents` was issued. **Fixed at the root** in `apps/web/src/components/ui/button.tsx`: wrap `children` in `<Slot.Slottable>` (Radix's own documented answer for "asChild plus icon slots") — a no-op when `Comp` is a plain `<button>`, since `Slottable` outside a `Slot.Root` ancestor renders its children through unchanged, so this is applied unconditionally rather than branched on `asChild`. Two new regression tests added to `button.test.tsx` (`asChild renders the child element in place of a <button>, without throwing` and `asChild plus iconStart/iconEnd still renders exactly one child element with no crash`) — both pass, and both would have caught this before it ever reached a live route. Re-verified end-to-end against a real running `next dev` server, real minted sessions, real cookies: `GET /en/agents` and `GET /ar/agents` for an authenticated Agent Designer both now return 200 and render the real registry, not a 500.

**Why the fourth bug took three passes to actually confirm fixed, recorded because the coordination discipline itself is the reusable lesson.** The live-infrastructure proof was run independently, in full, by two different background agents in sequence (the second dispatched by this wave's own coordinator specifically to keep moving while the first was mid-repair of an unrelated, real infrastructure problem it had found — see bug #2). Both runs independently found and confirmed the *same* two bugs (#3 and #4) with the same root causes, which is real, valuable corroboration rather than redundant work — but it also produced one real collision (a stray `LIVEPROOF-B3`-tagged agent row from one run's script being executed while the other run's owner believed it was the sole runner, caused by the coordinator's stand-down message not having reached the second agent before it had already started) and one genuinely false alarm along the way (this session's own final targeted re-verification script initially failed TOTP completion with `totp_invalid` — traced, by direct code reading rather than guessing, to a wrong field name in the throwaway script itself, `sessionId` instead of `partialSessionId` on `SignIn`'s `totp_required` result, silently accepted because `tsx` does not typecheck `scripts/*.ts` — a known, standing gap this project has carried since B-2's own review first named it. Not a product bug; fixed in the script, re-run clean). Both the collision and the false alarm were run down to their real, confirmed cause rather than assumed away, matching this session's own standing discipline (`tasks/lessons.md`'s repeated "verify at the smallest scope before designing around a finding" pattern) — and both are now separate `tasks/lessons.md` entries so the next wave that runs two live-infra proofs concurrently, or writes a throwaway `tsx` verification script, doesn't rediscover either the hard way.

**Verification — the full trail, three layers.**

*Static/unit, run repeatedly through this wave, clean at every checkpoint that matters, final state below.* `pnpm --filter @shj3/web exec tsc --noEmit -p .` clean. `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean (including the new `asChild` fix and both new route trees). `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **17/17 stages, clean, run as the final checkpoint after every fix landed**: format (after one whole-repo `prettier --write` closing pre-existing debt from the concurrent module-building session, confirmed non-semantic), lint, typecheck, all 8 custom gates (including `message-placeholders-isolated`, which caught 8 real un-isolated `{placeholder}`s across the new `en.json`/`ar.json` `agents` namespace on first pass — fixed with the same `⁨…⁩` FSI/PDI wrap the existing `iam` namespace already uses, not skipped), Python stage unaffected (23 files, 2/2 import-linter contracts, 181 tests), **`vitest run --project unit`: 92 files, 1024 tests, zero regressions** (1023 pre-existing + the new A.9 regression test), plus 8 new `components`-project tests for `button.tsx` (6 pre-existing + 2 new `asChild` regression tests), all passing.

*Real infrastructure, application layer — the live-infra proof's Part A, run twice independently and once more by this session directly against the fix.* Real SQL Server/Redis containers throughout (never mocked). Both full independent runs confirmed, in order: `CreateAgent` (real `ownerTenantId` resolution via `TenantProfile`) → `EditAgent`/`UpdateAgentVersionConfig` → `PublishAgentVersion` (real `v1.0` label) → an illegal edit against the now-Published version genuinely rejected by the live `TR_AgentVersions_publishedImmutable` trigger (error 51110, row confirmed unchanged) → `CloneAgent` (real `"Cloned"` history entry) → fork→publish→rollback (real `"RolledBack"` history entry, `GetAgent` confirmed back to the original current version) → `BindTool`/`UnbindTool` visible from *both* real, independent reads (`ToolBindingRepository.listForVersion`, what the wizard reads, and `ListSkills`'s `boundAgentVersionCount`, what `/tools` reads) → `TripCircuitBreaker`/`ResetCircuitBreaker` with real Redis state transitions (Closed→Open→Closed) and a real SQL ledger (`ManualTrip`/`ManualReset`, real actor ids). Bug #3 (above) is the one step that failed on the first two runs and passed on the third, after the fix — the fix's own dedicated re-verification (a separate, minimal throwaway script exercising only that sequence) confirmed it a fourth time, in isolation, immediately before this entry was written.

*Real infrastructure, HTTP layer — real `next dev`, real minted sessions, real cookies, the live-infra proof's Part B, plus this session's own final independent re-run after both fixes.* The client-binding discipline `tasks/lessons.md` already documents (pin an explicit `User-Agent` via `clientBindingOf` at sign-in, send the identical `User-Agent` **and** an explicit `X-Forwarded-For` on every follow-up request) was applied correctly throughout — zero binding-mismatch false alarms this time, the third occurrence of this exact class of mistake being successfully avoided rather than repeated. Confirmed, in this session's own final, independent, post-fix run: `GET /en/agents`/`GET /ar/agents` for a real Agent Designer (`agents:manage`, no `agents:publish`) — 200, the real registry, not a 500; `GET /ar/agents` genuinely carries `dir="rtl"`; `GET /en/tools` — 200; `GET /en/agents/new` — 200, the real create form; `GET /en/agents` for a real Super Admin (`agents:manage` **and** `agents:publish`) — 200; a genuinely unauthenticated request (no cookie at all) to both `/en/agents` and `/en/tools` — 200, the real sign-in prompt, not a crash or a silent redirect. The earlier independent runs additionally confirmed the manage-vs-publish split in the wizard's own rendered copy (an Agent Designer sees `publishLabelNoPermission`'s text; a Super Admin sees the plain publish label) and an unpermitted role (`LiveAgent`, no `agents:manage` at all) seeing the real permission-denied copy on both routes — not independently re-run a third time in this session's own final pass, since it exercises the same, already-twice-proven `requirePermission` call, not new code the two bug fixes touched.

**Cleanup — re-queried directly by this session, not trusted from any agent's own exit code or summary.** `platform.Tenants`: `sewa`/`customs`/`libraries`/`sharjah` all `Active`, all four SQL schemas present. `platform.StaffUsers` = 5 (the exact seeded count). `platform.StaffCredentials` = 0 (every throwaway credential — from every live-proof run and this session's own final HTTP re-check — removed). `sewa.Agents` = 1, exactly the seeded `SEWA & Utilities Billing Agent`, still `Published`. `sewa.AgentWizardDrafts` = 0. `sewa.CircuitBreakerEvents WHERE reason IN ('ManualTrip','ManualReset')` = 0. `scripts/` directory confirmed back to its pre-wave file list (`bootstrap-platform-schema.ts`, `generate-python-models.mjs`, `generate-tailwind-theme.mjs`, `install-hooks.mjs`, `seed-agents-tools-demo-data.ts`, `seed-iam-demo-data.ts`, `seed-system-skins.ts`, `verify.mjs`, plus `gates/`) — every throwaway probe/repair/verification script from every run (`live-proof-b3-agents-tools.ts`, `__repair-sewa.ts`, `_verify-a9-fix.ts`, `_verify-b3-http.ts`, `_debug-totp.ts`, and others) deleted. The `next dev` server this session started for its own final HTTP re-check was stopped and port 3000 confirmed free.

**A real, separate bug found and self-fixed by the seed-script agent, recorded for completeness.** `modules/tools/application/seed-demo-data.ts`'s `seedCircuitBreakers` unconditionally called `breakers.appendEvent(...)` for the seeded-Open SEWA breaker on every run, even when `ensureCircuitBreaker` found the config already existed — an append-only ledger with no dedup guard, so a second `pnpm db:seed:agents` run silently grew a duplicate `ThresholdBreached` event (exit code stayed 0; invisible to a plain idempotency check). Found during that agent's own "don't just trust exit code 0" verification pass, fixed by gating the event append + matching Redis state write on whether the breaker config was newly created (checked via `breakers.list()` before `ensureCircuitBreaker`), confirmed by re-running the seed script twice more and watching the event count stay pinned at 1.

**Judgment calls, gathered here for scanning (each also flagged in the file that makes it):** `/agents/new` as a standalone step-1 form rather than a full wizard mount (above); `resolveOwnerTenantId()` via `TenantProfile` rather than a platform-scope escalation (above); `activeHref` resolved client-side via `usePathname()`, closing B-2's own deferred item (above); B5 read-only / wizard-step-4-only bind surface (above, honoring B-3's own pre-implementation research verbatim); steps 5/6/9 honestly stubbed, step 7 real (above, per the brief's explicit instruction); the registry's narrower Publish scope vs. the wizard's (above); circuit-breaker `reason` text is collected as a real form field in `/tools` tab 4 even though the underlying use case documents it is accepted but never persisted — collected anyway since the API contract requires it, not fabricated to look persisted; per-agent `usagePerDay` (the registry's "412/day" column) has no seeding path anywhere in `DemoAgent`/`DemoDataSeeder` — documented as a known gap in the seed script rather than invented, reads back `null`.

**What was deliberately left out of scope, named rather than hidden:** the `apps/ai`-side MCP implementation itself (Google ADK MCP wiring, a real FastAPI router) — B-3's own pre-implementation research had already scoped this out as belonging to whichever wave builds the agent runtime, and it remains out of scope here; a real "Connect & discover" click against the real stack genuinely returns `ai_runtime_unavailable` with an honest `detail` string, not a faked success. `TestApiConnector` always resolves `{ok:false, reason:"tools.test_execution_not_implemented"}` — no real outbound-HTTP infra exists yet; `/tools` tab 3 surfaces this plainly rather than fabricating a success path. Two small, real SQL-layer gaps named in B-3's own pre-implementation research (`TR_ToolBindings_serverMustBeConnected`'s missing `isEnabled` guard on its first block; `ApiConnectors.authMode` having no CHECK constraint) were not touched this pass — neither was exercised by anything this wave's own binding/connector flows do, and fixing either would be scope creep against an already-large wave. `tests/isolation/setup.ts`'s one deliberately-unhandled retention-semantics edge (bug #2, above) is named in that fix's own doc comment, not solved.

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/modules/agents/application/{update-agent-version-config,update-agent-version-config.test}.ts`; `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/{composition.ts,actions.ts,page.tsx,agents-screen.tsx}`; `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/new/{page.tsx,new-agent-form.tsx}`; `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/[id]/edit/{page.tsx,wizard-shell.tsx}` + `steps/{basic-steps,tools-step,stub-step,channels-step,guardrails-step,publish-step}.tsx`; `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/tools/{composition.ts,actions.ts,page.tsx,tools-screen.tsx,skills-tab.tsx,mcp-servers-tab.tsx,api-connectors-tab.tsx,circuit-breakers-tab.tsx}`; `scripts/seed-agents-tools-demo-data.ts`. Modified: `apps/web/src/modules/agents/adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-agent-repository.ts` (`forkOrReuseDraftVersion` version-numbering fix), `apps/web/src/modules/agents/testing/fakes.ts` (matching fix), `apps/web/src/modules/agents/application/get-or-create-wizard-draft.test.ts` (regression test), `apps/web/src/modules/tools/application/seed-demo-data.ts` (circuit-breaker-event dedup fix), `apps/web/src/components/ui/{button.tsx,button.test.tsx}` (`Slottable` fix + regression tests), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/{layout.tsx,backoffice-shell.tsx}` (nav entries, real `activeHref`), `tests/isolation/setup.ts` (`hardDeleteRegistryRow` FK-ordering fix), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (`agents`/`tools` namespaces, `backoffice.nav`), `package.json` (`db:seed:agents`).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** Three of the four real bugs found here were pre-existing defects this wave's own code merely exposed for the first time (the isolation-suite FK ordering, and — most seriously — the `Button asChild`/`Slottable` defect, which had shipped invisibly in B-1's own atom for as long as nothing had ever used `asChild` for real), not defects introduced by this wave's own new code; the fourth (the version-numbering bug) was a genuine defect in this wave's own new logic, caught only because the live-infrastructure proof exercised the exact rollback-then-refork sequence a unit test alone never would have thought to construct. All four were found only because this wave insisted on running the real, live-infrastructure proof twice (independently, by two different agents, with real corroboration) rather than accepting a green `verify.mjs --staged` as sufficient — consistent with this session's own standing lesson about gates that pass before being fed the one input that would trip them. The `Button asChild` bug in particular is the sharpest instance of that lesson this session has produced: it is invisible to typecheck, lint, every custom gate, and every unauthenticated/permission-denied code path, and would have shipped to every real Agent Designer as a broken registry page had the verification stopped at "the unauthenticated page renders."

### B-4 · Knowledge / Graph RAG — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The full B-4 slice across both runtimes, split between two parallel halves under one orchestrating session and then integrated, gated and live-proven by that same session directly. `apps/ai` (Python, built directly by the orchestrator): the tenant-aware Cypher builder extended for the retrieval/graph-admin surface it previously had no caller for (`mentioning_chunks`, `browse_nodes`/`browse_edges`, `merge_chunk_ref`/`merge_chunk_edge`, `ignore_duplicates`, `chunk_ids_for_source`, each added to the builder's own "every public method needs a negative isolation test" enumeration); new `GraphStore`/`VectorStore`/`EmbeddingPort`/`RerankPort`/`KnowledgeSqlReader` ports and real adapters (`Neo4jGraphStore` with the inline post-retrieval re-filter, `QdrantVectorStore`, an `OpenAiEmbedder`/`DeterministicLocalEmbedder` pair selected by whether a live key is configured, a `CohereReranker`, a `SqlAlchemyKnowledgeReader` using `schema_translate_map` for tenant-scoped reads under the real ADR-0005 SELECT-only grant); `EmbedAndIndexChunks` and `HybridRetrieve` application use cases with real, proven degradation branches; a new tenant-scoped `/v1/knowledge/*` FastAPI surface (11 endpoints) plus the `authenticate()` dependency `trace_context.py`'s own docstring had sketched but nothing built yet. `apps/web` (TypeScript, built by a dispatched child agent against a locked HTTP contract): the full `modules/knowledge/` layer (17 use cases, 9 ports, real Prisma repos, fakes), the `(backoffice)/knowledge/` route (sources/graph/retrieval/conflicts tabs, nav entry), `scripts/seed-knowledge-demo-data.ts` and `scripts/process-knowledge-outbox.ts` (the honest stand-in for the not-yet-built `shj3-worker` deployment). Both halves, the integration between them, and a real UI-level live-infrastructure proof were then verified directly by the orchestrating session — not delegated and not trusted from either half's own report.

**Why this entry describes real bugs found in *both* halves, not just the seam between them.** Every previous B-wave's review entry in this file found real defects at integration time; this one is no different, except that this wave's two halves were built far enough apart (different runtimes, different agents, a locked contract neither could see the other violate) that several defects were only reachable once both existed together and were driven through a real browser against the real stack — exactly the class of bug this project's own `tasks/lessons.md` keeps naming as invisible to any static gate.

**Nine real, root-caused bugs found and fixed during integration and live verification — the load-bearing part of this entry.**

1. **A relationship-type naming drift between the graph builder and the SQL constraint that is supposed to mirror it, found before it could ever be exercised.** `cypher_builder.py`'s `RELATIONSHIP_TYPES` used `HAS_PROVIDER`; `CK_GraphEdgeRecords_relationshipType`/`TR_GraphEdgeRecords_typeMatchesLabels` (the authoritative SQL ledger's own constraint) requires `PROVIDED_BY`. Renamed to match the SQL side (ground truth per this project's own lessons — the SQL ledger is what a re-index rebuilds the graph *from*), and added the SQL constraint's other four relationship types (`PAYABLE_VIA`, `MENTIONS`, `FROM_DOCUMENT`, `MERGED_INTO`) the builder had never had a caller for either. Had this shipped unnoticed, a decision recorded in `GraphEdgeRecords` (the rebuild source of truth) could describe an edge the builder refuses to ever write — silently breaking re-index for exactly the relationship type most retrieval traversals lead with.
2. **`SUM()` over zero matching rows is `NULL`, not `0`, in `TR_Chunks_recountSource`** — a real, pre-existing bug in an already-migrated trigger from an earlier wave, found only because a live-infra proof happened to delete a source down to its last chunk (FR-KNOW-04's own acceptance case). `COALESCE(..., 0)`'d at the root in `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` and reapplied live to every already-provisioned tenant schema. Full writeup and the general SQL-aggregate gotcha in `tasks/lessons.md`.
3. **A Qdrant payload-projection bug that silently discarded every search result.** `VectorStore.search()` requested `with_payload=["chunk_id"]` (a narrow projection for the caller) and then, as the ADR-0009 belt-and-braces pattern borrowed for the vector store too, checked a *different* field (`source_owner_tenant`) that was never in that projection — always `None`, so the "defence in depth" check silently dropped every hit. Found by comparing a minimal, unfiltered reproduction against the adapter's real output for an identical query, not by trusting an empty result as "correctly found nothing."
4. **Qdrant's real, already-provisioned collections use the unnamed default vector; `data-model.md` §7.2 documents a named `"text"` vector.** Confirmed directly against a live collection (`GET /collections/sharjah_knowledge`) and against `QdrantProvisioner.create()`'s own real code before writing the new point-level adapter — matched the adapter to what is actually running (this project's own "a doc's worked example can be stale relative to an already-tested sibling" lesson, hit a second time), and flagged the drift explicitly in `data-model.md` §7.2 itself as a real, un-migrated follow-up rather than silently papering over it.
5. **The Neo4j Python driver silently drops a node's labels (and flattens a `Path` to an unwalkable list) when `Record.data()` serializes a response** — confirmed by a direct five-line probe against the real container before any production code depended on the assumption, not from the driver's own docs. Every new read method (`search_entities`, `retrieval_subgraph`, `browse_nodes`, `browse_edges`) projects every field a caller needs as its own scalar column rather than returning the raw graph value and expecting to walk it afterward.
6. **`requirePermission()` was found mid-session with its real check disabled** (`if (false && !isAllowed(...))`, a comment naming it as another concurrent session's own deliberate, self-reverting E2E negative-path proof technique on this shared checkout) — restored the real check the first time it was found still disabled by the full-gate run; confirmed reverted correctly by the other session's own process shortly after, and the full 17-stage gate re-run clean once it settled. Named here because a defensive `if(!isAllowed())` check anyone one else could plausibly leave toggled off mid-session is exactly the kind of thing a full-gate run, not a scoped one, exists to catch.
7. **A dynamic `await import(...)` inside `seed-knowledge-demo-data.ts`'s `seedConflictRow` broke the calling `runWithTenant()`'s `AsyncLocalStorage` binding** — `getTenantDb()` threw `MissingTenantContextError` even though the function only ever runs inside a bound `runForTenant(...)` callback. Fixed by converting to a static top-level import (this codebase's own established convention everywhere else in the script), confirmed by re-running the seed script clean end to end afterward. A second, distinct instance of the AsyncLocalStorage-boundary family this project's `tasks/lessons.md` already names once, for a different boundary shape (dynamic import, not a Server Component/Action hop).
8. **`messages/{en,ar}.json`'s six knowledge error-code containers used a flat key that merely contains a dot** (`"recrawlError": {"knowledge.source_not_found": "..."}`) where the calling code builds the lookup path at runtime (`t(\`recrawlError.${errorCode}\`)`) — `next-intl` needed real nesting (`recrawlError.knowledge.source_not_found`), not a flat key whose text happens to look right to a human reader. Found by an independent E2E testing pass as "intermittent click flakiness" across screens well beyond `/knowledge` (a failed lookup renders empty/raw-key rather than crashing, which reads as a timing problem in whatever test happens to hit it). Fixed at the root in both locale files; full writeup, including why the flat form is so easy to miss in review, in `tasks/lessons.md`.
9. **`conflicts-tab.tsx` was the one B6 tab with no real `<h2>` page heading** — every sibling tab (`sources`, `graph`'s two sections, `retrieval`'s three sections) renders a real heading element in addition to its `DataTable`'s visually-hidden caption; conflicts rendered only the hidden caption. A real, if minor, accessibility/structural gap found while writing the live-UI proof's own heading assertion, fixed by adding the missing `<h2>{t("heading")}</h2>` to match every sibling's established pattern.

**The hard, named requirements — each proven live, not merely asserted.** The tenant-aware Cypher builder's own G-numbered isolation matrix went from a partial set (G1-G3, G7-G9, G11-G13, G15-G16) to real, passing, live-Neo4j coverage of **G4 (multi-hop traversal), G5 (duplicate detection), G6 (cross-tenant merge refusal), G10/G10b (retrieval subgraph + mentioning-chunks — the exact graph half of hybrid retrieval, previously dead code with no caller), and the explorer's `browse_nodes`/`browse_edges`** — 20 real isolation tests total, `sewa`/`customs` seeded side by side with structurally-identical, value-distinct data throughout, run against the real container. **Graceful degradation, both paths, both proven live twice**: graph made genuinely unreachable (`SHJ3_NEO4J_URI` pointed at a closed port mid-run) → retrieval still returns 200 with the real vector-half result, `degraded: true`, `"graph_unavailable"` recorded, grounding confidence measurably lower than the identical healthy-path run — never an exception, never a failed conversation. Rerank degradation proven honestly on every real retrieval call this session made (no live Cohere key in this environment): `rerankApplied: false`, `"rerank_unavailable"` recorded, the unreranked hybrid order preserved (proven distinctly from a reranked order via a fake reranker that reverses its input, in `tests/application/test_hybrid_retrieve.py`). **Full re-index on embedding-model change, never partial**: `domain/reindex-selection.ts`'s `selectChunksForReindex` is a small, pure, directly-tested function proving `EmbeddingModelChange` (and `Manual`/`Restore`/`GraphMerge`) always selects every in-scope chunk regardless of current state, while only `Reconciliation` narrows to drifted rows — the one reason with a hard, tested invariant, not a comment. **A real cross-tenant isolation attempt, blocked, at the HTTP layer**: `customs`'s own tenant header against data ingested as `sewa` returned zero results across retrieval, graph browse and duplicate detection in the AI-service-level proof; a full UI-level equivalent (an under-permissioned real principal, `sewa`'s Agent Designer with `agents:manage` but no `knowledge:manage`) was blocked with the real permission-denied screen, not a redirect or a crash.

**Real, integrated live-infrastructure verification — two layers, both against the real running stack (SQL Server, Redis, Neo4j, Qdrant, and — once found to be the missing piece — the real `shj3-ai` container, not a stale hand-launched process; see below).**

*Layer 1 — the AI-service HTTP API directly, done by the orchestrator while the TypeScript half was still being built.* A real tenant, a real chunk ingested through the actual `/v1/knowledge/ingest/embed-and-index` endpoint → real Qdrant + Neo4j writes, confirmed by direct re-query → a real hybrid retrieval call returning both graph- and vector-half contributions with text resolved from real SQL Server → the graph-down degradation proof → a real cross-tenant isolation attempt blocked across three separate query paths → a clean G11/G12 invariant sweep. 21/21 checks passed; all proof data (SQL, Neo4j, Qdrant) confirmed removed afterward by direct re-query, not trusted from the script's own exit code.

*Layer 2 — the real backoffice UI, through a real `next dev`, real staff sign-in (this project's own `mintStaffSession`/`e2e/.auth/*.json` toolkit, no bespoke auth workaround), a real signed-in browser session driven by Playwright.* Signed in as Ahmed Saeed (the one seeded, credentialed principal holding `knowledge:manage`): added a real Document source with pasted text through the real dialog → the outbox drained inline (the TypeScript child's own fix for the identical AsyncLocalStorage-boundary class of bug named above, applied to `addSourceAction`) → the entity graph explorer showed the real extracted entities, live search narrowing correctly → a manually-authored graph node (`+ Add node`, FR-KNOW-10) combined with real duplicate detection against the real ingested entity, confirmed correct independently via direct API calls returning the real candidate pair — the UI-driven version of this one interaction sequence proved intermittently flaky across repeated runs in this session's own testing (residual, not chased further; named honestly below, not hidden) → the retrieval playground returned real scored results with the honest rerank-degradation notice → **re-index all sources** produced a real `ReindexJob` row → both locales rendered (`/ar/knowledge` genuinely `dir="rtl"`) → the conflicts tab's default-policy selector persisted a real change across a hard reload, then was restored → the permission boundary held for both an unauthenticated request (real sign-in prompt) and an under-permissioned real principal (real permission-denied screen, and the missing `<h2>` heading found and fixed along the way) → cleanup removed the real source through the real UI, re-confirmed absent by direct SQL/Neo4j/Qdrant re-query, not trusted from the click succeeding. **9-10 of 11 real browser-driven tests passed consistently across the repeated runs this verification took** (see "what was genuinely flaky," below, for the honest accounting of the rest) — every hard requirement named in this wave's brief was covered by at least one clean pass.

**What was genuinely flaky, named plainly rather than hidden.** Two UI-level test interactions showed intermittent failure across repeated runs of the same throwaway Playwright spec, both independently re-verified as *not* product defects: (a) the manual-add-node → immediate-detect-duplicates sequence sometimes rendered "No duplicate candidates" even though the identical scenario, replayed via direct `POST /v1/knowledge/duplicates/detect` calls against the real API, correctly returned the real candidate pair every time it was tried directly — a client-side refresh-timing sensitivity in that one specific two-step sequence, not a backend defect, and not chased to a root cause given the backend's independent correctness proof; (b) one retrieval-playground run returned no results immediately after this session moved the AI service from a hand-launched process to the properly-supervised `docker compose up -d ai` container, most likely a genuine cold-start timing window on the container's first real request rather than a repeat of (a)'s class of issue, and did not reproduce on the next run. Neither affects the checklist items above, all of which have at least one clean, complete, real pass.

**A separate, wider finding from a full run of the *pre-existing* E2E suite, reported honestly rather than either hidden or over-claimed as this wave's own regression.** Asked to confirm the i18n dotted-key fix (bug #8) cleared the "click flakiness" it had been reported to cause across screens beyond `/knowledge`, a full `pnpm exec playwright test` run of every pre-existing spec (`agents`, `appearance`, `iam`, `tools`, both `roles` specs) showed 12 of 27 tests failing, all on a first-interaction timeout (a dialog field or button never becoming available inside 30s) — spanning modules this wave's own code does not touch at all. Isolating one failure (`iam.spec.ts`'s invite-user dialog) directly reproduced the same "dialog interaction times out" symptom against a *fresh* Playwright run, with no correctness error visible in the page's own accessibility snapshot — consistent with this session's own dev server and shared docker stack having been under continuous, heavy, many-hours-long iterative load from this same session's own repeated manual test runs (agents/tools/appearance/iam were never touched by this wave's own file changes, and this wave's own knowledge-module Playwright runs succeeded repeatedly against the identical, concurrently-loaded environment in the same window). Reported here rather than silently reconciled: whoever next needs a clean signal from the full pre-existing suite should re-run it against a freshly-started `next dev` process, not this session's now very long-lived one, before treating a repeat of these 12 failures as a real regression.

**Addendum 2026-09-09 — re-run confirms the theory, no code change needed.** A dedicated verification pass killed the lingering `next dev` process (started 2026-09-09 19:33, ~1h40m old, holding port 3000 since a prior session), confirmed the four store containers and `shj3-ai` healthy, and ran `pnpm exec playwright test` fresh end to end (webServer auto-started a brand-new `next dev`, global setup reseeded idempotently, one worker, no retries). Result: **27/27 passed**, test-for-test identical to the original baseline — the same 12 that failed under the long-lived dev server (`agents`, `appearance`, `iam`, `tools`, both `roles` specs) all passed cleanly this time, no re-runs needed. This confirms the theory above as environmental (dev-server/stack strain from a very long-running session), not a regression introduced by this wave — no code change was required or made. One incidental server-side error was observed in the fresh run's log (`MissingTenantContextError` from `resetOwnPreferenceAction` immediately after appearance test 8 passed) that did not fail any test and was not chased further, being outside this pass's scope — worth a look if it recurs.

**What was deliberately deferred, named rather than silently checked off.** (1) **The four non-`Document` source types' real fetch mechanics** (`UrlCrawler`/`Database`/`SharePoint`/`ApiFeed`) — real, persisted, schedulable rows exist and the UI says so plainly (`sources.fetchNotWiredNote`); only `Document` (pasted/uploaded text) is wired to real chunking/embedding/graph-extraction this pass. (2) **Automatic source-conflict detection during ingestion** — this wave's scope for conflicts is real CRUD, resolution semantics, the three named policies and the grounding-penalty effect on scoring, all proven live; a chunk-comparison detection pipeline that raises a `SourceConflict` automatically as two sources are ingested does not exist yet, and the two seeded conflict scenarios stand in for it. (3) **The full `shj3-worker` Kubernetes Deployment** — `scripts/process-knowledge-outbox.ts` is a real, honest, runnable stand-in (a long-running polling loop, documented as exactly that), not a container/Helm chart; `addSourceAction`/`recrawlSourceAction` also drain inline so a demo never depends on the loop being up. (4) **`RemoveSource`'s cascade is a best-effort approximation** — no cross-source-support query exists in the locked AI contract to determine authoritatively whether another source also supports an entity before deleting it; this is named in the TypeScript child's own code comments, not hidden. (5) **The Qdrant named-vector design in `data-model.md` §7.2 vs. the real unnamed-vector collections already provisioned** (bug #4, above) — the doc itself now carries an explicit "known drift, not yet reconciled" note; migrating every tenant's real collection is a real follow-up requiring a rebuild, out of this pass's scope.

**Files.** New, `apps/ai`: `src/shj3_ai/domain/knowledge.py`; `src/shj3_ai/ports/{graph_store,vector_store,embedding,reranker,knowledge_sql}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/graph/graph_store.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/vector/qdrant_vector_store.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/embedding/{openai_embedder.py,__init__.py}`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/rerank/{cohere_reranker.py,__init__.py}`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/sql/{engine.py,knowledge_repository.py}`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/inbound/{tenant_auth.py,knowledge_router.py}`; `src/shj3_ai/application/{embed_and_index,hybrid_retrieve,graph_admin,reconcile_inspect}.py`; `tests/domain/test_knowledge.py`; `tests/application/{fakes,test_embed_and_index,test_hybrid_retrieve}.py`. Modified, `apps/ai`: `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/graph/cypher_builder.py` (relationship-type fix + new query methods), `src/shj3_ai/app.py` (router mount), `tests/graph_isolation/{test_cypher_builder,test_real_neo4j_isolation}.py` (new query-path coverage). New, `apps/web`: the full `modules/knowledge/{domain,ports,adapters,application,testing}/` tree (17 use cases + 9 ports + fakes — file-by-file list omitted here for length, all under that one module root) and `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/knowledge/{composition,actions,page,knowledge-screen,sources-tab,graph-tab,retrieval-tab,conflicts-tab}.tsx`; `scripts/{seed-knowledge-demo-data,process-knowledge-outbox}.ts`. Modified: `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/layout.tsx` (nav entry, additive), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (`knowledge` namespace + the six error-container nesting fixes), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/knowledge/conflicts-tab.tsx` (missing heading), `scripts/seed-knowledge-demo-data.ts` (static-import fix), `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` (`TR_Chunks_recountSource` COALESCE fix, reapplied live), `docs/data-model.md` §7.2 (named-vector drift note), `e2e/global-setup.ts` (`console.log`→`console.info`, a pre-existing, unrelated lint-clean fix made while getting the full gate green), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/application/require-permission.ts` (confirmed restored, not edited by this wave — see bug #6).

**Full gate, final state.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: **17/17 stages clean** (format, lint, typecheck, all 8 custom design/architecture gates including `no-raw-cypher` and `no-unscoped-store-clients`, Python format/lint/types/import-linter, 252 Python tests, 1076+ TypeScript unit tests). `no-raw-cypher` re-confirmed firing on a deliberately reintroduced violation and passing clean immediately after removal — twice, once before this wave's own new files existed and once after, both against the final code. `pnpm -r run typecheck` and `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` both run standalone, both clean. Python stage (`ruff format --check`, `ruff check`, `mypy --strict`, `import-linter`, `pytest -m "not integration and not isolation"`) clean; the isolation-marked suite (20 tests) run separately against the real Neo4j container, clean.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** This is the first B-wave in this project built as two genuinely parallel halves (a different runtime each, a locked HTTP contract as the only shared context) rather than one continuous session's own direct work — and six of the nine bugs above (the relationship-type drift, the Qdrant payload-projection bug, the named-vector doc drift, the driver-label-dropping assumption, the AsyncLocalStorage-across-dynamic-import bug, the i18n nesting bug) were only reachable once both halves existed together and were driven for real, none of them visible to either half's own unit tests or to a clean `verify.mjs` run in isolation. That is the concrete argument for this project's own standing discipline of never marking a multi-runtime wave complete on a green gate alone — the gate was green, correctly, at multiple points during this wave before any of these six were found. The two residual UI-flakiness items (duplicate-detection-via-UI timing, one cold-start retrieval miss) are the one place this entry is not claiming full closure — both have independent evidence the underlying feature is correct, and both are named rather than smoothed over.
