### B-5 · Agent runtime — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** The full B4 (orchestration) / B7 (flow engine) / B12 (governance) surface, entirely inside `apps/ai` — no new `apps/web` module needed, confirmed before writing a line of code by reading `guardrails-step.tsx`/`publish-step.tsx` and the B-3 review entry directly: per-agent guardrail overrides with a mandatory, `LEN(...) >= 10`-enforced reason were already real and complete (`modules/agents`' `SetGuardrailOverride`), so this wave's own B12 scope is entirely the *engine* enforcing global policy correctly, not a new UI. New: `domain/{governance,orchestration,flows,ids}.py` (pure, vendor-free); `ports/{chat_model,config_reader,orchestration_store,circuit_breaker,tool_invoker,flow_reader,flow_state_store}.py`; `application/{process_turn,execute_flow,fallback_chat}.py`; real adapters — `adapters/outbound/chat/{litellm_chat_model,deterministic_chat_model}.py`, `adapters/outbound/sql/{orchestration_repository,flow_repository}.py`, `adapters/outbound/cache/{redis_client,redis_circuit_breaker,redis_flow_state_store}.py`, `adapters/outbound/tools/skill_invoker.py`; the real inbound surface, `adapters/inbound/conversation_router.py` (`POST /v1/conversations/{id}/turns`, SSE + `Accept: application/json`); a real seed script, `scripts/seed-agent-runtime-demo-data.ts` (wired as `db:seed:agent-runtime`); 57 new tests (`tests/domain/{test_governance,test_orchestration,test_flows}.py`, `tests/application/{orchestration_fakes,test_process_turn,test_execute_flow}.py`).

**A real, load-bearing gap found before writing the pipeline, not after.** `platform.Policies`/`OverridablePolicies` — B12's own global guardrail catalogue, the thing `mask_pii_in_transcripts`/`grounding_threshold`/`allow_competitor_discussion` are supposed to be rows of — had **zero seeded rows anywhere in this environment**, confirmed by grepping every seed script this project has (`seed-iam-demo-data.ts`, `seed-agents-tools-demo-data.ts`) and finding none of them touch `platform.Policies` at all. The wizard's own `GetGuardrailSettings`/`SetGuardrailOverride` code, and the `CK_PolicyOverrides_reasonSubstantive`/`TR_Policies_syncOverridable` constraints protecting it, were all real and correctly built — there was simply nothing in the table for any of it to read. This wave closes that gap for real: `seed-agent-runtime-demo-data.ts` seeds the six policies FR-GOV-01 names as a floor (`mask_pii_in_transcripts`/`prompt_injection_filter` locked; `grounding_threshold`/`allow_competitor_discussion`/`block_financial_advice`/`restrict_in_scope_services` unlocked), a `RouterConfig` singleton, a real demo `Flow` ("Pay SEWA Bills," all five B7 node types plus the mandatory escape node), and a `ToolBinding` to B-3's already-seeded `fetch_sewa_bill` skill — idempotent, re-run and spot-checked (deleted one row, re-ran, confirmed it recreated correctly) before being treated as done.

**"Structurally unchangeable," precisely.** Per the brief's own instruction to find this project's existing DB-level precedent rather than inventing a new mechanism: `platform.OverridablePolicies`'s *absence-of-parent-row* pattern (`TR_Policies_syncOverridable` — a locked policy simply has no row here, so `PolicySettings`/`PolicyOverrides` cannot legally FK to it, Super Admin included) was already built, by an earlier wave, as the real DB-level guard — this wave did not need to add a second one. What this wave *does* add is the matching structural guarantee on the runtime side: `domain/governance.py`'s `mask_pii`/`detect_prompt_injection` are called unconditionally from `ProcessTurn`, never behind a `Policy`/`PolicyOverride` read — there is no code path, no config value, and no per-agent override that can skip either call, matching architecture.md §7's own words ("a locked policy cannot be toggled by any role, so the check is structural, not configurable") literally rather than approximately.

**Judgment calls, flagged plainly:**
1. **Full B12-tab-1 "manage the global policy catalogue" admin screen is out of scope, named rather than silently assumed unnecessary.** The checklist's own wording ("global policies with locked ones structurally unchangeable") is about the *mechanism*, already real per above; a screen letting a Super Admin edit `Policy.isLocked`/`defaultValueJson` directly has no precedent module to extend (`governance` does not exist under `apps/web/src/modules/`) and was not required by any checklist bullet. Left for whoever builds B14 tab 1's environment/promotion screen, the nearest existing wave boundary.
2. **A generic MCP wire-protocol client is real, separable future work, not built this pass.** `SkillInvoker` (the real `ToolInvoker` adapter) executes `Native` skills via a small, real, deterministic in-process registry matching B-3's own already-seeded skill catalogue by name (`fetch_sewa_bill`, `fetch_etisalat_du_bill`, `book_jawaher_centre_slot`, `escalate_to_live_agent` — real seeded skill keys, not invented ones) and `ApiConnector` skills via a real outbound HTTPS call; `McpTool`-kind bindings resolve to a clean, honest `tool_execution_not_wired` failure, exactly matching B-3's own precedent for `TestApiConnector`'s honest non-implementation.
3. **Step-up verification/assurance-level gating (FR-FLOW-11, B11 tab 2) is not enforced this pass.** `ToolBindingRow.required_assurance`/`FlowNodeDef.required_assurance` are read and carried through, never checked against `Principal.meets_assurance()` — found, not silently worked around: `FlowNodes.requiredAssurance`'s real, closed vocabulary (`Anonymous`/`Verified`/`VerifiedPlusOtp`/`VerifiedPlusDocument`) is a *different* enum from `Principal.assurance`'s `L0`–`L3` scale, and no mapping between them exists anywhere in this codebase yet — flagged here rather than guessed at, for whoever wires B11's real verification flow into the pipeline next.
4. **True low-latency, incremental token streaming is not wired.** `ChatModel.complete()` (every adapter, including `LiteLlmChatModel`) returns one awaited, complete response — no adapter here exposes a token-level async generator. The SSE endpoint (`conversation_router.py`) is real and emits api.md §5.2's exact event grammar (`turn_started` first, exactly one `done`/`error` last, one `tool_call_started`/`tool_call_finished` pair per real tool call), but every event, including `token`, is assembled from the already-completed pipeline result and re-chunked, not proxied live from a provider stream — and no `:heartbeat` keep-alive is emitted, since nothing here is actually long-lived enough to need one yet. Real, separable future work behind the same `ChatModel.complete()` seam.
5. **`POST /v1/conversations/{id}/turns/{turnId}/cancel` and `GET .../stream` (reattach) are not built.** Both api.md §5.1 endpoints presuppose a genuinely async, cancellable, resumable in-flight turn; this pass's pipeline runs synchronously to completion within one request, so there is nothing yet to cancel or reattach to. Not stubbed with fake semantics — simply not present, named here rather than shipped as a decoy.
6. **Automatic circuit-breaker trips flip real, live Redis state but do not append a durable `CircuitBreakerEvents` row.** `prisma/sql/002_tenant_grants.sql` grants `shj3_ai_ro` no INSERT on that table (only `shj3_app`, the web-tier principal, holds it) — B-3 already owns that table's write path for the manual trip/reset it built, and widening the grant for one automatic-transition row would cross a deliberately narrow, already-shipped boundary. `CircuitBreakerPort.trip()`'s own docstring names this precisely and leaves the durable-ledger write for a small `shj3-web` reconciliation job, not invented here.
7. **A real, root-cause DB grant gap found and fixed, not routed around.** `prisma/sql/002_tenant_grants.sql` granted `shj3_ai_ro` INSERT/UPDATE on `ConversationTurns`/`OrchestrationTraces`/`ReindexJobs` only — `OrchestrationTraceSteps` and `GroundingCitations`, the two child tables this wave's own pipeline must write every turn, were missing from the grant entirely (a gap that predates this wave; nothing had ever needed to write either table for real before). Fixed at the root in the grants file itself, guarded identically to the three existing grants (principal-exists + `OBJECT_ID`-exists). Could not be proven against a real least-privilege principal in this dev environment — confirmed directly that no `shj3_ai_ro`/`shj3_app` principal exists locally (`sys.database_principals`, zero rows matching `shj3%`), matching `002_tenant_grants.sql`'s own documented "single `sa` connection serves every role" local-dev fallback — so this fix is real and correctly guarded, but its *negative* half (a write to a table outside the grant genuinely failing) is unverifiable until a real staging/production environment with real service principals exists.
8. **A real, root-cause Dockerfile gap found and fixed, not routed around.** `docker/ai.Dockerfile`'s `dev` stage (the one `docker-compose.yml`'s `ai`/`worker` services actually run) had no ODBC driver install at all, unlike its `runtime` sibling — confirmed live (`docker exec shj3-ai-1 python -c "import pyodbc"` failed with `ImportError: libodbc.so.2` against the already-running, already-"healthy" container) before touching the file. Fixed by mirroring the `runtime` stage's own install block into `dev`. **Could not rebuild the image in this session** — this sandbox has no outbound network access during `docker build` (confirmed: both a direct `docker build` and a bare `curl` to `packages.microsoft.com` hung/failed identically), so the fix is real, correct, and verified conceptually (matches the already-working `runtime` stage's own approach) but the `dev`-target image itself was not rebuilt this pass. The real, live-infra proof below therefore ran the actual `shj3_ai` application code on the **host** (the same `apps/ai/.venv` `pytest`/`ruff`/`mypy` already use, which already had ODBC Driver 17 installed), pointed at the same real Docker-hosted SQL Server/Redis/Neo4j/Qdrant every other wave's proof uses — a real proof against real infrastructure, just not routed through the container process specifically for this one session. Whoever next has real internet access from a build context should rebuild `ai`/`worker` and confirm the fix; the source-level fix does not need to change.

**Nine real bugs found and fixed during live verification — the load-bearing part of this entry, and see `tasks/lessons.md` for four of them written up as standalone, reusable lessons.**
1. **`FlowVersion.is_current.is_(True)` compiled to invalid T-SQL** (`WHERE ... IS 1` — SQL Server's `IS` accepts only `NULL`). Fixed to `== True` (`# noqa: E712`); found the moment the very first real flow lookup ran.
2. **`platform.Policies.defaultValueJson`/trace `argumentsMasked` rejected bare JSON scalars** — SQL Server 2022's `ISJSON()` requires an object or array. Fixed by wrapping every such value as `{"value": <scalar>}` and by adding a `_masked_arguments_json()` helper that validates the masked/truncated result is still real JSON, falling back to a guaranteed-valid wrapper when masking or truncation broke it.
3. **`ConversationTurns.role`/`.contentFormat`, `OrchestrationTraces.guardrailPreResult`/`guardrailPostResult` all used plausible-but-wrong string literals** (`'user'`/`'assistant'`/`'PlainText'`/`'Passed'`/`'Refused'` in place of the real `'Citizen'`/`'Assistant'`/`'Text'`/`'Pass'`/`'Blocked'` vocabulary) — none catchable by `mypy` (plain `str` columns) or by fake-backed tests (no CHECK-constraint enforcement in a fake); each found only by a real INSERT against the real, running SQL Server container.
4. **`CK_ConversationTurns_refusalPaired` violated for the `BLOCKED` status** — `wasRefused`/`refusalReason` were paired correctly for `REFUSED` but not for `BLOCKED`/`FAILED`, the other two terminal statuses that also end a turn with no real answer. Fixed by deriving both fields from one shared `was_refused`/`refusal_reason` computation covering all three.
5. **`OrchestrationTraceSteps.errorCode` (`VARCHAR(64)`) overflowed with an unbounded provider-error message.** The raw `ChatModelUnavailableError` text (real, sometimes long) was put in the 64-char code column instead of the 1000-char `resultSummary` column two lines away. Fixed by keeping `errorCode` a short, stable code (`"orchestration.fallback_triggered"`) and moving the real (PII-masked) error text to `result_summary`.
6. **A `ToolCall`-kind trace step could be constructed with no `toolBindingId`**, violating `CK_OrchestrationTraceSteps_toolCallHasBinding`, in the one path where the flow engine's runtime lookup found no live `ToolBindingRow` for a node's own configured target. Fixed by using the flow node's own authored `toolBindingId` (guaranteed non-null by `CK_FlowNodes_toolCallFields`) rather than requiring a live config-row match.
7. **`AgentFlowBindings` needs `flowVersionId`/`isEnabled`/`ordinal`, not just `agentVersionId`/`flowId`** — missed in the first draft of the seed script (the Prisma schema field list was mis-remembered from the earlier read rather than re-checked at the point of writing the INSERT).
8. **Two DB-level "publish requires the paired field" constraints** (`CK_Flows_publishedHasVersion`, `CK_FlowVersions_publishedPaired`) **required inserting as `Draft` first, then flipping to `Published` together with the field it's paired with** (`currentVersionId`, `publishedAt`) — an insert with `status='Published'` and the paired field still `NULL` (true at insert time, since the referenced row doesn't exist yet) fails outright, mirroring the exact two-phase pattern this project's B-3 wave already established for `AgentVersions`/`TR_AgentVersions_publishedImmutable`.
9. **A duplicate trace-step ordinal** — the flow engine's free-text-escape branch (checked before the main per-node loop) appended its own step using the raw `starting_ordinal` parameter without incrementing it, while every other branch in the same function increments first. On a turn's very first step being an escape, this collided with the pipeline's own `GuardrailPre` step at ordinal 1, tripping `UQ_OrchestrationTraceSteps_traceId_ordinal` on the real INSERT. Fixed with a one-line `ordinal += 1`; a new regression assertion (`len(ordinals) == len(set(ordinals))`) added to the existing escape unit test, since the prior test asserted step *count* and *kind* but never ordinal *uniqueness*.

**Real, live-infrastructure verification — the full trail, against the real Docker-hosted SQL Server/Redis/Neo4j/Qdrant stack (already running throughout; confirmed via `docker ps` before starting), run from the host process for the reason named in judgment call #8 above.**

- **Full pipeline, real end to end, three separate times (one per mode):** a real turn against the real seeded SEWA billing agent, in each of Sequential/Parallel/SupervisorWorker mode (flipped live via `UPDATE sewa.RouterConfigs`), produced a real `GuardrailPre → [flow: Question→ToolCall→Message] → Route → [mode-specific execution] → Merge → Retrieval → GuardrailPost` trace end to end, `200 OK`, every stage visible on the response's own `trace.hops[]`.
- **Three execution modes, distinct traces, proven live with three real published agents in scope** (the seeded SEWA agent + two throwaway candidate agents seeded for this proof, removed afterward): Sequential produced exactly 1 routing-stage `AgentInvoke`; Parallel produced exactly 2 (primary + 1 secondary, `isSecondaryAgent` correctly distinguishing them, "Merged 2 replies" on the Merge step); SupervisorWorker produced exactly 3 (2 workers + 1 supervisor review) — a real, monotonically increasing, inspectably distinct shape per mode, not merely a label change. (The automated version of this same proof, `test_the_three_modes_produce_genuinely_distinct_trace_shapes`, passes as one of the 57 new unit tests, independent of live infrastructure.)
- **Forced primary-model failure → fallback, live:** a real, throwaway agent version seeded with `primaryModel='b5-test-forced-fail-primary'`/`fallbackModel='b5-test-working-fallback'`, invoked with `SHJ3_CHAT_FORCE_FAIL_MODELS` set to the primary's own name — the real HTTP response showed `usedFallbackModel: true` and a trace step reading `"...: b5-test-forced-fail-primary failed -> fallback b5-test-working-fallback"` with `errorCode: "orchestration.fallback_triggered"` — both models and the trigger, named on the persisted trace, exactly matching FR-AGENT-12's own acceptance wording.
- **Cost ceiling, live:** a real turn with `budget.maxTokens=1` in the request body terminated with `status: "failed"` and a real `Merge`-kind step reading `status: "Blocked"`, `errorCode: "orchestration.cost_ceiling_exceeded"`.
- **Hop ceiling, live:** a real SupervisorWorker turn (3 candidates) with `budget.maxHops=1` terminated identically, `errorCode: "orchestration.max_hops_exceeded"`.
- **Free-text escape, live, mid-flow, context preserved:** a real conversation's Redis flow state pre-seeded at the `ask_account` `Question` node, sent `"actually, i have another inquiry"` — the real trace showed `FlowEscape` at ordinal 2 (immediately after `GuardrailPre`, the exact case bug #9 above was found in), followed by a real, full re-routing (`Route → AgentInvoke → Merge → Retrieval → GuardrailPost`) proving the turn did not simply stop, and — read directly back from Redis after the call — the flow's own live state was still `currentNodeKey: "ask_account"` with `escapeContextJson` now holding the citizen's own escape text: the flow is genuinely resumable, not reset.
- **PII masking, live, the exact proof this wave's brief demanded:** a real turn containing a real Emirates-ID-shaped string (`784-1990-7654321-5`) was sent, and the real, persisted `sewa.ConversationTurns.contentMasked` row was read back directly: `"My Emirates ID is [REDACTED:EMIRATES_ID], please help me with my bill."` — then a `SELECT COUNT(*) ... WHERE contentMasked LIKE '%784-1990-7654321-5%'` against both `ConversationTurns` and `OrchestrationTraceSteps` (`argumentsMasked`/`resultSummary`) returned **zero rows** — the raw PII string is provably absent from everything this turn persisted, not merely "probably masked."
- **A real tool call via the real B-3 tools registry, twice over, in the same turn:** the seeded `fetch_sewa_bill` `Skill`/`ToolBinding` (bound to the real SEWA billing agent's real current version) was invoked once by the flow engine's own `ToolCall` node and, independently, a second time by the agent's own tool-calling decision — both real, both recorded as distinct `ToolCall` trace steps with the real `toolBindingId`, both returning a real, deterministically-computed (not canned) bill amount.

**Cleanup — re-queried directly, not trusted from exit codes.** Every throwaway `Conversation` (10 total across the scenarios above) and every `ConversationTurn`/`OrchestrationTrace`/`OrchestrationTraceStep`/`GroundingCitation` row they produced: deleted, re-queried as zero rows. Both throwaway test-only agents (`b5-fallback-test-agent`, `b5-third-candidate-agent`) and their versions: deleted (via `currentVersionId = NULL` + `status = 'Draft'` first, to satisfy `CK_Agents_publishedHasVersion` before the FK-ordered delete), re-queried as absent. The one Redis key touched for the escape proof (`sewa:conv:<id>`): deleted. **Kept, deliberately, as real permanent seed data** (not throwaway): the six `platform.Policies` rows, the `sewa.RouterConfigs` singleton (reset to its `Sequential` default before finishing), the real `ToolBinding` and demo `Flow` on the real, already-seeded SEWA billing agent — all now backed by the committed, idempotent `scripts/seed-agent-runtime-demo-data.ts` rather than left as invisible ad-hoc state, spot-checked by deleting one row and re-running the script to confirm it recreates correctly.

**Full gate, final state.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: **17/17 stages clean** — format, lint, typecheck, all custom design/architecture gates, Python format/lint/`mypy --strict`/import-linter, 309 Python tests (57 new), 1076 TypeScript unit tests. `pnpm -r run typecheck` and `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` both run standalone, both clean (one real `no-unused-vars` finding in the new seed script, fixed). Import-linter's two contracts (`adapters → application → ports → domain`, the vendor-free "swap test") both kept, confirmed against every new file. A broader, exploratory `mypy --strict` sweep across every `tests/` file in `apps/ai` (not just this wave's own) surfaces roughly 30 pre-existing errors in B-3/B-4 files this wave never touched (`tests/application/fakes.py`, `tests/graph_isolation/test_real_neo4j_isolation.py`, `tests/vector_isolation/test_real_qdrant_isolation.py`, three generator-return-type findings) — confirmed pre-existing and out of this wave's scope by running `mypy` scoped to only this wave's 31 files (clean) versus the same command against the whole tree (identical ~30 errors present either way); named here for whoever owns those modules next rather than silently fixed in an already-very-large diff, and confirmed **not** part of the project's own official gate (`py:types` in `verify.mjs` reports "no issues found in 68 source files" clean — it does not check `tests/`, so this pre-existing gap does not block the real gate).

**Files.** New, `apps/ai`: `src/shj3_ai/domain/{governance,orchestration,flows,ids}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/ports/{chat_model,config_reader,orchestration_store,circuit_breaker,tool_invoker,flow_reader,flow_state_store}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/application/{process_turn,execute_flow,fallback_chat}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/chat/{__init__,litellm_chat_model,deterministic_chat_model}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/sql/{orchestration_repository,flow_repository}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/cache/{redis_client,redis_circuit_breaker,redis_flow_state_store}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/outbound/tools/{__init__,skill_invoker}.py`; `src/shj3_ai/adapters/inbound/conversation_router.py`; `tests/domain/{test_governance,test_orchestration,test_flows}.py`; `tests/application/{orchestration_fakes,test_process_turn,test_execute_flow}.py`; `scripts/seed-agent-runtime-demo-data.ts`. Modified: `src/shj3_ai/app.py` (router mount + `mark_model_clients_ready()` wiring, closing `runtime_state.py`'s own previously-named gap), `apps/ai/pyproject.toml` (`httpx` promoted to a real runtime dependency; import-linter forbidden-modules list updated to match), `prisma/sql/002_tenant_grants.sql` (the `OrchestrationTraceSteps`/`GroundingCitations` grant fix, judgment call #7), `docker/ai.Dockerfile` (the `dev`-stage ODBC install fix, judgment call #8), `package.json` (`db:seed:agent-runtime`).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** Every one of the nine real bugs above was invisible to `mypy --strict`, `ruff`, import-linter, and a full, fake-backed unit-test suite (309 tests, all green) simultaneously — each is a real SQL Server dialect/constraint/vocabulary fact that only a real INSERT against a real, running container could ever surface, which is exactly this project's own standing argument for never trusting a green gate alone on a wave that writes to real infrastructure for the first time. Two real, root-cause infrastructure gaps (the SQL grant, the Dockerfile ODBC install) were found and fixed rather than routed around, though the grant fix's negative half and the Dockerfile fix's own image rebuild are both honestly unverifiable in this specific sandboxed session (no real least-privilege DB principal locally; no outbound network access during `docker build`) — both are named plainly rather than claimed as fully closed. Four deliberately deferred items (step-up/assurance gating, a generic MCP client, true incremental token streaming, the turn cancel/reattach endpoints) are each real, separable future work behind seams this wave already built (`ToolBindingRow.required_assurance`, `ToolInvoker`, `ChatModel.complete()`), not silently dropped scope.

### B-8 · Identity & transactions — complete 2026-09-09

**Delivered.** Two new, fully layered `apps/web` modules — `identity` (domain/ports/application/adapters/testing) and `transactions` (domain/ports/application/adapters/testing) — plus the Python-side step-up gate wired into both real tool-call paths in `apps/ai`'s B-5 pipeline (the flow engine's own `ToolCall` node in `ExecuteFlowStep`, and an agent's own tool-calling decision in `ProcessTurn`'s `invoke_agent`), a minimal but real `/identity` backoffice screen, and the exact enum reconciliation B-5's own review flagged and left for this wave.

**The reconciliation, closed at the root.** B-5 found `FlowNodes.requiredAssurance`/`ToolBindings.requiredAssurance` (the persisted `Anonymous`|`Verified`|`VerifiedPlusOtp`|`VerifiedPlusDocument` enum) and `Principal.assurance`/`iam/domain/assurance.ts`'s `AssuranceLevel` (`L0`-`L3`, a rank) had never been reconciled anywhere. Two docs disagreed about what the reconciliation even was: `docs/data-model.md` §4.11 (written earlier) proposed collapsing `VerifiedPlusOtp`/`VerifiedPlusDocument` onto a shared rank `L2` and left `L3` unallocated, explicitly marking RISK-006 open; `docs/requirements.md`'s own RISK-006 row records a **later, explicit product-owner decision** superseding that — four distinct ranks, `L3` = document-verified — and the already-shipped, already-tested `iam/domain/assurance.ts` (B-2) was built to that later decision, not to data-model.md's table. Per this project's own "trust the already-tested sibling over the doc" discipline, the later decision and the real code win; `docs/data-model.md` §4.11 and `docs/api.md` §9.9's own stale inline comment are corrected in this same change. The bijection itself is real code, not just a comment: `identity/domain/assurance-mapping.ts` (TypeScript) and `apps/ai/domain/identity.py` (Python), each with its own unit tests, both used by the real step-up gates on both runtimes rather than each re-deriving the mapping ad hoc.

**`VerificationProvider` port + mock adapter — already real, confirmed rather than rebuilt.** `iam/ports/verification-provider.ts`/`iam/adapters/outbound/mock-verification-provider.ts` (B-2) already implement all four levels as a first-class, drivable fixture, and `platform/config.ts` already refused `SHJ3_VERIFICATION_ADAPTER=mock` in production (ADR-0006 rule 6) before this wave started. This wave's own job here was narrower: build `identity`'s real orchestration around that port (`CompleteVerification`, `BeginVerification`, account-ownership confirmation) and give **payments** the identical discipline — `transactions/adapters/outbound/payment/mock-payment-gateway.ts`'s `MockPaymentGateway` is a new, equally real, non-stub fixture (deterministic settlement, idempotent by key, drivable to a decline), and `SHJ3_PAYMENT_GATEWAY_ADAPTER` is a new, additive `platform/config.ts` env var with the identical boot-time production refusal, test-for-test mirrored against the existing verification-adapter guard. Both the object-level (`createMockPaymentGateway`/`createMockVerificationProvider` throw) and process-level (`validateConfig` throws) guards are proven directly — not simulated — in both the unit-test suite and the live proof below.

**Account-ownership check + step-up rules evaluated before the tool call.** `identity/application/evaluate-step-up.ts` is the TypeScript-side gate (`InitiatePayment` calls it before ever touching `PaymentGateway.createIntent`); `apps/ai/domain/identity.py`'s `satisfies_required_assurance` plus a new `IdentityReader` port (real adapter: `SqlAlchemyOrchestrationRepository.get_assurance`, reading `CitizenIdentities` fresh every turn — never cached on the conversation) is the pipeline-side gate. Two genuinely distinct real tool-call paths are both gated, proven pre-execution:
- The flow engine's own `ToolCall` node (`ExecuteFlowStep`): a new `FlowRunOutcome.STEP_UP_REQUIRED` outcome, checked *before* `ToolInvoker.invoke()` is ever called, preserving the flow's exact position (a pause, not a failure — the citizen resumes at the same node after stepping up). The effective required level is the node's own authored override if set, else the bound tool's own floor — a flagged judgment call, since neither doc names which wins when both are set (`ExecuteFlowStep._required_assurance_for`'s own doc comment).
- An agent's own tool-calling decision (`ProcessTurn.invoke_agent`): the identical gate, applied to the model's own tool-call response rather than a flow node, with a new `TurnStatus.STEP_UP_REQUIRED` that `_finish`'s `was_refused` computation deliberately excludes (api.md §3.5: "a pause, not a failure").
`identity/application/confirm-and-link-service-account.ts` is the account-ownership check itself, honoring `VerificationConfigs.accountOwnershipCheckEnabled` and failing closed on both `not_matched` and `unavailable` (never conflating an outage with a denial, per that port's own doc comment).

**Payment gateways, transactions, receipts, refund approve/decline — real, working, end to end.** `InitiatePayment` (step-up gate → idempotency check → `PaymentGateway.createIntent` → `Transactions` row) → `GetReceipt` → `RequestRefund` (real `UQ_RefundRequests_pendingPerTransaction`-respecting flow) → `ApproveRefund`/`DeclineRefund` (real gateway call, real `PaymentEvents` row, real audit entry via `TenantAuditSink`, `RefundRequests`/`Transactions` state machines enforced in both the domain layer and the real DB). `TransactionStatus`/`RefundStatus` are real, transcribed-from-the-constraint state machines (`domain/transaction-status.ts`, `domain/refund-status.ts`), not free-form strings. **A real gap named, not silently routed around**: api.md §6.11's own prose describes a `refund_pending_gateway` intermediate state for "an approval whose gateway call fails" — grepping the real schema found no such column value exists anywhere (`CK_RefundRequests_status`/`CK_Transactions_status` are both closed, three-and-seven-value sets respectively with no such member). `ApproveRefund`'s own doc comment names this and behaves honestly instead: a failed gateway call leaves both rows exactly as they were (a real, retryable failure), never a status value that would violate the live CHECK constraint.

**Identity stitching — verified sessions only, proven at three independent layers.** `StitchIdentity`'s own pre-check refuses an `L0` session before any I/O; `IdentityLinkRepository.create()`'s own belt-and-braces refusal (both the fake and the real Prisma adapter) catches a bypass of layer 1; `CK_IdentityLinks_verifiedOnly` is the real, unbypassable database constraint, proven directly in the live verification below via a raw SQL `INSERT` attempt that never goes through this codebase's own application layer at all.

**Transactions retained 7 years, independent of transcript retention.** The schema-level mechanism (`Transactions.retentionExpiresAt AS DATEADD(YEAR, 7, initiatedAt) PERSISTED`, `TR_Transactions_blockDelete`, `PrivacyConfigs` carrying no transaction-retention column at all) already existed from the original holistic schema wave; this wave's own `TransactionRepository` port deliberately offers no delete method at all (matching `OrchestrationStore`'s own established "don't offer a method the grant/rule would reject anyway" pattern), and the live proof below exercises the real trigger directly. **Named, not silently assumed:** B-9 (the transcript-retention purge job) is not built yet, so there is currently nothing for this carve-out to collide with in this environment — the independence is structural (by construction, per the schema) rather than proven against a real, running competing purge job, since none exists to run against.

**Judgment calls, flagged plainly:**
1. **A node's own `requiredAssurance` override wins over its bound tool's floor when both are set** (`execute_flow.py`'s `_required_assurance_for`) — neither `docs/data-model.md` §4.6 nor §4.11 names which wins; a flow author configuring a *stricter* per-node requirement than the tool's own floor is the only directionally sensible reason to set it at all.
2. **The backoffice `/identity` screen is a real, functional two-section page (step-up rules, pending refunds) — not a full five-tab reproduction of B11's wireframe.** Verification-provider toggles, gateway method/mode configuration, and receipt settings all have real ports/repositories/adapters built and unit-tested (`identity`/`transactions` modules), but no backoffice UI yet — named as deferred UI polish, the same category of scope trim B-5 took for B12 tab 1's admin screen.
3. **`refund_pending_gateway` (api.md §6.11's own prose) has no real schema support** — see above; a real gap in the doc, not silently invented as a new enum member that would violate a live CHECK constraint.
4. **The direct-agent-tool-call gate is proven by 4 passing unit tests, not by a live HTTP/DB proof** — see the live-verification section below for why (a real, external network restriction, not a code gap), and why the flow-engine gate and every identity/payment/stitching/retention requirement *were* proven live instead.
5. **B-9's transcript-retention purge job does not exist yet** — the 7-year carve-out's *independence* from it is structural (nothing to collide with today), not proven against a real competing purge run.

**A real, load-bearing gap found and fixed during this wave, not left dangling.** `iam/adapters/outbound/mock-verification-provider.ts`'s own `MOCK_IDENTITY.emiratesIdHash` constant (`"sha256:9f2c4e1a..."`, 71 characters) silently violated `CitizenIdentities.emiratesIdHash`'s real `CHAR(64)` width and `CK_CitizenIdentities_noPlaintextId`'s `LEN(...) = 64` requirement — invisible to every prior wave because nothing before B-8 had ever taken a `VerifiedIdentity` from this port and actually persisted it to a real row. Found live, on the first real `INSERT`; fixed at the root (the constant itself, now a bare 64-hex-char digest) with its own test (`toContain("sha256:")` → `toMatch(/^[0-9a-f]{64}$/)`, asserting the real shape rather than a human-readable marker). A second, related gap found in the same pass: `CompleteVerification`'s first draft always created a fresh `CitizenIdentities` row when `existingCitizenIdentityId` was `null`, which is wrong the moment the *same real person* verifies from a second, independent anonymous session — `UQ_CitizenIdentities_emiratesIdHash` rejects the resulting duplicate. Fixed by wiring `CitizenIdentityRepository.findByEmiratesIdHash` (already on the port, never called from here) into the use case, with a new regression test proving the dedupe. Both are written up as standalone lessons in `tasks/lessons.md`.

**Real, live-infrastructure verification — the full trail.**
- **A real, throwaway TypeScript script** (`scripts/tmp-b8-live-proof.ts`, deleted after this run) against the real Docker-hosted SQL Server, real "sewa" tenant schema:
  - **Four real assurance levels**: `MockVerificationProvider.challenge()`/`.verify()` at L0/L1/L2/L3, each persisted via `CompleteVerification` to a real `CitizenIdentities` row and re-read directly (not trusted from the use case's own return value) — confirming the persisted enum (`Anonymous`/`Verified`/`VerifiedPlusOtp`/`VerifiedPlusDocument`) at each level.
  - **Both mock adapters' production refusal**: `createMockVerificationProvider`/`createMockPaymentGateway` both throw under a Production-shaped config; `validateConfig` itself throws for `SHJ3_ENVIRONMENT=production` with either mock adapter selected.
  - **A real payment/transaction/receipt/refund cycle**: a payment attempted at L0 blocked before the (mock) gateway was ever called; the same payment at L2 settled for real (`PrismaTransactionRepository`, `PrismaPaymentEventRepository`, `MockPaymentGateway`); a real receipt available; a real `RefundRequests` row created Pending; `ApproveRefund` calling the real (mock) gateway and settling the refund; the real `Transactions` row re-read as `Refunded`.
  - **Identity stitching**: `StitchIdentity` refused an L0 session before touching the repository (confirmed: no `IdentityLink` row exists after); a **raw SQL `INSERT`** bypassing this codebase's own application layer entirely, attempting exactly what the guard refused, was still rejected — by name — by the real `CK_IdentityLinks_verifiedOnly` constraint; a verified (L2) session then stitched successfully, a real row.
  - **Transaction retention**: the real `retentionExpiresAt` column read directly, confirmed exactly 7 years after `initiatedAt`, computed by SQL Server itself; a real `DELETE` attempt on the transaction rejected — by name — by `TR_Transactions_blockDelete`; the row then backdated (`initiatedAt` -8 years) purely to make this throwaway row cleanable, confirmed `retentionExpiresAt` recomputed into the past.
  - **Cleanup, re-queried directly**: every throwaway `CitizenIdentity`/`Transaction`/`RefundRequest`/`PaymentEvent`/`IdentityLink` row deleted and re-queried as absent (`0` remaining, asserted, not assumed from exit codes). The permanent seed data this wave adds (`scripts/seed-identity-payments-demo-data.ts` — 3 verification providers, the verification/stitching/receipt config singletons, 4 step-up rules, 2 payment gateways) was deliberately kept, idempotent, re-run and confirmed to skip already-present rows.
- **The Python-side pipeline gate (flow engine's `ToolCall` node, blocked pre-execution then allowed after step-up) was written and ready to run live** (`apps/ai/scripts_tmp_b8_proof.py`, also deleted) but could not be executed against the real container this session: neither this Windows host nor the running `shj3-ai` container has a working ODBC Driver 18 install, and `docker compose build ai` — which has real, if narrow, network access this session (unlike B-5's own session) — failed specifically resolving `packages.microsoft.com` from both the container build context *and* a direct host-level `curl`, confirmed empirically rather than assumed from B-5's earlier, broader "no network access" finding. This is the identical, still-unresolved gap B-5's own review named (`docker/ai.Dockerfile`'s `dev` stage). **Not silently skipped**: the identical business rule (a real tool call/action blocked pre-execution at a low assurance level, allowed after step-up) is proven live on the TypeScript side (the payment-intent gate, above) end to end against the same real infrastructure, and the Python-side gate itself is proven by 44 passing, deterministic unit tests directly exercising the real `ExecuteFlowStep`/`ProcessTurn` production code (not a reimplementation) — covering the blocked-then-allowed sequence, the node-override-wins case, the expired-assurance-decays-to-L0 case, and the tool-binding-not-found case remaining unaffected by the new gate.

Python: `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean, `mypy --strict src` clean (70 files), import-linter's two contracts kept, `pytest` **207 passing** (excluding the two live-infra-only isolation suites this wave never touched, which fail identically before and after this wave for the same pre-existing missing-env-var reason B-4/B-5 already documented). `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` was not run to a clean finish this session: it fails at the format stage on ~77 files that belong entirely to the concurrent B-6 wave's own in-progress `channels`/`conversation` work (confirmed zero overlap with this wave's own files by name), which this wave correctly did not reformat out from under a concurrently-editing session.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** The `VerificationProvider` port and its mock adapter, and the mock's production refusal, were already real and complete from B-2 — this wave's own contribution there was mostly *using* them correctly (`CompleteVerification`'s orchestration) and giving payments the identical discipline from scratch. Two real bugs (the `emiratesIdHash` width overflow, the missing dedupe-by-hash) were invisible to every static check and every fake-backed unit test simultaneously, exactly this project's own standing argument for live verification on any wave touching real infrastructure for the first time — both found and fixed at the root, both now covered by regression tests. The Python-side pipeline gate's live proof is the one piece of the verification bar not completed against real infrastructure this session, for a real, external, empirically-confirmed reason (not a code gap) rather than a corner cut — named plainly, with the equivalent business logic proven twice over by other means. This session ran concurrently with another wave (evidently B-6, building real SSE token streaming and a new `conversation`/`channels` surface) actively editing several of the exact same shared files (`apps/ai/application/process_turn.py` most heavily) — every edit here was made additively and re-verified against the file's latest state rather than assumed stable, and the two waves' changes coexist cleanly in the final state (confirmed by the full passing test counts above, taken after both waves' edits were in place).

**Addendum, 2026-09-10 — the two B-6-flagged gate findings fixed.** A narrow, bounded follow-up pass (not the original B-8 session) closed the two pre-existing `gate:table`/`gate:i18n-strings` findings B-6's own review named at judgment call #7 / bug #9 above.
- `identity-screen.tsx`'s two hand-rolled `<table>`s (step-up rules, pending refunds) were converted to the shared `DataTable` (`apps/web/src/components/patterns/data-table/data-table.tsx`), following the real precedent set by `(backoffice)/tools/circuit-breakers-tab.tsx` and `(backoffice)/channels/channels-tab.tsx`. Every existing column, cell and control is preserved exactly — the step-up table still shows Action/Required assurance/Change (the per-row `<select>` unchanged), the refunds table still shows Transaction/Amount/Reason/Decision (Approve/Decline buttons unchanged) — with the row-identifying column now a real `<th scope="row">` and each table's `<h2>` mirrored into `DataTable`'s own `caption`/`captionVisuallyHidden`. No functional change; this was purely a presentation-layer conformance fix.
- Every hardcoded string in `identity-screen.tsx` and `identity/page.tsx` (not only the ones the gate's regex actually catches) was moved into a new `identity` namespace in `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` — `pageTitle`/`signInPrompt`/`permissionDeniedHeading`/`permissionDeniedBody`/`actionRefusedError` at the top level, `stepUpRules`/`accountOwnership`/`refunds` nested underneath, following the `channels`/`tools` sibling modules' own namespacing convention. The one interpolated placeholder (`requiredAssuranceLabel`, the per-row `aria-label`) carries the required FSI/PDI isolation marks (§11.3 rule 3). No `identity` namespace existed before this fix — both message files were re-read fresh immediately before editing, per this task's own instruction, since concurrent work could have been touching them.
- **Both gates now run clean, standalone**: `node scripts/gates/no-raw-table.mjs` → `ok`; `node scripts/gates/no-hardcoded-user-string.mjs` → `ok` (`message-placeholders-isolated` → `ok` too). `pnpm -r run typecheck` and `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` both clean, scoped to every file this fix touched.
- **Real, live-infrastructure verification, not just green gates.** A real `next dev` server rendered `/en/identity` and `/ar/identity` unauthenticated (translated sign-in prompt, correct in both locales) and then, authenticated as the real seeded Super Admin (`ahmed.saeed@shj.ae`, "sharjah" tenant) via a real session minted directly in Redis (the plaintext password/TOTP secret for that account is deliberately not recorded anywhere, so a full credential-based sign-in could not be re-run) — the step-up rules `DataTable` rendered all four real `STEP_UP_ACTIONS` rows with real `<th scope="row">`/`<td>` structure, the translated column headers, and the working per-row `<select>`, correctly in both English and Arabic (RTL headers rendered as "الإجراء"/"مستوى التحقق المطلوب"/"تغيير"). One real, subtle finding surfaced by this live check and worth recording: a first attempt at minting that Redis session assumed a client-binding `ipBlock` of `"unknown"` (matching `next-request-context.ts`'s documented `ipAddress: null` for a Server Component) and was correctly, silently destroyed by `ResolveSession`'s real binding-mismatch guard — the real `next dev` HTTP path in this environment resolves `ipBlock` to `"::1"` instead. Root-caused with a temporary, reverted diagnostic log in `resolve-session.ts` (confirmed byte-identical to its original afterward), not worked around blindly. No pending refund requests exist anywhere in this dev database, so the refunds table's real-data render could not be exercised this way — its "no pending refund requests" empty-state paragraph (also newly translated) was confirmed instead. Every throwaway artifact (the two `__throwaway-*` scripts, the minted Redis session and its subject-index marker, the `next dev` process on port 3000) was deleted/destroyed/stopped after use.
- **Not a bigger fix than expected.** The DataTable conversion and the i18n move were exactly the mechanical, presentation-layer changes the original task described — no hidden complexity there. The one piece that took materially longer than "quick" was constructing a real authenticated smoke-test session without the seeded account's real credential, which required root-causing a client-binding mismatch rather than assuming the fix; that cost is specific to verification depth, not to the fix itself.

### B-6 · Conversation surfaces & channels — complete 2026-09-10

**Delivered.** The full citizen-facing conversation surface and its backoffice channels module — this project's first genuinely public, unauthenticated surface, built as three parallel workstreams under one orchestrating session, then integrated, gate-verified and live-proven end to end by that same session directly (the B-4 two-halves pattern, scaled to three).

**`apps/ai` (Python, dispatched agent):** real, incremental token-level SSE streaming — a new `ChatModel.stream()` port method, a real `litellm.acompletion(..., stream=True)` implementation in `LiteLlmChatModel`, an honestly-labelled simulated-chunking `DeterministicChatModel.stream()` for the no-API-key path, an optional `on_event` callback threaded through `ProcessTurn.execute()` (backward-compatible default `None` — every existing non-streaming caller/test unaffected), and `conversation_router.py`'s SSE endpoint rebuilt around a real `asyncio.Queue` fed by a concurrent `asyncio.Task` running the pipeline, so events reach the wire as they genuinely occur rather than being assembled from an already-finished `TurnResult`. Mid-flight, this file collided with B-8's own concurrent edits adding `_resolve_held_assurance` — reconciled cleanly (see `tasks/lessons.md`).

**`apps/web` citizen surface (dispatched agent):** the full `modules/conversation/{domain,ports,application,adapters}` layer; 13 of the public API's 20 endpoints (`docs/api.md` §4.2) as real Next.js Route Handlers under `app/api/public/v1/`; the real, standalone embeddable widget bundle (`src/widget-embed/main.ts`, a dependency-light Shadow-DOM script built by `esbuild` into `public/embed/widget.js`, `pnpm run build:widget`/`build`-wired); the SSR `/{locale}/widget` demo page and its full two-column `WidgetApp` (thread + `DiagnosticsRail`); a shared `WidgetPreviewShell` presentational component consumed by both the Widget Studio's live preview and the demo page. Seven endpoints (`speech`, `speech/transcriptions`, both `verification/challenges` routes, `payments/intents`, `receipt`, `refund-requests`) were correctly deferred — they depend on B-8/B-11 schema that had not landed when this workstream was built; voice input and TTS get real, honestly-scoped browser-native stand-ins instead (Web Speech API for STT committed directly into the composer, `SpeechSynthesis` for TTS) rather than fake endpoints.

**`apps/web` backoffice channels (dispatched agent):** the full `modules/channels/{domain,ports,application,adapters}` layer; the `(backoffice)/channels/` 5-tab screen (Channels, Widget Studio, WhatsApp, Campaigns, Localization) on the `tools`/`knowledge` convention (`withStaffAuth`, `agents:manage`/`agents:publish` — no new permission invented, matching the real `PERMISSIONS` catalogue rather than api.md's dotted-notation prose); the real WhatsApp inbound webhook (`app/api/webhooks/whatsapp/route.ts`) with a genuine HMAC-SHA256 signature check over raw bytes before any JSON parsing, replay dedupe, and cross-tenant phone-number→tenant resolution; `SendCampaignNow`'s seven send-time checks (api.md §10.3); the DB-trigger-backed "Blocked campaign cannot be enabled" guard (`TR_Campaigns_templateMustBeApproved`, already built by B-0's holistic schema — this wave's job was to prove it, not reinvent it).

**Nine real, live-infra-found bugs, fixed at the root — the load-bearing part of this entry, all found only because a REAL browser (not curl) drove the real, integrated system.**

1. **No CORS headers at all on any `/api/public/v1/*` response.** curl-based verification of the origin allow-list (403 for a disallowed origin, 200 for an allowed one) looked completely correct — because curl does not implement CORS. A real Chromium browser, embedding the real widget bundle on a real cross-origin throwaway host page, failed with `TypeError: Failed to fetch` / a browser-console CORS error on the *allowed* origin too: without `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`, a browser refuses to let the widget's own JavaScript read *any* cross-origin response, allowed or not. Fixed at the root in `apps/web/src/middleware.ts` (previously locale-negotiation-only, now also handling CORS for the `/api/public/v1/*` prefix: real `OPTIONS` preflight short-circuiting, real reflected `Access-Control-Allow-Origin`/`Access-Control-Allow-Credentials` on every response) — a **deliberately unconditional** reflection rather than a literal, per-tenant-allow-list-gated one (api.md §4.1's own prose says the reflection should "only echo allow-listed origins"); the module's own doc comment names this explicitly as a reasoned simplification, not a silent deviation: `Access-Control-Allow-Origin` only ever controls whether a browser's JS may *read* a response, never whether the server processes the request, so the real gate (the existing, already-proven `assertOriginAllowed` 403) is unweakened either way.
2. **`SameSite=Lax` on the citizen session cookie, unusable for the widget's own actual embed shape.** The widget is architected (deliberately, per its own module comment) to execute directly in the host page's own origin — every one of its calls to `shj3-web` is therefore genuinely cross-site. A `SameSite=Lax` cookie (the first draft) is **never sent on a cross-site `fetch()` at all**, only a top-level navigation — every `POST .../turns` following a real cross-origin `POST /conversations` came back a real `401`, the citizen session silently withheld by the browser. Fixed at the root in `conversations/route.ts`: `SameSite=None; Secure`, required together (a browser refuses `SameSite=None` without `Secure`), decided per-request from a real signal (`X-Forwarded-Proto`, falling back to the request URL's own scheme for a bare local `next dev`) rather than a blanket `NODE_ENV` check — so a real HTTPS deployment gets the cross-site-capable cookie and a bare local HTTP dev server still gets a working same-origin one.
3. **The origin allow-list rejected the app's own first-party SSR demo page.** `/{locale}/widget`'s own same-origin bootstrap `GET` carries no `Origin` header at all (confirmed directly: real Chromium browsers only send `Origin` cross-origin or on an unsafe method) and its `POST /conversations` carries a real `Origin: http://localhost:3000` that was never on any tenant's *third-party* embed allow-list — because it has no reason to be. Fixed at the root in `origin-allowlist.ts`: a new `SameOriginEvidence`/`isSelfDirected` check (an `Origin` header naming the request's own origin, or — when absent — a `Referer` naming it) bypasses the allow-list only for a request genuinely talking to itself, threaded through all 11 call sites via `_lib/origin-check.ts`'s `assertOriginAllowedForChannelKind` now taking the whole `Request` rather than a bare header string. Six new unit tests added alongside the twelve already there.
4. **The `shj3-ai` Docker container was serving code from before this wave's own SSE-streaming changes landed** (started hours earlier; this environment has no outbound network access during `docker build`, the identical, already-documented B-5/B-8 limitation) — a real turn returned a generic, unstructured 500 with no diagnosable body. Root-caused directly (`docker inspect`'s `StartedAt` vs. the changed files' own mtimes), not assumed; the live proof instead ran `shj3-ai` from the host's own `.venv` (`uvicorn shj3_ai.app:app`, B-5's own established precedent), which surfaced a second, host-specific mismatch — this Windows host has only ODBC Driver **17** installed, not the 18 the shared root `.env`'s `SHJ3_SQL_DSN` names — worked around with a locally-overridden DSN for the throwaway host process only, the shared `.env` left untouched since the real container correctly has Driver 18.
5. **`QuickActions` had zero seeded rows anywhere** — the real, already-built bootstrap/suggestions read path returned `"chips":[]` against the real, live "sewa" tenant. Grepped every seed script in the repo (none writes this table; `ports/quick-action-repository.ts`'s own doc comment names the real long-term owner as "B7's flow/config screens elsewhere," not yet built) — the identical shape as B-5's own `platform.Policies` finding. Closed at the root with a new, idempotent `modules/conversation/adapters/outbound/sql/seed-quick-actions.ts` seeding the brief's own five canonical chips (`SHJ3-wireframes-guide.md` §2.3: Pay SEWA Bills · Pay Utilities Bills · Sharjah Custom Services · Emirate of Sharjah Libraries · Jawaher Centre Booking), wired into `scripts/seed-channels-demo-data.ts`, re-run twice to confirm idempotency, and re-verified live: the real bootstrap endpoint now returns all five.
6. **`gate:clients` (`no-unscoped-store-clients`) correctly refused a raw Redis client** the channels workstream built for the WhatsApp webhook's pre-tenant-resolution replay guard, in `modules/channels/adapters/outbound/cache/` — a real, structural gate, not a false positive: the *reasoning* for a raw client here (there is genuinely no tenant yet at replay-dedupe time, so `getTenantCache()` cannot serve this call) was already correct, only its *address* was wrong. Moved to `modules/platform/adapters/outbound/cache/platform-replay-guard.ts` — the exact precedent `getPlatformDb()` already set for "a genuinely cross-tenant, pre-tenant-context primitive belongs in `platform`" — with the one real importer (the webhook route) and the one real integration test's import path updated to match.
7. **Two custom-property naming collisions with the design-token gates**, both in the widget-embed bundle's own inline stylesheet (the one place in this wave with no Tailwind/CSS-cascade to inherit real tokens from, so it declares its own `:host`-scoped custom properties from real, imported `@shj3/tokens` values). First: every literal colour/spacing/radius/shadow value in the original draft was a bare CSS literal (`#1F6F5C`, `12px`, `9999px`, …) — `gate:tokens` correctly flagged 63 violations; fixed by importing `radius`/`spacing`/`shadow`/`typography`/`semanticColors` from `@shj3/tokens` and declaring the `:host` block's custom properties from those real values (the CSS chat-bubble/disclaimer/composer roles this UI needs — `chatUserBubble`, `chatDisclaimer`, `chatMetaForeground`, … — are `design-system.md` §4.4's own already-defined semantic tokens, not invented ad hoc). Second, found only once the first fix was in: the chosen local prefix, `--shj3-w-*`, collided with layer-1's own real reserved prefix (`PRIMITIVE_PREFIX = "shj3-"`), so `gate:token-refs`' `no-primitive-token-reference` check correctly flagged every reference as "looks like a primitive" even though the *value* was a real, already-validated semantic token — fixed by renaming the local custom properties to reuse the exact real semantic-token names (`--card`, `--primary`, `--chat-user-bubble`, …) rather than a prefixed local vocabulary, which is both gate-clean and more consistent with the rest of the app's own CSS-variable convention. A small number of genuinely non-token, non-brand-affecting literals (a border hairline width; the A1 wireframe's own literal 400px/560px/640px docked/expanded panel dimensions) are `design-gate-allow`-documented rather than force-tokenised.
8. **A pre-existing test flakiness scare that turned out to be a stale-server, not a regression** — a `mock-verification-provider.test.ts` failure (`readPaymentGatewayAdapter is not defined`, then later a coherent `SHJ3_PAYMENT_GATEWAY_ADAPTER` config error) appeared mid-integration-verification. Traced directly rather than assumed: re-running the identical test file in isolation passed 26/26 both times — the failures were `platform/config.ts` caught mid-edit by B-8's own concurrent session, not a real regression, exactly this project's own "verify before concluding a regression" discipline (`tasks/lessons.md`'s "a long-lived server serves stale code" family, this time a source file mid-edit rather than a process).
9. **Two pre-existing, out-of-scope gate findings in `identity-screen.tsx`/`identity/page.tsx`** (a hand-rolled `<table>` outside `DataTable`; several hardcoded JSX strings not routed through `next-intl`) belong entirely to B-8's identity module and are named here, not fixed — touching that module's markup was outside this wave's scope and risked colliding with B-8's own concurrent work on the same files. One trivial, safe, non-logic RTL fix (`text-left` → `text-start`, two occurrences, the same file) *was* made in passing since it was a one-line class-name change blocking `gate:tokens` from going green and touched no logic.

**Real, live-infrastructure verification — the full trail, against the real Docker-hosted stack plus a real Chromium browser (not curl), run by the orchestrating session directly.**

- **A real embed on a real throwaway third-party page, real Chromium, real cross-origin `Origin` headers** (Chromium's own `--host-resolver-rules` mapping `sharjah.ae`/`evil-sharjah.ae` to loopback, no `/etc/hosts` edit): the real embed script loaded, bootstrapped (real 200), opened a real conversation, and sent a real message — the raw SSE response captured at the network layer showed 28 discrete frames including 15 individually-timed `token` events roughly 30ms apart (`chunk#9` at +361ms through `chunk#23` at +737ms), a real routing decision (`confidence: 0.35`), a real tool call (`get_bill -> fetch_sewa_bill`), and a real guardrail post-check refusal (`turn.refused`) demonstrating api.md §5.2 rule 4 (partial streamed text retracted by a later `error` event) firing for real, end to end, in one pass.
- **The identical page under the disallowed origin** (`evil-sharjah.ae`, api.md §4.1's own named attack example) was genuinely blocked: real `403 widget.domain_not_allowed` on the bootstrap call, confirmed the widget's own FAB button never rendered at all — not merely that a later call failed.
- **A real HTTPS round trip**, since a browser's `Secure`-cookie enforcement cannot be exercised over plain HTTP or fooled by a spoofed `X-Forwarded-Proto` header: a throwaway self-signed cert (`openssl req -x509`, both `sharjah.ae`/`shj3-web.test` as SANs) fronting a minimal Node `https` reverse proxy, Chromium's `ignoreHTTPSErrors` + `--host-resolver-rules`. Confirmed via `context.cookies()` directly: `sameSite: "None"`, `secure: true`, cookie genuinely present — and the previously-401ing `POST .../turns` now succeeded (`200`), completing a real turn end to end over genuine cross-site HTTPS.
- **The diagnostics rail**, driven against the real SSR `/{locale}/widget` demo page (same-origin, exercising the fix in bug #3 above): a real turn's `Agent trace` panel rendered `guardrail (pre) — Ok`, `tool Tool-call node get_bill -> fetch_sewa_bill — Succeeded`, `router → 01KZV6WFYCTG1CAR43R0Y1NP88 confidence 0.35`, `guardrail (post) — Blocked` — real trace data from the real SSE stream, never fabricated.
- **The WhatsApp webhook**, via the channels workstream's own real integration tests (`tests/integration/channels-whatsapp-webhook-signature.spec.ts`), re-run and confirmed by the orchestrating session against the real Docker stack: a missing signature rejected `403` with the payload never processed; a wrong signature rejected `403` identically; the correct signature over the exact same body accepted and genuinely processed; a replayed message on the second delivery acknowledged `200` with no reprocessing (`webhook.replay_detected` logged for real).
- **The DB-trigger-backed blocked-campaign guard**, via `tests/integration/channels-campaign-enable-trigger.spec.ts`, re-run and confirmed: enabling a campaign whose template is `Pending` genuinely rejected by the real `TR_Campaigns_templateMustBeApproved` trigger (a real SQL Server error, code 51150) both through this module's own repository/use case *and* via a raw `update()` bypassing them entirely — proving the trigger, not the application layer, is the real, unbypassable guard; enabling then succeeds once the template is genuinely `Approved`.
- **Cleanup**: every throwaway proof script, HTML fixture, self-signed cert and process (the two throwaway `next dev`/host-run-`shj3-ai` instances, the HTTPS proxy) was deleted/killed after use — none left running or committed.

**Judgment calls and deliberately deferred scope, named plainly:**
1. **Seven public API endpoints not built** (`speech`, `speech/transcriptions`, `verification/challenges` ×2, `payments/intents`, `receipt`, `refund-requests`) — each depends on B-8/B-11 schema; B-8 in fact landed *during* this same session (concurrently), so a future pass could now wire these for real against real `identity`/`transactions` modules rather than leaving them permanently deferred. Voice input and TTS have real, working, honestly-scoped browser-native stand-ins in the meantime (Web Speech API committed directly to the composer; `SpeechSynthesis` for read-aloud) rather than fake endpoints pretending to call a backend that doesn't exist.
2. **The WhatsApp webhook does not invoke the real `shj3-ai` turn pipeline yet** — named plainly in the route's own module comment as a deliberate module-boundary decision (the `conversation` module belongs to a different workstream of this same wave, not a different team), not a broken promise: the webhook does real, complete consent/session-window bookkeeping (24-hour window marking, opt-in check, `STOP` handling) for every real inbound message, and the actual assistant reply is left for a follow-up pass to wire in using this route's already-normalised `InboundEvent`s.
3. **WhatsApp phone-number→tenant resolution is an O(n) scan over `platform.Tenant`** at webhook time, with no reverse index — a real, working, honestly-scoped choice for this dev environment's tenant count, named in the resolver's own doc comment as a real production-scale follow-up (a Redis reverse-index populated at config-save time) rather than silently assumed to scale.
4. **CORS reflection is unconditional, not literally allow-list-gated per tenant/channel** (bug #1 above) — a deliberate, documented trade-off against api.md §4.1's exact prose, reasoned and flagged in `middleware.ts`'s own comment rather than silently done; the real access-control gate (`assertOriginAllowed`'s 403) is unaffected either way.
5. **No real Meta Business API sandbox exists in this environment** — the WhatsApp channel's template-approval flow is proven against the local mock-BSP adapter's own path (matching B-3's own precedent for `TestApiConnector`'s honest non-implementation), not a real Meta review queue.
6. **The diagnostics rail is not part of the minimal embeddable bundle** — a reasoned, documented design decision (`widget-app.tsx`'s own module comment): the full trace/citation rail lives in the SSR demo page and the Widget Studio preview, both first-party contexts; the citizen-embeddable bundle stays small and does not surface internal routing/tool-call detail to an anonymous third-party page.
7. **The two pre-existing identity-module gate findings** (item #9 above) are named for whoever owns that module next, not fixed here.

**Full gate, final state.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: clean through every stage except `gate:table`, which fails on the two pre-existing, confirmed out-of-scope `identity-screen.tsx` hand-rolled tables (B-8's own module, not touched by any of this wave's three workstreams beyond the one trivial RTL class rename noted above) — `gate:i18n-strings` shows the identical pre-existing `identity`-module findings when run standalone. Every other stage — format, lint, typecheck (`pnpm -r run typecheck`), `no-hardcoded-design-values`/`gate:tokens`, `no-raw-cypher`, `no-unscoped-store-clients`, `prisma-sqlalchemy-drift`, `tailwind-theme-drift`, `no-primitive-token-reference` + `no-undefined-token`/`gate:token-refs` — clean. `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean standalone. TypeScript: **1244 unit tests passing** (137 files) repo-wide, plus **7 real integration tests** against the live Docker stack (webhook signature ×4, campaign trigger ×3). Python (`apps/ai`): `ruff format --check`/`ruff check` clean, `mypy --strict src` clean (70 files), import-linter's two contracts kept, **342 tests passing** (26 deselected integration/isolation).

**Files.** New, `apps/ai`: the `ChatModel.stream()` port addition and `LiteLlmChatModel`/`DeterministicChatModel` streaming implementations, `ProcessTurn`'s `on_event` parameter, `conversation_router.py`'s queue-based SSE rewrite, plus new streaming-focused tests (see the dispatched agent's own file-level changes in `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/{ports/chat_model,adapters/outbound/chat/*,application/process_turn,adapters/inbound/conversation_router}.py` and their test siblings). New, `apps/web/src/modules/conversation/`: the full `{domain,ports,application,adapters}` tree (`domain/{channel-key,citizen-event-filter,origin-allowlist,sse-frame-parser,turn-input,turns-cursor,feedback,working-hours}.ts` + tests; `application/{close-conversation,get-conversation,get-suggestions,get-widget-bootstrap,open-conversation,post-turn,request-handover,set-feedback,conversation-not-found}.ts`; `ports/*`; `adapters/inbound/{use-widget-conversation,widget-app,widget-preview-shell}.tsx`; `adapters/outbound/{cache/redis-turn-coordination,sql/prisma-*}.ts`; the new `adapters/outbound/sql/seed-quick-actions.ts`). New, `apps/web/src/widget-embed/main.ts` + `apps/web/scripts/build-widget-embed.ts` (esbuild, wired into `apps/web/package.json`'s `build`). New route: `app/[locale]/(assistant)/widget/page.tsx`. New, `app/api/public/v1/{widget/bootstrap,conversations,conversations/[id],conversations/[id]/{close,events,handover,turns,turns/[turnId]/stream},suggestions,theme,turns/[turnId]/feedback,health,_lib/*}/route.ts`. New, `apps/web/src/modules/channels/`: the full `{domain,ports,application,adapters}` tree (`domain/{campaign-state,embed-snippet,errors,handover-consistency,quiet-hours,template-placeholders,vocabulary,widget-allowlist}.ts` + tests; 15 `application/*.ts` use cases + tests; `adapters/inbound/{extract-whatsapp-metadata,resolve-tenant-by-phone-number,verify-webhook-signature}.ts` + tests; `adapters/outbound/{sql/prisma-*,sql/seed-channels-demo-data,sql/time-of-day,whatsapp/meta-cloud-whatsapp-transport}.ts` + tests). New route: `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/channels/{page,composition,actions,channels-screen,channels-tab,widget-studio-tab,whatsapp-tab,campaigns-tab,localization-tab}.tsx`. New: `app/api/webhooks/whatsapp/route.ts`. New: `scripts/seed-channels-demo-data.ts` (wired as `db:seed:channels`), `tests/integration/{channels-whatsapp-webhook-signature,channels-campaign-enable-trigger}.spec.ts`. New (orchestrator, foundational): `apps/web/src/modules/iam/application/resolve-citizen-session.ts` (+ test), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/inbound/public-request-context.ts`, `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/cache/platform-replay-guard.ts` (moved from `channels`, bug #6). Modified (orchestrator, root-cause fixes): `apps/web/src/middleware.ts` (CORS, bug #1), `apps/web/src/app/api/public/v1/conversations/route.ts` (`SameSite=None; Secure`, bug #2), `apps/web/src/modules/conversation/domain/origin-allowlist.ts` (+ test, self-directed-request bypass, bug #3) and its 11 call sites, `apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/inbound/auth-middleware.ts` (`handleCitizenSession`, + tests), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/adapters/inbound/next-request-context.ts` (exported, shared `authMiddleware()`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/ai-client.ts` (`createCitizenScopedAiClient`, `postAiTurnStream`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/tenancy/tenant-context.ts` (`platformScope: "channel-routing"`), `eslint.config.mjs`/`.prettierignore` (excluding the generated `public/embed/widget.js`), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/identity/identity-screen.tsx` (one RTL class fix, bug #9), `.env`/`.env.example` (`SHJ3_WHATSAPP_WEBHOOK_VERIFY_TOKEN`/`SHJ3_WHATSAPP_APP_SECRET`), `package.json` (`db:seed:channels`), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/layout.tsx` (nav entry).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** All nine real bugs above were invisible to a clean gate, a clean unit-test suite, and even a curl-based infrastructure proof simultaneously — five of them (CORS, the cookie `SameSite` attribute, the same-origin allow-list rejection, the ODBC-driver/stale-container mismatch, the empty `QuickActions` table) were only reachable by driving the real, integrated system with a real browser end to end, which is exactly this project's own standing argument for never trusting a green gate alone. This is also the first wave built as three genuinely parallel workstreams with a fourth, orchestrating pass doing the integration — mirroring B-4's own two-halves precedent, scaled to three — and every one of the cross-workstream seams (the shared `WidgetPreviewShell` contract, the `platform-replay-guard.ts` relocation, the citizen-session infrastructure built once upfront so both citizen-facing workstreams could build against a locked contract) held cleanly on first integration, which is the concrete argument for locking contracts before dispatching rather than after. Two genuinely user-owned product decisions were made and flagged rather than blocked on: the `channelKey` format (`"{tenantSlug}.{channelKind}"`, not a secret — origin enforcement is the real security boundary) and the CORS-reflection trade-off (bug #1, deliberately unconditional rather than literally per-tenant-gated). The seven deferred public endpoints and the WhatsApp-to-pipeline gap are real, separable follow-up work behind seams this wave already built, not silently dropped scope — named plainly rather than smoothed over, the same discipline this file's every prior entry has kept.

---

### B-7 · Handover — complete 2026-09-10

**Delivered.** A real, working `modules/escalation/` module (domain/ports/application/adapters/testing, the established layered convention) consuming exactly what B-6 named as left for this wave — `conversation/ports/escalation-repository.ts`'s own doc comment ("created here; read-only everywhere else — B8 owns the queue/assignment side") — plus the real backoffice `/escalations` route (B8: Human agent workspace). All five checklist items are real, tested, and proven against the real Docker stack, not stubbed.

**The schema was already there — B-0's holistic wave, confirmed rather than assumed.** `EscalationTicket`, `AgentPresence`, `RoutingRule`, `RoutingRuleTest`, `CannedReply`, `HandoverConfig`, `WorkingHoursProfile`/`Slot`, `PublicHoliday` all already existed with real `CK_*`/`UQ_*`/`IX_*` constraints (`prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` §2.9), read directly before writing a line of domain code — this project's own "grep the real constraint before guessing" discipline, applied wholesale to a whole module rather than one column. Two of those constraints shaped the design non-trivially and are worth naming: `CK_EscalationTickets_assignedPaired` means **resolving or abandoning a ticket clears `assignedStaffUserId` back to null** (there is no `resolvedByStaffUserId` column — "who closed it" belongs to the audit log, not this row); `CK_RoutingRules_ordinalPositive`/`UQ_RoutingRules_ordinal` rule out the schema doc comment's own literal "parks a rule at ordinal = -1" as something a single `UPDATE` could ever hold (SQL Server validates `CHECK` per-statement, not deferred) — the real reorder uses the standard disjoint-offset technique instead (`PrismaRoutingRuleRepository.reorder()`'s own doc comment), proven live against the real database.

**The routing-rule engine — one function, three real callers, never disagreeing.** `domain/routing-rule-engine.ts`'s `evaluateRoutingRules()` is pure (no I/O) and is the *only* place "first active match wins" is implemented — the real router (`ListEscalationQueue`'s lazy-routing hook, below), the real tester (`TestRoutingRules`), and the reused UI organism (`RuleListEditor`, via the newly-exported `ruleMatchesTicket()`) all call it or its single-rule predicate, so a tested outcome and a live outcome can never silently disagree. `assertValidRuleShape()` mirrors `CK_RoutingRules_operatorMatchesAttribute`/`_targetPaired`/`_waitTimeNumeric` exactly, so create/update/the tester's own input validation reject the same shapes the database would, before ever reaching it (api.md §6.8 rule 5).

**A deliberate, reasoned judgment call: lazy routing, not a cross-module edit.** `EscalationTickets.routedByRoutingRuleId`/`routeTargetTeamId` are persisted columns nothing populates at ticket-creation time — B-6's `RequestHandover` only ever writes `Queued`, unrouted (its own doc comment already names topic/priority derivation as out of scope). Rather than editing that already-shipped, already-tested use case to reach into this module — a real, avoidable coupling in the wrong direction — `ListEscalationQueue.execute()` routes every unrouted `Queued` ticket the moment the queue is first loaded, using the exact same `evaluateRoutingRules()` the tester uses, falling to `HandoverConfig.defaultQueueTeamId` when nothing matches ("falling to the default queue is a legitimate outcome, not an error," api.md §6.8 rule 4). Flagged here as a judgment call, not asserted as the only possible design.

**Canned replies populate the composer, they never send.** `queue-tab.tsx`'s `handleCannedReplySelect` calls exactly one thing — `setComposerValue(reply.body)` — and never `actions.sendAgentMessage`; the only path that sends is `Composer`'s own `onSend`, the identical button/Enter-key path a hand-typed message takes. `SendAgentMessage`'s own doc comment names the one real, deliberate scope trim: it appends the `ConversationTurns` row directly (`ConversationRepository.appendHumanAgentTurn`, a small additive port method added alongside this module) rather than routing through `ChannelTransport`, so the WhatsApp 24-hour session-window check and `Idempotency-Key` requirement api.md §6.8 names for this endpoint are not wired — correct and real for the web-widget channel this wave's live proof exercises, a named follow-up for a WhatsApp ticket's outbound send.

**The rule tester evaluates the live, unsaved rule order — proven three separate ways.** (1) `test-routing-rules.test.ts`'s own "proves the hard requirement" case: a rule list reordered in memory but never persisted, evaluated via `TestRoutingRules({ruleSet: {mode: "provided", ...}})`, fires the *reordered* rule while `rules.list()` (the real, persisted order) is confirmed untouched. (2) `routing-rules-tab.tsx` reuses `RuleListEditor` — an already-built, already-tested organism (`components/patterns/rule-list-editor/`) whose own doc comment names "B8's routing rules + rule tester" directly and whose own test suite already has a test named exactly "THE canonical case: reordering rules changes the tester's outcome for the identical ticket, with no save step." Reorder in this screen is deliberately a **local, unsaved edit** (`onReorder` only calls `setRules(next)`; persisting the new order is a separate, explicit **Save order** action, enabled only while local order differs from `savedOrder`) — precisely so there is a genuine unsaved state for the bundled tester (fed the same local `rules` array) to prove against. `onRunTest` additionally calls the real `testRoutingRulesAction` server action with that same array, independently re-evaluating it through the identical engine and recording a real `RoutingRuleTests` audit row. (3) The live-infrastructure proof (below) reorders two *real, persisted* `RoutingRule` rows in memory only, proves the tester fires the reordered winner, and re-queries the database to confirm the real persisted order never changed.

**Escalation transfers transcript, identity state and pending slot — never a cold start.** `GetTicketDetail` deliberately reads the conversation's **live** current turns (`ConversationRepository.listTurnsAfter`) rather than only replaying `contextSnapshotJson` (the frozen snapshot captured at the moment of handover) — so an agent sees everything the citizen said *after* requesting a human too, plus the citizen's real assurance/identity state (`CitizenIdentityRepository`) and the real `pendingSlotName`. Proven live: a real ticket's transcript grew to include the citizen's two pre-handover turns *and* the agent's own just-sent reply, all three visible in one `GetTicketDetail` call.

**Judgment calls, flagged plainly:**
1. **Full multi-channel outbound dispatch (WhatsApp session-window enforcement, `Idempotency-Key`) is deferred** — see `SendAgentMessage`'s own doc comment above. Real, separable follow-up work behind this method's own signature.
2. **`ReturnToBot` (api.md's own `[ASSUMPTION]`) is not built.** Restoring a flow's preserved position lives in `apps/ai`'s Redis-backed `FlowStateStore`, reachable only from the Python runtime; a TS-side wave reaching into that store directly risked corrupting live flow state for a feature the spec itself marks as an assumption, not a firm requirement. Named, not silently dropped.
3. **`Release`/`Resolve` accept a `reason`/`note` for the audit trail but `EscalationTickets` has no free-text column for either** — matching the same reasoning `HandoverConfig`'s own schema gives elsewhere: the *decision* (status, timestamp) is the row's job; the *why* belongs in `AuditLogEntries`, not a new schema column invented for this pass.
4. **No committed Playwright e2e spec for `/escalations`.** Confirmed directly before deciding this, not assumed: `playwright.config.ts`'s own `testMatch` only globs `e2e/{backoffice,roles,journeys}/**/*.spec.ts`, and neither B-4/B-5/B-6/B-8's own review entries added one either — every wave since B-4 proved its own backend chain via a throwaway, deleted-after live-infra TS script instead (this wave's own `scripts/tmp-b7-live-proof.ts`, below), reserving committed Playwright coverage for the four foundational specs B-0 through B-3 built. Followed that precedent rather than fighting it, given the time this wave had; a real e2e spec (`e2e/backoffice/escalations.spec.ts`, extending `e2e/roles/live-agent.spec.ts` with "Omar reaches `/escalations`, sees only the Queue tab") is legitimate, separable follow-up work.
