5. **`customerContextSummary` (a real, nullable `EscalationTickets` column) is never populated or read** — B-6 never wrote it either. `GetTicketDetail` computes the customer-identity line live from `CitizenIdentityRepository` instead, which is more correct for "never a cold start" than a frozen summary column would be; the column itself is a named, harmless gap for whoever next wants a cheaper read path.

**A real, found-and-fixed gotcha, the same family this project has hit four times before.** `AgentPresence.staffUserId`/`EscalationTickets.assignedStaffUserId` are both `@db.Char(26)` — the live-proof script's first hand-typed staff-id fixture (`"live_proof_agent_a"`, 19 characters) got silently space-padded at rest, and the very next equality-scoped claim assertion failed in a confusing way. Fixed by using real `newUlid()`-generated ids for every throwaway fixture (the script) and for the seed script's own `createdByStaffUserId`/`updatedByStaffUserId` values (`seed-escalation-demo-data.ts`, which had the identical latent bug before anything ever queried by that column) — exactly `tasks/lessons.md`'s own prescribed fix for this exact class of mistake, hit a fifth time.

**A second, smaller finding: a vitest project's include glob is narrower than it looks.** A first-draft `queue-tab.test.tsx` (RTL, proving the canned-reply-into-composer distinction) was written and passing locally under a plain `vitest run` invocation — but `vitest.config.ts`'s real `unit` project only globs `apps/web/src/**/*.test.ts` (no `.tsx`) and the `components` project only globs `apps/web/src/components/**/*.test.tsx` — neither pattern matches a route-level `.test.tsx` under `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/...`, and `playwright.config.ts` doesn't reach that path either. The file would have shipped as dead, never-discovered test code — passing 0 times, forever, silently — a worse outcome than not writing it. Deleted once found; the same distinction this wave's live-infra script proves is instead documented via the reused, already-tested `RuleListEditor` organism's own generic proof plus this wave's own real unit tests, and named as a deferred Playwright gap above (item 4).

**Real, live-infrastructure verification.** A real, throwaway TypeScript script (`scripts/tmp-b7-live-proof.ts`, deleted after this run) against the real Docker-hosted SQL Server, real "sewa" tenant schema — every assertion below passed on a clean run:
- A real `Conversation` + 2 real `ConversationTurns`, a real `RequestHandover` producing a real, unrouted `EscalationTicket`.
- **Real lazy routing**: `ListEscalationQueue` routed the ticket to the real "SEWA Billing" team (rule 1, Topic=Billing) over "Senior Agents" (rule 2, Priority=High) — the exact wireframe `[rule]`, against real seeded `RoutingRule` rows (`scripts/seed-escalation-demo-data.ts`, new, idempotent, wired as `db:seed:escalations`: the four wireframe rules plus two additional real teams — "Senior Agents", "WhatsApp-Trained Agents" — since IAM's own seed gives "sewa" only one team; nine canned replies, 3 billing/3 customs/3 library).
- **The rule tester, both modes, against real persisted rows**: `mode: "persisted"` fired SEWA Billing and reported `differsFromSaved: false`; the identical two real rules reordered *only in memory* and re-tested via `mode: "provided"` fired Senior Agents instead and reported `differsFromSaved: true` — and the real, persisted `RoutingRules` order was re-queried afterward and confirmed unchanged.
- **A real claim** (Assigned, `assignedStaffUserId` set) and **a real second-claimant race**, rejected by name (`TicketAlreadyClaimedError`/`handover.ticket_already_claimed`) via the conditional `UPDATE ... WHERE status = 'Queued'`.
- **A real agent message**: a genuine `ConversationTurns` row (`role: "HumanAgent"`), confirmed by a raw re-query; `GetTicketDetail`'s live transcript showed all three turns (citizen, assistant, agent) in order; the ticket flipped `Assigned` → `Active`; three real, topic-specific (Billing) canned replies attached.
- **A real resolve**: `status = Resolved`, `assignedStaffUserId` cleared — `CK_EscalationTickets_assignedPaired`, confirmed by a raw re-query, not assumed.
- **A real offline-drain**: a second ticket claimed by the same agent, presence flipped to `Offline`, the held ticket requeued (not abandoned) with `assignedStaffUserId` cleared, `activeTicketCount` at 0 (`CK_AgentPresence_offlineHasNoTickets`), and — the specific api.md §6.8 guarantee — **`queuedAt` and `priority` both untouched by the requeue**, confirmed against the value captured before the drain.
- **Cleanup**: every throwaway `ConversationTurn`/`EscalationTicket`/`Conversation`/`AgentPresence`/`RoutingRuleTest` row deleted and re-queried as absent (0 remaining, asserted). The permanent seed data (`RoutingRule`/`CannedReply`/`Team` rows) was deliberately kept.

**Full gate, final state.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: clean through every stage except the format check, which fails only on the one pre-existing, confirmed out-of-scope `identity-screen.tsx` (B-8's own module, not touched by this wave at all) — the identical "leave a concurrently-owned file's formatting alone" call B-6's own review already made for the same file. Run individually: `pnpm --filter @shj3/web run typecheck` and repo-wide `pnpm -r run typecheck` both clean; `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean; all eight custom design/architecture gates (`gate:tokens`, `gate:cypher`, `gate:clients`, `gate:drift`, `gate:tailwind-theme`, `gate:token-refs`, `gate:table`, `gate:i18n-strings`) clean. `pnpm exec vitest run --project unit`: **1290 tests passing** (up from B-6's 1244; 46 new, all real, all exercising the production code directly — no fakes standing in for the assertions themselves). `pnpm exec vitest run --project components`: **556 tests passing**, confirming the reused `RuleListEditor`/`Composer`/`DataTable`/`ChatThread` organisms are unaffected.

**Files.** New, `apps/web/src/modules/escalation/`: `domain/{escalation-reason,ticket-lifecycle,presence,routing-rule-engine,routing-rule-hash}.ts` (+ tests); `ports/{ticket-repository,agent-presence-repository,routing-rule-repository,routing-rule-test-repository,canned-reply-repository,handover-routing-config-repository,team-repository}.ts`; `application/{get-agent-presence,set-agent-presence,list-escalation-queue,get-ticket-detail,claim-ticket,release-ticket,resolve-ticket,send-agent-message,list-canned-replies,list-routing-rules,create-routing-rule,update-routing-rule,delete-routing-rule,set-routing-rule-enabled,reorder-routing-rules,test-routing-rules}.ts` (+ tests for the ones with real branching logic); `adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-*.ts` (7 real adapters); `testing/fakes.ts` (including a new, full-interface `FakeConversationRepository` — this module is the first outside consumer of that port, and no shared fake for it existed yet anywhere in the codebase, confirmed by grep). New backoffice route: `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/escalations/{page,composition,actions,escalations-screen,queue-tab,routing-rules-tab}.tsx`. New: `scripts/seed-escalation-demo-data.ts` (wired as `db:seed:escalations`). Modified (small, additive): `apps/web/src/modules/conversation/ports/conversation-repository.ts` + `adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-conversation-repository.ts` (`appendHumanAgentTurn`), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/layout.tsx` (nav entry + a stale doc-comment module list fixed in passing), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (the `escalations` namespace + one `backoffice.nav` key), `package.json` (`db:seed:escalations`).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** The schema, the routing-rule-tester UI mechanism (`RuleListEditor`) and the permission keys (`escalations:handle`/`routing:manage`) were all already real and correct from earlier waves — this wave's own job was almost entirely *using* them correctly rather than inventing anything new, which is exactly the shape this project's `docs/` intake process is supposed to produce. The one genuinely new piece of business logic is `evaluateRoutingRules()` plus the lazy-routing judgment call built around it; both are proven against real, persisted SQL Server rows, not just fakes. Two deferred items are real, separable, and named rather than smoothed over: the WhatsApp outbound-dispatch path and a committed Playwright spec for this screen — the latter skipped deliberately, matching every wave since B-4's own precedent rather than being an oversight. The CHAR(26) staff-id gotcha is now the fifth recorded instance of the identical mistake in this codebase; `tasks/lessons.md` names it plainly rather than promoting a sixth near-miss to "surprising."

### B-9 · Evaluation, governance & analytics — complete 2026-09-10

**Delivered.** Three new, real modules — `modules/evaluation` (B13), `modules/governance` (B14) and `modules/analytics` (B1) — built in parallel by three separate agents against exactly the layered convention every prior wave established (domain/ports/adapters/application/testing), plus the three new backoffice routes (`/evaluation`, `/governance`, `/command-centre`), plus the missing publish-gate hook into B-3's real `PublishAgentVersion`. All eight checklist items and three of the cross-module wiring rows are real, proven against the real Docker stack (not stubbed), and the full combined gate is clean.

**The single largest fact of this wave: the schema was already fully designed and constraint-complete from B-0's holistic build — every table, every `CK_*`, and (the genuine surprise) two of the three "hard DB-level guarantees" the brief asked for were *already implemented*, not gaps.** Read directly from `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` before any application code was written, not assumed from `docs/data-model.md`'s prose (which names a `TR_PromotionRequests_gateMustPass` trigger that does **not** exist under that name — its behaviour was folded into one already-shipped, already-correct trigger, `TR_PromotionRequests_decisionRules`, confirmed by direct inspection rather than treated as a gap to fill):
- **`TR_PromotionRequests_decisionRules`** (real, `AFTER INSERT, UPDATE` on `PromotionRequests`) already enforces, at the database level: the environment chain (`fromEnvironmentKey → toEnvironmentKey` must match `platform.Environments.promotesToKey`), the gate-must-pass rule for any promotion targeting the live environment (requires a real, passing `GateEvaluations` row for the exact `agentVersionId`), separation of duties (`decidedByStaffUserId ≠ requestedByStaffUserId`), **and writes the matching `AuditLogEntries` row inside the same trigger — the same transaction as the decision, by SQL Server's own trigger-execution semantics.** This is FR-GOV-14/FR-GOV-29's atomicity guarantee, already real, already structural, before this wave touched anything.
- **`AuditLogEntries` was already append-only at the DB grant level** (`DENY UPDATE, DELETE ... TO PUBLIC` and again explicitly `TO shj3_web`) plus `TR_AuditLogEntries_blockMutation` (an `INSTEAD OF UPDATE, DELETE` trigger that always throws) as belt-and-braces against a future `db_owner` session — real, already shipped.
- **What genuinely did not exist**: any application-layer enforcement of the publish gate itself (`GetDraftValidation`'s own doc comment, read before writing a line of this wave's code, explicitly named this as deferred to "a later wave" — this one), and the entire domain/ports/application/adapter/UI layer needed to actually *drive* the already-designed schema (golden sets, regression scoring, environments/promotions UI, observability, privacy/erasure/retention UI, command-centre metrics/explorer/queues). That or-real gap is what this wave actually built.

**Evaluation (`modules/evaluation`, B13).** Golden sets/cases (`CreateGoldenSet`, `AddGoldenCase`, `AddCaseFromTranscript` — the real cross-module contract `modules/analytics` calls, catching the real `UQ_GoldenCases_sourceConversation` unique-constraint violation as `evaluation.case_already_added` rather than a raw 500, exactly the wireframe's "button disables itself with confirmation text" as a **data fact**, not a UI memory), `RunGoldenSetNow`/`RunAllSuites` (never overwrite history — every run is a new `RegressionRun` row), `UpdatePublishGate`/`GetGateStatusSummary` (the live consequence strip, computed from a real `GateEvaluation`, matching the wireframe's exact seeded sentence shape). **The scoring pipeline is real, not fabricated**: `apps/ai` gained exactly two new, narrow, tenant-scoped endpoints — `POST /v1/evaluation/turns` (runs one turn pinned to a specific `agentVersionId`, reusing the real `process_turn` pipeline directly rather than duplicating guardrail/retrieval/tool-call logic, so a `mustRefuse` case scores against the real `wasRefused`/guardrail outcome and a tool-accuracy case scores against the real `OrchestrationTraceStep` rows a turn actually produced) and `POST /v1/evaluation/score-similarity` (real OpenAI embedding cosine similarity between the actual response and a case's `expectedBehaviour` — the real accuracy signal). **Why two endpoints and not the async job-with-`Idempotency-Key` pair `docs/api.md` originally documented**: `shj3_ai_ro`'s real SQL grant has no write access to `RegressionRuns`/`RegressionCaseResults`/`GoldenSets`/`Conversations` (§2.17's grant table, real and unchanged) — the batch orchestration (one call per golden case, persisting the run/case-result rows) has to live on `apps/web`, which holds the write grant, so `apps/ai`'s own two endpoints are ordinary synchronous request/response calls, not a job. `docs/api.md` §5.7 corrected in place to match (the stale `POST /v1/evaluation/runs` + `GET .../runs/{runId}` pair struck, replaced with the real two-endpoint contract and a dated correction note; the idempotency-example table at the top of the doc fixed too, since it cited the same stale endpoint as a long-running-job example). `EvaluateGateForVersion` implements the real cross-module `PublishGateChecker` port (`evaluateForPublish`/`evaluateForPromotion`/`recordEvaluation`, the last returning a real, persisted `GateEvaluation.id` — the one thing the governance module's promotion flow needs to satisfy `TR_PromotionRequests_decisionRules`'s own DB check) — applies `PublishGate.minAccuracy`/`minGroundedness`/`redTeamMustScore100`/`blockOnBoundLocaleBelow100` against the most recent completed run per relevant golden set, plus the real cross-schema locale-readiness join (`AgentLocaleBindings ⋈ platform.LocaleSettings.translatedPercent`, FR-EVAL-09's own distinct signal from a `LanguageParity`-kind golden set's accuracy score — both real, both wired, never conflated). **Wired into B-3's real publish flow**: `PublishAgentVersion` now takes an optional `gate` dependency (existing callers untouched — this is additive, not a breaking constructor change); when supplied, a blocked publish returns a new, structured `{reason:"gate_blocked", agentName, versionLabel, reasons[]}` result *before* `AgentRepository.publishVersion` is ever called, and a `GateEvaluation` row is persisted either way (passed or blocked) so the decision itself is auditable. Since no DB trigger gates the publish transition (unlike promotion), this enforcement is real, deliberate application-layer logic — the one place this wave added genuine new business logic to an already-shipped module, not merely a new module.

**Governance (`modules/governance`, B14).** Environments/promotions read/write against the real, already-enforced schema above — `RequestPromotion` proactively calls the gate BEFORE attempting an insert targeting the live environment (so a blocked promotion names the real blocking set/score/threshold in the UI, not just a raw SQL exception), while the real DB trigger remains the structural backstop for any other caller. Audit log: read-only port (no update/delete method exists on the type at all — misuse is impossible by construction, not just convention), writes go through the pre-existing, already-tested `TenantAuditSink` (`modules/platform/adapters/outbound/sql/audit-sink.ts`, reused rather than duplicated — its own `usp_WriteAuditLogEntry` call already schema-qualifies correctly, see the raw-SQL finding below). Observability: real, on-demand `RecordServiceHealthSample` aggregates real `OrchestrationTraceStep` timing/status data into `ServiceHealthSample` rows (`Healthy`/`Degraded` computed from the real `>5% error rate` / latency-budget rule) — honestly scoped, see deferred items. Privacy & data: real `PrivacyConfig` singleton (transcript retention `Days30|Days90|Year1|Year7`, data residency, both real, both audited on change), real consent-ledger read via `modules/channels`' own existing `ConsentRepository` (reused, not duplicated), real `ErasureRequest`/`ErasureTask` processing (SQL Server deletion/anonymization proven live; Neo4j/Qdrant/Redis — see deferred items), real `RunRetentionSweep` (see deferred items for its current real scope). **Two real, live-infra proofs, independently run**: (1) the governance agent's own — a real `ApprovePromotion` producing both the status change and a new `promotion.decided` `AuditLogEntries` row together, a deliberate self-approval attempt genuinely refused (FR-GOV-15, error 51192), and — the DB-level append-only proof this brief specifically demanded — **a real raw `UPDATE`/`DELETE` against a real `AuditLogEntries` row, attempted as the real dev connection, genuinely refused by `TR_AuditLogEntries_blockMutation` (error 51001)**, not merely hidden by the UI; (2) this session's own final integrated proof (below), tying all three modules to one real agent version end to end.

**Command centre (`modules/analytics`, B1).** `ComputeDailyMetrics`/`GetOverviewMetrics` — real, on-demand rollup computation (see deferred items for why "on-demand" rather than continuous), aggregating real `Conversation`/`ConversationTurn`/`MessageFeedback`/`OrchestrationTraceStep` rows into `ConversationMetricsDaily`/`IntentMetricsDaily`, idempotent upsert per the schema's own documented rule. Conversation explorer: real filter/list/transcript (reusing the real `contentMasked` PII-redaction convention, never a second redaction mechanism), real `ExportConversations` (a real `TranscriptExport` insert — the pre-existing `TR_TranscriptExports_audit` trigger writes the matching audit entry automatically, in the same transaction, confirmed rather than assumed), real `AddConversationToGoldenSet` calling straight into the evaluation module's `AddCaseFromTranscript` (the real, live-proven cross-module wiring row: "Add a transcript to a golden set → case count increments"). Feedback & knowledge-gap queues: real `MarkFeedbackIssueFixed`/`ReopenFeedbackIssue` (respecting `CK_FeedbackIssues_fixedPaired`), real `ResolveUnansweredQuestion` (respecting `CK_UnansweredQuestions_resolutionPaired` — exactly one of a knowledge-source id or a flow id, validated in application code before the DB would reject it). The KPI grid was originally hand-assembled as a bare `<table>` (a real `gate:table` design-gate failure caught by the full verify run) — fixed at the root by pivoting it into a proper `DataTable<KpiTableRow>` (one row per metric, one column per date range) rather than suppressing the gate, matching design-system.md §5.5 #41's actual rule.

**A real, live bug found (not by any of the three agents' own unit tests — none of which had reason to construct this exact input shape) and fixed at the root: `Conversations.wasContained` could disagree with `outcome` for real.** `PrismaConversationRepository.create()` seeds every conversation `wasContained: true`; neither `close()` nor `markEscalated()` (both real, already-shipped B-6/B-7 write paths) ever cleared it back to `false` when `outcome` later became `Escalated` — so a real, live conversation in this dev environment (created by an earlier wave, never this wave's own code) carried `outcome: "Escalated"` **and** `wasContained: true` simultaneously, an internal contradiction `modules/analytics`'s aggregation counts independently into `escalatedCount` and `containedCount`, which is exactly the double-count `CK_ConversationMetricsDaily_coherent` forbids. `ComputeDailyMetrics` threw for real, live, against this environment's real data the first time it ran — confirmed to be a genuine application-logic gap, not a seed-script bug (grepped every `scripts/*.ts`: none writes `Conversations` at all). Fixed at the root: a new, pure, unit-tested domain function (`modules/conversation/domain/containment.ts`, `containmentClearedBy(outcome)` — deliberately monotonic, only ever clears `wasContained` to `false`, never resurrects `true` from a later non-`Escalated` close, since a conversation's history of human involvement cannot be undone by how it eventually ends) now backs both real write paths. The one pre-existing bad row (`01M240K4SK8XVZ814E31TF69MM`, tenant `sewa`) was corrected directly (`wasContained` flipped to `0`, matching what the fix would have produced had it existed) after confirming, by direct query across all four provisioned tenant schemas, that it was the only one; `ComputeDailyMetrics` re-run for real afterward against the real corrected data succeeded (`conversationCount=15, containedCount=14, escalatedCount=1, abandonedCount=0` — coherent). `modules/analytics`'s own port doc comment, written earlier in this same wave, had rationalized the buggy shape as intentional ("a conversation can be `wasContained=true` yet later escalate") — corrected in place once the real constraint proved that reasoning wrong; see `tasks/lessons.md`'s new entry on trusting a same-wave doc comment's reasoning over a real constraint violation.

**A second real bug found and fixed at the root, by the same live-infra proof: a raw SQL query with no per-tenant schema qualification.** `PrismaLocaleReadinessRepository.listForVersion()` (the locale-gate join) wrote `FROM AgentLocaleBindings alb INNER JOIN LocaleSettings ls` with bare, unqualified table names — Prisma's own generated queries route correctly per tenant via the connection string's `schema=` parameter (ADR-0011), but a hand-written raw SQL string gets none of that; SQL Server resolves a bare identifier against the connection's actual default schema, not the calling tenant's. Failed with a real `208 Invalid object name` against any real, provisioned tenant schema — never caught earlier because every prior caller of the gate was a unit test against a fake, which has no schema to get wrong. Fixed by schema-qualifying with `sqlSchemaFor(currentTenant(...))`, the exact pattern the pre-existing `TenantAuditSink` already uses correctly for its own raw `EXEC` call — confirmed empirically (gate evaluations only succeeded after the fix) and typechecked clean. See `tasks/lessons.md`'s new entry.

**A third, smaller thing found and fixed in integration, not by either sub-agent in isolation: the agent-wizard's publish-error banner had no real rendering for a `gate_blocked` rejection.** `wizard-shell.tsx`'s existing `t(\`publishError.${result.reason}\`, {defaultValue: result.reason})` pattern (correct for every prior flat-string `reason`) cannot express FR-EVAL-08's structured detail (`agentName`/`versionLabel`/`reasons[]`) for the new `gate_blocked` case — it would have rendered the literal string `"gate_blocked"` to a real user. Fixed with a small, local `formatGateBlockedBanner`/`formatGateBlockedReason` pair (duplicated from, not imported from, `evaluation`'s own identical `publish-gate-tab.tsx#toBlockingCondition` formatting — this route may not cross the feature-module boundary into a sibling feature for a UI-only concern) that composes the real per-metric sentence (naming the golden set/locale, the real observed score, the real threshold) from the structured result, with four new, real i18n keys (`agents.wizard.publishError.gate_blocked`/`gateReason.{score,redTeam,boundLocale,suiteFailure}`, EN+AR).

**Integrated, end-to-end live-infra proof (this session, after all three modules and the fixes above landed).** Against the real Docker stack (SQL Server/Neo4j/Qdrant/Redis/shj3-ai, all healthy) and a real "sewa" tenant, using real `SHJ3_OPENROUTER_API_KEY`/`SHJ3_OPENAI_API_KEY`:
- A real agent version **genuinely blocked** from publishing (`reason: "gate_blocked"`) by a real, computed accuracy score below a deliberately tightened `minAccuracy` threshold (`0.99`) — the real blocking golden set, its real score, and the real threshold all present in the structured result exactly as FR-EVAL-08 requires.
- The **same** agent version then **genuinely passing** once `minAccuracy` was lowered (via the real `UpdatePublishGate` use case) below the achieved score — a real gate re-evaluation, not a bypass.
- A real promotion for that same published version, requested and approved through `modules/governance`, with a real, atomically-written audit entry — `AuditLogEntries` row count **9 → 10**, hash-chained, confirmed by direct query.
- Real command-centre metrics reflecting the activity: `ComputeDailyMetrics` picked up the real conversations the evaluation run itself created via the real per-turn pipeline. **A real, previously-unconfirmed fact, named plainly rather than assumed either way**: evaluation-origin conversations are **not** currently distinguished from citizen-origin ones anywhere in the schema or this wave's code, so they *are* counted in real command-centre metrics today — a genuine, flagged gap (see below), not silently decided.

**Full gate, final state.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: **all 17 stages clean** — format, lint, typecheck, all eight custom design/architecture gates (`gate:tokens`, `gate:cypher`, `gate:clients`, `gate:drift`, `gate:tailwind-theme`, `gate:token-refs`, `gate:table` — fixed, not suppressed, per the KPI-grid finding above — `gate:i18n-strings`), **1412 unit tests passing** (up from B-7's 1290; two new for `containmentClearedBy` alone, the rest across the three new modules), full Python stage (`py:format`/`py:lint`/`py:types`/`py:arch`/`py:test`: **358 passed**). Run individually per this brief's own instruction: repo-wide `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean; `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean (exit 0).

**Judgment calls and gaps, flagged plainly — every one of these was named by the agent or session that found it, not discovered later:**
1. **No `shj3-worker` process exists** (confirmed: only `apps/web`/`apps/ai`) — `ConversationMetricsDaily`/`IntentMetricsDaily`/`ServiceHealthSample`'s own schema doc comments describe a continuously-running OTel-consuming worker that does not exist anywhere in this repo. `ComputeDailyMetrics`/`RecordServiceHealthSample` are real, correct, on-demand computations over real data (not fabricated numbers) — genuinely deferred is only *continuous, automatic* freshness. Building a full always-on worker/scheduler service is a separate, large undertaking, correctly out of scope for this wave.
2. **`FeedbackIssue`/`UnansweredQuestion` clustering is exact/near-exact text matching, not semantic.** `ClusterFeedbackAndGaps` groups by a normalized-text hash key — a real, defensible, honestly-scoped first cut, not the semantic clustering a production system would eventually want. `rootCause` classification is a real, named, rule-based heuristic over the triggering turn's own trace signals, not a black box.
3. **Erasure verification for Neo4j/Qdrant/Redis is real query/deletion logic, structurally correct, but this session could not exercise all three live end-to-end** in the time available — the SQL Server store was proven live; the other three stores' real deletion/verification code exists and is correct by inspection but is named here as not independently, live-proven store-by-store this wave, unlike the SQL Server path.
4. **`RunRetentionSweep`'s real purge counts for Qdrant/Neo4j/Redis were not exercised against non-trivial live data this wave** — the SQL Server side (`Conversation`/`ConversationTurn`/`OrchestrationTrace`) is real and proven; `transactionsSkipped` is a real, honest count (never silently omitted), matching the structural 7-year carve-out.
5. **Per-target observability latency budgets are a single global default** (`>1500ms p95` or `>5%` error, per `docs/requirements.md`'s own global threshold language) rather than the wireframe's per-dependency budgets (MCP tool 300ms, Graph RAG 350ms, WhatsApp egress 250ms) — no per-target budget configuration exists anywhere in this codebase to read from; named as a deliberate scope cut, not silently applied.
6. **Evaluation-origin conversations are not excluded from command-centre analytics** — confirmed live in the integrated proof above. A real, separable follow-up (a channel/flag distinguishing evaluation-run conversations from citizen ones) rather than something this wave silently decided either way.
7. **`RunAllSuites`' "every agent version to re-run" is derived from this tenant's own regression-run history** (every distinct `(goldenSetId, agentVersionId)` pairing already exercised at least once) rather than an association model the real schema does not have (no "which agent normally runs against which set" table exists) — a real, named, reasonable interpretation of FR-EVAL-06, not an invented feature.
8. **No committed Playwright e2e spec for `/evaluation`, `/governance`, or `/command-centre`** — matching every wave since B-4's own precedent (a throwaway, deleted-after live-infra script proves the real backend chain instead); legitimate, separable follow-up work, not an oversight.

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/modules/evaluation/**` (domain/ports/adapters/application/testing, ~25 files + tests), `apps/web/src/modules/governance/**` (~30 files + tests), `apps/web/src/modules/analytics/**` (~25 files + tests); new backoffice routes `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/{evaluation,governance,command-centre}/**`; `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/adapters/inbound/evaluation_router.py` + `domain/evaluation.py`; `scripts/seed-governance-demo-data.ts` (seeds `platform.Environments`, previously entirely unseeded). New, small, this session: `apps/web/src/modules/conversation/domain/containment.ts` (+ test). Modified, small and additive: `apps/web/src/modules/agents/application/publish-agent-version.ts` (optional `gate` dependency), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/composition.ts` (real `publishGateChecker()` wiring), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/[id]/edit/wizard-shell.tsx` (the `gate_blocked` banner fix), `apps/web/src/modules/conversation/adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-conversation-repository.ts` (the containment fix), `apps/web/src/modules/analytics/ports/metrics-repository.ts` (corrected doc comment), `apps/web/src/modules/evaluation/adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-locale-readiness-repository.ts` (schema-qualification fix), `apps/web/src/modules/iam/domain/permissions.ts` + `permissions.test.ts` (`evaluation:manage`/`governance:manage`, restricted to SuperAdmin/EntityAdmin), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/layout.tsx` (three nav entries + doc-comment update), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (new `evaluation`/`governance`/`commandCentre` namespaces + the wizard's `gate_blocked` keys), `docs/api.md` (§5.7's corrected contract + the idempotency-example table fix), `eslint.config.mjs` (one documented, narrowly-scoped `crosscutting → feature:evaluation` boundary carve-out for `governance`'s real, product-decided dependency on the publish-gate port). One real data correction against the live dev database: `sewa.Conversations` row `01M240K4SK8XVZ814E31TF69MM`'s `wasContained` flipped `1 → 0`.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** This was the largest single slice of Phase B — three parallel agents, ~80 new files, three new backoffice screens — and the thing worth naming plainly is that almost none of the "hard requirements" (atomic promotion+audit, append-only audit log, gate-must-pass) needed new database enforcement at all: B-0's holistic schema build had already shipped two of the three as real, working triggers, and this wave's actual job was building the application layer correctly on top of already-solid ground, catching two genuine live bugs (the `wasContained`/`outcome` drift, the unqualified raw-SQL schema) along the way that no unit test — fake-backed by construction — could ever have caught, only a real database could. The deferred items above are real and separable, not smoothed over: no live worker for continuous telemetry/rollups, exact-match rather than semantic clustering, and partial live verification of the three non-SQL-Server erasure stores are the honest state of B-9 today. With this wave complete, the only remaining Phase B/Phase F work is B-10, the in-product user guide module — everything it will document (every screen, every permission, every real behaviour) is now real and running, not a mock a guide would have to describe honestly as aspirational.

### Phase F · User guide module — complete 2026-09-10

**Delivered.** The real `HelpGuideShell` organism (design-system.md §5.5 #56 — the 57th and last named component), the real `/help` module (index + deep-linkable catch-all), 14 real guide entries covering every real backoffice/citizen screen this project has shipped (B-2 through B-9), a real Playwright screenshot pipeline run against the real running app, and a real drift-detection gate wired into `pnpm verify`/`pnpm gate` — proven to fire on a real missing-entry violation and a real stale-entry violation, both then reverted, per this project's own "never trust a gate that hasn't been fed a violation" discipline.

**Architecture decision, made explicitly rather than defaulted (per the brief's own instruction to justify this, not default silently): static content, not a data-backed `modules/userguide` layer.** `apps/web/src/modules/userguide/domain/guide-content.ts`'s own module comment states the reasoning in full; the short version: nobody edits guide prose from inside the running app (unlike every other feature module's real, user-mutated data), so the domain/ports/adapters/application layering that pattern exists to protect would be pure ceremony here. What *is* real and tested is the seam that actually needs to be real: `GUIDE_REGISTRY` (the single source of truth three real consumers read — the nav, the deep-link resolver, and the coverage gate) and the pure `getGuideEntry`/`getGuideNav` application functions, both unit-tested (7 tests) against the real registry and real content, not fakes. Versioning ("tied to the app version/commit") is a per-entry `lastVerifiedAgainst` field naming the real file(s) and date each entry's prose was checked against — a discipline aid, not a mechanically-enforced gate, since nothing here can typecheck a Markdown-shaped claim against a component's real behaviour.

**Real coverage: all 14 real, shipped pages got a full entry — none thinner than the others, with one deliberate, named exception.** `command-centre`, `agents`, `agents/wizard` (covers both `/agents/new` and `/agents/[id]/edit` — one entry, not two, for the one real wizard screen both routes render — see `guide-registry.ts`'s own comment for why), `tools`, `knowledge`, `iam`, `channels`, `escalations`, `identity`, `evaluation`, `governance`, `settings/appearance`, `settings/appearance/reset` all carry full-depth EN+AR content — purpose, a feature-walkthrough section per real tab/control group, 2-4 step-by-step how-tos, and a permissions note cross-referenced against the real `requirePermission()` call (or its absence) on that page. Every one of these was written by reading the real page's real component code and, decisively, `messages/en.json`'s own real, already-shipped translation strings for that screen — the actual rendered labels, tab names, and dialog copy, not the wireframe's original mockup naming (confirmed to differ in several places, e.g. `tools`'s real fourth tab is "Resilience & fallbacks", not the wireframe's original name). The one deliberately thinner entry is `citizen-widget` (`/widget`), scoped modestly per the brief's own instruction — see the next paragraph for the full reasoning, both on the routing side and the content side.

**Two flagged, reasoned judgment calls on the citizen-facing side, made explicitly rather than silently:**
1. **`/help` is fully public — no `withStaffAuth()` gate — for every entry, including the 13 staff-facing ones.** A real, considered trade rather than an oversight: the guide's content is documentation about how to use a screen, never the screen's own tenant data, and every real screen it documents remains exactly as protected as it already was (`requirePermission()` calls untouched by this wave). Gating the guide itself would need a second, harder design (a staff tree plus a separately-gated public subset) for no real security benefit — the same call countless real SaaS products make (a public help centre describing admin screens nobody outside the company can reach). Recorded in `help/page.tsx`'s own doc comment.
2. **The citizen-facing help affordance is intentionally minimal, and does not touch the real embeddable widget component.** A plain link to `/help/citizen-widget` was added to the standalone `(assistant)/widget/page.tsx` demo/test-harness page — not to `WidgetApp`/`AssistantWidgetShell`, the actual shared, already-tested organism real citizen sites load via `<script>`. Wiring a help affordance into that organism is real work for whoever next touches `modules/conversation`, named plainly in `widget/page.tsx`'s own comment rather than silently left unmentioned, since it changes a component this wave did not otherwise audit.

**The screenshot pipeline is real infrastructure, not a one-off.** `scripts/capture-user-guide-screenshots.ts` (wired as `pnpm userguide:screenshots`) reuses this project's own real E2E infrastructure — `e2e/.auth/super-admin.json`'s pre-minted staff session, `e2e/support/mint-session.ts`'s exact client-binding fingerprint (now exporting `E2E_USER_AGENT` alongside the already-exported `PLAYWRIGHT_CLIENT_IP` specifically so this new consumer reuses it verbatim rather than retyping the literal — this project's own repeatedly-hit "client binding must match on both sides" lesson, applied here pre-emptively rather than rediscovered a fourth time). It checks the real server is reachable (`/api/healthz`) with a clear error rather than a confusing timeout, re-seeds through the exact same five-step chain `e2e/global-setup.ts` already uses (idempotent, safe to re-run), then drives a real headless Chromium against the real running app and writes 14 real PNGs to `apps/web/public/user-guide/screenshots/`. **Run for real, twice, in this session** (once before, once after the layout fix below) against the real stack — file sizes ranging 33KB–135KB, visually confirmed (the `/iam` screenshot shows the real Users table, the real Help icon in the real header, real seeded rows).

**The drift-detection gate (`scripts/gates/user-guide-coverage.mjs`, wired as `gate:user-guide` in both `pnpm gate` and `scripts/verify.mjs`) is proven in both directions, not just written and trusted.** It reads the real `app/[locale]/**/page.tsx` tree independently of the registry and cross-checks bidirectionally: every real route must be covered (or explicitly exempt — only `/` and `/help` itself are, both named and justified in `guide-registry.ts`), and every covered route must still be real. **Two real bugs in the gate's own first-draft regex were found and fixed by running it for real before trusting it**, both now documented in the gate's own comments: (1) `coversRoutes: ["/agents/new", "/agents/[id]/edit"]` itself contains a literal `]` inside `[id]`, which a naive `[^\]]*` character class cannot skip past — fixed with a non-greedy `[\s\S]*?` plus a lookahead for the real closing bracket; (2) the root page (`[locale]/page.tsx`) resolves to a bare `"page.tsx"` with no leading `/` once relative-path'd, which a bare `/page\.tsx$` suffix-strip silently failed to match, reporting a bogus `/page.tsx` route. **Both proven, not just fixed**: a throwaway route with no guide entry (`(backoffice)/zz-gate-probe/page.tsx`) made the gate fail with the exact real route named, then was removed and the gate went green again; separately, a temporarily-injected stale `coversRoutes` entry (`"/this-route-does-not-exist"`) made the gate fail from the opposite direction, then was reverted and confirmed clean — this project's own "never trust a gate that passes before it's been fed a violation" discipline, applied to a brand-new gate rather than an existing one for once.

**A real environment gap found and fixed at the root, unrelated to this wave's own feature work: this dev checkout's local `.env` was missing `SHJ3_PAYMENT_GATEWAY_ADAPTER`**, a variable `.env.example` has documented (`=mock`) since B-8, but which this specific machine's `.env` never received — surfaced only because the screenshot script's seeding chain calls `seed-e2e-credentials.ts`, which touches envelope encryption, which validates the *whole* config, not just the keys it uses. Fixed by adding the exact documented default to the local, git-ignored `.env` — a one-line, low-risk fix to unblock real seeding, not a design change.

**A real layout bug found by the live browser verification pass itself, not by any static gate, and fixed before this entry was written — the clearest proof this wave's "verify against the real thing" discipline is not decorative.** `HelpGuideShell`'s root shell used `min-h-full` (which needs a defined ancestor height to resolve against — none of `[locale]/layout.tsx`'s ancestors provide one, unlike `AppShell`'s own `min-h-dvh`), and the `<aside>` nav had no `align-self` override — so a flex row's default `align-items: stretch` silently stretched the nav to match its sibling's height exactly. Invisible on every entry with short content, but a real Arabic `/help/settings/appearance` render (genuinely long prose) stretched the nav to **8434px**, confirmed directly via `getBoundingClientRect()` against the real running page, not assumed from reading the JSX. Fixed with `min-h-dvh` (matching `AppShell`'s own precedent) plus `md:sticky md:top-0 md:self-start` and a real `maxBlockSize: "100dvh"` on the `<aside>` — re-verified directly afterward: the nav now measures a real, bounded 742px and stays pinned while the content column scrolls past it, the standard docs-site sidebar behaviour. Both design gates and the component's own 7 tests re-run clean after the fix; all 14 screenshots re-captured against the corrected layout.

**Live-infrastructure proof, run for real against the real stack (`next dev` on `:3000`, all four store containers already healthy), not simulated:**
- `/en/iam` screenshot shows the real Help icon rendered in the real header (the placeholder `onClick: () => {}` this wave replaced with `href: \`/${locale}/help\`` — `backoffice-shell.tsx`'s own updated doc comment names the swap).
- `/en/help` (index): side menu shows all three real groups (Admin / configurator, Settings, Citizen assistant) and all 14 real entries, exactly mirroring `(backoffice)/layout.tsx`'s real nav plus the two settings routes and the one citizen entry.
- **Deep-linking proven with a fresh navigation, not a client-side route change**: `page.goto("/en/help/agents/wizard")` directly (no prior visit to the index) renders the real `<h1>Agent designer wizard</h1>` — confirming a direct URL to one guide entry genuinely loads that entry.
- **Search proven as a real URL round-trip**: typing "iam" into the search field navigates to `?q=iam` and the side menu narrows to exactly one real match ("Users, teams & roles") — server-resolved via `getGuideNav(locale, query)`, not a client-side array filter.
- **A real 404** for `/en/help/not-a-real-page` (`notFound()`, confirmed via the real HTTP status code, not just visually).
- **Arabic/RTL confirmed for real**: `/ar/help/settings/appearance` renders with a real `dir="rtl"` attribute on `<html>` and real, correct Arabic prose (`"الإعدادات ← المظهر"`), the active entry correctly highlighted in the (now RTL-mirrored) side menu.

**Full gate, final state — re-run after every fix above, not just once at the start.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`: **18/18 stages clean** (the 17 pre-existing plus the new `gate:user-guide`), including **182 test files / 1419 unit tests** (7 new for `modules/userguide`, 7 new for `HelpGuideShell` itself), the full Python side untouched and still green. Independently re-run per this brief's own instruction: `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean across both real sub-projects; `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean (exit 0, repo-wide). `pnpm run gate` (the eight-plus-one design/architecture gates run standalone) also clean.

**Deferred, named plainly rather than smoothed over:**
1. **No per-locale screenshots** — one EN screenshot per entry, per `capture-user-guide-screenshots.ts`'s own doc comment's reasoning (Arabic/RTL correctness is proven by the live browser check above, not by doubling the image set). Revisit if a future requirement genuinely needs a per-locale image.
2. **Dynamic-segment routes are screenshotted via their static sibling** (`/agents/new`, not a real `/agents/[id]/edit` with a resolved id) — every dynamic route in `GUIDE_REGISTRY` today has one; a future entry that genuinely has no static sibling would need the capture script extended to resolve a real seeded id first.
3. **The production embeddable widget (`WidgetApp`/`AssistantWidgetShell`) itself carries no in-widget help affordance** — only the standalone demo page does. Named above and in that page's own code comment, not silently absent.
4. **The `lastVerifiedAgainst` per-entry field is a discipline aid, not an enforced gate** — nothing in this project's no-CI environment (ADR-0008) can mechanically fail a stale claim the way `gate:user-guide` mechanically fails a missing/stale *route mapping*. The maintenance rule's "add or update the guide entry" half is enforced by the coverage gate for existence; its "keep the prose accurate" half still depends on the next author actually doing it, same as every doc in this repository.

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/modules/userguide/**` (`domain/guide-content.ts`, `domain/guide-registry.ts`, `application/get-guide-entry.ts` + test, `application/get-guide-nav.ts`, `content/index.ts` + 14 per-slug content files); `apps/web/src/components/patterns/help-guide-shell/**` (the organism, its types, its test, its `index.ts`); `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/help/**` (`page.tsx`, `[...slug]/page.tsx`, `help-page-shell.tsx`, `help-entry-view.tsx`, `build-nav-groups.ts`); `scripts/gates/user-guide-coverage.mjs`; `scripts/capture-user-guide-screenshots.ts`; `apps/web/public/user-guide/screenshots/*.png` (14 real files). Modified, small and additive: `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/backoffice-shell.tsx` (the real `help.href` wiring, replacing the placeholder), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(assistant)/widget/page.tsx` (the citizen help link), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (+ `help` namespace, + `widget.demoPage.helpLinkLabel`), `e2e/support/mint-session.ts` (exported `E2E_USER_AGENT`), `package.json` (+ `gate:user-guide`, + `userguide:screenshots`), `scripts/verify.mjs` (+ the `gate:user-guide` stage). One local, git-ignored `.env` fix (the payment-gateway-adapter default, unrelated to this wave's own feature scope, named above).

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** This closes Phase F and, with it, all 57 named design-system components and every Phase B module (B-0 through B-10) — the project's last major phase. The most important thing to verify independently, beyond re-running the gate suite, is the coverage gate's own two directions: add a route, confirm it fails; remove a route with a real entry, confirm the stale-entry direction also fires — both were proven in this session and then reverted, but a reviewer should not take that on faith alone given how narrow the gate's own regex-based parsing necessarily is (documented plainly in the gate's own header comment as a deliberate, narrow heuristic, not a general TypeScript parser). The two judgment calls (public `/help`, minimal citizen affordance) are real product decisions with real trade-offs on both sides, not obviously-correct defaults — worth a deliberate second look from whoever owns this product's actual security/UX posture, not just an implementation review.

### Phase D follow-up · Original-checklist reconciliation + E2E expansion to every shipped screen — complete 2026-09-10

**Scope of this pass, stated up front.** Three things, dispatched together: (1) reconcile several original, never-checked-off checklist boxes against what every later wave's own Review entry actually shipped (the shadcn token-gate item, the B-1 baseline-components item, the cross-module wiring checklist); (2) expand Phase D's committed E2E suite from the four screens the original pass covered to as many of the remaining ~10 real, shipped screens as this pass could genuinely complete and verify; (3) update `docs/requirements-traceability.md` to match. All three are now real, not aspirational — this entry accounts for each honestly, including what still isn't done.

**Part 1 — reconciling the original checklist, verified directly rather than assumed:**
- **The 14 shadcn-scaffold token-gate violations (line ~144)** — re-read all 14 files in full and re-ran `node scripts/gates/no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` directly: clean, zero violations, every arbitrary-value/z-index/raw-length instance the box named is gone (fixed incrementally by Wave 2A/2B, which this same Review section already documents doing so for `switch.tsx`/`progress-bar.tsx`/`tooltip.tsx` specifically — the checkbox was simply never flipped). Checked off, with the one honest caveat named inline: the box's own broader "full spec in design-system.md §5.3" ask was not separately re-audited as an exhaustive checklist against all 14 in this pass.
- **The B-1 baseline components (lines ~208-213)** — DataTable, Wizard, PermissionMatrix, ChatThread+MessageMetaRow, Composer, AppShell (sidebar/header/Help icon/breakpoints), and the a11y baseline (focus-visible/reduced-motion/status-never-colour-alone) were all re-verified against the real files, not the prose describing them, and all check off clean. **One genuine, narrower gap found and named rather than folded in as done: `KpiTile` is built and unit-tested but consumed by zero real screens** — `command-centre/overview-tab.tsx`, the one screen `kpi-tile.tsx`'s own doc comment names as its target, renders a `DataTable`-based KPI grid instead. Left open for whoever next touches that tab.
- **Cross-module wiring (lines ~356-365)** — a dedicated research pass over every prior wave's own Review entry found real, already-proven citations for 2 of 7 unchecked items (bind-tool→registry, reorder-rule→different-route — both B-3/B-7), and no prior proof for the other 5. Of those 5: **2 were proven live for real in this pass** (approve-template→campaign-unblocks, via a throwaway script against the real `sewa` schema, restored after; reassign-team→membership-updates, via a new, permanent, committed `iam.spec.ts` test) and **the reorder-rule item was additionally upgraded from "proven once by a throwaway script" to "a permanent, committed, regression-checked Playwright test"** (`escalations.spec.ts`). **3 remain genuinely unproven, named honestly rather than faked**: SEWA breaker auto-trip-on-degradation, unresolved-conflict→confidence→refusal, handover-node→ticket-reason — all three need the `apps/ai` Python runtime exercised directly (a real failing tool call, a real retrieval/scoring pipeline, a real flow execution), deeper than this pass's time budget.

**Part 2 — E2E expansion, real infrastructure, both new bugs found and fixed at the root:**
- `e2e/global-setup.ts` extended from 5 seed steps to 10 (`knowledge`, `channels`, `escalations`, `identity-payments`, `governance` added), each verified idempotent by running twice in a row against real data.
- **A real idempotency bug found and fixed**: `seed-knowledge-demo-data.ts`'s source-creation helpers (`seedSourcesForTenant`/`seedSewaConflict`/`seedLibrariesConflict`) had no existence guard at all — contradicting the file's own "safe to re-run" doc comment — and threw a real `UQ_KnowledgeSources_knowledgeCollectionId_name` violation on its very first re-run against already-seeded data. Fixed with the same by-natural-key guard every sibling seed script already uses; re-verified idempotent across two consecutive real runs.
- **A real permission-gating bug found and fixed**: `whatsapp-tab.tsx`/`campaigns-tab.tsx` (channels) rendered Approve/Reject/"Send now" to any `agents:manage` principal regardless of holding the stricter `agents:publish` the underlying server action actually requires (`actions.ts`'s own module comment names the rule) — found live building `channels.spec.ts`'s golden path as an `agents:manage`-only principal, whose click reached a real, correctly-enforced 403 only after the control was already shown. Fixed at the root (`channels/page.tsx`'s new `canPublish`, threaded through `ChannelsScreenProps` to both tabs, matching `agents-screen.tsx`'s own established "never render, don't merely disable" convention) — a committed regression test in `channels.spec.ts` proves the fix directly.
- **New committed spec files, one per real screen, each with golden path + validation error + permission boundary** (exact case-by-case detail in `docs/requirements-traceability.md` §2.5-2.11): `e2e/backoffice/knowledge.spec.ts`, `channels.spec.ts`, `escalations.spec.ts`, `identity.spec.ts`, `evaluation.spec.ts`, `governance.spec.ts`, `command-centre.spec.ts`. Plus one new test added to the existing `iam.spec.ts` (team reassignment — see Part 1's cross-module wiring item). `governance.spec.ts` needed one small, real supporting script (`scripts/seed-self-approval-promotion.ts`) since no "request a promotion" form exists anywhere in the shipped UI yet — the same "spawn a `tsx` script for anything touching real Prisma directly" boundary `e2e/support/mint-session.ts` already established, reused rather than reinvented.
- **A real, significant environment finding, unrelated to any product code, found and fixed mid-pass**: the host's `C:` drive filled completely (0 bytes free — Docker Desktop's WSL2 virtual disk had grown to 135GB across this whole long session's image builds/tenant-provisioning cycles), which produced roughly an hour of genuinely confusing, hard-to-diagnose E2E flakiness (Radix `Select` interactions silently not registering, with no error) before being traced to the actual root cause via `docker ps` failing outright. Fixed via `wsl --shutdown` + `docker system prune -a -f --volumes` (34GB reclaimed directly; free space went from ~0 to ~68GB). Recorded in `tasks/lessons.md` as its own lesson — check this before chasing a UI-framework theory the next time E2E behaviour looks inexplicably flaky in this environment.
- **A second real, narrower bug found while debugging the above**: `next-intl`'s Unicode bidi-isolate marks (U+2068/U+2069) around every interpolated message value break a naive `getByText("literal substring")` — the text renders visually identical (a screenshot from the exact failure moment showed it, character for character) but the assertion fails with "element(s) not found" because the invisible marks sit inside the literal substring. Fixed by switching the affected assertions to a wildcard regex; recorded in `tasks/lessons.md` as a generalization of the already-known agent-name instance of this same family.
- **A real, found-but-not-fixed schema gap, named rather than routed around silently**: `RoutingRuleTest.firedRoutingRuleId` is `onDelete: NoAction`, so a routing rule that has ever been evaluated by the tester can never be deleted afterward (a real, unhandled FK violation) — found deterministically while `escalations.spec.ts`'s own first draft tried to clean up its fixture rules after itself. The correct fix (`onDelete: SetNull`) needs a real migration across every provisioned tenant schema, out of this pass's scope; the test itself was redesigned to follow this codebase's own established idempotent-seed convention (create-once, never delete) rather than fighting the gap.

**Full gate, re-run after every fix above:** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` → **18/18 stages clean** (182 test files / 1419 unit tests, the full Python side, `py:format`/`py:lint`/`py:types`/`py:arch`/`py:test` all green). `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean across both real sub-projects. `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean repo-wide (one real violation found and fixed along the way — an `import("@playwright/test").Locator` inline type annotation in `escalations.spec.ts`, replaced with a proper top-level `type Locator` import).

**Deferred, named plainly:**
1. **`KpiTile`'s non-consumption** (Part 1) — a real, narrow gap for whoever next touches `command-centre/overview-tab.tsx`.
2. **The three unproven cross-module wiring scenarios** (Part 1) — all three need `apps/ai` exercised directly.
3. **`channels.spec.ts`'s own "approve → unblocks" proof lives outside committed E2E** — no seeded demo `StaffUser` combines `sewa` tenant membership with `agents:publish`; the real business logic was proven live via a throwaway script instead (see `channels.spec.ts`'s own module comment for the full reasoning and `docs/requirements-traceability.md` §2.6). A future pass adding one more seeded principal (an `EntityAdmin` in `sewa`, say) would close this cleanly.
4. **Sharding mutating specs into isolated tenants** (`playwright.config.ts`'s `workers: 1`) is more valuable now than before — more specs mutate shared fixtures than at the time of the original pass's own note on this — still not done.
5. **The citizen widget remains the single largest block of untestable requirement IDs** (`conversation`/`orchestration`/`flows` — ~42 IDs) — unchanged from the original pass, named again here since it is now, by a wide margin, the most valuable single gap left to close.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** The disk-space incident is worth reading even by someone who will never touch this exact host — it is a real, generalizable lesson about diagnosing "flaky" E2E behaviour (check the environment's own resources before chasing framework-specific theories), not a one-off quirk of this machine. The two real product bugs (channels permission-gating, knowledge seed idempotency) were both found by the specific discipline this project has followed all session — building real E2E coverage against the real stack, not just reasoning about what should work — and both are exactly the kind of thing that would have shipped silently otherwise. The `RoutingRuleTest` FK gap is a real, if narrow, product defect (a routing rule, once tested, becomes permanently undeleteable) that a future pass should actually fix at the schema level rather than continuing to route around it in test fixtures.

### Phase D second follow-up · Routing-rule FK fix, KpiTile reconciliation, citizen widget E2E — complete 2026-09-10

**Scope of this pass, in the priority order it was given.** (1) Fix the `RoutingRuleTest.firedRoutingRuleId` FK gap named-not-fixed by the entry immediately above. (2) Resolve the `KpiTile`/`command-centre` doc-comment-vs-reality mismatch the same entry named. (3) Real, committed, permanent E2E coverage of the citizen conversation widget — the single largest remaining block of untestable requirement IDs. (4) Time-permitting, the three cross-module scenarios needing `apps/ai` exercised directly. All of 1–3 are done and proven live; 4 was not reached and is left honestly open, not rushed.

**1. `RoutingRuleTest.firedRoutingRuleId` — fixed at the root, not routed around again.** `onDelete: NoAction` → `onDelete: SetNull` in `prisma/tenant/schema.prisma`, a real, hand-written migration (`prisma/migrations/20260910090000_routing_rule_test_fired_rule_set_null/migration.sql`, matching the exact FK name/shape the init migration generated) applied live via the real `PrismaMigrationExecutor`/`PrismaMigrationStatusStore`/`RunTenantMigrations` pipeline (not a hand-run SQL script) to all four provisioned tenant schemas (`customs`/`libraries`/`sewa`/`sharjah`). **A second, load-bearing schema fact found while building this, not assumed from the FK edit alone**: `CK_RoutingRuleTests_firedPaired`'s old symmetric equality (`(fellToDefaultQueue = 1) = (firedRoutingRuleId IS NULL)`) would have made the FK's own `SET NULL` action fail its own CHECK the first time a fired-then-deleted rule was ever exercised — loosened to a one-way implication (`fellToDefaultQueue = 0 OR firedRoutingRuleId IS NULL`, `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql`, the same `IF EXISTS … DROP … ADD` re-definition shape `CK_Tenants_status`'s own comment already established as this codebase's convention for redefining a named CHECK). Recorded as a new `tasks/lessons.md` entry — a real, generalizable gotcha ("loosening `onDelete` to `SetNull` needs every CHECK that assumed the old always-paired shape re-checked, not just the FK").

Proven live, both directions: (a) a real create-rule → run-tester (recording a real `RoutingRuleTests` row referencing it) → delete-rule sequence now succeeds and nulls the reference, `firedRuleOrdinal` (a plain snapshot int, no FK) surviving; (b) a deliberately reverted constraint (`customs` tenant only, restored immediately after) reproduced the *original* failure verbatim (`RoutingRuleTests_firedRoutingRuleId_fkey` FK violation on delete) — proving the negative path genuinely was broken before this fix, not merely asserting the positive path looks right. `e2e/backoffice/escalations.spec.ts` — the test that found this gap in the prior wave and worked around it by never deleting — now exercises the real fix directly: both fixture routing rules are deleted for real via the UI at the end of the golden path (a real `DestructiveConfirmDialog`, scoped to avoid the trigger/confirm label collision this file's own `pickOptionByArrowDown` neighbours already document the convention for), confirmed to disappear with no error. Full suite re-run clean after the revert-and-restore probe (see Verification below).

**2. `KpiTile` — confirmed a deliberate divergence, not a gap, against the real wireframe.** `docs/SHJ3-wireframes-guide.md`'s own B1 tab 1 section is a literal 4-row × 3-column table (`Today`/`Last 7 days`/`Last 30 days` as three **permanent, simultaneous** columns — `GetOverviewMetrics`'s own doc comment already states this explicitly, read from the real wireframe, not assumed) — a shape `KpiTile`'s anatomy (one value, one delta, one sparkline, all for a *single* range) cannot represent without either losing two of the three columns or rendering three tiles per metric, which no spec anywhere describes. `command-centre/overview-tab.tsx`'s `DataTable<KpiTableRow>` is the correct, faithful choice, not an oversight — the prior wave's own review already named *why* it was built that way (a real `gate:table` violation on a first-draft bare `<table>`, fixed by pivoting to `DataTable` per §5.5 #41's actual rule) without reconciling it against `KpiTile`'s existence at all. Resolved the doc/reality mismatch rather than forcing a swap that would have been a real regression against the wireframe: `kpi-tile.tsx`'s own doc comment and `docs/design-system.md` §5.4 both corrected in place to name the real reason (a single-range dashboard tile, not this specific 3-column-comparison screen) and to state plainly that the component remains real, tested, and available for a future single-range dashboard use — not dead code, just not this screen's shape.

**3. Real, committed, permanent E2E coverage of the citizen conversation widget — `e2e/journeys/conversation-widget.spec.ts`.** `playwright.config.ts`'s own `testMatch` already included `journeys/**/*.spec.ts` (anticipated since before the widget existed, per that file's own comment) — no config change needed, confirming the file belongs there rather than under a new `e2e/widget/` directory. Drives the real SSR `/{locale}/widget` demo page (`channelKey=sewa.WebWidget`, the same tenant/agent B-6's own manual proof used) rather than rebuilding B-6's already-proven, throwaway cross-origin/HTTPS proof — same-origin here, so the already-shipped `origin-allowlist.ts` self-directed bypass applies with no re-proof needed, per this wave's own brief. `SHJ3_OPENROUTER_API_KEY` is empty in this dev environment, so every turn runs the real `DeterministicChatModel` — real pipeline stages (route → tool call → guardrail), no live-model cost or flakiness, and its own documented `_INTENT_TRIGGERS` ("check my bill") is what deterministically fires a real bound tool call for the golden path.

Two real tests: a golden path (bootstrap → disclaimer dismiss → a plain streamed reply → a message that fires a real flow-backed tool call, visible end to end in the Agent trace rail (router, two tool-call steps, both guardrail stages) → thumbs-up/thumbs-down toggled → a real citizen-initiated handover → a second, independent staff browser context finding this exact ticket, claiming it, and reading its real, non-cold-start transcript) and a negative path (a turn against a conversation id outside the caller's own session — real `404 conversation.not_found`, proving api.md §4.1's "no enumeration" rule holds, via a real `page.evaluate` fetch carrying real cookies, not curl). 13 requirement IDs newly covered (`docs/requirements-traceability.md` §2.13): `FR-CONV-02/03/04/05/06/07/10/11/12/16`, `FR-ORCH-01/11/14`, plus a partial, honestly-scoped proof of `FR-HAND-20`'s `UserRequest` reason specifically (the `Handover`-flow-node-triggered reason remains unproven — see Deferred below). `docs/requirements-traceability.md`'s "genuinely blocked" total drops from ~147 to ~117 of 378 (the exact 30-ID `conversation`+`orchestration` reduction), and §2.7's `escalations` section, §3, and §5 all updated to match — nothing claimed beyond what this pass actually verified.

**Two real product gaps found and fixed at the root while building this coverage, not merely worked around in the test:**
1. **`useWidgetConversation().requestHandover()` had no caller anywhere in this tree.** A real, complete use case already wired to the real `POST /conversations/{id}/handover` endpoint, and the real, already-translated `widget.handover.requestAction`/`queuePosition` message keys, both sat unused — `widget-app.tsx` never rendered a way for a citizen to ask for a person directly (only the flow-triggered `handover_triggered` SSE path touched local UI state, and even that never called the real endpoint). Fixed at the root: a real `Button` in `widget-app.tsx`, hidden once a handover is already active, wired to the real hook function — not a raw `fetch()` call reached around the gap from inside the test.
2. **No seeded `StaffUser` combined `sewa` tenant membership with `escalations:handle`** — the identical shape of gap the prior wave's own review named for `channels.spec.ts`'s deferred proof, and its own suggested fix ("a future pass adding one more seeded principal would close this cleanly"). Closed the same way: `scripts/seed-iam-demo-data.ts`'s `USERS` array gained one honestly-flagged, E2E-only sixth row (Khalid Al Marzouqi, `LiveAgent`, `sewa` — appended, not inserted, so the original five wireframe rows' own ordinal guarantees are untouched), threaded through `seed-e2e-credentials.ts`/`mint-e2e-sessions.ts` (`e2e/.auth/live-agent-sewa.json`) and `e2e/global-setup.ts`'s own doc comment.

**Four real, live-found bugs in this test's own design, all fixed before the spec was trusted — not shipped as flaky:**
1. **A purely numeric E2E marker (`Date.now()`) was silently eaten by FR-CONV-13's real PII-masking pipeline** — a bare 13+ digit run reads as a card number and gets redacted to `[REDACTED:CARD_NUMBER]` before the row is ever persisted, so the marker never reached the staff-visible transcript. Confirmed by direct SQL query against the real persisted row (this file's own "verify empirically" discipline) rather than assumed to be a selector bug. Fixed by switching to a base-36 marker; recorded in `tasks/lessons.md`.
2. **`queue-tab.tsx`'s `handleSelect()` on a `Queued` ticket also attempts to claim it**, and a fresh staff session's `AgentPresence` defaults to `Offline` — claiming failed with a real, correct error and silently reset the selection to nothing, with no visible link between the error banner and the empty transcript pane until a real screenshot showed both on the same page. Fixed in the test (set `Available` first, matching what a real agent would do, plus an explicit wait for the real `setAgentPresence` POST and a page reload before touching the queue); the underlying UX gap (a confusing dead end for a fresh session, only explained by the page's own error banner) is real and named in both `tasks/lessons.md` and `docs/requirements-traceability.md` for whoever next touches that screen — not fixed here, out of this pass's scope.
3. **Found only by a full, repeated-run regression check, not by any single clean run: `AgentPresence.activeTicketCount` is capacity-capped (`CK_AgentPresence_capacity`, `maxConcurrentTickets = 5`) and only decrements on a real release/resolve.** The test's own working draft claimed a ticket and left it `Assigned` forever, matching this suite's usual tolerance for harmless fixture accumulation — a tolerance that is wrong specifically for a *capacity* counter. After five clean runs, Khalid's own count hit the cap and the sixth run's claim was correctly refused, with no error banner this time (unlike bug 2) — the ticket just stayed `Queued`, and the test's own "search every row for the marker" loop reported "not found," reading exactly like a selector bug three tables away from the real cause. Fixed at the root: the golden path now clicks `Resolve` on the ticket once its marker is confirmed, keeping every run self-cleaning and the capacity check perpetually satisfied. Recorded in `tasks/lessons.md` as a generalizable pattern (any capacity-bounded resource a test claims must be released, never just left behind).
4. **A real, cross-spec circuit-breaker interaction, found only by running the *full* suite, not any single spec in isolation:** `tools.spec.ts`'s own "seeded-Open SEWA bill breaker" fixture is written to Redis by `seed-agents-tools-demo-data.ts` **only the first time** the breaker's SQL row is created (by design, to avoid duplicate ledger events on re-seed) — so it is a real, live, time-sensitive value from then on, not a re-assertable static fixture. This pass's own widget spec calls the exact same bound tool ("check my bill" → `fetch_sewa_bill`) the breaker governs, and — run manually, repeatedly, over roughly an hour of debugging — its real cooldown (120s) and half-open-probe mechanism closed the breaker for real days before `tools.spec.ts` next checked it, correctly reporting `Closed` where the fixture's own doc says `Open`. Found by reading `seed-demo-data.ts`'s own idempotency-guard comment directly and checking the live Redis state by hand — not by reasoning about what "should" still hold. Fixed by restoring the exact state shape the seed itself writes on first creation (a throwaway script, deleted after); recorded in `tasks/lessons.md` as a second, concrete instance of the exact risk `playwright.config.ts`'s own `workers: 1` comment already named ("circuit breaker resets/trips"), now crossing from config-level resets into a live tool call as a second way the same class of interaction can happen.

**Verification, every step run for real against the live Docker stack (`sqlserver`/`redis`/`neo4j`/`qdrant`/`ai`, all healthy) — nothing accepted on faith, including a full, repeated-run regression check that is what actually found bugs 3 and 4 above:**
- The routing-rule FK fix: the live migration-apply proof (all four tenants, `applied` then `skipped` on re-run), the schema-verification query (`sys.foreign_keys`/`sys.check_constraints`, confirmed `SET_NULL` and the corrected CHECK definition on every tenant), the positive functional proof (create → test → delete succeeds, id nulled, ordinal/queue-status preserved), and the negative proof (constraint reverted on `customs`, delete genuinely failed with the exact original FK error, then restored and re-verified) — all via throwaway scripts, deleted after, no debris left in any tenant's real data.
- `e2e/backoffice/escalations.spec.ts` re-run standalone: **4/4 passing**, including the new real delete-both-rules assertions.
- `e2e/journeys/conversation-widget.spec.ts`: **6 total runs across this pass**, in this order — 2 clean (before the full-suite check), 1 clean **as part of a full `pnpm exec playwright test` run** (confirming bugs 1/2's fixes hold outside standalone execution), then that same full run's own 5th failure (`tools.spec.ts`, bug 4) triggered the investigation that found bug 3 too (surfaced identically the *next* standalone run, against an already-warm server) — both fixed, then **3 more clean standalone runs in a row** with real DB checks after each confirming `activeTicketCount` returned to `0` and the ticket count grew by exactly one `Resolved` row per run, never accumulating live capacity. This is the real value of the repeated-run + full-suite discipline this project already establishes: neither bug 3 nor bug 4 is visible in a single clean run of any single spec.
- **Full gate**: `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **18/18 stages clean** (format, lint, typecheck, all 9 gates, `test:unit` 1419/1419 across 182 files, full Python side `py:format`/`py:lint`/`py:types`/`py:arch`/`py:test` 358/358). `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean across both real sub-projects. `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean repo-wide.
- **Full `pnpm exec playwright test` (every spec)**, run twice at the end as the regression check against this pass's shared-file edits (`seed-iam-demo-data.ts`/`seed-e2e-credentials.ts`/`mint-e2e-sessions.ts`/`global-setup.ts`/`queue-tab.tsx`-adjacent `widget-app.tsx`): first run — 53 passed, 5 failed (bugs 3 and 4 above, plus three more investigated below); after fixing 3 and 4, a targeted re-run of all four remaining failing specs together — **2 of 4 now pass** (`appearance.spec.ts`, `escalations.spec.ts` — both confirmed transient/flaky on re-run, not real regressions) and **2 of 4 fail again, identically, confirmed pre-existing and unrelated to anything this pass touched**:
  - `agents.spec.ts`'s publish-redirect step — already a known, named flake (`docs/requirements-traceability.md` §4, from the original Phase D pass, which already added a `toPass()` retry specifically for this and left it as an accepted, documented flake, not something this pass introduced or needs to re-litigate).
  - `iam.spec.ts`'s "reassigning a user's team" test — a **newly diagnosed, real, pre-existing bug**, not merely flaky: `iam.spec.ts:54` asserts `row.getByText("—", { exact: true })` unscoped, and the row's own Team *and* Role cells both legitimately show the identical "—" placeholder for a freshly-invited user — a strict-mode violation (2 elements) every single time this exact assertion runs, deterministically, not intermittently. The same test file's *own* later comment (a few lines down) already documents this exact ambiguity and the correct fix (scope to `getByRole("cell")` at a specific index) — but only applies that fix to a *later* assertion in the same test, never to this first one. Confirmed unrelated to this pass (operates entirely in the `sharjah` tenant; this pass's own IAM changes only touched the `sewa` tenant's `USERS` array) and left unfixed as genuinely out of this pass's stated scope — named here precisely, with the exact line and root cause, rather than left as a vague "pre-existing flake."

**Deferred, named plainly:**
1. **The three cross-module scenarios needing `apps/ai` exercised directly were not reached** (item 4 of this pass's own priority order) — SEWA breaker auto-trip-on-degradation and unresolved-conflict→confidence→refusal remain exactly as the prior wave left them. The third, handover-node→ticket-reason, is now *better understood* rather than closed: this pass's own reading of `use-widget-conversation.ts` confirms the SSE `handover_triggered` event still only sets local UI state and never calls the real `/handover` endpoint, so a flow-triggered ticket with the correct reason may not be wired on the `apps/web` side yet either, not only unproven on the `apps/ai` side — a sharper, more useful starting point for whoever picks this up next than "unproven."
2. **`queue-tab.tsx`'s silent claim-failure-on-select UX gap** (found above) — real, narrow, not fixed, named in both `tasks/lessons.md` and `docs/requirements-traceability.md`.
3. **`iam.spec.ts:54`'s real, pre-existing, deterministic strict-mode bug** (found above, this pass) — a precise, one-line fix (scope to `getByRole("cell")`, matching the same test's own later assertion) for whoever next touches that file; not fixed here, genuinely out of this pass's scope, but too precisely diagnosed to leave as a vague "known flake."
4. **Firefox is still not configured for `e2e/journeys/**`** despite `docs/testing.md` §2.4 naming Chromium *and* Firefox for this specific layer — `playwright.config.ts`'s `projects` array still has only `chromium`. Real, cheap, separable follow-up.
5. **`channels.spec.ts`'s own deferred "approve → unblocks" proof** (prior wave's item 3) is unchanged — the new `sewa`/`LiveAgent` principal added this pass holds `escalations:handle`, not `agents:publish`, so it does not close that specific gap; a future pass still needs its own `EntityAdmin`-in-`sewa` fixture for that one.
6. **Sharding mutating specs into isolated tenants** (`playwright.config.ts`'s `workers: 1`) — unchanged, still real, still not done, and this pass found a second, sharper reason it matters (the circuit-breaker cross-spec interaction, bug 4 above) beyond the config-level resets the existing comment already named.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this wave.** The routing-rule fix is the cleanest of the three main tasks: a real, narrow, correctly-scoped schema change, proven both ways (positive and negative) against real infrastructure, with the one non-obvious risk (the CHECK constraint's own symmetric equality) caught before shipping rather than after. The `KpiTile` finding is a good example of *not* fixing something that looked like a gap on paper — the doc comment was wrong, not the screen — and the fix that matters is correcting the doc, not the code. The widget E2E work is the most valuable single thing this pass did, and the full-suite regression discipline at the end is what actually made it trustworthy: two of the four bugs it found (capacity exhaustion, the cross-spec circuit-breaker reset) are invisible to any single clean run of any single spec, and would have shipped as a "passing" test that quietly broke a sibling spec or itself on its sixth real-world run. The two remaining failures after every fix (`agents.spec.ts`, `iam.spec.ts`) were both run down to a specific, confirmed-unrelated root cause rather than left as an unexplained "pre-existing" hand-wave — one already documented and accepted by an earlier wave, one newly diagnosed and named precisely here for whoever picks it up next.
