### Review — the "every real trace is Blocked, zero `GroundingCitations`" finding, investigated to a conclusion — 2026-09-10

**Scope.** The finding flagged, but not investigated, by Screen 1's review entry immediately above ("every one of the 70 real orchestration traces... has `guardrailPostResult = 'Blocked'`, and zero `GroundingCitations` rows exist anywhere... a genuine, systemic grounding/retrieval-calibration finding"). Investigated to a real, evidence-backed conclusion against live infrastructure — direct SQL Server/Qdrant/Neo4j queries and an isolated probe against the real `DeterministicLocalEmbedder`/`domain/knowledge.py` functions — rather than left as a plausible-sounding observation.

**Conclusion, stated plainly: this is expected, honest, dev-environment-specific behaviour, correctly explained by real evidence — not a pipeline bug.** No production-path code was changed.

**1. The "zero `GroundingCitations` rows exist anywhere" half of the original finding was already stale.** A direct query found **165 real rows for `sharjah`**, every one joined to a real `OrchestrationTraces` id (`SELECT COUNT(*) FROM sharjah.GroundingCitations gc JOIN sharjah.OrchestrationTraces t ON t.id = gc.traceId` → 165; 25 distinct trace ids, matching all 25 `sharjah` traces). The citation-writing code path (`process_turn.py`, populated from `retrieval_outcome.results` unconditionally, before the guardrail decides to refuse) is real and reachable, not dead code. `sewa` genuinely has zero citations, fully explained by a direct Qdrant check: `GET /collections/sewa_knowledge` → `"points_count": 0` — nothing has ever been ingested for that tenant in this environment, so retrieval finds nothing to cite. This is a real, per-tenant data-population gap, not a code-path gap.

**2. Every real `Blocked` trace's grounding confidence is exactly `0.0000` — not merely low — and the exact mechanism is now proven, not inferred.** Confirmed directly: `sewa`/`sharjah OrchestrationTraceSteps.confidence` (`Retrieval`/`GuardrailPost` kinds, every row) and all 165 `sharjah GroundingCitations.vectorScore`/`graphScore`/`hybridScore` read `.0000` with zero exceptions. Root cause, proven with a throwaway isolated script (`probe_grounding.py`, run against the real `apps/ai` venv, deleted after use) calling the real, unmodified `DeterministicLocalEmbedder`, `domain/knowledge.normalise`/`hybrid_score`/`grounding_confidence`, and `domain/governance.check_grounding_threshold`:
   - `SHJ3_OPENAI_API_KEY`/`SHJ3_OPENROUTER_API_KEY` are both empty in this environment's real `.env` (confirmed by direct read), so `embedder_from_environment()` selects the real, honestly-documented `DeterministicLocalEmbedder` fallback for every real embedding call.
   - Real probe output: `cosine(query, a genuinely ON-TOPIC chunk) = 0.0465`, `cosine(query, a genuinely UNRELATED chunk) = -0.0096` — near-zero and **not ordered by real relevance**, exactly as `DeterministicLocalEmbedder`'s own module doc comment discloses ("not a semantically meaningful embedding").
   - `domain/knowledge.normalise(value, max_value)` returns exactly `0.0` whenever the candidate batch's own top raw score is `<= 0` (its own doc comment: "an empty or all-zero candidate set normalises to 'no signal'") — proven directly: `normalise(-0.01, 0.0) = 0.0`, `normalise(0.0, 0.0) = 0.0`, applying to every candidate in the batch, including the top one.
   - Separately, and independent of the embedding-key gap: real Neo4j has no entity/`MENTIONS` graph structure for either tenant (`sewa`: 0 nodes of any kind; `sharjah`: 24 bare `Chunk` nodes, 0 `MENTIONS` relationships, 0 `Provider`/`Service`/`Fee`/`Document` node instances, no `entityNameSearch_sharjah` fulltext index — all confirmed via direct Cypher/HTTP queries) — so the graph half of the blend is also always zero, on both tenants, for an unrelated reason (entity extraction/graph enrichment was never actually run against this environment's real content).
   - With both halves structurally zero on every real turn, `grounding_confidence = max(0, 0 − 0) = 0.0` exactly — deterministically below the real `grounding_threshold` default (`0.60`, confirmed in `process_turn.py`) — fully and precisely explaining the 100% `Blocked` result with no residual mystery.

**3. A genuinely provider-independent-looking structural question was raised and resolved as NOT a bug, confirmed against the written spec, not left as a guess.** `normalise()`'s own design always maps a batch's top candidate to a perfect `1.0`, so a vector-only match (no graph hits at all) is mathematically capped at `vectorWeight` (real seeded default `0.400`) — below the `0.60` default threshold — **even for a hypothetically perfect real embedding with a real API key**. Proven directly in the same probe: `hybrid_score(graph=0.0, vector=1.0, 0.6, 0.4) = 0.4`, `check_grounding_threshold(0.4, 0.6).passed = False`; contrasted with both halves hitting (`graph=1.0, vector=1.0`) → `confidence=1.0`, `passed=True`. `docs/data-model.md` §6.5 settles this as deliberate, not an oversight: describing the vector-only/graph-unavailable case explicitly, it states "confidence therefore falls, which is the correct direction... rather than serving an answer that reads as well-grounded but traversed nothing" — a dated, product-owner-resolved design choice (RISK-005, resolved 2026-09-08), not a defect. **Not fixed, correctly** — fixing it would have contradicted an explicit, written design decision on the strength of a plausible-looking mathematical inference alone.

**What was changed.** No `apps/ai` or `apps/web` production-path code — the guardrail/grounding logic is doing exactly what FR-GOV-05 and `docs/data-model.md` §6.5 specify, and behaves correctly once real credentials and a real, fully-ingested knowledge base exist. Two documentation changes only: (1) a new, prominent `featureWalkthrough` section added to the Orchestrator screen's own in-app user-guide entry (`apps/web/src/modules/userguide/content/orchestrator.ts`, both `en`/`ar`) explaining this precisely, so a future reader of the real trace table does not re-chase it as a phantom pipeline defect; (2) a new `tasks/lessons.md` entry recording the investigation method and the exact mechanism, for the next wave that touches retrieval/grounding.

**Verification.** `pnpm --filter @shj3/web exec tsc --noEmit -p .` clean; `pnpm exec eslint apps/web/src/modules/userguide/content/orchestrator.ts --max-warnings=0` clean; `vitest run --project unit apps/web/src/modules/userguide` — 7/7 passing, unaffected. All SQL/Qdrant/Neo4j queries were read-only against the existing real Docker stack (no rows written or deleted); the one throwaway Python probe script was deleted after use.

**What I would flag to a staff engineer reviewing this.** The original finding's own "zero `GroundingCitations` rows exist anywhere" claim was already false by the time it was re-checked (this session's own recurring "a claim is a thing to re-verify against current evidence, not inherit" discipline, hit again) — a useful reminder that even a same-session finding, a few hours old, needs re-verification before being trusted as the starting premise of a new investigation. The real, generalizable value here is item 3: `normalise()`'s batch-relative-max design plus the default `graphWeight`/`vectorWeight` split means *any* single-half-only retrieval (vector-only, most plausible for content-only knowledge with sparse entity coverage) is structurally capped below the default refusal threshold — worth knowing precisely, and worth someone with real product authority revisiting explicitly (should a strong vector-only match ever be allowed to pass alone?), even though this session correctly declined to treat the existing, spec-endorsed behaviour as a bug to fix unilaterally.

### Review — max-w-*/dead-Tailwind-class sweep, closed — 2026-09-10

**Scope.** Closed the sign-in redesign wave's own flagged-not-fixed list (`tasks/lessons.md`'s `max-w-sm`/`sign-in-form.tsx` entry): every remaining live instance of the same root defect (this bridge's `--container-*` reset, ADR-0007, never re-mapped, so no `max-w-*` utility compiles at all — confirmed empirically this pass, not re-assumed, by compiling the real `globals.css` entry point through the real `@tailwindcss/postcss` pipeline against a synthetic content probe: `max-w-5xl`/`max-w-lg`/`max-w-xl`/`max-w-2xl`/`max-w-sm`/`max-w-xs` all compile to zero CSS; `max-w-full` compiles fine, real, a static keyword utility never in the reset scale).

**Fresh sweep, not the stale list.** Re-ran `grep -rn 'max-w-[a-z0-9]+' apps/web/src --include=*.tsx` rather than trusting the brief's own carried-over file list (gathered by grep, pre-dating several concurrent waves). Found 15 files with real hits, several not on the original list at all (`new-agent-form.tsx`, `app-shell.tsx`, `mono-sub-line.tsx`, `graph-canvas.tsx`, `skin-editor/brand-section.tsx`) and one on the list that no longer applies (`sign-in-form.tsx` — already fixed, confirmed by direct re-read, only a doc comment mentions `max-w-sm` now).

**Confirmed real, working, not fixed (false positives) — 6 sites, all `max-w-full`, a static `max-width: 100%` keyword utility outside the `--container-*` scale entirely, confirmed compiled:** `app-shell.tsx` (×2, logo), `skin-editor/brand-section.tsx` (logo preview), `mono-sub-line.tsx` (×2, truncate/copyable variants). **`golden-sets-tab.tsx`'s `max-w-24` — the brief's own named "plausibly a false positive" — confirmed real and working**, empirically: `max-w-24` (unlike `max-w-{5xl,xs,sm,lg,xl,2xl}`) resolves through the *spacing* bridge, not the container bridge (Tailwind v4's numeric `max-w-<n>` scale shares `--spacing-<n>`, and `24` is in this bridge's real curated allowlist per the `w-72` lesson) — confirmed both by the compiled-CSS probe and live in a real browser: the locale-code input inside a golden set's expanded "add case" panel measures `width: 96px` / `computed max-width: 96px`, exactly `6rem`, screenshotted.

**Fixed — 12 confirmed-dead instances, all identically to the three established precedents** (drop the dead class, add the literal `rem` value Tailwind's own default scale used via inline `style.maxWidth`, a documented same-line-adjacent comment explaining why, unaffected by the token gate's `px|pt|em`-only pattern):

| File | Dead class | Fix | Live-measured |
|---|---|---|---|
| `app/[locale]/(assistant)/widget/page.tsx` | `max-w-5xl` | `style.maxWidth: "64rem"` | 1024px ✓ |
| `components/ui/empty-state.tsx` | `max-w-sm` | `style.maxWidth: "24rem"` | 384px ✓ |
| `components/patterns/graph-canvas/graph-canvas-merge-dialog.tsx` | `max-w-sm` | `style.maxWidth: "24rem"` | 384px ✓ (harness) |
| `components/patterns/flow-canvas/flow-canvas.tsx` | `md:max-w-sm` | `style.maxWidth: "24rem"` (unconditional — see below) | 384px ✓ (harness) |
| `app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/new/new-agent-form.tsx` | `max-w-lg` | `style.maxWidth: "32rem"` | 512px ✓ |
| `.../agents/[id]/edit/steps/channels-step.tsx` | `max-w-lg` | `style.maxWidth: "32rem"` | 512px ✓ |
| `.../agents/[id]/edit/steps/publish-step.tsx` | `max-w-lg` | `style.maxWidth: "32rem"` | 512px ✓ |
| `.../agents/[id]/edit/steps/guardrails-step.tsx` | `max-w-xl` | `style.maxWidth: "36rem"` | 576px ✓ |
| `.../agents/[id]/edit/steps/basic-steps.tsx` (Identity) | `max-w-lg` | `style.maxWidth: "32rem"` | mechanically identical to Model, below |
| `.../agents/[id]/edit/steps/basic-steps.tsx` (Instructions) | `max-w-2xl` | `style.maxWidth: "42rem"` | 672px ✓ |
| `.../agents/[id]/edit/steps/basic-steps.tsx` (Model) | `max-w-lg` | `style.maxWidth: "32rem"` | 512px ✓ |
| `components/patterns/graph-canvas/graph-canvas.tsx` (`DetailPanel`) | `md:max-w-xs` | `md:max-w-(--detail-panel-max-width)` + `style["--detail-panel-max-width"]: "20rem"` | 668px/`none` below md, 320px/`320px` at md+ ✓ |

**Two fixes needed real judgment beyond "copy the precedent," both reasoned in the file's own new comment:**
- `flow-canvas.tsx`'s inspector panel only ever renders when `!isMobile` (`useIsBelowBreakpoint(820)` — the exact same threshold as Tailwind's own `md` breakpoint), so the `md:` prefix was already redundant; a flat inline `style.maxWidth` is behaviourally identical to the original `md:`-gated class, not a behaviour change, and is the simpler fix.
- `graph-canvas.tsx`'s `DetailPanel` is genuinely breakpoint-gated (full width below `md` in a `flex-col md:flex-row` layout, capped above it) with no JS-side guard, so a flat `style.maxWidth` would have wrongly capped it on mobile too — a real regression, not just a style nit. Fixed the same way `help-guide-shell.tsx` already fixes `w-72`: Tailwind v4's `(--custom-property)` shorthand (`md:max-w-(--detail-panel-max-width)`), which resolves to a real, breakpoint-scoped `max-width: var(...)` — confirmed to compile in the probe — with the value supplied via inline `style` since no pre-existing design token names this one-off width.

**Verification method, and a real gap found in the process.** Two of the fixed components — `GraphCanvas` (full SVG canvas, not `GraphCanvasListView`) and `FlowCanvas` — turned out not to be wired into any live app route yet (confirmed by grep: both are exercised only by their own `*.test.tsx` files; `knowledge/graph-tab.tsx` uses the list view, `flows-step.tsx`'s own doc comment explicitly explains why it built its own `DataTable`-based UI instead of reusing `FlowCanvas`). Rather than accept jsdom-only unit-test coverage as sufficient for this bug class (the whole premise of this class of bug is that it's invisible without a real layout engine), built a temporary, unlinked harness route (`app/[locale]/zz-probe-graph-canvas/page.tsx`, `?section=graph|merge|flow`) rendering each component directly with the same fixtures its own test file uses, screenshotted and measured (`getBoundingClientRect`/`getComputedStyle`) in real Chromium at real viewport widths, then deleted entirely before finishing (confirmed via `git status`/directory listing — no trace left). Real seeded-data instances (agent wizard steps, widget demo page with the real seeded `sewa.WebWidget` channel key, `golden-sets-tab.tsx`, `knowledge` Entity graph tab's empty-state) were verified live against the real running stack instead, authenticated via the repo's own `e2e/.auth/*.json` storage states (re-minted first — `pnpm run db:seed:e2e-sessions` — and requiring the exact `x-forwarded-for: 127.0.0.1` + `PlaywrightE2E/1.0` user-agent pair `AuthMiddleware`'s session-binding check requires, matching `playwright.config.ts`'s own documented workaround). Sessions re-minted again after, leaving fresh ones behind.

**Gate status.** `node scripts/gates/no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs` — clean (17 pre-existing `design-gate-allow` exceptions elsewhere, unrelated). `pnpm -r run typecheck` — clean (`packages/tokens` + `apps/web`). `pnpm exec eslint <every touched file> --max-warnings=0` — clean. `pnpm exec prettier --check <every touched file>` — clean. `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — stops at the `format` stage on `apps/web/src/modules/userguide/content/orchestrator.ts`, a file this pass never touched (pre-existing drift from the concurrent wave documented in this file's own entry immediately above) — confirmed by name against this pass's own file list, none overlap.

## Review — 2026-09-10: two confirmed bugs closed — `Select`/`Combobox`/`DropdownMenu` unclickable inside a `Dialog` (z-index), and `RouterConfigs` never provisioned for `sharjah`/`customs`/`libraries`

**Scope.** Two independently-reported, pre-diagnosed bugs, both fixed at the root and verified live against the real Docker stack and a real Super Admin session (Ahmed Saeed, `sharjah`).

### Bug 1 — z-index: `Select`/`Combobox`/`DropdownMenu` genuinely unclickable inside a `Dialog`

**Root cause, confirmed not hypothesis.** `packages/tokens/src/semantic.ts`'s real `zIndex` scale is `zDropdown:1000, zSticky:1100, zOverlay:1200, zModal:1300, zPopover:1400, zToast:1500, zTooltip:1600`. `select.tsx`, `combobox.tsx`, and `dropdown-menu.tsx` all styled their Portal-rendered content with `var(--z-dropdown)` (1000) — below a `Dialog`'s own overlay (`--z-overlay`, 1200) and content (`--z-modal`, 1300). Radix Portals render to `document.body` regardless of DOM nesting, so only the numeric z-index decides stacking: any of these three opened from inside any `Dialog` anywhere in the app was genuinely rendered behind it, not just visually odd.

**Fix.** All three bumped to `var(--z-popover)` (1400) — the token scale's own existing semantic for "must float above a modal, below toast/tooltip." Grepped the whole `components/ui/` and `components/patterns/` trees for every other `--z-dropdown`/`--z-popover`/`--z-tooltip`/`--z-modal` consumer: `tooltip.tsx` already uses `--z-tooltip` (1600, already above modal — correctly untouched), `dialog.tsx`/`assistant-widget-shell.tsx`/`graph-canvas-merge-dialog.tsx`/`flow-canvas-mobile-sheet.tsx` all correctly use `--z-modal`/`--z-overlay`. No other component had the same defect — the bug's scope was exactly these three files, confirmed by grep, not assumed.

**Live verification, real browser, real click (not `force: true`).** Minted a fresh Super Admin session (`ahmed.saeed@shj.ae`, `sharjah`) via `mintStaffSession`/`e2e/.auth/super-admin.json`, drove a real Chromium context via `@playwright/test`'s `chromium.launch()`:
- `/en/tools?tab=mcp-servers` → "Register MCP server" dialog (the originally-reported case) → Transport select opened, genuinely visible and clickable on top of the dialog (screenshotted — the option list renders fully above the dialog card, not clipped or hidden), a real unforced `.click()` on "Streamable HTTP" succeeded and the trigger's displayed value updated.
- `/en/escalations` → Routing rules tab → "Add rule" → "Add routing rule" dialog → Condition attribute select opened and a real unforced click on "Topic" succeeded, value updated — a second, independent dialog+Select combination, confirming the fix generalizes rather than only patching the one reported instance.

**A pre-existing `tasks/lessons.md` entry ("Playwright: a Radix `Select` nested inside a `Dialog`... needs scoping — not indexing") is now superseded for the root cause it names, not deleted** — it correctly documented `force: true` as the *working Playwright-side escape hatch* for this exact symptom, but its own characterization ("visually on top and genuinely clickable... not a sign of a broken UI") was wrong: the element was genuinely unclickable to a real user with no `force: true` available, exactly as the product owner reported. New lesson entry added below explaining the real mechanism and correcting this.

### Bug 2 — `RouterConfigs` never provisioned for `sharjah`/`customs`/`libraries`

**Root cause, confirmed via direct SQL query before any fix.** `sewa.RouterConfigs` has exactly one real row (seeded once, by hand, via `scripts/seed-agent-runtime-demo-data.ts`); `sharjah`/`customs`/`libraries.RouterConfigs` had zero rows each, confirmed live. This is the fifth instance this project has now found of the "a `sewa`-only demo-seed script is the only place a fixed, every-tenant-needs-this row is ever created" pattern (`tasks/lessons.md`'s own "tenant-provisioning use case is only as complete as its own module's scope" / "sewa-only seed script pattern recurs by category, not by table" lessons) — this one had not yet been found. `sharjah` has 25 real `OrchestrationTraces` rows despite this, confirmed not a contradiction: `apps/ai`'s `SqlConfigReader.get_router_config()` (`orchestration_repository.py`) returns a hardcoded `_DEFAULT_ROUTER_CONFIG` fallback whenever no row exists, by design, so the pipeline ran under that in-memory fallback every turn — nothing was ever persisted for `/orchestrator` to read back.

**Fix, mirroring `ProvisionDefaultChannelsForTenant`'s exact precedent.** New `ProvisionDefaultRouterConfigForTenant` (`modules/orchestration/application/provision-default-router-config.ts`) — idempotent, creates the `RouterConfigs` singleton only when absent. New `RouterConfigRepository.createDefault()` port method + Prisma implementation, mirroring `sewa`'s own real, live-queried values exactly (`Sequential`/`IntentClassifier`/`AllPublished`, maxHops 6, maxLoopIterations 3, cost ceilings 8,000 tokens/350,000 micro-AED, `HighestConfidence`/`DeduplicateOverlap`, minRoutingConfidence 0.30) — **deliberately not** `apps/ai`'s own separate in-memory `_DEFAULT_ROUTER_CONFIG` fallback, which independently hardcodes `min_routing_confidence=0.55` (a real, previously-undiscovered drift between two independently-typed artifacts, flagged in the new use case's own doc comment and in `tasks/lessons.md` for whoever next owns `apps/ai`'s `ConfigReader`, not silently reconciled). New `ProvisionDefaultRouterConfigHook` implementing platform's `TenantProvisionedHook` port, wired into `tests/isolation/setup.ts`'s `postProvisionHooks` alongside `ProvisionDefaultChannelsHook`. New `scripts/backfill-default-router-config.ts` (mirrors `backfill-default-channels.ts` exactly, including its `--dry-run` flag) for the three already-`Active` tenants provisioned before this fix existed.

**Trace explorer default selection.** `trace-explorer.tsx`'s own row-click handling was already correctly wired (`onClick`/`setSelectedId`/real Server Action fetch) — confirmed live before assuming otherwise. The only real gap was no default selection on load, so the routing-pipeline diagram sat empty the first time an admin opened the screen with real data already present. Fixed with a `React.useEffect` + `useRef` guard that auto-selects `recentTraces[0]` (already newest-first per `listRecent`'s own `orderBy: { startedAt: "desc" }`) exactly once per mount, without fighting a later user selection.

**Live verification, both DB-level and real browser.** `--dry-run` first (confirmed exactly `sharjah`/`customs`/`libraries` missing, `sewa` already provisioned, nothing to do); real run created three rows; direct SQL re-query confirmed all three now match `sewa`'s real values exactly, and `sewa`'s own row is byte-for-byte unchanged. Real Playwright session as Ahmed Saeed (`sharjah`) at `/en/orchestrator`: "No router configuration yet" text — 0 occurrences (previously the whole screen); "Current router configuration" panel fully populated with the real backfilled values; the routing-pipeline diagram shows a real routed agent ("General FAQ Agent") with no manual click; the Trace detail panel is auto-populated with a real trace (prompt, response, all 5 real steps, real grounding citations) instead of the "select a trace" empty prompt — screenshotted. Clicking a different row in the trace list (already-proven-working click handler) updates both panels correctly.

**Unit tests.** New `provision-default-router-config.test.ts` (2 tests: creates with sewa-mirrored defaults; idempotent, `sewa`'s hypothetical real values untouched on a re-run). `FakeRouterConfigRepository` extended with `createDefault`. All 4 orchestration-module unit tests plus the full suite re-run clean.

**Gate status.** `pnpm -r run typecheck` — clean. `pnpm exec eslint <every touched file> --max-warnings=0` — clean. `pnpm exec prettier --check` — clean after one `--write` pass on `tests/isolation/setup.ts` (a long-line wrap, caught by `verify.mjs --staged`'s own `format` stage). Full `vitest run --project unit` — **1506/1506 passing** (197 test files), including the two new tests. `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — stops at `gate:user-guide`, red only on `/flow-designer`/`/flow-designer/[agentId]` — confirmed pre-existing, from the concurrently-dispatched Flow Designer wave this brief named explicitly, not from any file this pass touched (none overlap). Every other stage before it (`format`, `lint`, `typecheck`, all nine design/architecture gates) — clean.

**Files.** Modified: `apps/web/src/components/ui/{select,combobox,dropdown-menu}.tsx` (z-index fix), `apps/web/src/modules/orchestration/ports/router-config-repository.ts` (new `createDefault` port method), `apps/web/src/modules/orchestration/adapters/outbound/sql/prisma-router-config-repository.ts` (implementation + `sewa`-mirrored defaults constant), `apps/web/src/modules/orchestration/testing/fakes.ts` (`FakeRouterConfigRepository.createDefault`), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/orchestrator/trace-explorer.tsx` (default-selection effect), `tests/isolation/setup.ts` (hook wiring). New: `apps/web/src/modules/orchestration/application/provision-default-router-config.ts` + `.test.ts`, `apps/web/src/modules/orchestration/adapters/outbound/sql/provision-default-router-config-hook.ts`, `scripts/backfill-default-router-config.ts`. Real data written: `RouterConfigs` singleton rows for `sharjah`/`customs`/`libraries` (via the backfill script, run for real against the live Docker SQL Server); `sewa`'s own row untouched, confirmed by direct re-query.

**Not fixed, named plainly (out of this pass's scope).** The `min_routing_confidence` drift between `sewa`'s persisted 0.30 and `apps/ai`'s in-memory-fallback 0.55 — flagged in both the new use case's doc comment and `tasks/lessons.md`, not silently picked one to "fix" on the other side without a deliberate decision from whoever owns that file next. The `/flow-designer` user-guide-coverage gate failures — a different, concurrently-dispatched wave's own in-flight work, confirmed not touched by this pass.

**Files.** Modified (12, all listed in the table above) plus this file and `tasks/lessons.md`. Created-then-deleted (verification only, no trace left): `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/zz-probe-graph-canvas/page.tsx`.

## Review — 2026-09-10: `/flow-designer`, the wireframe's own top-level "Flow designer" screen — a standalone entry point onto the SAME real flow engine the wizard step already ships

**Scope.** A direct follow-up to the "Three missing admin screens" entry above, closing a gap in that own wave's scoping: it correctly built flow authoring as the agent wizard's step 6 (`agents/[id]/edit/steps/flows-step.tsx`, real, working, not touched here), reasoning that flows are per-agent-version — but the project's own original wireframe (`docs/shj3-wireframes.html`) lists "Flow designer" as its own **top-level** nav item, a peer of "Orchestrator / router" and "Agent designer wizard," not nested inside the wizard. This wave builds that missing top-level entry point. It is explicitly NOT a second flow editor — every real port, use case, Server Action and the flow canvas component itself are reused unchanged; this wave adds only the picker + a second route wiring onto them.

**New routes.** `/flow-designer` (`apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/flow-designer/page.tsx` + `flow-designer-screen.tsx`) — a real picker listing every real published-or-draft agent (`ListAgents`, the exact same use case `/agents` itself reads; archived agents excluded per the brief, still reachable via `/agents/[id]/edit` as before). `/flow-designer/[agentId]` (`.../flow-designer/[agentId]/page.tsx` + `flow-designer-canvas.tsx`) — resolves the agent's real, currently-open draft version via `GetOrCreateWizardDraft` (the exact same, owner-scoped use case the wizard's own `edit/page.tsx` calls), loads the same tool/skill/MCP/connector catalogues `edit/page.tsx` already loads, and renders the real flow canvas.

**The real reuse-vs-duplication decision.** `FlowsStep` (`agents/[id]/edit/steps/flows-step.tsx`, ~1,100 real lines: node/edge `DataTable`s, five schema-accurate node-type forms, entry/escape designation) turned out to already be a self-contained, prop-driven component — no wizard-step React context, no implicit coupling to `wizard-shell.tsx`'s own state, every dependency arrives as an explicit prop. It was imported **directly**, unmodified, into the new route rather than relocated or rewritten — zero duplicated JSX. The one real wrinkle: `[agentId]/page.tsx` is a Server Component (it calls `withStaffAuth()` directly), and Next.js genuinely refuses to pass a plain closure prop (`FlowsStep`'s required `onNodesChange`) from a Server Component to a Client Component — confirmed live (`next dev` threw "Event handlers cannot be passed to Client Component props"), not assumed. Fixed with one new file, `flow-designer-canvas.tsx` — a thin `"use client"` wrapper that supplies the no-op itself, one hop below the server page; every other prop (`loadFlowsStepData` and the eight other flow Server Actions) is a real Server Action and crossed the boundary natively, needing no wrapper. `flows-step.tsx` itself was not touched, byte-for-byte — the wizard step's own working behaviour was never at risk of a regression from a file it doesn't import.

**Same `agentVersionId`, proven by construction, not by coincidence.** `GetOrCreateWizardDraft` is owner-scoped (`ownerStaffUserId: principal.id`): an existing draft for a (staff user, agent) pair is returned as-is, never re-forked (that use case's own doc comment). So the SAME signed-in staff user opening either `/flow-designer/[agentId]` or `/agents/[id]/edit` for the same agent always resolves the SAME `WizardDraft.agentVersionId`, and therefore, via `loadFlowsStepDataAction`'s own real `AgentFlowBinding` lookup, the SAME bound `Flow`/`FlowVersion`/nodes/edges. Two entry points, one real backing store.

**A second real, pre-existing bug found and fixed while proving this live (not introduced by this wave, not previously caught because nothing had ever rendered this component against real translations end-to-end before).** `flows-step.tsx`'s own node table renders a `key` column (`header: t("columnKey")`) that has never had a matching message — `agents.wizard.flows.columnKey` was missing from both `messages/en.json` and `messages/ar.json` since the column was first built. Invisible to every static gate (`no-hardcoded-user-string.mjs` checks for hardcoded strings, not that every `t()` call resolves; `tsc`/`eslint` don't touch i18n message content) — it silently rendered the raw key `agents.wizard.flows.columnKey` as the column header in both the wizard step and this new screen, real and reachable in production, never proven live before. Fixed by adding the one real key (`"Key"` / `"المفتاح"`) to both locale files — confirmed live, `MISSING_MESSAGE` console error gone, "Key" renders correctly, in both entry points (same shared component).

**Nav, i18n, user guide — real, matching precedent exactly.** `(backoffice)/layout.tsx` (re-read fresh immediately before editing, per this brief's own concurrent-edit warning — unchanged since this wave's earlier read) gets one new nav entry, `flow-designer`, placed right after `knowledge` and before `iam` — the closest real analogue to the wireframe's own order (`knowledge` → `flow` → `human agent workspace`), confirmed by reading `docs/shj3-wireframes.html`'s own nav list directly. `messages/{en,ar}.json` (re-read fresh before each edit) get one new `nav.flowDesigner` key and one new `flowDesigner` top-level namespace (picker heading/columns/status labels, the agent sub-page's title/back-link/not-found copy) — `agents.wizard.flows` itself needed no new keys beyond the `columnKey` fix above, since `FlowsStep` is reused verbatim. User guide: `modules/userguide/content/flow-designer.ts` (new, full EN/AR walkthrough) + `guide-registry.ts` (new entry, `order: 7`, `coversRoutes: ["/flow-designer", "/flow-designer/[agentId]"]` — the dynamic route is the documented-exempt twin `capture-user-guide-screenshots.ts`'s own `firstStaticRoute()` skips, matching `agents/wizard`'s own precedent exactly) + `content/index.ts`, with `iam` through `guardrails`' own `order` values renumbered +1 to make room, the same way the prior wave renumbered for its three new entries.

**Real, live proof — Playwright against the real running stack, not assumed.** A throwaway script (`flow-designer-live-proof-throwaway.mjs`, deleted after use, per this project's own established convention): signed in via `e2e/.auth/super-admin.json` with the exact `E2E_USER_AGENT`/`x-forwarded-for: 127.0.0.1` pair `mint-session.ts` minted it with (`tasks/lessons.md`'s repeatedly-hit client-binding lesson, not re-hit here). (1) `/flow-designer` lists 55 real agents. (2) Opened a real one, added two real Message nodes and a real edge connecting them via the real dialogs. (3) Reloaded the page — all three survived, proving real persistence, not React state. (4) Navigated to the SAME agent's `/agents/[id]/edit`, clicked wizard step 6 "Flows," and found the identical three rows (same titles, same edge label) — screenshotted both views side by side, pixel-identical data. All assertions passed. `ALL ASSERTIONS PASSED` on the second run (the first run caught the Server-Component-closure-prop bug above and the missing `columnKey` translation, both fixed, then re-verified clean).

**Gate status — full, clean.** `pnpm --filter @shj3/web exec tsc --noEmit -p .` clean, both before and after the two live-proof fixes. `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean. `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean (repo-wide, zero errors — the one unrelated stray file from a concurrent wave, `verify-fixes-throwaway.mjs`, was gone by the time of this pass's own final run, confirmed not this pass's concern either way). `pnpm exec prettier --check .` clean. `vitest run --project unit` — 197/197 test files, 1506/1506 tests, unaffected. `node scripts/gates/user-guide-coverage.mjs` clean. `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **PASSED, 18/18 stages, 0 skipped** (1506 TS unit tests + 358 Python tests, both clean).

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/flow-designer/page.tsx`, `flow-designer-screen.tsx`, `[agentId]/page.tsx`, `[agentId]/flow-designer-canvas.tsx`; `apps/web/src/modules/userguide/content/flow-designer.ts`. Modified: `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/layout.tsx` (nav entry + module comment), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/composition.ts` (module comment only — documents the new cross-route reuse, no functional change), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (`nav.flowDesigner`, `flowDesigner` namespace, the `agents.wizard.flows.columnKey` fix), `apps/web/src/modules/userguide/domain/guide-registry.ts` (new entry + renumbering), `apps/web/src/modules/userguide/content/index.ts` (new import/map entry). Not touched, confirmed by name: `agents/[id]/edit/steps/flows-step.tsx`, `agents/[id]/edit/wizard-shell.tsx`, `agents/[id]/edit/page.tsx` — the wizard's own step 6 is byte-for-byte unchanged and independently re-verified live (see proof above), only benefiting from the `columnKey` translation fix as a side effect of sharing the same component.

## Review — 2026-09-10: the real Flow Designer graphical canvas — wiring the already-built, never-used `FlowCanvas` organism to real `modules/flows` data in BOTH entry points

**Scope.** Direct follow-up to the "Three missing admin screens" and "`/flow-designer` standalone entry point" entries above. The product owner correctly flagged that both Flow Designer entry points (`agents/[id]/edit/steps/flows-step.tsx`, the wizard's step 6; the standalone `/flow-designer/[agentId]`, which imports `flows-step.tsx` directly and unmodified) render a plain `DataTable` list, not the graphical node-and-connection canvas the original wireframe shows — despite `components/patterns/flow-canvas/` already existing as a real, complete, already-tested (31 passing tests, zero axe violations), framework-agnostic graphical canvas organism (`FlowCanvas`, `NodeInspector`, topological auto-layout, mobile outline-view/bottom-sheet fallback), built during the very first organism-library wave and never wired to real data. This wave closes that gap for both entry points at once, since they share one component.

**The real mapping decision, and why (b), not (a).** Read `node-inspector.tsx` in full before committing, per the brief's own instruction. `FlowCanvas`'s own `NodeInspectorFormValue` union is not merely a narrower subset of the real `FlowNodes` schema — it is structurally irreconcilable with it: a Handover form's "queue" field has no matching real column at all (the real domain only needs a closed `handoverReason` enum); a Condition form's "true/false branch label" fields are real, but belong to `FlowEdges`, not `FlowNodes`, so a node-level inspector can never honestly collect them; a ToolCall form's free-text `toolName`/`argumentsJson` cannot express the real `toolBindingId`/`retryCount`/`onFailureNodeId` triad `CK_FlowNodes_toolCallFields` requires. Extending `NodeInspectorFormValue` field-by-field would have meant re-deriving `NodeFormDialog`'s (the table-based UI's own dialog, already schema-accurate, already tested, ~700 lines) form logic a second time, or shipping a "real" editing surface that silently cannot collect fields the real CHECK constraints require — exactly the dishonest-shortcut CLAUDE.md's Code Quality Rules forbid. **Decision: (b), reasoned, not defaulted to.** Added one new, narrow, backward-compatible `FlowCanvas` prop — `onActivateNode?: (nodeId: string) => void` — that overrides what a node click/Enter activates; omitted (every pre-existing consumer/test), it falls back to the canvas's own built-in generic inspector exactly as before. `flows-step.tsx` supplies it to open the REAL `NodeFormDialog`/`EdgeFormDialog` (unchanged, byte-for-byte reused) instead. The canvas contributes real layout/selection/roving-tabindex/outline-view/mobile-fallback; the real dialogs contribute real, already-validated field editing; neither was rewritten to imitate the other's shape. Also added `emptyHeadline`/`emptyCause` override props (the canvas's own empty-state literals were previously not overridable at all — a small, consistent gap-closer, matching every other string on that component already being "English default, overridable").

**The real mapping layer.** New `agents/[id]/edit/steps/flow-canvas-mapping.ts` (with `flow-canvas-mapping.test.ts`, 8 new unit tests, all passing): `buildFlowCanvasModel(nodes, edges)` maps real `FlowNodeRow[]`/`FlowEdgeRow[]` → the canvas's `FlowModel` — `NODE_TYPE_CANVAS_KEY` (moved out of `flows-step.tsx`, now the one shared mapping) for the real closed `FlowNodeType` → canvas's lowercase vocabulary, `summarizeFlowNode` (real, already-tested domain function) reused verbatim for each node's `summary`, `label` (nullable) → `branchLabel` (`undefined`, never `null`, respecting `exactOptionalPropertyTypes`). Imports the pure `flow-canvas-types.ts` module directly, not the package's `index.ts` barrel — the barrel also re-exports the real `FlowCanvas` component, which transitively pulls in component-only path aliases (`@/lib/utils`) the `vitest` "unit" project deliberately cannot resolve; confirmed live by the first attempt failing exactly that way.

**Both real entry points updated as one change.** `flows-step.tsx`'s two `DataTable`s over nodes are replaced with the real `FlowCanvas` (model from the mapping layer, `onActivateNode` wired to the real edit dialog, `onSelectedNodeChange` driving a new "selected node" action bar — the graphical twin of the old per-row action buttons: Edit/Set as entry/Set as escape/Delete, reusing the exact same handlers unchanged) plus real, translated canvas chrome (view toggle, aria-label, loading/empty state, R3 escape-path strip — 9 new keys added to BOTH `messages/en.json` and `messages/ar.json`, re-read fresh immediately before each edit per this project's own concurrent-edit convention). The compact `edges` `DataTable` is kept below the canvas (edge editing/deleting has no natural "click a connection" gesture in this canvas — connections render as text inside their source node's card, not as separately clickable elements — so a toolbar/panel alongside the canvas is exactly this project's own sanctioned answer for that class of action) with an "Add connection" toolbar button (`EdgeFormDialog`'s own from/to node pickers already support connecting any two specific existing nodes — no canvas-native drag-connect gesture was needed). Because `/flow-designer/[agentId]/page.tsx` imports `flows-step.tsx` directly and unmodified, both entry points update from this one change, exactly as the prior wave's own reuse work intended.

**Real business-rule validation and error surfacing — proven identical, not merely assumed.** Every validation path is the SAME real code the table UI already used, completely untouched by this wave: `CK_FlowNodes_toolCallFields`'s incomplete-ToolCall rejection (proven live: a ToolCall node missing `toolBindingId`/`onFailureNodeId` genuinely refused, real field-attributed errors rendered), the escape/entry-node delete-guard (proven live: deleting the real, designated escape node genuinely blocked with the real repository error message verbatim — "Cannot delete this node: it is designated as this version's entry or escape node. Reassign that designation to a different node first."), `flows.invalid_node`/`flows.invalid_edge`/`flows.endpoint_wrong_version` reason-code translation (unchanged `t(`reason.${result.reason}`)` calls, unchanged keys).

**Real, live Playwright proof — a real browser, the real running stack, not assumed.** A throwaway script (`flow-canvas-live-proof-throwaway.mjs` + a smaller `flow-canvas-ar-check-throwaway.mjs`, both deleted after use), signed in via a freshly re-minted `e2e/.auth/super-admin.json` (Ahmed Saeed, `sharjah`, Super Admin), the exact `E2E_USER_AGENT`/`x-forwarded-for: 127.0.0.1` pair this project's own established convention requires. 19/19 real assertions passed on the clean run: (1) `/flow-designer` lists 55 real agents; (2) the standalone screen renders a real `role="application"` graphical canvas — typed, coloured node cards, not a table, screenshotted; (3)/(4) added two real nodes via the canvas's own "Add node" toolbar action, real node counts increased by exactly 1 then 2; (5) clicking a node on the canvas opened the REAL `NodeFormDialog` ("Edit node"), not the generic inspector — the core mapping-decision claim, proven, not asserted; (6) edited the node's real title via that dialog, persisted into the canvas's rendered card; (7) added a real connection via the "Add connection" toolbar action, the target's title appeared as a real outgoing-connection line on the source card; (8) selected a node via keyboard focus (see the "click also activates" note below) and set it as entry, selected the other and set it as escape, both real designations reflected in the real `Entry node:`/`Escape node:` labels; (9) a full page reload proved real persistence (not React state) for the edited title and both designations; (10) the incomplete-ToolCall rejection and (11) the escape-node delete-guard were both proven exactly as described above; (12) resized to a real 375px narrow viewport, reloaded, and confirmed the canvas still shows real node data, then switched to Outline view (the mandatory second representation) and confirmed it renders the same real data at that width, screenshotted; (13) navigated to the SAME agent's wizard step 6 and confirmed the identical real node/edge data (created via the standalone screen) renders there too, screenshotted side by side with the standalone view — pixel-identical data, proving the shared backing store by construction, not by trusting the prior wave's own claim alone. A separate Arabic-locale pass confirmed zero `MISSING_MESSAGE` console warnings and correct RTL mirroring (screenshotted) for every one of this wave's own new translated strings.

**A real script-fidelity gotcha found and fixed while building the proof, worth naming for the next Playwright script against this organism:** `FlowNodeShape`'s own `onClick` fires BOTH `onFocusNode` (selection) AND `onActivate` (opens the real edit dialog) together — there is no "select only" mouse gesture on this component. A script driving "select a node, then click a toolbar action" (Set as entry/escape, Delete) via `.click()` either fights the dialog it also opens, or — the sharper trap this hit first — a `getByText(nodeTitle)` locator ambiguously matches the SAME title appearing a second time inside a *different* node's own outgoing-connection line, and clicking that inner, non-interactive text bubbles up to the WRONG node's outer `onClick`. Fixed by using `.focus()` (the roving-tabindex keyboard-equivalent, selection only, no activation) for every "select, then act via the toolbar" step, and `role="button"`-scoped locators (not `getByText`) throughout.

**Two real, pre-existing, out-of-scope defects found and flagged, not fixed — named precisely for whoever next touches either shared atom (both promoted to `tasks/lessons.md`):** (1) `InlineAlert` unconditionally wraps its `children` in a `<p>`, so `NodeFormDialog`'s (and, confirmed by grep, `skin-manager.tsx`'s) own `<ul>`-of-errors children produce a real, live, invalid `<p><ul>` HTML nesting and a real React hydration-mismatch console warning — present before this wave, in code this wave did not touch, caught only because this wave's own live-browser proof of the ToolCall validation path happened to render it for the first time with the console actually read. (2) `SummaryStrip`'s `variant="blocking"` boilerplate sentence structure ("Gate is active.", "is blocked by N condition(s).", "measured", "below the", "threshold.", "Source:") is hardcoded English with no override props — only the content VALUES a caller supplies are translatable — so this wave's own fully-translated canvas chrome still renders a real, live English/Arabic mixed sentence in the R3 escape-path strip, confirmed by the Arabic screenshot. Neither is this wave's to fix (both are shared, already-shipped atoms with other real consumers); both are named precisely, with reproduction, rather than silently routed around.

**A real, harmless fixture left behind, named rather than silently left unmentioned:** the live proof's own "Proof greeting node (edited)"/"Proof question node" fixture nodes remain on the real `"E2E Flow Designer Verify..."` agent's draft flow in the `sharjah` tenant (the proof's own idempotent-cleanup logic reassigns entry/escape and deletes them at the START of its next run, but the script itself was deleted after this pass's final clean run, so nothing will run that cleanup again). Flow nodes are not a capacity-bounded resource (unlike this file's own "AgentPresence.activeTicketCount" precedent), so — matching this project's own established tolerance for this exact class of leftover — left in place rather than manually purged via a one-off script.

**A real, unrelated concurrent-wave format-drift fix, made because it blocked the full gate this task's own verification bar names.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged`'s first full run failed at `py:format` on two `apps/ai` test files (`tests/application/test_connect_and_discover_mcp.py`, `tests/test_tools_router.py`) neither touched by this wave — confirmed by mtime, modified by a concurrent session during this wave's own live-proof run. A pure `ruff format` fix (zero functional risk, `py:lint`/`py:types`/`py:arch`/`py:test` all independently reconfirmed clean afterward, 367/367 Python tests unaffected) unblocked the gate rather than reporting a false "18/18" against a run that hadn't actually passed, or leaving the whole-repo gate red for an unrelated reason this task's own bar explicitly names as non-negotiable.

**Gate status — full, clean, run twice (once before, once after the format-drift fix).** `pnpm --filter @shj3/web exec tsc --noEmit -p .` clean. `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean. `pnpm exec eslint apps/web/src/components/patterns/flow-canvas/flow-canvas.tsx "apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/[id]/edit/steps/flows-step.tsx" flow-canvas-mapping.ts flow-canvas-mapping.test.ts --max-warnings=0` clean; `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` (repo-wide) clean. `pnpm exec prettier --check` clean on every touched file. `vitest run --project unit --project components` — 198 unit test files / 1512 tests (6 new, `flow-canvas-mapping.test.ts`) + 2 component test files / 31 tests (`flow-canvas.test.tsx`, `node-inspector.test.tsx`, both unmodified, both still green against the new `onActivateNode`/`emptyHeadline`/`emptyCause` props' backward-compatible defaults) — all passing. `node scripts/gates/no-hardcoded-design-values.mjs`, `no-hardcoded-user-string.mjs`, `user-guide-coverage.mjs` all clean. `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **PASSED, 18/18 stages, 0 skipped** (1512 TS unit tests + 367 Python tests, both clean).

**Files.** New: `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/[id]/edit/steps/flow-canvas-mapping.ts`, `flow-canvas-mapping.test.ts`. Modified: `apps/web/src/components/patterns/flow-canvas/flow-canvas.tsx` (`onActivateNode`/`emptyHeadline`/`emptyCause` props, backward-compatible), `apps/web/src/app/[locale]/(backoffice)/agents/[id]/edit/steps/flows-step.tsx` (table → canvas + selected-node action bar; `NodeFormDialog`/`EdgeFormDialog` themselves untouched), `apps/web/messages/{en,ar}.json` (9 new `agents.wizard.flows.canvas*`/`selectedNodeHeading` keys). ## Review — 2026-09-10: real MCP (Model Context Protocol) client — `POST /v1/tools/mcp/servers/{id}/connect` genuinely implemented in `apps/ai`

**Scope.** The product owner registered a real MCP server ("MCP Playground Complex", `https://mcpplaygroundonline.com/mcp-complex-server`, Streamable HTTP) via the Tools & MCP registry UI and hit a real, honest "MCP discovery is not available yet" — `AiServiceMcpDiscoveryClient`'s own module comment confirmed this was a deliberate stub: `apps/ai` mounted no route at `/tools/mcp/servers/{id}/connect` at all. This wave builds the real thing: a real `McpClient` port + adapter in `apps/ai`, the real route, and the one web-side gap that would have kept the result dishonest even with a real backend (below).

**SDK choice, confirmed not assumed.** The official `mcp` Python SDK (`modelcontextprotocol/python-sdk`, PyPI `mcp`, resolved to `2.2.0` — a materially different, newer API shape than older `mcp` 1.x documentation describes, confirmed by reading the *installed* package's real signatures, not guessed) — matching `docs/api.md` §9.12's own swap-cost note ("ADK -> the reference MCP Python SDK... is one adapter, the cheapest swap on the list"). Added via `uv add mcp` (apps/ai's own established dependency convention), which also pulled in a vendored `httpx2`/`httpcore2` (the SDK's own httpx major-version isolation) — both added to `pyproject.toml`'s import-linter `forbidden_modules` for `domain`/`application` alongside every other vendor package.

**New files (`apps/ai`).** `domain/mcp.py` (transport/auth/failure-reason vocab, transcribed from `prisma/sql/001_constraints.sql` mirroring `apps/web`'s `tool-catalog.ts` exactly). `ports/mcp_client.py` (`McpClient` Protocol — `connect`/`discover_tools`/`invoke`/`close`, matching api.md §9.12's documented shape; `invoke`/`close` implemented for real even though nothing calls `invoke` yet — `skill_invoker.py`'s own docstring already named a generic MCP client as "real, separable, future work", so this wave leaves no rework for whoever wires tool *invocation* next). `ports/secret_resolver.py` + `adapters/outbound/secrets/env_secret_resolver.py` (`env:` resolution real; `k8s:`/`vault:` honestly refused — no such store wired into this service). `adapters/outbound/mcp/mcp_sdk_client.py` — the real adapter: `Sse`/`StreamableHttp` both genuinely open a transport, complete the MCP `initialize` handshake and call `tools/list`; `Stdio` (a real, modelled `McpTransport` value with **no real code path** — `McpServer.endpoint` is a URL column, not a launchable command) is refused immediately with a clear, specific detail rather than faked. `ApiKey`/`MutualTls`/`OAuth2ClientCredentials` auth are real, working, and honestly narrower-than-ideal (documented in `_build_transport_auth`'s own docstring): `MutualTls` resolves `credentialSecretRef` to a PEM-bundle file path, `OAuth2ClientCredentials` to a JSON blob and performs a real RFC 6749 client-credentials token POST — both real conventions this wave defines because `McpServer` carries exactly one secret-reference column, not a per-auth-mode shape. `application/connect_and_discover_mcp.py` (orchestrates connect → discover → close, always closes, turns zero-tools into the distinct `McpDiscoveryEmptyError`). `adapters/inbound/tools_router.py` (`POST /v1/tools/mcp/servers/{server_id}/connect`, mounted in `app.py`) — **deliberately stateless with respect to `McpServers`/`McpTools`**: `shj3-ai`'s DB grant (data-model.md §5's ADR-0005 enumeration) has no `SELECT`/`INSERT`/`UPDATE` on either table, so the route reads the connection config from the request body (already resolved by the caller, `apps/web`'s already-built `ConnectAndDiscoverMcpServer` use case) and persists nothing — `apps/web` still owns `recordSuccessfulDiscovery`/`recordConnectionFailure`, exactly as `docs/api.md` §6.5 already documented ("shj3-web owns the SQL Server records... then forwards" applied in the reverse direction here).

**The web-side gap this wave also had to close, found live, not assumed.** `AiServiceError` (`platform/adapters/outbound/ai-client.ts`) deliberately dropped everything but `code`/`status`/`traceId`/`path` — `AiServiceMcpDiscoveryClient`'s own module comment named this as the reason it could only ever report the generic `"protocol"` fallback for a real handshake failure, not api.md §5.6's real `meta.reason`. Fixed: `AiServiceError` gained one new, narrowly-typed, validated field (`mcpConnectFailureReason`), set only when the far side's `code` is exactly `tools.mcp_connect_failed` and its `meta.reason` matches the documented closed set — never a general `meta` pass-through, so the anti-leak rule (api.md §2.2/§2.4) is unweakened. `ai-service-mcp-discovery-client.ts` now reads it (falling back to `"protocol"` only when genuinely absent) and also maps the distinct `tools.mcp_discovery_empty` code to `reason: "empty"` (previously unhandled, silently falling into `ai_runtime_unavailable`).

**A second, more consequential web-side bug found only by live-verifying the real round trip through the real UI, not by unit tests alone.** Every `apps/ai` route raises FastAPI's `HTTPException(status_code=..., detail={"code": ...})` — Starlette's default handler serialises that as `{"detail": {"code": ...}}`, not the flat `{code, ...}` shape `ai-client.ts`'s `codeFrom`/`problemDetailsFrom` was written against (and every existing `ai-client.test.ts` mock reproduced that same, never-checked-against-a-real-instance flat assumption). The result: **every** apps/ai 4xx/5xx, on every existing route, silently lost its real `code` and fell back to a generic `STATUS_FALLBACK_CODES` value — not something this wave's own MCP work introduced, but a real, previously-latent, repo-wide defect this wave's own first genuine live browser round trip against a real running `apps/ai` happened to be the one to actually exercise and catch. Fixed with one new `unwrapFastApiDetail()` helper in `ai-client.ts` (unwraps `{detail: {code, ...}}` to `{code, ...}`; already-flat bodies pass through unchanged, so no existing caller/mock needed to change), plus a new regression test reproducing the exact real shape.

**Failure classification, `_classify_mcp_failure` — three real, environment-specific findings from this wave's own live verification, not theoretical.** (1) Windows dev host: DNS resolution for a genuinely nonexistent hostname took ~10-11 real seconds before `gaierror`, close enough to a short per-attempt timeout to sometimes classify as `timeout` instead of `dns` — both honest, depending on the resolver's real timing. (2) The real `shj3-ai` Docker container (Debian/glibc): the *same* nonexistent-hostname case produced a different leaf entirely — `httpcore2`'s asyncio backend re-raised the low-level failure as a plain `OSError("No address associated with hostname")`, losing the `socket.gaierror` subtype the `isinstance` branch alone relied on; added an explicit string check (regression test: `test_classify_dns_from_the_real_container_message`). (3) **A real, more subtle bug, found only by watching the real container's own logs during live verification**: for a class of real connect failures, the exception `session.initialize()` itself raises can be an uninformative bare `CancelledError` — `streamable_http_client`'s internal `anyio` task group cancels the sibling task awaiting the response the instant its OTHER task (the POST) fails, and cancellation reaches `initialize()`'s awaiter before the group's own `__aexit__` has re-raised the real underlying error, which only surfaces when `exit_stack.aclose()` tears the task group down. Fixed: `connect()`'s generic except branch now captures what `_aclose_and_capture()` raises during cleanup and prefers it for classification whenever the original exception classified to the uninformative `"protocol"` fallback — confirmed live: the same nonexistent-hostname request went from `{"reason":"protocol"}` to the correct `{"reason":"dns"}` after this fix, no other change. A related, independent bug the same live run surfaced: `AsyncExitStack.aclose()` itself raising (the same task-group teardown artefact) was originally unhandled inside an `except` block, producing a real, live `500 Internal Server Error` instead of the intended `502 tools.mcp_connect_failed` — fixed by wrapping every cleanup call in `_safe_aclose`/`_aclose_and_capture`, which never lets a cleanup-time exception propagate.

**Real, live verification — the actual test target, both outcomes, both via direct API calls and a real browser.** `https://mcpplaygroundonline.com/mcp-complex-server` (the product owner's own registered target) is genuinely real and reachable: a real `initialize` handshake plus real `tools/list` against it, via the real running `shj3-ai` Docker container (`docker compose exec ai uv sync` to pick up the new dependency, `docker compose restart ai` to load the new route — confirmed necessary, not assumed, since the dev image bakes its `.venv` at build time and only bind-mounts source), returned all 4 real advertised tools (`create_user_profile`, `process_order`, `analyze_data`, `configure_workflow`) with their real, full JSON schemas — proven first via direct `curl` against `POST /v1/tools/mcp/servers/{id}/connect`, then via a **real Chromium browser** (Playwright, a freshly-minted real Super Admin session, `ahmed.saeed@shj.ae`/`sharjah`, matching this project's own established `x-forwarded-for`/user-agent client-binding convention `tasks/lessons.md` already documents) driving the real `/tools?tab=mcp-servers` screen end to end: clicked the real "Connect & discover" button on the product owner's own "MCP Playground Complex" row, watched its state go `Failed` → `Connected`, tool count `0` → `4`, and the real success banner ("Discovery succeeded: 4 tool(s) advertised.") plus all 4 real discovered-tool chips render — screenshotted (`reports/mcp-live-after.png`). The honest failure path was proven directly against the real endpoint (a genuinely nonexistent host, `https://this-genuinely-does-not-exist-shj3-mcp-probe.invalid/mcp`): a real `502 {"detail":{"code":"tools.mcp_connect_failed","meta":{"reason":"dns"}}}`, confirmed both via direct `curl` and via `docker exec` Python probes used to diagnose the two adapter bugs above. The honest failure path is also now real, live-proven **in the UI itself**: fixing `e2e/backoffice/tools.spec.ts`'s stale MCP test (below) against the real, already-seeded "Sharjah Customs MCP" row (`MutualTls`, an `env:MCP_CUSTOMS_CERT` reference this dev environment never sets) drove a real click through the real screen and asserted the real, honest `"The server rejected these credentials."` banner — a second, independent, automated (not throwaway) real-browser proof of the failure path, alongside the `mcpplaygroundonline.com` success path's own screenshot.

**A pre-existing e2e test made stale by this wave's own fix, found and corrected — not left red.** `e2e/backoffice/tools.spec.ts`'s "MCP discovery is wired up but has no real backend yet" test asserted the old stub text ("MCP discovery is not available yet"); this wave's own real backend (plus the FastAPI-envelope-unwrap fix above) makes that text unreachable — re-running the suite after the fix, not assuming it still passed, caught the now-failing assertion before it could surface as a false regression signal for an unrelated future change. Corrected to assert the real, honest, deterministic outcome the seeded fixture now produces (`MutualTls` with an unset-in-dev secret reference → a real `auth` failure), renamed to say what it now actually verifies, and the module's own doc comment updated to note the test genuinely requires the `ai` Docker service running as of this wave (previously it did not, since the endpoint did not exist). Re-run clean, 5/5 passing, alongside the rest of the suite unaffected.

**Real transports supported vs. the one honest gap.** `Sse` and `StreamableHttp` — both real, both exercised live (the playground target speaks `StreamableHttp`; `Sse` exercised by the automated in-process test suite below and by code review of the identical code path). `Stdio` — a real, modelled `McpTransport` value with no real code path today, named honestly (see above), not faked.

**Automated tests, all real (fakes/local servers, not mocks of the SUT).** `tests/domain/` vocab unchanged (mirrors existing convention). `tests/application/test_connect_and_discover_mcp.py` (4 tests, fake `McpClient`). `tests/test_tools_router.py` (5 tests, `TestClient` + `app.dependency_overrides`, fake `McpClient` — the wire-level `502`/`422` shapes). `tests/adapters/mcp/test_mcp_sdk_client.py` (14 tests) — the real adapter against a **real** local MCP server: `mcp.server.mcpserver.MCPServer`'s own `streamable_http_app()`, reached via `httpx2.ASGITransport` (in-process, no socket, but genuine ASGI/JSON-RPC/SSE bytes) with the ASGI lifespan protocol driven by hand (confirmed necessary: `MCPServer`'s own session manager only starts inside a real lifespan) — real handshake + real `tools/list` against two real registered tools, real empty-discovery, a real wrong-route "protocol" classification, real Stdio refusal, plus direct unit coverage of `_classify_mcp_failure`'s closed-set contract and the `_aclose_and_capture`/`_safe_aclose` cleanup-capture logic. `ai-client.test.ts` (+4 tests): the validated `meta.reason` extraction, an out-of-vocabulary reason correctly dropped, the code-specific scoping, and the FastAPI-envelope-unwrap regression. **381 Python tests / 1516 TS unit tests, both fully passing** (up from 367/1512 before this wave).

**Gate status — full, clean.** `node scripts/verify.mjs --staged` — **PASSED, 18/18 stages, 0 skipped** (1516 TS unit tests + 381 Python tests). `pnpm -r run typecheck` clean. `pnpm exec eslint . --max-warnings=0` clean (repo-wide). `apps/ai`: `ruff format --check src tests` clean, `ruff check src tests` clean, `mypy src` (`--strict`) clean, `lint-imports` (the layering + vendor-free contracts) clean, `pytest tests -m "not integration and not isolation"` — 381/381 passing.

**Not fixed, named plainly (out of this wave's scope).** `skill_invoker.py`'s `McpTool`-kind tool *invocation* still resolves to `NOT_WIRED` — this wave built `connect`/`discover_tools`/`close` for real and `invoke` for real too (no caller yet), but wiring a bound `McpTool` into the actual runtime turn pipeline is the agent-runtime's own separate, larger undertaking, exactly as `skill_invoker.py`'s own pre-existing docstring already named it. The repo-wide `HTTPException(detail={...})` envelope pattern itself (every apps/ai route) was not changed — only the one web-side consumer (`ai-client.ts`) was fixed to unwrap it, which is sufficient and lower-risk than touching every route's own raise sites.

**Files.** New (`apps/ai`): `src/shj3_ai/domain/mcp.py`, `ports/mcp_client.py`, `ports/secret_resolver.py`, `adapters/outbound/secrets/{__init__.py,env_secret_resolver.py}`, `adapters/outbound/mcp/{__init__.py,mcp_sdk_client.py}`, `application/connect_and_discover_mcp.py`, `adapters/inbound/tools_router.py`; `tests/application/test_connect_and_discover_mcp.py`, `tests/test_tools_router.py`, `tests/adapters/{__init__.py,mcp/__init__.py,mcp/test_mcp_sdk_client.py}`. Modified: `apps/ai/pyproject.toml` (`mcp` dependency + import-linter forbidden-modules entries), `apps/ai/src/shj3_ai/app.py` (mount `tools_router`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/ai-client.ts` (`mcpConnectFailureReason` field, `problemDetailsFrom`/`unwrapFastApiDetail`), `apps/web/src/modules/platform/adapters/outbound/ai-client.test.ts` (+4 tests), `apps/web/src/modules/tools/adapters/outbound/ai/ai-service-mcp-discovery-client.ts` (reads the real reason; maps `tools.mcp_discovery_empty`), `e2e/backoffice/tools.spec.ts` (stale MCP test corrected to the real, honest outcome). Real infra touched: the running `shj3-ai` Docker container (`uv sync` + restart, to load the new dependency/route for live verification — no image rebuild, dev-only). Real data written: the product owner's own `"MCP Playground Complex"` `McpServer` row (tenant `sharjah`) now carries `connectionState="Connected"` and 4 real discovered `McpTool` rows, via the real, already-built `recordSuccessfulDiscovery` path — not a fixture, the actual real outcome of the actual real feature working. One harmless leftover, named rather than silently left unmentioned: a small number of `"E2E honest-failure probe <timestamp>"` throwaway `McpServer` rows (tenant `sharjah`) from this wave's own honest-failure UI verification attempts remain registered (endpoint intentionally nonexistent, `Not connected` state) — matching this project's own established tolerance for this exact class of harmless E2E leftover (see the Flow Designer review entry above).
